# Prayer times outside Tunisia — Plan (Europe and the Arab countries)

Status: plan only, nothing is implemented. Written for `android-app/` on branch `codex`. It merges, on 2026-10-09, two plans written on 2026-10-08: one for Europe and one for the Arab League countries. Both share one engine, one place model and one time model. Each had been reviewed adversarially on its own: the Europe plan twice (about a hundred problems found in a first draft and 34 in the revision), the Arab plan once (85 confirmed findings and 72 minor ones). The merge itself was checked for lost content and broken references (Appendix A). On 2026-10-11 it was revised for the owner's audience note (below), then for the answers to the open questions (13; Appendix A), then for the owner's rule on official tables and the per-country answers that apply it (principle 5, 6.5, 8.8; Appendix A).

Its inputs:

- **A code map** of every Tunisia-specific assumption, file by file, by seven readers: formula, places, scheduling, Hijri, UI, build and derived features.
- **Europe:** research into how European Muslim bodies compute their times; three competing designs for the time model, judged against each other; numbers recomputed with an independent prototype.
- **Arab countries:** one researcher per Arab League country (21; Tunisia is done), each followed by an independent skeptic who re-fetched the official data and refitted it with their own solar code. Six topic studies: time zones on Android, the sun model and fitting, offline place data, what existing software uses, Ramadan and Eid practice, and Shia, Ibadi and Zaydi rules. ICOP's Salat Calendar and Accurate Times, and a prototype of the method Accurate Times documents (2026-10-09).
- **Reviews:** the rounds above. In the Arab round, nine reviewers (country facts in four groups, code claims, consistency with the Europe plan, engine and time zones, completeness, writing rules) each had every finding checked by a second skeptic.

**Goal (owner).** android-app computes prayer times for places in Europe and in every Arab League country with its own math: no web API and no prayer-time library at runtime. Java's `java.time`, the Android platform and the device's time-zone database are platform facilities, not third parties.

**Audience (owner, 2026-10-11).** The app is designed for Tunisians. The first release with places abroad serves Tunisians who live in Tunisia and sometimes travel: for Umrah and Hajj, to family, for work or on holiday. Internationalizing the app, for people who live in other countries, is a later phase (14). So the app keeps its Tunisian name and conventions everywhere (principle 9), and its new part is the place abroad, set by GPS or picked from a city list (D20). Tunisians who live abroad use the same features, and Google Play offers the release in their country too (M9); nothing else is built for them alone (Q28).

**How to read it.** Sections 1–6 and 9–14 hold for both regions (Appendix C keeps the Ramadan and Eid design for later), with region-specific parts inside 4 (4.2, 4.3), 9 (9.2; 9.3–9.6), 11 and 12. Section 7 is about Europe and section 8 about the Arab countries. For one region, read 1–6, its chapter, then 9–14. Decisions keep their former ids (D1–D16, A1–A18; D17–D19 and A19 come from the merge, D20 from the audience note), and so do the engine extensions (E1–E13; E14 comes from D17, E15 from A3, E16 from overseas France, E17 from Libya, E18 from Kosovo) and the milestones (M0–M9, AM0–AM10). The open questions have stable ids (Q1–Q37; Q26 and Q27 come from the merge, Q28 from the audience note, Q29–Q32 from the mosque dataset, Q33–Q37 from the France review). Appendix B maps the sections of the two former plans to this one.

**Contents.** 1 Goal and principles · 2 Scope and regions · 3 What the user gets · 4 Decisions for the owner · 5 Facts shared by both regions · 6 Shared design · 7 Europe · 8 The Arab countries · 9 Validation and tests · 10 Files · 11 Milestones · 12 Known limits · 13 Open questions · 14 Later · Appendix A: sources · Appendix B: map of the former plans · Appendix C: kept for later: Ramadan, Eid and the Hijri date abroad

**In short.**

*Both regions*

- **For Tunisians who travel.** The first release abroad is for Tunisian users on a trip. With auto-location on, a GPS fix moves the place abroad and back; without it, the user picks the place and nothing changes by itself (D20). Ramadan and Eid stay Tunisian (A10, A11). What only people living in other countries need waits for internationalization (14): another app name (A13), and each country's month names and week (D19). Shia methods are never offered (A4).
- The app already does the hard part for Tunisia. `InmPrayerFormula` reproduces INM's tables to the minute, offline. But it is welded to Tunisia: UTC+1 without summer time, delegation ids, and Fajr and Isha that always exist. Its output, `DayPrayerTimes(hour, minute)`, cannot say "after midnight", "before midnight" or "does not exist tonight".
- A **new engine** joins it in the shared module, in new files. It computes each time as a UTC instant from the sun at that moment. It never presents an estimate as a computed time: every time is *computed*, *estimated by a named rule*, or (in theory) *absent*. Its extensions (E1–E19: rounding per prayer, horizon models, sun-evaluation modes, zone points, Ramadan rules, dated revisions, sun models, an Asr before Maghrib, shifts by elevation, a twilight latitude, seasonal offsets, France's validity envelope…; E5 serves the fitting harness only and E11 is not built) let it reproduce official tables; each is a parameter whose default leaves the Europe presets unchanged. INM's code and its 565,020-time golden test stay unchanged, and so does the TV's behaviour.
- The engine takes a **method**: angles or fixed intervals, minute offsets, the Asr factor, a high-latitude rule, rounding, and for authorities their fitted conventions. Each method shows one of two chips (A6): «رسمية» where its times follow an official source for the place, «فلكية» where the app computes them. Each country gets a default; the user is asked once only where European practice is split (D4).
- The app gains a **place** that is not a Tunisian delegation: a GPS point or a city from a bundled catalogue, each with its IANA time zone. The behaviour "outside Tunisia, silencing switched off" goes away.
- Times can now fall after midnight or before it, and on summer-time days. The schedulers, adhkar windows and screens get the fixes this needs, guarded by tests that first pin today's Tunisian behaviour.
- **Ramadan and Eid stay Tunisian** in the first release (A10, A11): the date row keeps Tunisia's calendar everywhere, and the Eid silence and the Ramadan Isha bump apply only to Tunisian places. The official methods' own Ramadan rules, such as Saudi Isha at Maghrib + 120, still apply, from computed calendars (E8). The design for Ramadan and Eid abroad is kept in Appendix C.
- Tunisia keeps INM bit for bit. Every region is open in every build (owner, 2026-10-19).

*Europe*

- Europe has no single authority, and nearby mosques often differ by 30–50 minutes at Fajr. The app ships 27 presets tied to European bodies or practice, beside the shared reference methods (MWL, ISNA, Karachi 18°/18°, Umm al-Qura, Egyptian, INM's own…). Where practice is split (France, Belgium, the Netherlands, Switzerland, Germany, Italy, Spain, the UK, Sweden), the user is asked once, with the entries the owner's mosque dataset shows (5.6); in France the list puts the Rhône mosques' published table first in Rhône and Diyanet first in Bas-Rhin (D4). In the UK and Portugal the Asr factor is asked too.
- North of 48.56°N, the sun never reaches 18° below the horizon on some summer nights, so Fajr and Isha need a rule: about 19 such mornings in Paris, 60 in London, 122 in Oslo. Above about 65.7°N the sun itself does not set, and near the polar circle Asr can disappear. The default rule (D5) is the angle-based rule, applied every night, because it is what mosques there publish most often (5.6); it can move a real time on some nights, and the method sheet says so. Local Relative Estimation, the Muslim World League's 2009 rule, is an option.
- Isha can fall at 01:07 in Paris in June, and Fajr at 23:53 the evening before in Kazan in May.

*Arab countries*

- Unlike Europe, almost every Arab country has a **state authority whose timetable the mosques follow**. So the target is not "a sound method" but "the mosque's minute". The research fetched the official tables of 15 countries (plus Lebanon's Dar al-Fatwa Ramadan imsakiya, a Shia office in Lebanon and one relayed week from Bahrain; Bahrain's full 1448 calendar was found on 2026-10-11, Q7). Most of them can be **reproduced by computation**, as INM's was: Oman 98 % of minutes exact, Saudi Arabia 98 % per prayer (97.8 % or more on held-out years), Algeria 94–95 % (and its 67 other places' monthly offsets derived from its own rule), Jordan about 98 % on the 2023 calendar its zones were fitted on (and 185 of 186 minutes exact on a held-out winter sample of 31 zone-days of 2025 with the method of 8.6), Qatar 96–99 %, Morocco 98 % for four of the six times, Libya 97 % (with the low-precision sun its table was made with), Bahrain 95 % (all within 1 minute). Almost every miss is 1 minute. The exceptions are KACST's previous-date days in Saudi Arabia (up to 2 minutes), a few data errors, Libya's Asr in Jalo and Awjila (+ 8 min) and two Algerian towns whose Dhuhr offset sits on a whole minute (8.1, 8.3, 12).
- What makes this possible is fitting each authority's **conventions** from its tables:
  - the rounding direction per prayer (Saudi Arabia rounds four times up and sunrise down);
  - a horizon lower than the textbook one (UAE, Jordan, Morocco);
  - fixed minutes (Oman + 5 on three prayers, Yemen + 10 on Dhuhr);
  - the reference points of a zone (Bahrain computes Fajr and sunrise at the eastern tip of Dilmunia and the other times at the western tip of Salman Town);
  - "perpetual" tables computed once for a fixed year (Qatar, Kuwait, Palestine), which the fits read at that year and the app shows as printed, at that year (A8, revised by the owner on 2026-10-17);
  - rules that change on a date (Qatar moved Fajr 1 minute earlier on 2020-08-20).
- Most authorities publish **zones, not formulas**: 86 in Oman, 41 locations in the UAE, about 190 localities in Morocco, 15 zones in Jordan, about 80 cities in Egypt. Wherever an authority's tables apply, the app maps the user's point to the authority's own place by the authority's own attachment rule, otherwise to the nearest of its places with no reach limit, and computes with that place's fitted parameters plus its published or derived offset (Algeria: the wilaya's listed place among the 70, with the monthly offset derived on the phone from the ministry's own rule, A16). A place the authority does not name, bulk attachments included, takes the authority's method at its own point only where the attached table would put a prayer before it begins there, or its sunrise after the place's own, by more than the table itself does at its own place on its worst day plus 4 minutes (the validity guard, 6.5). By default the app computes at the user's point only where the authority computes for any point (Saudi Arabia beyond 25 km of its 173 listed places) or publishes no table for the place (Iraq's Kurdistan Region) (A2).
- Neither the generic high-latitude rule nor a polar fallback ever acts there: every Arab League place reaches every twilight angle every night.
- For four countries **no usable official table was found** (Sudan, Somalia, Djibouti, Comoros). Sudan's tables are printed only. For the other three nothing was found online, including in web searches of 2026-10-11 (Q16). There each country gets its own formula, fitted from the owner's mosque dataset (A3, 5.6): 18°/18° in Sudan, MWL in Somalia and Djibouti, and the Comoros' traditional Moroni table. Lebanon's Sunni authority (Dar al-Fatwa) publishes a Ramadan imsakiya for Beirut (1445 is on its site) but no year-round table was found; the imsakiya equals a perpetual year table that a mosque-screen service serves under Dar al-Fatwa's name, so its profile, `lb_dar_al_fatwa`, Lebanon's default (A4), is fitted from both, gives Beirut's table to every place in Lebanon (the imsakiya prints no other place, offset or rule; A2) except places on the north-eastern border and in the far south, which the validity guard gives the same method at their own point (6.5, 12); Beirut's table shows «رسمية» and those places «فلكية» (6.11, Q10).
- **ICOP's Salat Calendar** (astronomycenter.net) gives a second, expert opinion: a preset per country that agrees with the fitted official rules in most countries (8.5). It is used only as supportive evidence: to seed fits (Sudan's among them, A3) and inform open questions. It never replaces an official table. ICOP's Accurate Times is the official software of Jordan and the UAE.
- Iraq, Lebanon and Bahrain have large **Shia** communities with their own times (Maghrib after the eastern redness, a Jafari midnight). The app never offers a Shia method (A4): the authority's table is the default there, as in every Arab country.
- Three zone families keep **summer time** (Egypt, Lebanon, Palestine). Morocco went back to permanent GMT on 2026-09-20, so phones with time-zone data older than tzdata 2026c show a clock 1 hour ahead there.
- **Ramadan changes two official methods**: Saudi Isha moves from Maghrib + 90 to + 120, and Qatar's Maghrib (and so its Isha) is 1 minute later.

---

## 1. Goal and principles

1. **Own math, offline.** Every time is computed on the phone from formulas written down in this repository. No network call, no prayer-time or astronomy library. Official timetables, published tables and open-source libraries are used only as references, as test data and as the source of fitted parameters, never at runtime.
2. **Tunisia keeps its numbers.** Tunisian delegations keep INM's formula, the only method in Tunisia (D7), and give exactly today's times. `InmPrayerFormula`, `InmPrayerTimes`, `PrayerFormulaSettings`, `delegation_params.json` and the TV's `formula.js` stay byte-identical. The one exception is a separate fix: Arram (delegation 495) gains INM parameters so the current formula computes it (owner, 2026-10-11). The TV and the desktop keep today's behaviour; a few of their shared files gain defaulted parameters, keys or extra data they never use (6.12). The few visible Tunisian changes are listed in section 3.
3. **Never present an estimate as a computed time.** A time is computed (the sun reaches the angle) or estimated by a rule the user can see and name. Today the code silently turns a missing Fajr into solar midnight (`InmPrayerFormula.kt:208`, `coerceIn(-1.0, 1.0)`); the new engine never does.
4. **Instants, not wall clock.** Times are computed as UTC instants and converted with the time zone's rules for that moment, so summer-time days and times on another civil day are correct by construction.
5. **Reproduce the official source; otherwise the method is the user's.** Where an authority publishes the times mosques call, the default reproduces its times everywhere its tables apply (owner, 2026-10-11): at the authority's own places, with their published offsets or offsets deduced from how the authority makes them, and at any other place by the authority's own rule for attaching it, otherwise the nearest official place in the country, with no reach limit. Tunisia is the first case: INM's official times are per delegation, and every Tunisian place takes its nearest delegation (D7). The authority's method at the user's exact coordinates is only an option («حسب موقعي بالضبط»), or the default where the authority itself computes for any point (KACST). One guard applies (6.5): a place the authority does not name itself never gets a prayer before it begins there, or a sunrise after its own, beyond the margin the authority's own table takes at its own places and a 4-minute allowance (6.5); such a place gets the authority's method at its own point, the way the authority computes its own places. Where no official table is known (the chooser countries and the `mwl` defaults of 6.4, Greece until AM0 looks for the Thrace muftis' tables, the A3 countries and Iraq's Kurdistan Region), the app offers sound defaults, says what it uses, and makes it easy to match one's own mosque. In mainland France, where no authority publishes and neighbouring mosques differ, every time stays inside a validity envelope set from the recognised positions, and the bounded custom method lets a user match their mosque within it (owner, 2026-10-12; 6.11, 7.7). The app bundles no mosques (owner, 2026-10-12).
6. **Say how sure we are.** Each method carries its evidence: a published definition, fitted from official tables, or approximate. When fitted, it also carries the measured share of exact minutes, which «طريقتك» shows with the tier-A gate (9.5). The chip says only whether the times are official (owner, 2026-10-20): «رسمية» where they follow an official source for the place (a national body's own definition in its own country: Tunisia's INM, the Islamic Community of Bosnia and Herzegovina, Turkey's Diyanet; `sa_umm_al_qura` anywhere in Saudi Arabia; an authority's table at the place's zone in that zone's own country), «فلكية» everywhere else (6.11). A country without official data is never presented as official.
7. **Never stop silently.** A location abroad is not an error. Nothing switches auto-silence off because the phone is outside Tunisia, and a silence setting that does not fit every day is checked when it is edited and capped as D16 says.
8. **One engine.** Both regions share the engine, the place type and the time model. Every extension is a parameter whose default keeps the Europe presets' output unchanged; only the presets 6.3 names change on purpose.
9. **Same app, for Tunisians.** Arabic only, with the Tunisian conventions everywhere, abroad included: the app's name (A13), «جانفي» and the other Gregorian month names, Tunisia's Hijri month names, and the week from Monday (D19). Western digits (0–9) everywhere. Signed numbers and percentages in Arabic text sit inside an LTR isolate. No prefs migration while the phone app is in its dev phase.

## 2. Scope and regions

- **Audience and phases.** The first release with places abroad is for Tunisians who travel (front matter). It ships only when both regions are done (owner, 2026-10-11). The places below are where their trips go. Tunisians who live abroad use the same features; for them the release is also offered on Google Play outside Tunisia (Q28, M9). Internationalization, for people who live in other countries, is a later phase (14).
- **Tunisia** is always open and keeps INM.
- **Europe**: the Europe list of D2 (the countries GeoNames files under Europe, plus Turkey and Cyprus). GPS works anywhere.
- **Overseas France** (owner, 2026-10-11): Réunion, Mayotte, Guadeloupe, Martinique, French Guiana, New Caledonia, French Polynesia, Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon, Saint-Barthélemy, Saint-Martin and Wallis-et-Futuna. Each keeps its own country code, catalogue rows and default (D2, 6.4, 7.6), so France's list never applies there. They sit behind the Europe switch. Wallis-et-Futuna keeps UTC+12 at 176°10'W, east of the 180° meridian: at that point its solar noon, 11:28 to 11:59 local time through the year, falls between 23:28 and 23:59 UTC on the previous date. So the engine picks each prayer day's solar cycle from the place's civil date, never from the UTC date (6.2, Solver, step 1; 9.1).
- **The Arab League**: its 22 members. The Western Sahara localities that Morocco's Habous publishes are listed under Morocco (A1). Mayotte is not an Arab place; it is part of overseas France above.
- **Land neighbours** of the Arab countries (6.8) get the default for their country, never an Arab authority profile. Israel is not in the first release (14). In Ceuta and Melilla, `ma_habous` at their Habous localities is also offered under «طرق أخرى» (6.8).
- **Places outside both regions** (Pakistan, for example, like the land neighbours Iran and Chad) get `mwl` (D4). Only a place whose country is unknown also shows «تأكيد الطريقة» (6.4).
- **High latitude.** Every Arab League place lies between about 12.4°S (Comoros) and 37.4°N (northern Iraq and Syria). The deepest angle any preset or custom method can use is 20°, and it is reached every night south of about 46.5°N (18° south of 48.56°N). So neither the D5 rule nor a polar fallback ever acts there; only the seasonal curve of `mcw`, `london_unified` and `gmp`'s Fajr and the dated summer form of `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost` (7.4) can, if the user picks one. In its own zone, no Arab place has Isha after midnight or Fajr before it; only cross-zone display can do that (a Casablanca place on a Muscat phone shows Isha after midnight), and the display of 6.6 marks it. In Europe, the rules of M2 (7.4) act from 44.5°N for Diyanet's presets, wherever a method's angle fails (north of 48.56°N for 18°, about 46.5°N for the deepest 20°), from about 45°N for the angle-based rule (D5's default, and `mwl_angle`), and at any latitude for the seasonal curve of `mcw`, `london_unified` and `gmp`'s Fajr and, between its dates, the summer form of `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost`.
- **Every region open (owner, 2026-10-19).** Each region used to sit behind its own developer switch in `PrefsManager` (read by `resolveFix` and the picker, a debug-only «مناطق التطوير» row in the settings) until its milestones were done; the owner removed the switches once both regions were built, so every build, a release included, takes a place in any country, and the picker lists every country's cities. From M5 on, the outside-Tunisia gate (`OutsideTunisia`, `disabled_outside_tunisia`, `RESULT_OUTSIDE_TUNISIA`) is gone in every mode.
- **Order.** Arab countries are built first (A15, decided), then Europe, and both ship together. The shared core is built once, and section 11 orders the milestones that way.

## 3. What the user gets

| Moment | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Leaving Tunisia | With auto-location on, the first fix abroad moves the place (Travel, below). Without it, nothing changes until the user picks a place: the delegation's times stay, shown and silenced at their real instants (D20). |
| Back in Tunisia | With auto-location on, the first fix in Tunisia gives the nearest delegation, as today. Without it, the place abroad stays until the user picks their delegation; no home is remembered (D20). |
| First run anywhere outside Tunisia (installed on a trip, or by a Tunisian who lives there) | Onboarding gains a location step: «تحديد موقعي» (GPS) or «اختيار مدينة». If the step is skipped, the app guesses from the network country or the phone's zone, shows a «تأكيد الموقع» banner, and still schedules. |
| First run in France, Belgium, the Netherlands, Switzerland, Germany, Italy, Spain, the UK or Sweden | A one-screen chooser lists the methods the mosques there use (5.6), each with today's Fajr and Isha, so the user can pick the one that matches their mosque (D4). In France the list is the same everywhere, except that in Rhône the Conseil des Mosquées du Rhône's published table comes first and in Bas-Rhin Diyanet's (D4); every French time stays inside a validity envelope set from the recognised positions (7.7). In the UK and Portugal the Asr factor is also asked once, since about half the mosques there use the Hanafi Asr. Other European countries get their default and one line naming it, with «تغيير». |
| First run in overseas France | Réunion: the Centre Islamique de la Réunion's table for the place's region (`re_cir`: the CIR's four regions and its elevation shifts), with the Asr factor asked once. Mayotte: a two-entry chooser, the island's traditional table first, then MWL. Martinique and French Guiana: MWL as their published pages give it (`mq_ccim`, `gf_a2cmgf`), and the other territories MWL, each with one line naming it (7.6). |
| First run in an Arab country | The authority's method is set automatically, with one line naming it and its chip (6.11): «رسمية» with the table it follows, for example «حسب جداول وزارة الأوقاف والشؤون الدينية – جدول مسقط», or «فلكية». |
| First run in Iraq, Lebanon or Bahrain | As in any Arab country: the authority's table (the Sunni Endowment, Dar al-Fatwa, the national calendar). No Shia method is offered (A4). |
| First run in Sudan, Somalia, Djibouti or Comoros | The country's own formula, fitted from mosque times (the owner's dataset, A3), with its chip and the line «لم نجد جدولًا رسميًا منشورًا على الإنترنت لهذا البلد.» No chooser (A3). |
| GPS in Paris | The place is saved as a point rounded to about 1 km and labelled «قرب باريس». Times come from the engine, and auto-silence stays on. |
| The list of times | Each time is in the phone's clock (D8). An estimated time carries «تقديري»; tapping it explains in one line which rule produced it and why. A time on the next civil day carries «بعد منتصف الليل»; one on the previous civil day carries «قبل منتصف الليل». Where the method has zones (6.5), a zone line, «جدول: مسقط», tappable to pick a neighbouring zone. When the place's time zone differs from the phone's, the place's UTC offset, for example «UTC+4», in an LTR isolate. |
| Prayer-tab settings | New rows: «طريقة الحساب», «وقت العصر» (majority or Hanafi), «الليالي القصيرة» (shown only where it matters: never in Tunisia, and in the Arab countries only if the user picks `mcw`, `london_unified` or `gmp`), and «تعديل الأوقات بالدقائق» (one stepper per time, −30 to +30). Where the method has zones, also «الجدول الرسمي المعتمد: مسقط», or «الجدول المعتمد: الرون» where the zone is not an authority's table (6.5, 6.11). A Tunisian place shows none of them: INM is the only method there (D7). The former «مصادر البيانات» row was removed (owner, 2026-10-19): the method's source and the place names' credit are at the foot of «طريقتك». |
| Friday | The Jumu'a silence starts at the authority's Friday time where it sets one (UAE: 12:45 by federal circular since 2026-01-02, in all seven emirates, Q13), otherwise at the place's or country's Friday time from the owner's dataset or a local publication (Martinique, French Guiana) (`SilenceDefaults`, 6.9), otherwise at Dhuhr (A18), and ends at the default of D12: 13:15 Tunis time in Tunisia; elsewhere 45 minutes after that start. |
| Ramadan | Saudi Isha is Maghrib + 120 on Umm al-Qura's Ramadan days. Qatar's Maghrib and Isha are 1 minute later on Umm al-Qura's Ramadan days (Q3). No other change was found in the countries checked through Ramadan, and Syria's and Mauritania's rules are taken to hold all year (Q12, Q6); Kuwait's and Yemen's are not known yet; Bahrain's 1448 calendar has none (Q7), nor has Libya's table (its row for 18 May 2018, in Ramadan, equals 18 May 2025, 8.2). Outside Tunisia, nothing else changes in Ramadan: no Isha silence bump, no Ramadan rows (A11, A14). |
| Hijri date and Eid | Tunisia's Hijri calendar and announcements at every place, as today, with Tunisian month names (D19). Outside Tunisia there is no Eid silence and no Eid row (A10, A11). |
| Travel | With auto-location on, moving from Tunis to Paris (or Paris to London, or Riyadh to Muscat) switches the place and the zone by the travel rule of 6.8, and the method by the per-country choice of 6.4, recomputes everything and reschedules. A notification «تم تغيير الموقع إلى …» names the new place. |
| Tunisia | Same times, and INM stays the only method (D7). Visible changes: the onboarding location step and, if skipped, its «تأكيد الموقع» banner; country groups below the governorates in the picker; neutral wording of a few location texts; the Dhuhr silence starts at a fixed 12:40 instead of at the adhan (D12); D16's check of edited silence windows and its cap; a silence start within 12 h of the adhan and an end at most 24 h after it, where today's sheets take up to 7 days and the slider's edge extension has no limit (6.9). A delegation kept on a phone set to another zone (a traveller) is now shown and silenced at the right instant, where today it is off by the zone difference, and its 13:15 Dhuhr and Jumu'a end is read in Tunis time (D12). Today's «أنت خارج تونس، تم إيقاف الكتم التلقائي» goes, and auto-silence stays on (D20). |

## 4. Decisions for the owner

Every decision is settled. On 2026-10-11 the owner set the audience (front matter) and decided the rows one by one, several with the owner's mosque dataset (5.6) and D6 with a web research (7.4); D9 was left to the best engineering choice, A9 follows from D8, and A10 and A11 close D10, D11 and D18. The decided rows start with **Decided** and keep the former recommendation among the alternatives; A9 starts with **Follows from D8**, and D10, D11 and D18 start with **Closed by**, their former recommendation kept in Appendix C. Only wording remains, with the mockups. The ids of the two former plans are kept: D for those first raised for Europe or the shared core, A for those first raised for the Arab countries. D17–D19 and A19 come from the merge itself: points where the two plans disagreed and neither settled it. The owner's audience note of 2026-10-11 added D20.

**When.**

- Before building starts (M0/AM0): nothing left to decide. D4's country defaults and choosers are built in M3/AM3 (11).
- With the mockups (M4/AM4): the wording of A6's chips and lines.
- Before the release, which carries both regions (M9): A13's listing lines are written, and the release is offered on Google Play outside Tunisia (Q28). The app's name stays; renaming waits for internationalization (14).

D5 and D6 also matter to the Arab track: every authority profile and generic preset carries their values (6.2).

### 4.1 Shared

| # | Question | Recommended | Alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| A15 | Which region first | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): Arab countries are built first; the app is released only with both regions.** No high-latitude rules to build (M2 skipped), official tables to validate against in 17 countries (Bahrain's 1448 calendar and Lebanon's Dar al-Fatwa Beirut table were added on 2026-10-11, Q7, Q10), and it covers Saudi Arabia (Umrah and Hajj) and the neighbours Libya and Algeria. Europe follows on the same core; it needs M2, since Paris already has about 19 mornings without an 18° Fajr (7.1). Places outside both regions stay behind the Europe switch until M2 and M8 are done (2). | Europe first; both built together; release the Arab countries first, alone (the former recommendation), optionally also opening places south of 45°N outside both regions (Iran, Pakistan, Chad…) with `mwl` |
| A13 | App name and Play listing | **Decided (owner's audience note, 2026-10-11): keep the name «أوقات الصلاة تونس»**: the app is for Tunisians, on a trip too. Renaming waits for internationalization (14). **Both listing lines decided** (owner, 2026-10-11). The package id stays. Before the release, the listing names each authority whose table a profile reproduces, with its official site as the source, and states that the app is independent and does not represent any government or ministry. Google Play requires both for apps that communicate government information. | Rename before the release, the former recommendation: the name does not suit an Egyptian, Saudi or European user |
| D20 | Trips (new, 2026-10-11) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): no home delegation, and nothing without auto-location.** A place abroad replaces the delegation like any other place: `setPlace(Coordinates)` clears the locality keys (6.8). With auto-location on, the Travel rule of 6.8 moves the place both ways. Without it, the app does nothing when the phone travels: the place stays until the user changes it, and its times are still shown and silenced at their real instants (6.6). | Keep the home delegation and bring it back on return; notice the trip from the network country or the time zone and offer the picker (both earlier recommendations) |
| D19 | Month names and week abroad (formerly European places only) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): Tunisian everywhere.** The forced `ar-TN-u-nu-latn` locale stays, so «جانفي» at every place; Hijri months as Tunisia writes them (ربيع الثاني, جمادى الثانية); the week from Monday. These are the names the user reads at home. Per-country names and week wait for internationalization (14): `ar-<CC>` for the Gregorian names and the week, the app's own table for the Hijri names (6.10, 6.11, Q11). | Follow the place's country, the former recommendation for Arab places (يناير in Egypt and the Gulf, كانون الثاني in the Levant and Iraq) and for Europe (يناير, since CLDR has no Arabic data for European countries and falls back to `ar`) |
| D7 | Can a user in Tunisia pick another method? | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): no.** Tunisia has one method, the existing formula that matches INM. A Tunisian place shows no method, Asr or adjustment row, and its times are exactly today's. A fix that the country step puts in Tunisia, offshore included, takes its nearest delegation (6.8). `tunisia_18_18` stays a preset for places abroad. | Yes, with INM as the default (the former recommendation); allow only the Asr factor and minute adjustments |
| D8 | Clock for display and alarms when the place's zone differs from the phone's | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): the phone's clock everywhere.** If the phone's clock is wrong, for any reason, that is the user's to fix, not the app's. When the place's zone differs from the phone's, one line gives the place's UTC offset (signed, in an LTR isolate). Alarms ring at the computed instants. No display in the place's own clock is planned. | The place's clock, from the start or later |
| D9 | How far the app's time model changes now | **Decided (the owner left the choice to engineering, 2026-10-11): instants first.** The phone moves to `PrayerDay` instants and a `PrayerClock` now, with no adapter (6.6). Why: the fixes of 6.9 already edit most of the files that the move to instants touches; the adapter's conventions (hours outside 0–23, the autumn-overlap side channel, a source scan for hour arithmetic) exist only to be removed later, and internationalization makes "later" certain; the M0 snapshots at `Africa/Tunis` give the rewrite its equivalence oracle. The cost: about 30 main and 17 test files change their prayer type in one milestone, and the regions ship a little later. | Staged, the former recommendation: an adapter to today's `DayPrayerTimes` first, instants once a region is validated on the phone |
| D12 | Default silence window for Dhuhr and Jumu'a (until the user edits it) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11).** The Dhuhr silence starts at a fixed 12:40 in Tunisia and at adhan + 5 min everywhere else. Tunisia keeps 13:15 as the end for Dhuhr and Jumu'a. It is read as 13:15 in `Africa/Tunis`, so a traveller who keeps the delegation on a phone set elsewhere is silenced until the Tunisian instant, with no offset exception (6.6). Elsewhere the end is adhan + 45 min, counted on Fridays from the A18 Jumu'a time, which the owner's dataset of Friday times, or a local publication (Martinique, French Guiana, 7.6), sets per country and place where no authority does (`SilenceDefaults`, 6.9; European values in summer and winter time, Q30); so the UAE window is 12:45–13:30. Tunisia's 13:15 stays (owner): with the most common Tunisian Jumu'a at 13:00 and a 20-min khutba, it covers about 43–45 % of mosques, against 90 % for 13:30 (5.6). | Adhan + 45 for every `Coordinates` place, and for a delegation on a phone not on +01:00 (the former recommendation); keep 13:15 everywhere and rely on D16 |
| D13 | Authority timetables as test data (the repository is public) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): test against full tables, commit none.** Official tables are used in full from the git-ignored `test-data-local/`, by tests that skip when the folder is absent; no authority row is committed. CI tests only licence-clean data: JPL Horizons and USNO (US government), the MIT-licensed adhan fixtures and the Python reference. Mawaqit is never used (its robots.txt disallows Anthropic agents). A7 applies the same to the Arab authorities. | About 24 pinned rows per authority after a terms check (the former recommendation); full tables where terms are silent |
| A7 | Arab authority data in the public repo | **Decided with D13 (owner, 2026-10-11).** Full tables stay in the git-ignored `test-data-local/`, and no row is committed. The zone lists (names, ids, published coordinates) in `assets/official-zones/` differ: the app needs them at runtime, so each is committed after its authority's terms are read. Algeria's PDF forbids copying without permission. QCH's daily town offsets are not stored: a fitted point per town reproduces them within 1 minute (8.8, Q3). | Pinned rows after a terms check; full tables wherever terms are silent |
| D16 | A saved silence start or end that does not fit every day | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): validate, propose, cap.** When the user edits a silence window, the editor checks it over the whole year at the place, as the adhkar reminders already are (6.9). If the end would come before the start on some days, it says on which days and proposes an alternative that fits all year (an end relative to the adhan, or a later fixed end). If a day still does not fit (prayer times shift over the year, or the place changed), the end that day moves later, to at most 30 min after the time the user chose, and never to the next day; if even that is before the start, the end that day keeps the offset from the adhan that it had when the user saved it (owner, 2026-10-11), so the prayer is still silenced: an end of 13:00 saved when Dhuhr was at 12:25 (adhan + 35) becomes 14:30 on a Paris day with Dhuhr at 13:55. The offset is stored with the setting. A fixed start takes the occurrence of its clock time nearest the adhan (within ±12 h). This also applies to existing Tunisian settings. | Adhan + 45 with a one-time notice (the former recommendation); today's rule, where an end before the start rolls a full day (about 23 h of silence); no silence on a day the capped end still does not fit (the plan's earlier reading) |
| A9 | Stale time-zone data on the phone | **Follows from D8 (owner, 2026-10-11): nothing.** No notice and no app-owned rules: a wrong phone clock is the user's to fix. Computed alarms stay right on a phone with stale time-zone data and automatic time; clock times the user typed ring by the phone's clock (6.7, 12). | A one-line notice when the phone's tzdata is older than the place zone's rules (the former recommendation); an app-owned offset table for the Arab zones |
| D17 | Sun model for reproducing official tables | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): the most precise option for each country**, even if that means several formulas and parameter sets. The engine gets an extension E14 `sunModel` from the start (M1): the existing Meeus sun, VSOP87 with Delta T, the prototype's sun (8.6), whose term tables ship in the shared module, or the low-precision TEXTBOOK sun that Libya's table was made with (8.2). Each authority profile uses the model, and the parameters, that reproduce its tables best in AM2; Jordan and the UAE start from VSOP87, as their 185 of 186 did. INM keeps its own Meeus path (principle 2). | Meeus everywhere, with E14 only if a fit misses the tier-A gate (the former recommendation). Switching models changes about 1–1.5 % of rounded minutes (5.2). |
| D18 | Hijri marks outside the Arab League, and a badge for an announced start | **Closed by A11 (owner, 2026-10-11).** No Hijri source outside Tunisia in the first release, so there is no mark to choose. The former recommendation is kept with Appendix C. | — |

RU: Moscow and Moscow oblast → `dum_rf`, the one table DUM RF publishes for both, with the validity guard (6.5); Tatarstan → `ru_dum_rt`, DUM RT's district tables; elsewhere in Russia MWL (owner rule of 2026-10-11: an official source's tables wherever they apply). Ask once, with these entries (6.4): FR `mdf_12` 12° / 12° (D15), `isna`, `diyanet_eu`, `mwl`, ordered by département (owner, 2026-10-11): in Rhône (69) `rhone_ctir`, the Conseil des Mosquées du Rhône's published table, is added and listed first, and in Bas-Rhin (67) `diyanet_eu`, the table the Grande Mosquée de Strasbourg shows, is listed first; that first entry is also the area's fallback, and a pick is kept for all of France; every French time stays inside the validity envelope (E19, 7.7), and no mosques are bundled (owner, 2026-10-12); BE `karachi` 18°/18°, `be_sunrise_90`, `diyanet_eu`, `mwl`; NL `diyanet_eu`, `mwl`, `isna`; CH `isna`, `fajr15_isha90`, `mwl`, `diyanet_eu`, `mdf_12`; DE and SE `diyanet_eu` (preselected), `mwl`; IT `mwl`, `isna`; ES `mwl` (preselected), `isna`; GB `isna`, `mwl`. A London profile fitted from the shared London timetable is later work (14). | The plan's earlier lists; a silent default everywhere (MWL, or Muslim Pro's choices) |
| D5 | Generic high-latitude rule, for methods without their own | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11, with the mosque dataset): the angle-based rule, every night.** Isha no later than sunset + angle/60 of the night and Fajr no earlier than sunrise − angle/60 of the night. It is the most common exact match among the calendars north of 45°N (5.6), and no calendar uses Local Relative Estimation; it may largely reflect apps' default settings (about 28 % of calendars), and the owner kept it knowing that. The method sheet says that it moves the real 18° Fajr on some nights (Paris 51, London 32 a year) and the 17° Isha on 25–65. Local Relative Estimation with ICOP's ramp (the MWL Fiqh Council with ICOP, 2009, made for 48.6–66.6°) stays an option under «الليالي القصيرة», and so do the Dutch clock caps (Fajr not before 03:30, Isha not after 23:30, every night) and Belgium's similar caps (7.4). **One exception (owner, 2026-10-11):** `mdf_12` carries the rule on absent nights only, ramped (7.4), so it keeps the real 12° time in mainland France, where 12° is reached every night. No Musulmans de France text gives a summer rule, and the northern French 12° calendars are split (65 of 108 cap, 43 keep the real angle; 5.6). The every-night cap stays an option under «الليالي القصيرة». `luxembourg_shoura` takes the same rule: the Shoura's current 2026 table keeps the real 13° on the 55 nights (24 May to 18 July) where the cap would move its Fajr and Isha by up to 21 min (Q37). `isna` and `mwl` keep the rule every night: 43 of 51 northern French 15° calendars cap. | Local Relative Estimation (the owner's earlier lean; no mosque in the data uses it); one-seventh or middle of the night; nearest day |
| D6 | When the sun does not rise or set, or Asr does not exist (near and above the polar circle) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11), after a web research into what polar mosques publish (7.4).** The default is the 5-hour minimum day and night of the Istanbul 2021 declaration (Diyanet with the ECFR): sunrise no later than Dhuhr − 2:30, Maghrib no earlier than Dhuhr + 2:30, and in summer a night of at least 5 h. For every preset but Diyanet's it acts only at places whose year has a day without a sunrise, a sunset or an Asr (from about 65.7°N), so Luleå, Oulu and Reykjavik keep their real times. Asr when the noon sun stays below the horizon: the midpoint between Dhuhr and Maghrib, as northern Norway does. The Diyanet presets keep Diyanet's own clamp everywhere, with Asr = Dhuhr. In all of Norway and on Svalbard the default is `irn`, which copies Islamic Council Norway's tables, one per municipality, as bonnetid.info publishes them and the Tromsø mosque republishes them in the north (from 63.56°N, Mecca's day on local noon in summer and, north of 66.33°N, in the polar night; IRN's own summer rule further south); its switch dates are fitted from those tables (owner; all of Norway since 2026-10-11, 7.4). | Nearest latitude, the former interim rule (no publisher uses it; it gives a 53-minute day in Tromsø in December); Mecca's day everywhere; Asr = Dhuhr for every method |
| D10 | Hijri source for a European place | **Closed by A11 (owner, 2026-10-11).** The calendar stays Tunisian everywhere in the first release (6.10). The former recommendation (Umm al-Qura on the device, per-event "follow my mosque" dates, later the Istanbul 2016 table) is kept in Appendix C. | — |
| D11 | Default Eid prayer time outside Tunisia and the Arab League | **Closed by A10 (owner, 2026-10-11)**: no Eid support outside Tunisia yet. The former recommendation, max(sunrise + 20 min, 08:00 at the place), is kept in Appendix C. | — |
| D14 | "Match my mosque" calibrator (type a few published times, get the closest method) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): later (v2).** v1 shows each method's Fajr and Isha for today, and per-time minute adjustments. In v1 a user matches a mosque with the bounded «مخصّصة» and the minute steppers (6.11, 2026-10-12). | In v1 |
| D15 | Presets with thin evidence: `gmp`, `dum_rf`, `mcw`, `london_unified`, `uk_18_nearest_day`, `mdf_12`, `cil_lisbon`, `ecfr_90`, `mwl_angle` | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11), published source first.** Only under «طرق أخرى», with the «فلكية» chip (6.11), except where the mosque dataset shows a practice: `mdf_12` becomes 12° / 12° with no minute offsets, a France and Switzerland chooser entry and France's fallback outside Rhône (69) and Bas-Rhin (67) (D4). The offsets the French calendars show may come from one app template, and the only text of Musulmans de France's own minutes (Fajr − 5, Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 5) is a 2012 reproduction of its GEDIS calendar, too old to adopt (owner, 2026-10-11); no text of 2020 or later gives any (Q32, answered 2026-10-11). Its summer rule acts on absent nights only (D5); Belgium's common practice gets its own entry, `be_sunrise_90` (Fajr = sunrise − 90, Isha 18°). `dum_rf`, since re-derived from DUM RF's own monthly Moscow tables (Q22), is «رسمية» at its Moscow zone (6.11); it is the default in Moscow and Moscow oblast, the area its sheet names (2026-10-11), and stays under «طرق أخرى» elsewhere. `gmp` follows the Grande Mosquée de Paris's own 2026 timetable (Q21); it stays under «طرق أخرى», since only 10 of 350 Île-de-France mosques in the dataset follow it. (The former `algeria` and `morocco` presets are replaced by the fitted profiles `dz_marw` and `ma_habous`, 6.3.) | `mdf_12` with the calendars' offsets (Fajr − 4, Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 3, Isha + 5); all nine only under «طرق أخرى»; hide them |

### 4.3 Arab countries

| # | Question | Recommended | Alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Scope; Western Sahara; Mayotte | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): Western Sahara under Morocco.** The scope is the 22 Arab League members. Western Sahara localities are listed and labelled as Moroccan and use Morocco's Habous tables, which cover them; their zone stays Africa/El_Aaiun, which has Morocco's rules. Mayotte (France, claimed by the Comoros) is not an Arab place. **Reopened (owner, 2026-10-11):** it gets a two-entry chooser, `yt_traditional`, the island's traditional table, preselected, then `mwl` (7.6). The island's one published table and 5 of its 7 dataset mosques follow it; `mwl` matches none. | City name and no country name (the former recommendation); under «الصحراء الغربية» (GeoNames code EH); give Mayotte the Comoros profile; Mayotte with the places outside both regions (`mwl`), as first decided |
| A2 | Default target | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11), per country, published source first (5.6).** The authority's official zone or city table wherever the authority publishes one: Jordan (mapped by the ministry's own rule, Q29: Amman, Balqa, Zarqa and Madaba follow Amman's table except the places it names apart, and its further places, with the places nearest them in their governorate, take Amman's time plus the monthly offset derived on the phone, as the calendar tells places it does not name to do; every place in Jordan gets one of these tables, never its own coordinates by default unless the validity guard acts, 6.5, 8.8), Palestine (the Al-Aqsa table plus the offset of the nearest place Dar al-Ifta lists, whatever the distance; in the Gaza Strip, the ministry's one table, A17; 8.8), Kuwait (Kuwait City's table plus the published offset of the nearest listed place, Kuwait City included, whatever the distance), Morocco (the nearest of Habous's Moroccan localities, Western Sahara included, with no reach limit: Habous publishes no rule for other places, 8.8), Oman (all 86 zones, every point fitted from the ministry's tables: the zone named after a town within 10 km, otherwise the nearest zone with no reach limit; 8.8), the UAE (Awqaf's own list attaches its 319 areas to its 41 locations; any other place takes, among the locations serving its emirate, the one with the nearest listed area, with no reach limit, 8.8), Libya (the nearest of the 22 Awqaf cities with no reach limit, as the Awqaf publishes no rule for other places, 8.8; Sabratha keeps Zawiya's table (owner), as the validity guard does not act there, 6.5) and Egypt (the nearest of EGSA's about 80 cities by town centre, whatever the distance: no town offsets are published, and Dar al-Ifta's fatwa 3012 tells village mosques to follow EGSA's calendar rather than add a time difference, 8.8; a place that is one of a city's named places, by its catalogue row or within the city's own area, takes that city and the guard does not act there, and beyond every area the guard checks the nearest city's table, 6.5). Algeria takes its wilaya's place among the ministry's 70 (the nearest of them where a wilaya has several), with the monthly offset derived on the phone from the ministry's own rule (A16, 8.8); the validity guard gives `dz_marw` at its own point to places in the far Sahara more than about 100 km from their listed place (6.5). Iraq (owner rule, 2026-10-11): the Sunni Endowment's city tables, Baghdad's fitted to its published table and 27 others computed as the Endowment computes them, each place taking the nearest city with no reach limit (8.8); only the Kurdistan Region, whose mosques answer to the region's own Awqaf, which publishes no table online, keeps exact coordinates with «فلكية» (Q8). Saudi Arabia takes the table of the nearest of KACST's 173 listed places within 25 km, whatever its type, and KACST at the point beyond, since KACST computes for any coordinates (8.8). Syria takes the nearest of the ministry's 14 tables, with no reach limit and no admin1 constraint (8.8). Qatar takes QCH's six Qatari places: the Ministry's Doha table, which QCH's calendar extends to the east coast from Al Khor to Al Wakra, and Al Shamal, Dukhan, Abu Samra, Mesaieed and Halul, each computed at a point fitted to QCH's data; any other Qatari place takes the nearest of the six, whatever the distance (8.8). Lebanon takes Dar al-Fatwa's Beirut table, its one published table, everywhere, with no reach limit and with the validity guard, since Beirut is the only place it publishes and it prints no offsets or rule for the others (6.5, Q10; the dataset does not decide there, 12). Bahrain and Yemen keep the plan's mapping (Yemen: one of Sanaa's 31 locations, the nearest of the place's own terrain class within 60 km, otherwise the nearest, with no reach limit, and the validity guard, with Mocha, near its threshold, settled by the scan; 6.5, 8.8). Mauritania (owner rule, 2026-10-11): its wilaya's table from the ministry's imsakiya («إمساكية ولايات الوطن»: 13 capitals, Nouakchott's for the three Nouakchott wilayas), whatever the distance, with the validity guard: east of a capital its sunrise can come after the place's own, and the guard acts at Aïn Ben Tili (Zouerat's sunrise about 16 min after its own); Bir Moghrein, Tichitt and the places west of their capital, near its threshold, are settled by the scan (6.5, 8.8). «حسب موقعي بالضبط» stays an option wherever the default is a zone. A place the authority does not name itself, the authority's own bulk attachments included, takes the attached official table only where no prayer comes before it begins there and its sunrise does not come after the place's own, beyond the table's own margin and 4 minutes (the validity guard, 6.5). The mosque dataset confirmed most of these; where it pointed elsewhere (Egypt's mosques compute at their own point), the published source wins; Sabratha's mosques use Zawiya + 1, close to the Awqaf formula at Sabratha's own point; Sabratha keeps Zawiya's table (owner), as the validity guard does not act there. | Exact coordinates in Egypt and Zawiya + 1 for Sabratha, as the dataset suggested; official zone everywhere; exact coordinates everywhere |
| A3 | Countries with no official table found (SD, SO, DJ, KM) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): one fitted formula per country, from the mosque dataset (5.6).** Sudan `sd_fitted`: 18° / 18°, Dhuhr and Maghrib + 0, at the user's coordinates (ICOP's preset; 2 mosques). Somalia `so_fitted` and Djibouti `dj_fitted`: MWL 18° / 17°, no offsets, at the user's coordinates (3 and 6 mosques). Comoros `km_fitted`: the traditional Moroni table as a daily formula for the whole country (Fajr = sunrise − 73, Dhuhr = transit + 4, Maghrib = sunset + 3, Isha = Maghrib + 74), the sunrise shown at the user's own point, and Asr = Maghrib − 168, the mosques' own rule (owner), which comes up to 19 min before the shadow-factor-1 Asr from late March to late September (E15). Nearest rounding. Chip: «فلكية», no official table being known (A6). | A first-run chooser of `mwl`, `egyptian` and `umm_al_qura` (the former recommendation); for the Comoros, the later of the shadow-factor-1 Asr and Maghrib − 168, or the shadow-factor-1 Asr alone |
| A4 | Shia methods | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): never offered.** IQ, LB and BH get the authority's table by default, as every Arab country does: `iq_sunni_endowment`, `lb_dar_al_fatwa`, `bh_calendar`. No Shia preset or profile ships (`iq_shia_karbala`, `lb_fadlallah`, `jafari_18_4`, `jafari_leva`, `tehran`), nor their rows (sunset apart from Maghrib, «غياب الحمرة», the Jafari midnight) or their Hijri files. The research on them stays in 8.2 and 8.4 for reference. | A one-time question in IQ, LB and BH (the first recommendation); the Shia methods under «طرق أخرى» only (the revision of 2026-10-11) |
| A5 | Qatar has two publishers | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): the Ministry of Awqaf.** It runs the mosques, and its API is the only source for any date. Qatar Calendar House as an option: it is the state's approved reference calendar (Council of Ministers decision 333-23/2008), and it has added + 1 min to Dhuhr and Asr since mid-2025. Mosques are taken to call the Ministry's minute (Q3). | QCH by default |
| A6 | Chips | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11) with A19; made binary (owner, 2026-10-20).** One chip per method, from the place and its mapped zone (6.11): «رسمية» where the times follow an official source for the place (a national body's own definition in its own country under principle 6, `sa_umm_al_qura` anywhere in Saudi Arabia since KACST computes for any point, and an authority's table at the place's zone in that zone's own country), «فلكية» everywhere else. From 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-20 the chip had five levels, by evidence and fit: «رسمية», «مطابقة للجدول الرسمي» (a zone passing the tier-A gate, 9.5), «طريقة منشورة», «مستخلصة من جداول» and «تقريبية». The owner found «تقريبية» did not inspire confidence, turned down names built on the source, and decided «either it is official or it is falakia». The tier-A record is still measured and shown in «طريقتك». Egypt: EGSA's city tables are «رسمية» at their cities, and the `egyptian` preset, EGSA's two angles at the place, «فلكية». | «رسمية» for every authority profile; the five levels by evidence and fit; no chip at all |
| A8 | Perpetual tables and quirks | **Revised (owner, 2026-10-17): reproduce the printed table.** Where the authority publishes a perpetual table (QA, KW, PS, LY, IQ, MR's reprinted imsakiya and LB's Beirut table, Q10), the method carries the table's reference year (`referenceYear`, E5): the app computes each date as its month and day of that year (29 February as the first leap year's from it), in the zone's standard time, the table's clock, and moves the result back to the date by whole days; E9 revisions and E8's Ramadan days still follow the actual date. The app then shows what the table prints, as the mosque dataset shows Kuwait's and Palestine's mosques do (5.6), and a perpetual table can pass the tier-A gate (9.5): Baghdad's does. The decision of 2026-10-11 computed every date for its actual year, 1 minute off the printed table on 4–26 % of times (Qatar 4–13 %, Libya about 10 %, Iraq 14 %, Kuwait 20–26 %) and 2–3 minutes on Lebanon's Fajr and Isha on about a fifth of the days. KACST's previous-date days, 1 or 2 early in each Hijri month, are not reproduced (8.7). The reference years are fitted on all six prayers (Kuwait 2011, Qatar 2006, Libya 2006, Iraq 2021, Palestine 2016, Lebanon 1931, Mauritania 2023). | Compute for the actual year (the decision of 2026-10-11); reproduce every quirk |
| A10 | Default Eid prayer time | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): no Eid support outside Tunisia yet.** Tunisia keeps sunrise, as today. The research's rules per authority (KACST's published Eid time, Palestine's sunrise + 35, EGSA's times) are kept in Appendix C. | An `eid` rule per method in the Arab League (the former recommendation); sunrise + 20 everywhere abroad |
| A11 | Ramadan and Eid dates for Arab places | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): no Ramadan or Eid support outside Tunisia for now.** The calendar stays Tunisian everywhere (6.10). The per-country announcement files, their publisher and the «+1 يوم» quick fix are kept in Appendix C. | Per-country announcement files on the project's Pages (the former recommendation); Umm al-Qura plus manual dates |
| A12 | Country detection near borders | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): the nearest cities only.** The city-based `CountryResolver` (6.8): the catalogue cities within 40 km, with the network country deciding among them (6.8). No country outlines are bundled and no border question is asked. | Natural Earth outlines with a one-time question in a 5 km band along borders (the former recommendation) |
| A14 | Extra rows: Imsak, first adhan | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): not stored.** No Imsak or first-adhan rows in the first release, not even in the method data; E11 is not built. The values the research found stay in 8.2 and 8.7. | Store them and show none (the recommendation of 2026-10-11); show Imsak in Ramadan where the authority defines it |
| A16 | Algeria outside Algiers, Djelfa and Adrar | **Revised (owner rule, 2026-10-11): reproduce the ministry's calendar everywhere it applies.** The 67 attached places' monthly offsets are not separate data: each is the ceiling of the largest difference, over the month's days (in the Adrar zone days 1–15 and 17 to the end), between the place's and its reference's unrounded times by the ministry's own formula, Asr on the signed noon shadow (8.2). The app derives them on the phone, as for Jordan's further places (Q19), so no offset is stored: 99.9 % of 1448's printed offsets and 98.9 % of the held-out 1447 Algiers grids, never more than 1 minute off. The final times match the printed calendar on 94.5 % of minutes and within 1 minute on all, the level of the reference tables themselves. A place takes the listed place of its wilaya (the calendar heads each grid «… والولايات المرتبطة بها»; the nearest of them where a wilaya has several), otherwise the nearest of the 70, with the validity guard (6.5). The calendar rounds all six up and has a margin only on Maghrib (+ 3); with the 4 minutes the guard allows beyond that margin, it acts in the far Sahara: beyond about 100 km from the listed place, the listed place's Maghrib comes before the place's own sunset on about 38 % of days, and such a place gets `dz_marw` at its own point (8.8). «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the formula at the user's point, which is 0–5 min earlier than the calendar on most days. | Compute the fitted method at the exact coordinates (the decision before the offsets were deduced): the same minute as the printed calendar on only 37 % of the attached places' times, earlier on 63 %, up to 5 min; store each year's offsets (table data, A7) |
| A17 | The Gaza Strip has two publishers | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11): the Gaza Ministry of Awqaf.** The de facto ministry's own times (`ps_gaza_awqaf`), because Gaza mosques call them (principle 5). Dar al-Ifta's national table (Gaza + 3; Rafah, Khan Younis and Deir al-Balah + 4) under «طرق أخرى». | Dar al-Ifta by default (its Fajr was 7 min later on 2026-10-08); a one-time chooser of both |
| A18 | Default Jumu'a time | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11).** An unset Jumu'a time (`PrefsManager` returns − 1, which today means "use Dhuhr") resolves from the profile's `jumua` rule: `AT_DHUHR`, or `FixedClock(time)` with E9 revisions. `ae_awqaf`: `FixedClock(12:45)` from 2026-01-02, and 13:15 from 2022-01-07 to 2026-01-01 (federal circulars, as reported by The National and Gulf News; in all seven emirates, Sharjah included, until a Sharjah statement says otherwise, Q13). Where no authority sets a Jumu'a time, a published Friday time (Martinique's CCIM, French Guiana's A2CMGF, 7.6; in Europe in winter time, mosques' published Friday times, Q30) or the owner's Friday-times dataset gives the place's or country's time through `SilenceDefaults` (D12); without an entry there, `AT_DHUHR`. So the order is: the authority's rule, then a published Friday time (13:00 in Martinique, 13:30 in French Guiana, and in Europe in winter time the mosques' published Friday times, Q30), then the dataset, then Dhuhr (owner, 2026-10-11). The dataset supports the UAE's 12:45 in Al Ain (6 of 7 mosques) but not clearly in Dubai (its 4 mosques show 12:45, 13:00, 13:00 and 13:20), where the circular wins (Q13), and Dhuhr in the other Arab countries (75–96 % of mosques). The Friday window starts at the resolved time, and D12's end counts from it: adhan + 45, giving 12:45–13:30 in the UAE. | `AT_DHUHR` everywhere |
| A19 | `jo_awqaf`'s evidence and chip (new) | **Decided (owner, 2026-10-11).** FROM_TABLES; «رسمية» at its zones (A6), with each zone's tier-A record (9.5) in «طريقتك». The ministry documents its method's structure (east and west sites, with ICOP; 8.6) but not the zone inputs, which are fitted. | PUBLISHED, which shows «رسمية» in Jordan under principle 6 |

## 5. Facts shared by both regions

The facts that concern one region only are in its chapter: Europe in 7.1–7.3, the Arab countries in 8.1–8.6.

### 5.1 What is Tunisian in the code today

The phone computes Tunisian times offline with `InmPrayerFormula` (`multiplatform/shared/src/commonMain/.../InmPrayerFormula.kt`), which reproduces INM to the minute (565,020 exact times per year, `scripts/prayer_formula/README.md`). These are the parts that block any place outside Tunisia.

**Formula and data model (shared)**

- `TIME_ZONE_HOURS = 1.0` (`InmPrayerFormula.kt:61`) is used in noon, the horizon events and `sunAltitudeDeg`. Paris, Berlin and Oslo come out 1 h early all summer, and London 1 h late all winter.
- `hourAngle` clamps `cosH` into [−1, 1] (`:208`). When the sun never reaches −18°, Fajr and Isha both land on solar midnight. In Tromsø on 2026-12-21 the output is out of order: sunrise 11:42 and Maghrib 11:44 come before Dhuhr 11:49, and Asr is 12:14.
- `PrayerTime(prayer, minutes / 60, minutes % 60)` (`:75`) assumes 0 ≤ minutes < 1440. London's Isha on 2026-06-21 becomes "25:02", and a Tromsø Fajr becomes "0:−14".
- Dhuhr and Asr use the sun at 0h UT. For Asr this costs up to 0.7 min at 36°N and 2.1 min at 70°N. That is fine for reproducing INM, not for other places.
- `DayPrayerTimes` (`PrayerModels.kt:21`) has a day of month and six non-null hour/minute pairs: no date, no zone, no other civil day, no "absent". It is read in about 60 files: the phone, the shared code, 17 TV files (8 of them production code) and the desktop.
- `InmPrayerTimes` and `PrayerDataLoader` are keyed by an `Int` delegation id. A non-numeric key would throw while parsing, and the Android loader would then silently fall back to an empty source, blanking the Tunisian times too.
- `PrayerFormulaSettings` (INM's values plus the TV's custom values) accepts angles 15–20° only (`ANGLES`), so France's 12° is rejected, and adjustments of −15..15 min. Its ranges are the TV's settings-file contract.

**Location (phone)**

- The persisted location is a delegation id (default 615, Tunis, `PrefsManager.kt:52`) plus a Tunisian sector label (locality id, name, kind, GPS flag). No latitude, longitude or zone is ever stored; a GPS fix picks the nearest delegation and its sector, then is dropped.
- A GPS fix counts as Tunisia when `isInsideTunisia` (`DelegationLocator.kt:95–97`) finds it inside the widened `TUNISIA_BORDER` polygon (`:41`) or inside the country outline of `neighborhoods.bin`; otherwise the picker gets `OutsideTunisia` (`:238`). The UI then cancels all silences, sets `disabled_outside_tunisia`, turns auto-silence off and shows «أنت خارج تونس، تم إيقاف الكتم التلقائي» (`MainScreen.kt:1722`, `:816–819`).
- `SilenceVerifyWorker` repeats the check every 6 h (`:30`, `:71`, `:129`) with the same `isInsideTunisia`, so the picker and the worker cannot disagree. Outside, it cancels all silences and sets the flag but leaves the auto-silence switch on; back in Tunisia, it clears the flag, updates the delegation when auto-location is on, and reschedules (`:71–85`).
- The `disabled_outside_tunisia` flag is honoured by `ScheduleRefreshCoordinator` (`:70`), `SilenceScheduler` (`:54`, `:178`, `:625`), `SilenceVerifyWorker` (`:73`, `:78`) and `MainScreen` (`:1672–1673`, `:6750`). It gates silence only; the Ramadan Isha bump never reads it (below).
- Onboarding has no location step, so a fresh install in Paris or Riyadh shows and schedules Tunis.
- The picker lists 2,083 Tunisian sectors (OSM/ISIE polygons, about 15 MB). Its search folds é/ü/ö but not ø/ß/ł.

**Scheduling (phone)**

- Prayer (hour, minute) values become alarms through `Calendar.getInstance()` in the **device** zone: `SilenceScheduler.kt:242–257` and six similar blocks (`:92`, `:151`, `:398`, `:640`, `:661`, `:688`), `WakeAlarmComputer.kt:277`, `MainScreen.prayerTimeMillis` (`:6724`). This is correct today only because a phone in Tunisia is on +01:00 all year.
- The day is assumed to run from Fajr to Isha before midnight, and each window is built from one civil day's (hour, minute) values.
  - Windows are checked against today's times and against yesterday's windows that still contain now (`carriedOverWindows`, `SilenceScheduler.kt:718–730`). The 00:01 reschedule keeps such a window's silence and its end alarm (`:315–329`, `SilenceBugsTest.M14`). Only a window that contains now is carried over, and a prayer after midnight cannot be expressed at all (`PrayerTime` above).
  - The next day is armed only after today's last window has ended (`:355–362`).
  - Each prayer has one SILENCE and one UNSILENCE request code (`ordinal*2`, `ordinal*2+1`, `:778–782`), so a prayer's windows on two days are never armed together: a carried-over window's end replaces today's end alarm of the same prayer, and a reschedule 2 min after that end sets it again (`:315–317`, `:359`).
  - An UNSILENCE keeps the silence while another enabled window still contains now (another prayer's window today, an Eid window, or a window carried over from yesterday); that window's own end alarm ends it (`SilenceReceiver.kt:76–83`, `SilenceScheduler.isOtherSilenceWindowActive` at `SilenceScheduler.kt:177–194`, `SilenceBugsTest.M11`). The check sees only yesterday's and today's windows, built in the device zone, and answers "no" while auto-silence is off or `disabled_outside_tunisia` is set (`SilenceScheduler.kt:178`).
- `DhikrReminderScheduler.resolveTime` (`:397–402`) rejects a whole day unless the six times are strictly increasing within 00:00–23:59. Every prayer-relative adhkar and hifz reminder then disappears on a day with an after-midnight Isha.
- Fixed silence endpoints are resolved in four places: `SilenceScheduler`, `SilenceAlarmComputer` (whose `overlapForTrigger` the phone calls from four places), `PrayerTimelineModel` and `EidSilenceWindows`. In all of them an end before the start rolls a full day, and a fixed start always lands on the prayer's own civil date.
- Silence endpoints: the shared sheet takes −10,080 to +10,080 min (`SharedSilenceSettingsSheet.kt:54`, `:104–105`), the precision sheet up to 9,999 through its 4-digit field (`PrayerEndpointControls.kt:61`, `PrayerSilenceEditorSheet.kt:216–219`, whose stepper turns 9,999 + 1 into 1,000), and the slider's edge extension has no limit (`PrayerSilenceRangeSlider.kt:495–537`); the midnight reschedule is armed in the phone's zone (`SilenceScheduler.kt:494–508`).
- The default Dhuhr and Jumu'a silence ends at a fixed 13:15 (`PrefsManager.kt:505`, `:510`, mode FIXED_TIME at `:487–488`). Where Dhuhr is later, the end rolls to the next day.
- `MAX_DELEGATION_SHIFT_MS` (45 min, `SilenceScheduler.kt:33`) is a margin over the largest spread between Tunisian delegations (39 min, `:31`). A move between cities abroad can shift times by hours.
- `MainScreen` drops any time whose hour is outside 0–23 from the timeline, the shared settings sheet and the per-prayer editor (`:2258`, `:2472`, `:5642`). These filters also hide the hour −1 sentinel of an unset Eid time.

**Calendar and derived features**

- Ramadan and Eid follow Tunisian announcements, fetched from the app's own GitHub Pages and scraped from Tunisian sources. "Today" is taken in `Africa/Tunis` unless a `todayProvider` is set (`RamadanOverrideChecker.kt:61–65`), and the date row shows a «بداية الشهر مؤكدة في تونس» badge.
- The default Eid prayer time is sunrise. It is computed in three places: `EidSilenceWindows.kt:60`, `SilenceScheduler.kt:764–770`, and the shared `RamadanOverrideChecker.getDefaultEidPrayerTime` (`:449`, read by `MainScreen:2208`, `:2217`). Sunrise is about 04:45 in London in May, and there is none in Tromsø in summer.
- The 90-minute Ramadan Isha bump is a temporary override, not a change of the user's setting (`PrefsManager.kt:396–484`).
  - Apply: on the first call inside the Ramadan window (`RamadanDetector.isRamadan`: the last day of Sha'ban to the first day of Shawwal, by that year's Tunisian dates, or by Umm al-Qura's while none are known) in an Umm al-Qura year not yet recorded, it records the year and, if Isha's duration (`after_ISHA`, 30 when unset) is below 90, saves `after_ISHA` and `end_offset_ISHA` (an absent key as UNSET), writes 90 and removes the end offset (`:434–459`).
  - Restore: the first call after that Ramadan (a later Umm al-Qura year than the recorded one, or the same year outside the window and past month 9, `:439–443`) puts the saved values back, removing a key saved as UNSET (`:461–474`).
  - An Isha edit is kept: every Isha writer, `setConfig` (the only writer the phone calls, `MainScreen.kt:6955`) and the single-field setters, drops the saved values (`PrefsManager.kt:476–483`), so nothing is restored over it (no test pins this yet: `WakeAndPrefsBugsTest.M3` covers only the restore after Ramadan, the user's end offset included; the M0 snapshots record it, 11).
  - Callers: `SilenceScheduler.scheduleAllInternal` (`SilenceScheduler.kt:204`, right after its auto-silence check) and the Application's official-date observer, at every process start and whenever the Ramadan, Shawwal or Dhu al-Hijja start changes (`TunisianPrayerTimesApplication.kt:74`). Neither reads `disabled_outside_tunisia`, so today a phone abroad is bumped and restored like one in Tunisia.

**UI and text**

- The method explainer (`PrayerTimeMethodDialog`, `PrayerTimeMethodDiagrams`) is built around INM: Tunis as the fixed example (`EXAMPLE_DELEGATION_ID = 615`), and hard-coded "12 + 1 − λ/15", "+7 min" and "18°" texts. Without INM data for the place and year, the dialog shows only «لا تتوفر معطيات الحساب لهذه المعتمدية في هذا التاريخ.» (`:151–152`). Fed a polar place, `DayAltitudeChart` would throw (`PrayerTimeMethodDiagrams.kt:434`).
- Strings say «معتمدية», «تونس» and "UTC+1 without summer time" (`prayer_method_strings.xml`, `strings.xml`, `calendar_strings.xml`).

**Also Tunisian, found by the Arab research.** The forced `ar-TN-u-nu-latn` locale and «جانفي» in the strings, the week that starts on Monday, the bundled date files read from the top level only, the 90-minute Ramadan Isha bump's once-a-year key and after-Ramadan test on `HijrahDate.now()` (Umm al-Qura's year and month), and the TV's forced `Africa/Tunis`. The first release keeps the locale, «جانفي» and the Monday week (D19), and so do the date files (6.10, 6.12) and the TV's zone (6.12). The Ramadan bump already follows the Tunisian dates; only the places it applies to change, and its restore never depends on the place (6.10).

**Already worldwide.** These work anywhere and need no change: the Qibla (GPS, WGS84 geodesic, bundled WMM2025), location acquisition, the permission flow, the point-in-polygon code, the search engine and the AlarmManager plumbing. No network service is involved in prayer times.

### 5.2 The sun model and sensitivity

- **The sun model is not the limit for truth.** Against JPL's DE441 ephemeris from 2020 to 2100, the app's Meeus (1900 epoch) sun is off by at most 11.7″ in declination and 3.7 s in the equation of time. Evaluated at each event's own moment, times at 15 European cities are within 3–4 s on ordinary days, and within about 30 s on the few days next to an event's disappearance. Every serious model (Meeus 1900 as in INM, Meeus 1998 ch. 25, NOAA, USNO) is within about 4 s of DE441; the J2000 Meeus/NOAA sun is no better than Meeus 1900, and the Spencer series is unusable (0.76° errors). The engine reuses the existing `sun()`.
- **But switching models changes about 1–1.5 % of rounded minutes**, which matters when reproducing a table to the minute (8.3, 8.6).
- **Conventions and geometry are the limit.** Horizon refraction varies by about 0.3° around its standard value: 1.5–2.5 min at 50°N, about 4 min at 60°N in June. Minute precision at high latitude is a convention, not physics.
- **Sensitivity at Arab latitudes.** One degree of altitude moves a time by 4.0–5.8 min at sunrise and sunset, and by 4.0–7.8 min at Fajr and Isha (both maxima at 37°N in June). 0.25° of longitude is 1 minute.

### 5.3 Time zones

**On Android.**

- `java.time` is native at minSdk 26 and, on API 26+, is backed by ICU's data, the same release as `java.util.TimeZone`.
- Android 10 and later receive tzdata through the Time Zone Data Mainline module, applied on reboot. Android 8.1–9 update it only if the maker ships its tz-update APK, and Android 8.0 only by OTA. AOSP stopped patching Android 10 in August 2026, with tzdata 2026c as its last. `android.icu.util.TimeZone.getTZDataVersion()` returns the device's release, for example "2026e".
- `Instant.atZone` is unambiguous. `ZonedDateTime.of` moves a time in the spring gap forward and takes the earlier offset in the autumn overlap. Android's lenient `Calendar` takes the later, standard-time instant in the overlap.
- Measuring the night by wall clock on a change night would shift one-seventh by 8.6 min and middle-of-night by 30 min. Measuring it between instants avoids that.

**Europe.** EU summer time runs from 01:00 UTC on the last Sunday of March to 01:00 UTC on the last Sunday of October. The UK, Norway and Switzerland follow it; Turkey, Russia, Belarus and Iceland do not change clocks. The EU proposal to abolish summer time is still blocked.

**Arab countries.** All 22 countries use whole-hour offsets, so rounding a time in UTC or in local time gives the same minute.

| Zone | Offset on 2026-10-08 | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Africa/Nouakchott | +00 | |
| Africa/Casablanca, Africa/El_Aaiun | +00 | **Permanent GMT since 2026-09-20 02:00** (decree 2.26.530 of 2026-06-25, Bulletin Officiel 7521; tzdata 2026c). From 2018-10-28 Morocco was on +01, with +00 in each Ramadan. A phone with older tzdata shows a clock 1 hour ahead today. |
| Africa/Algiers, Africa/Tunis | +01 | Fixed since 1981 and 2009 |
| Africa/Tripoli, Africa/Khartoum | +02 | Sudan moved from +03 on 2017-11-01 (tzdata 2017c) |
| Africa/Cairo | +03 (DST) | +02, with DST from the last Friday of April 00:00 to the end of the last Thursday of October (rule since tzdata 2023a). Back to +02 after 2026-10-29. |
| Asia/Beirut | +03 (DST) | +02, with DST from the last Sunday of March 00:00 to the last Sunday of October 00:00. In 2023 a last-minute delay was announced and withdrawn. |
| Asia/Gaza, Asia/Hebron | +03 (DST) | +02 with DST. 2026: 03-28 02:00 to 10-24 02:00 (Saturdays). The rules changed in almost every year since 2016; the last change was in 2024a. |
| Asia/Amman, Asia/Damascus | +03 | Permanent since 2022-10-28 (tzdata 2022e) |
| Asia/Baghdad, Asia/Riyadh, Asia/Kuwait, Asia/Qatar, Asia/Bahrain, Asia/Aden, Africa/Mogadishu, Africa/Djibouti, Indian/Comoro | +03 | Fixed. Several are tzdata links (Kuwait and Aden to Riyadh, Bahrain to Qatar, the East African ones to Nairobi); `ZoneId.of` accepts them. |
| Asia/Dubai, Asia/Muscat | +04 | Fixed |

**Border offsets that matter**: Tunisia +01 against Libya +02; Saudi Arabia +03 against the UAE and Oman +04; Ceuta and Melilla on Africa/Ceuta (+02 today, +01 in winter) against Morocco +00; Egypt, Lebanon and Palestine change twice a year. Mauritania and Western Sahara are now both +00.

### 5.4 Ramadan, Eid and the Hijri date

- `HijrahChronology` (Umm al-Qura) is on every device and covers 1300–1600 AH on Android (1882-11-12 to 2174-11-25). Only 1356–1500 is backed by the published Umm al-Qura table.
- **Europe.** The ECFR and Diyanet now apply the Istanbul 2016 criterion: an elongation of at least 8° and the moon at least 5° up at sunset anywhere on Earth, plus New Zealand and Americas conditions. In 2026, Ramadan began on 19 February for Diyanet, the ECFR and the CFCM, and on 18 February for Saudi Arabia and the Grande Mosquée de Paris. The CFCM's Eid al-Fitr was on 20 March, the same day as Umm al-Qura's. Corrections are needed per event in any mode.
- **Arab countries.** The table compares official start dates with Umm al-Qura over 9 events: Ramadan, Shawwal and Dhu al-Hijja of 1445–1447. The dates come from ICOP's lists of official first days.

| Group | Countries | Deviation |
|---|---|---|
| Same as Umm al-Qura every time | SA, AE, BH, KW, QA, YE (ICOP lists one Yemen; Sanaa and Aden may differ), PS, LB (Sunni), SD; SO and DJ with sparse data | 0 |
| A day later sometimes | EG, SY, DZ, TN, LY (2–3 of 9), JO (3; it follows the Global Hijri Calendar's eastern zone, 8.6), OM (4), MR (3 of 7) | + 1 |
| A day later usually | MA (6 of 9), KM (2 of 2 listed, sparse) | + 1 |
| Split by community | IQ (Sunni, Shia, Kurdistan), LB (Sunni; Shia: the Higher Islamic Shia Council and the Fadlallah office) | varies |

- Every deviation observed is + 1 day. Every state authority announces on the evening of the 29th, after Maghrib.
- ICOP lists one Libya, Tripoli's Dar al-Ifta. Libya's eastern-government Awqaf and Supreme Fatwa Committee were reported in dispute with it over Ramadan and Shawwal 1445 (Shafaq News, 2024). This is not verified (Q14).
- Qatar's announced Ramadan 1447 began on 2026-02-18, as Umm al-Qura's did. The Ministry API's own Hijri labels put it a day earlier, and 1 Ramadan 1448 too (2027-02-07, against Umm al-Qura's 02-08), but they are no fixed shift of Umm al-Qura, so the app keeps Umm al-Qura's days for Qatar (E8, Q3).

### 5.5 Place data
