- **GeoNames** (CC BY 4.0). Europe: `cities5000`, about 10–20k rows under 2 MB with alternate names (D1). Arab countries: `cities1000` holds about 3,365 places in the 21 countries. Adding every `PPLA`–`PPLA4` and `PPLC` seat from `allCountries` keeps towns with population 0 (Al Majma'ah, for example). As a compact binary this is about 0.1–0.15 MB in the APK. Arabic names come from `alternateNamesV2` (`ar`, the preferred name first), then Wikidata labels (CC0). The `dem` column gives an SRTM elevation per place.
- **Natural Earth** 10 m admin-0 polygons are public domain. Simplified for the Arab region and its neighbours, they come to about 60–200 KB. They offer point-of-view variants (Moroccan, Egyptian, Palestinian, Saudi). The default layer has Somaliland as its own unit. The app does not use them (A12).
- **Time zones** follow the country in the Arab region: one zone each, except Palestine (Gaza and the West Bank), Western Sahara, and Ceuta and Melilla (Africa/Ceuta, from the Europe catalogue). No time-zone polygons are needed. In Europe each catalogue city carries its own zone (6.8).
- `android.location.Geocoder` needs a network backend. The app's own nearest-place search and point-in-polygon code are enough offline.

### 5.6 The owner's mosque dataset (2026-10-11)

The owner supplied a dataset of 17,758 mosques in 63 countries (`mosque-dataset/mosques.db`, git-ignored and never committed, like the official tables of D13). Each mosque has its coordinates, its zone, a full calendar of the six times (366 month/day rows with no year field, read as 2026 with 29 February dropped; local clock, summer time applied) and one Friday time, read on Friday 2026-10-09. The owner collected it from the mosques themselves, by email and WhatsApp: they shared their internal timetables and agreed that they may be analysed, processed and copied (Q31, closed). It is supporting evidence, never the source of truth. The rule (owner, 2026-10-11): a published source comes first; the dataset only chooses among published options, fills a gap where nothing is published (the four countries of A3, the Friday defaults of D12), or raises a question. So where an authority publishes a method or a table, that comes first, and Tunisia follows INM's formula whatever the Tunisian calendars show, since several Tunisian mosques said they were not sure of their own settings (D7). Five analyses, each checked by a skeptic, used it on 2026-10-11 for A2, A3, D4, D5, D6, D12 and A18, and the Algeria analysis of the same day for A16. A France review the same day, each analysis also checked by a skeptic, used it again for D4's order by département, D5's `mdf_12` exception, A1's reopening for Mayotte and «العشاء ليس قبل» (6.11). On 2026-10-12 it measured how close the French list, a nearby-mosque default and other apps come to French mosques, and how far the bounded editor reaches (7.7). On 2026-10-13 an independent review re-measured the French records (the git-ignored `work/france-prayer-review/`); 7.7 gives both analyses. The scripts and results are in the session scratchpad.

- **What it is and is not.** Many calendars are computed by the mosques' display software (nearest-minute rounding, whole-minute adjustments, an Asr up to about 1.5 min off the shadow rule) or are prepared city calendars, not what an authority prints. Shares are shares of the mosques in the dataset, not of every mosque in a country, and identical calendars inflate counts (Jordan: 505 mosques share one calendar). Algeria (6,646) and Tunisia (2,522) dominate it.
- **Arab authorities (A2).** Mosques copy the official zone or city table in Jordan (636 of 686), Palestine (156 of 163: the Al-Aqsa table plus a whole-minute town offset, 99.4–99.7 % exact), Kuwait (83 of 84), Morocco (286 of the 744 mosques near the 24 Habous localities checked, at a median of 100 %), Baghdad (13 mosques at 100 %), Libya's cities (50 of 125 on the one official day at hand), Al Ain and four Omani mosques. Followers sit a median 14 km from their zone point in Jordan and 71 km in Palestine, where the method at their own coordinates would give their minute only 57 % and 30 % of the time. In Egypt (46 of 52) and Saudi Arabia (80 of 117) the calendars compute the authority's angles at each mosque's own point, perhaps in an app; the published tables still win there (A2). In Jordan, 160 of 635 followers use a zone other than the nearest point: Zarqa, Madaba and Balqa outside the valley follow Amman, as the ministry's calendar attaches them (8.8, Q29).
- **The four countries without tables (A3).** Sudan's 2 mosques use 18°/18° (ICOP's preset). Somalia's 3 and Djibouti's 6 use MWL 18°/17°, possibly their software's default. 16 of 24 Comoros mosques, on 9 calendars, use one traditional table, the Moroni table in all three islands: Fajr = sunrise − 73, Dhuhr = transit + 4, Maghrib = sunset + 3, Isha = Maghrib + 74, Asr = Maghrib − 168, with values held in 5-day blocks.
- **Algeria (A16).** The ministry's method at each mosque's own point is within 1 minute on 62–85 % of times per prayer and within 2 minutes on 85–94 %. The mosques run 0.5–0.9 min later, and the most common setting adds + 1/+ 1/+ 4/+ 2. Against the full 1448 calendar (2026-10-11), the mosques fit the printed time of their nearest listed place better than the formula at their own point: within 60 km (6,266 mosques) 46.7 % against 30.8 % exact and 79.1 % against 72.9 % within 1 minute; beyond 60 km (372, mostly in the Sahara) the reverse, 13.2 % against 24.3 %. (An earlier check on one printed Oran day had found the opposite.)
- **A year from the followers (owner, 2026-10-17).** A mosque that reproduces an official table on the official days collected stands in for that table's other days of 2026 (9.5, Coverage): Morocco's 58 localities, Tripoli, Kuwait, Vienna and five Omani zones gained a held-out year that way, a year before the collectors would give one (9.3).
- **Perpetual tables (A8).** Kuwait's and Palestine's mosques call the perpetual tables as printed. Their Dhuhr fits an old year's sun (Kuwait 1974 or 1999, Palestine 1998) better than 2026, so computing for 2026 differed from them by 1 minute on about 10–25 % of times; since A8's revision (2026-10-17) the app shows the tables as printed, as they do.
- **Europe (D4, D15).** Practice is split and local. In France, 12°/12° is the most common (about 39–46 % of mosques), mostly with Fajr − 4, Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 3 and Isha + 5, offsets that may come from one app template and are not adopted: `mdf_12` has none, since no Musulmans de France text of 2020 or later gives any and the only one found, a 2012 GEDIS reproduction with other minutes (Fajr − 5, Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 5), is too old and matches none of the French 12° calendars (D15, Q32); then ISNA 15°/15°. Belgium uses Fajr = sunrise − 90 with an 18° Isha (24 %) or 18°/18°. The Netherlands and Germany mostly use Diyanet's tables (26 %, 44 %). Italy and Spain split between MWL and ISNA. In Ireland, 9 of 21 mosques show the perpetual Dublin table that the Islamic Foundation of Ireland publishes (Q23). The UK follows no single formula (52 % irregular; London shares one timetable whose shape matches the London Unified Timetable). About half the mosques in the UK (49 %) and Portugal (47 %) use the Hanafi Asr, elsewhere at most 6 %. French practice is also regional (2026-10-11 review): around Lyon the mosques call the Conseil des Mosquées du Rhône's published 2020 table as printed, by date in 2026 and with no correction by town (26 of 73 Rhône mosques, the Grande Mosquée de Lyon among them, and 12 more that set their own Isha in winter or summer, mostly a fixed clock time; 1 of the 10 Turkish mosques and none around Villefranche-sur-Saône; just outside Rhône, 3 mosques in Isère and 1 in Ain; Q33); in Bas-Rhin 26 of 41 use Diyanet's tables, as the Grande Mosquée de Strasbourg shows; Paris is fragmented (31 % of Île-de-France mosques follow no formula, and 18 mosques still share the Grande Mosquée de Paris's former fixed clock table, its Ramadan 2023 and 2024 imsakiyat, while 10 follow the mosque's 2026 rule, Q21). Some entry of the French list of D4 (`rhone_ctir` included in Rhône) matches the Fajr and Isha of 26 % of French mosques within 5 min (2026-10-11) and all six times of 19 % (7.7); most reach their mosque's minute only with «مخصّصة» or the adjustments (6.11). Even neighbouring mosques differ (7.7). 243 of 1,679 French mosques hold Isha at a fixed clock time in winter, 119 at 19:00 (6.11). Overseas, only Mayotte has mosques in the dataset (7 records on 4 calendars).
- **Short summer nights (D5).** No calendar north of 45°N publishes Local Relative Estimation (0 of about 950; median error 35–43 min). The angle-based rule, applied every night, is the most common exact match (167 Fajr and 95 Isha calendars). The angle-based rule may largely reflect apps' default settings, about 28 % of the scored calendars; the owner kept it as D5 knowing that. In northern France, of the calendars where D5 would move a time by 3 min or more, 65 of 108 at 12° apply the cap and 43 keep the real angle, and 43 of 51 at 15° apply it (D5). In Luxembourg, 9 of 14 mosques print the Shoura's 2026 table, which keeps the real 13° on the nights the cap would move (Q37). The Netherlands cap Fajr at 03:30 and Isha at 23:30 every night, and Belgian mosques use similar clock caps, about 03:24 and 23:30.
- **Polar days (D6).** The northernmost mosque is Luleå (65.6°N), where the sun always rises and sets, so the dataset cannot show how a missing time is filled. A web research the same day found what polar mosques publish instead (7.4): Islamic Council Norway's tables, which the Tromsø mosque republishes, place Mecca's day on the local solar noon in the polar seasons; Diyanet keeps a day and a night of at least 5 hours and puts Asr at Dhuhr when the sun does not rise; Russia, Iceland and Greenland publish no polar rule.
- **Friday (D12, A18).** In the Arab countries the Friday time equals that day's Dhuhr for 75–96 % of mosques; the UAE shows 12:45 (8 of 14). In summer-time Europe the values are clock times (Belgium 14:00, the UK 13:30, Spain 14:00, Madrid 14:45, the Balkans 13:00), but a quarter of them (24 %) are earlier than that day's solar noon − 5 min, so most likely winter values left in place; these are the dataset's winter evidence (France 651, Italy 325, Spain 118; Q30, 6.9). In Tunisia, Jumu'a starts at 13:00 in 987 of 2,217 mosques and between 12:40 and 13:15 in 73 %.
- **Tunisia (D7).** Only 2.5 % of 2,472 Tunisian mosque calendars equal INM to the minute, though 75–90 % of times are within 1 minute: a different calculator or rounding, not a different method, and the mosques said they were unsure of their settings. INM's formula stays the only method (owner, 12). Arram (delegation 495) has no INM parameters and meteo.tn publishes no table for it, so today its localities snap to a neighbouring delegation; it will use the current INM formula at its own coordinates (owner, 2026-10-11), a separate fix outside this plan.
- **Israel.** About 90 mosques of Arab towns in Israel are filed under Palestine. The dataset is respected as it stands (12, 14).

## 6. Shared design

### 6.1 Overview

```
multiplatform/shared (commonMain; new files unless marked)
  engine/   SolarEngine       sun at an instant: InmPrayerFormula.sun / julianDay (read-only), Vsop87Sun or TextbookSun (E14)
            Vsop87Sun         E14: VSOP87 with Delta T and its term tables (D17)
            TextbookSun       E14: the low-precision sun of Libya's table (ly_awqaf, 8.2)
            PrayerEngine      day(place, date, method, ramadanDay, zone) -> PrayerDay   (UTC instants + status)
            HorizonModel      E2, E3
            SunEvaluation     E4
            HighLatitude      LRE, angle-based, clock caps (NL, BE), 1/7, middle, nearest day, MCW seasonal, Diyanet takdir, DUM RF's June Isha ramp, IRN's southern summer rule, DUM RT's summer intervals, the summer forms of Zagreb and Slovenia (7.4)
            PolarFallback     5-hour minimum day and night with its per-place gate, polar Asr midpoint (D6); Diyanet's ungated clamp; irn's Mecca cycle
            InmPrayerDays     INM output relabelled as instants at +01:00 (no new math)
            PrayerClock       next, current and prayer day by instant (6.6)
  method/   PrayerMethod, PrayerMethods (presets), CountryMethodDefaults
            RamadanDays       E8: the per-method source of Ramadan days (worked out by the repository)
            ArabAuthorityProfiles   the official profiles of 8.7, with revisions (E9)
  place/    PrayerPlace (Inm | Coordinates), ZoneResolver, CountryResolver
            OfficialZone, OfficialZones, ZoneMapping   6.5
  silence/  SilenceEndpoints  fixed start/end resolution of D16
            SilenceWindowPlanner   windows as instants over prayer days D−2..D+1, the endpoint domain (6.9)
            SilenceDefaults   Jumu'a times by country and place, from the owner's dataset and the published times of Martinique and French Guiana (D12, A18)
android-app
  PrayerTimesRepository   the only dispatch point: place + method (+ mapped zone) -> INM or engine -> PrayerDay instants
  PrefsManager            place, method choice and Jumu'a time per country
  place/PlaceCatalogue    bundled European and Arab cities
  assets/places/          europe-cities.tsv, arab-cities.bin
  assets/official-zones/<authority>.json
  schedulers, MainScreen, adhkar, hifz, wake   the fixes of 6.9
  ui/                     method sheet, chooser, adjustments, markers, explainer section
scripts/
  places/build_catalogue.py
  official_tables/collect_<cc>.py    read-only collectors (9.3)
  prayer_formula/prayer_engine_reference.py   the independent reference (9.1)
  prayer_formula/fit_authority.py    the fitting harness (9.4)
```

### 6.2 The engine

Everything below is pure Kotlin plus `java.time`, tested with `./gradlew :shared:javaTest`. Before any code, the math is written out in a new "Shared engine" section of `scripts/prayer_formula/README.md`, coefficients included. It holds the single order of operations below, then one part for the high-latitude rules and one "Arab authorities" part with a section per authority. The Python reference of 9.1 follows that README without reading the Kotlin.

The Arab extensions keep their labels, E1 to E13; D17 adds E14, A3 adds E15, overseas France adds E16, Libya adds E17, Kosovo adds E18 and mainland France adds E19 (7.7). E5 lives only in the fitting harness (A8) and E11 is not built (A14); each of the others is a field of `PrayerMethod` or of an official zone (6.5), and its default leaves the Europe presets unchanged.

**Sun.** The engine calls `InmPrayerFormula.sun(jd)` (declination and equation of time, Meeus 1900 epoch) and `julianDay`, and never edits them. Delta T is ignored: under 3″ for the sun. Through E14, profiles may instead use VSOP87 with Delta T, or the TEXTBOOK sun Libya's table was made with, where it reproduces their tables better (D17).

**Solver.**

1. **The solar cycle of D.** D is a civil date in `place.zone`, and N is its noon in the zone's standard time: `ZonedDateTime.of(D, 12:00, place.zone)` as an instant, moved later by the summer-time saving in force then (`ZoneRules.getDaylightSavings`), so that a summer-time change never moves U. U, the UT date of D's cycle, is the date N has in the place's local mean time, UT + λ/15 h (λ in degrees, east positive). The cycle's mean noon M = 0h UT of U + 12h − λ/15 h then lies within 12 h of N, and the transit t₀ = M − EoT(t₀)/60 h is iterated like the events of step 2 (E6's per-event points take their own λ in M, with the same U). U = D wherever a zone keeps to its own side of the date line, which includes every authority's place. U = D−1 at Wallis-et-Futuna (UTC+12 at 176°10'W: 2026-10-13 has its transit at about 23:31 UTC on 10-12, 11:31 on 10-13 in the place's zone, where reading D as the UT date would return 10-14's cycle) and at GPS places in Samoa, Tonga or Kiribati's Line Islands; U = D+1 in the western Aleutians (America/Adak, UTC−9 or −10 at about 174°E). The transit then falls on D in `place.zone` wherever the zone's standard time, whole days aside, keeps within 10.5 h of the place's mean solar time (12 h, less the equation of time and the summer-time saving); every zone on land keeps within about 3.7 h, summer time included (Siorapaluk in northwest Greenland, on America/Nuuk at 70.6°W, in summer; the western Aleutians 3.5 h). Only a place in the phone's zone (6.8: no catalogue city within 40 km), on a phone that keeps a far zone, can be farther off: the cycle is still U's, consecutive dates still take consecutive cycles, and its transit may fall on the previous date, up to about 16 min before midnight in that zone, or on the next date, up to about 14 min after midnight, or 1 h 14 min in the zone's summer time (12).
2. Each event of altitude *a* on side *s* (−1 morning, +1 evening) iterates t = M − EoT(t)/60 h + s·H(a, φ, δ(t)) from t₀, re-evaluating δ and EoT at t. It stops when t changes by less than 0.1 s, after at most 10 passes. Here H = acos((sin a − sin φ sin δ)/(cos φ cos δ))/15.
3. When |cos H| > 1 the event is **absent**; the value is never clamped.
4. Near the boundary days, where the plain iteration can oscillate, a bracketed root-finder takes over.
5. **Neighbouring days and dated rules.** The sunrise and sunset of D−1 and D+1 that the night needs (Night, below) come from those dates' own cycles, chosen by step 1: U−1 and U+1, at sea and in a far phone zone too, each transit 24 h ±30 s from D's, across a summer-time change too. Every event belongs to the cycle it was solved in, whatever its own local date (an Isha after midnight is still D's). A date the zone skips takes the next date's cycle, since `ZonedDateTime.of` moves a time in a gap later (Samoa skipped 2011-12-30; no zone skips a date in `SUPPORTED_YEARS` under today's rules), so `day()` still does not throw. U serves only the sun, in steps 1–4 and E4's UT instants (below), so a place's sun does not depend on which side of the date line its clock keeps; E4's `LOCAL_MIDNIGHT_SHADOW` keeps 00:00 of D in `place.zone`. Every other rule that names a date reads D: E8's Ramadan days, E9's revisions, E18's day of the year, the dated rules of 7.4, the month and `offsetWindow` of the zone offsets (8.8), and the Friday of the Jumu'a (6.9).

**E4 — When the sun is evaluated.** `sunEvaluation: Map<TimeEvent, SunEvaluation>`, with `SunEvaluation { EVENT_INSTANT, DAILY_0H_UT, DAILY_10H_UT, KACST, LOCAL_MIDNIGHT_SHADOW }`. The default, `EVENT_INSTANT`, is the solver above: the sun at the event's own UT instant.

- `DAILY_0H_UT` takes declination and equation of time once, at 0h UT of U, the cycle's UT date (the civil date at every authority's place; Solver, step 1), for all six events (Oman, 98 %).
- `DAILY_10H_UT` does the same at 10:00 UT, noon in Libya (`ly_awqaf`, 8.2).
- `KACST` takes the declination at 21:00 UT of U and the equation of time at 09:00 UT of U, that is 00:00 (next day) and 12:00 Riyadh time, whatever the place's zone. It serves Fajr and Asr; sunrise, Dhuhr and Maghrib fit best at the event instant (Q4, 13 city-years of 2025–2027). The fit is flat from 20:45 to 21:30 UT, midnight at the place's longitude fits no better, and the equation of time at the transit fits as well as at 09:00 UT. VSOP87 and the USNO sun do no better than the Meeus sun (within ±0.4 point per prayer), so `sa_umm_al_qura` keeps MEEUS (E14).
- `LOCAL_MIDNIGHT_SHADOW`, for Asr only: the noon shadow takes the declination at 00:00 local time of the civil date (21:00 UT the day before in Yemen), and the Asr instant is then solved as usual (`ye_awqaf`: 96.5 % exact, against 77.6 % with the declination at transit; Q2).

The harness explores more modes (transit, among others). A mode joins the enum only when a shipped profile needs it.

**Events.**

| Event | Definition |
|---|---|
| Sunrise, sunset | the altitude of the method's horizon model (E2): a = −0.8333° by default (16′ semi-diameter + 34′ refraction), with no elevation dip; every European preset keeps the default |
| Dhuhr | transit; `bh_calendar` adds 1.1 min, the whole disc past the meridian (E7) |
| Asr | altitude atan(1/(k + tan\|φ − δ_transit\|)), k = 1 or 2, on the geometric sun, or on the apparent sun (E13); absent when \|φ − δ_transit\| ≥ 90°; or with tan(φ − δ_transit) signed, without the absolute value (E15, `dz_marw` in Algeria); or Maghrib − n minutes (E15, `km_fitted`, `yt_traditional`) |
| Maghrib | sunset; an angle (`maghribAngle`, E12) is supported, but no shipped method uses it (A4) |
| Fajr | an angle before sunrise, or a fixed number of minutes before sunrise |
| Isha | an angle after sunset, or a fixed interval after Maghrib (with its method offset) or after sunset, with an optional Ramadan value (E8), an optional latest clock time (ICCI's 22:45) and, in a custom method only, an optional earliest one (6.11) |

**E2 — Horizon model.** Sunrise and sunset each get an altitude from `HorizonModel`:

| Model | Altitude | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `Standard` | −0.8333° | default |
| `Fixed(deg)` | as given | INM's −0.83° plus its own dip (INM path only); `ae_awqaf`'s `Refraction` fallback (−1.4°); `sa_umm_al_qura`'s sunrise (−0.83°; its sunset stays `Standard`, Q4); `apbif`'s sunrise and sunset (−1°, the sun's centre, as APBIF defines sunset and its tables show for sunrise; 7.5) |
| `Pressure` | −(0.2667° + 0.5667°·e^(−h/8434 m)) | Yemen: each official location's fitted elevation, or the catalogue `dem` at exact coordinates; a fixed altitude per terrain class fits 91–92 %, against 97–99 % (Q2) |
| `Refraction(P, T, fallback)` | semi-diameter + refraction from the pressure and temperature of the mapped zone (Hohenkerk & Sinclair, Explanatory Supplement 1992, §3.281), plus the geometric dip of its morning or evening elevation; `sunriseAltitudeDeg` and `sunsetAltitudeDeg` stay null for these zones | Accurate Times (Jordan, UAE, and Iraq's Sunni Endowment, Q8): confirmed by the prototype, with Bennett's formula scaled by pressure and temperature standing in for Hohenkerk & Sinclair (8.6) |
| `PerZone(fallback)` | the zone's fitted sunrise and sunset altitudes | Morocco |

- Sunrise and sunset may differ: Morocco's two horizons are nearly equal at the coast (sunrise 0–0.07° lower), but the sunrise one falls faster with height (Imilchil, about 2,150 m: −2.38° against −2.15° for Maghrib), and Jordan's zones have separate morning and evening elevations.
- **Where the elevation comes from.** A place mapped to an official zone uses the zone's elevations, pressure and temperature (6.5). At exact coordinates, the place's `elevationM` (6.8). A place with no elevation uses h = 0.
- **`PerZone` and `Refraction` with no zone.** The fallback model applies whenever the profile runs at exact coordinates: A2's «حسب موقعي بالضبط», a place beyond every zone's reach or sent to its own point by the validity guard (6.5), the guard's references at a place and at a table's own place (6.5), or a place outside the authority's country (`ma_habous` chosen in Europe included), except a place in Ceuta or Melilla mapped to its Habous locality (6.5, 6.8) and a place in Jerusalem mapped to `jo_awqaf`'s Jerusalem entry (8.8). The AM2 fits finalise the fallbacks: `ma_habous` uses the sunrise and Maghrib formulas of 8.2 with the place's elevation (h = 0 when it has none: the coastal fit), within 1 minute on 97–100 % of the days checked and 2 at most (Q5); `ae_awqaf` uses `Fixed(−1.4°)`; `jo_awqaf` uses the nearest Jordanian zone's inputs inside Jordan, otherwise the median of its zones; `iq_sunni_endowment` uses the place's elevation with 1010 mbar and 10 °C, the inputs of Accurate Times' Iraqi entries other than Baghdad. At exact coordinates E6 does not apply, because the user's own point serves every event, and a zone's E7 fractions are dropped; `bh_calendar`'s Dhuhr + 1.1 is a method field (E7) and stays.

**E3 — No dip on twilight.** `dipOnTwilight = false` for every method except INM, which keeps its own path. Jordan, Morocco, the UAE, Yemen and Fadlallah all prove it from high places, and no European body examined dips at all.

**E13 — Asr on the apparent sun.** `asrSun: { GEOMETRIC, APPARENT }`, GEOMETRIC by default. APPARENT measures the shadow on the refracted sun: the noon altitude and the Asr altitude both include refraction (with the place's pressure and temperature), so Asr comes 5–10 s later than the geometric rule. Jordan, the UAE (8.6) and Iraq (Q8) need it, and so does Morocco at standard pressure and temperature (Q5: 712 of 720 Habous Asr times against 641 geometric; 87 of 88 against 75 on archived 2024 tables); every other method keeps GEOMETRIC until a fit shows otherwise.

**E14 — Sun model (D17).** `sunModel: { MEEUS, VSOP87, TEXTBOOK }`, MEEUS by default: the existing `InmPrayerFormula.sun`. VSOP87 is the VSOP87D Earth series with FK5 correction, nutation, aberration and Delta T by the Espenak–Meeus polynomial, as in the prototype (8.6); its term tables ship in the shared module. Each authority profile takes the model that reproduces its tables best in AM2 (D17); Jordan and the UAE start from VSOP87. TEXTBOOK is the low-precision sun Libya's table was made with (8.2): Meeus's mean longitude L, a mean anomaly of L − 282°, the equation of centre 1.9146 sin M + 0.019993 sin 2M, the declination with ε = 23.44°, and an equation of time reduced to the equator (−C + y sin 2λ, y = tan²(23.5°/2), no 4L term). An accurate sun, Meeus or VSOP87, cannot reproduce even Tripoli's Dhuhr column, so for D17 "most precise" means the closest reproduction of the table.

**E15 — Asr before Maghrib or on the signed shadow (A3, A1, A16).** `asr: AsrRule = Shadow(factor)`, `BeforeMaghrib(minutes)` for the Comoros' traditional table and Mayotte's (`km_fitted`, `yt_traditional`; Maghrib − 168), the mosques' own rule, chosen by the owner, which comes up to 19 minutes before the shadow-factor-1 Asr from late March to late September; or `SignedShadow(factor)` for Algeria (`dz_marw`): the ministry's noon shadow is tan(φ − δ) without the absolute value, which changes Asr only where the sun passes north of the zenith (south of the Tropic in summer), up to 16 minutes earlier than the shadow rule at In Guezzam in June–July; `dz_marw` stores `SignedShadow`. For a place whose `countryCode` is not DZ, the repository passes it with `Shadow` (south of the equator the signed shadow would give no meaningful Asr). Inside Algeria, a mapped zone, the validity guard's own point (6.5) and «حسب موقعي بالضبط» all keep `SignedShadow`. A field of `PrayerMethod`; the default keeps every other method unchanged.

**E16 — Shifts by elevation band (overseas France, 7.6).** `elevationShifts: List<ElevationShift>`, empty by default. `re_cir` carries the Centre Islamique de la Réunion's published table: 100 m 1 min, 200 m 1.5, 300–400 m 2, 500–700 m 3, 800–1000 m 4, 1100–1500 m 5, 1600–2000 m 6, 2100–3000 m 7. The CIR lists only heights, so each shift holds from its listed height up to the next: below 100 m none, 100–199 m 1 min, 200–299 m 1.5, 300–499 m 2, 500–799 m 3, 800–1099 m 4, 1100–1599 m 5, 1600–2099 m 6, 2100 m and above 7. The 1.5 stays fractional until the single rounding (step 10, E1); E7's whole-minute rule is about method offsets, not these shifts. Fajr and sunrise move that much earlier, Maghrib and an Isha by angle that much later, and Dhuhr and a shadow Asr stay; an Isha or Asr by interval follows Maghrib (step 5). Maghrib's shift is applied in step 4, the others in step 7. The elevation is the place's `elevationM` (6.8); with none, there is no shift.

**E17 — Twilight latitude (Libya).** `twilightLatitudeScale: Double = 1.0`. Libya's table computes Fajr and Isha at the latitude scaled by 1 − e² = 0.99330562 (about 0.22° lower at Tripoli); sunrise, sunset and Asr use the real latitude. Without it, Tripoli's Fajr and Isha match 333 rather than 360 of 372 minutes (8.2).

**E18 — Seasonal offsets (Kosovo, 8.8).** `seasonalOffsets: Map<TimeEvent, List<Double>>`, empty by default. `xk_bik` carries, per time, 36 minute values at equal steps through the year (about one every 10 days), interpolated linearly by day of the year and added with the method offsets (Maghrib's in step 4, the others in step 7) before the single rounding: BIK's takvim follows no angle, point or horizon (8.8). `ie_ifi` carries them on its Fajr and Isha intervals, sunrise and Maghrib, fitted to the IFI's Dublin table (7.5). The values are fitted parameters, not the table (D13).

**E19 — Validity envelope (mainland France, 7.7).** The engine's times are computed starts: the start of each prayer's time, Fajr's also serving as the fast's cutoff (no imsak is shown abroad, A14), and sunrise, which ends Fajr's time. E19 bounds these starts, not a mosque's adhan or iqama, which can come later and still be a valid prayer (7.7). At a mainland French place every computed start stays inside bounds set from the recognised positions: Fajr between the 18° instant (solar midnight on a night without it) and the 12° instant (no upper bound on a night without 12°, which only a custom method beyond 54.56° of latitude meets), sunrise not after its computed time, Dhuhr not before transit + 1.2 min, Asr not before the factor-1 shadow, Maghrib not before sunset + 1 (no upper bound: a later Maghrib is a delayed start, not an invalid one; the custom editor's input stops at + 10, 6.11), and Isha not before the 12° instant, or, for a rule that estimates Isha on short nights, not before the Moonsighting Committee's «Shafaq Ahmer» curve. A time beyond a bound moves to it, rounded inward, with status `ESTIMATED` and the bound named in `explain()`; the 18° floor and the bounds set by the sun's disk leave a time that is past them by 1 min or less where it is, and move one past them by more to the bound itself (7.7). It is a rule of the engine, not a method field: it checks every method at a mainland French place and a custom method anywhere (6.11; outside mainland France the editor's Fajr floor is 20°); INM in Tunisia (D7) and the presets and profiles at a place outside mainland France do not use it. On a night too short for it, an E19 floor yields to the order guard (Event order, below), which holds Isha at the night's lowest point and rounds it to the nearest minute.

**Night.** The night runs from sunset to sunrise, never from Maghrib to Fajr (Q20), and is measured between instants. For Isha of day D it runs from sunset(D) to sunrise(D+1); for Fajr of day D, from sunset(D−1) to sunrise(D). Each day therefore also computes its neighbours' sunrise and sunset (chosen by step 1 of the Solver), and finds the lowest point of the nights N(D−1) and N(D) (Event order, below). The night takes the sunrise and sunset of steps 1–3, before any minute offset, and serves every rule of 7.4 that takes a share of the night (Local Relative Estimation, one-seventh, middle of the night, nearest day, angle-based, the Moonsighting one-seventh). The night dial's thirds (6.9) are another measure and keep Maghrib to Fajr.

**E1 — Rounding per event.** `rounding: Map<TimeEvent, Rounding>` with `Rounding { NEAREST, UP, DOWN }`, NEAREST for all six by default; NEAREST rounds 30 s and more up. It is applied once, on the UTC instant (step 10); less than one minute from the lowest point of their night, Maghrib, Isha, Fajr and sunrise round to the nearest minute whatever their mode (Event order, below). An Isha by interval counts from Maghrib after all of Maghrib's adjustments (step 4) and uses Maghrib's mode, so "rounded Maghrib + 90" (SA, QA) holds, Ramadan included. Saudi Arabia: Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr and Maghrib UP, sunrise DOWN. Algeria: all UP. Syria: Fajr and sunrise DOWN, the rest UP. Bahrain: Maghrib NEAREST, the rest DOWN.

**E5 — Reference year: in the fitting harness only (A8).** Qatar's, Kuwait's, Palestine's, Libya's and Iraq's perpetual tables, Mauritania's reprinted imsakiya and Lebanon's Beirut table (Q10) were computed once for a fixed year and are reused every year. The fitting harness (9.4) evaluates them at that year, reading each time at the table's fixed offset (+03 for Qatar, Kuwait and Iraq, +00 for Mauritania, +02 for Palestine, Libya and Lebanon), so the fitted parameters are right: Qatar 2006, Kuwait 1999, Palestine 2012 for now (its Dhuhr points to 1998), Libya 2006 (a non-leap year; the table has no 29 February row), Iraq 2021 (also non-leap; its 29 February row is checked in 2028), Mauritania 2023, the year the reprint first appeared (refitted on all six prayers on 2026-10-11: from 2003 on, only years of 2015's leap phase fit, and they fit alike, Q6), Lebanon's Beirut table a year in 1903's leap phase (1902 fits almost as well); every reference year is to be refitted on all six prayers (A8); Kuwait's February and March sheets alone fit 2007 better than 1999 (330 against 319 of 354 cells exact), in the same leap phase. Kuwait's sheet prints its own 29 February row, for years divisible by 4, which the harness reads at the leap day after the reference year (2000-02-29 for 1999; Q15). The engine computes every date for its actual year (A8).

**E6 — Per-event points.** An official zone may carry a `morningPoint` (Fajr, sunrise) and an `eveningPoint` (the rest) besides its `point`, and, since 2026-10-17, `eventPoints`: a time computed at a point of its own instead (the reference's S6 lists what moves with it). Bahrain's calendar needs it: the SCIA states two points (Dilmunia 26.27N 50.68E for Fajr and sunrise, Salman Town 26.22N 50.44E for the rest, Q7), but each time fits its own, Isha at the stated 18° only near 26.15N 50.70E and Asr 3 km north of Maghrib's 26.15N, so the zone holds Fajr's point as `morningPoint`, Maghrib's as `eveningPoint` and sunrise, Asr and Isha in `eventPoints` (its fit record, `fits/bh_calendar/README-part.md`). UAE: the eastern edge, stored as a `morningPoint` computed at build time from the fitted distance east of the zone point. This is Accurate Times' "City Settings" (8.5): the zone point is the western edge, and Fajr and sunrise are computed D km east of it. Jordan's ministry states the same rule for its east and west sites (8.6).

**E7 — Fractional zone offsets.** Method offsets stay whole minutes. A fitted zone may add fractional minutes per event, applied before the single rounding: Syria's Asr and Isha + 0.25, and Mauritania's interior sunrise − 2.5 (with NEAREST, round(x − 2.5) = floor(x) − 2). Bahrain's Dhuhr + 1.1 is not a zone fit but the SCIA's stated rule (the whole disc past the meridian, Q7), so it is a method field, `dhuhrAfterTransitMin = 1.1` on `bh_calendar`, added in step 7 before the single rounding; it holds at exact coordinates too (A2's «حسب موقعي بالضبط», or `bh_calendar` chosen outside Bahrain). Palestine's town offsets stay whole minutes: Qalqilya's published + 1.5 is applied as + 2 (8.8).

**E8 — Ramadan.** `ramadanOffsets: Map<TimeEvent, Int>` (Qatar: Maghrib + 1) and an Isha interval with a Ramadan value (Saudi Arabia: 90, or 120 in Ramadan). `ramadanDays: RamadanDays`:

- `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED`: Gregorian dates whose `HijrahDate` month is 9. This is what KACST's precomputed tables do (2026-02-18 to 2026-03-19, and 2027's month 9). Saudi announcements have matched Umm al-Qura every time since 1440 (the last difference was in 1439). With no announcements abroad in the first release (A11), Saudi Isha stays on these computed days; Appendix C moves it to the announced Ramadan (Q4).
- No authority calendar (Q3). Qatar's Ministry API applies its + 1 on its own Hijri labels, which are no fixed shift of Umm al-Qura: a day behind on 7 of 11 dates sampled in 1442–1444, equal in 1446 (1 Ramadan = 2025-03-01, Qatar's announced day), a day ahead from Jumada II 1447 to Rabi I 1448 (1 Ramadan 1447 = 2026-02-17, against the announced 02-18), equal again from Rabi II to Sha'ban 1448 (2026-10-11 aside), and a day ahead in Ramadan 1448, whose + 1 it already applies from 2027-02-07 (Umm al-Qura's 1 Ramadan is 02-08). Qatar Calendar House's dates equal Umm al-Qura on every date checked, and it and both Doha mosques of the owner's dataset applied the + 1 from 2026-02-18 to 03-19. So `qa_awqaf` uses `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED` like `qa_qch`, and the API's shifted edge days are quirk days of the fit (8.3). With `sa_umm_al_qura`, these are the only profiles that name `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED`.
- `PLACE_CALENDAR` (the default): the place's Hijri calendar. Outside Tunisia there is none in the first release (A11), so it means `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED` there (6.10). The generic presets (`umm_al_qura`) use it, and so would an authority profile computed outside its country, which «طرق أخرى» no longer offers there (6.4, owner 2026-10-18).

The rule applies to the evening of each Ramadan day, not to the eve of 1 Ramadan. The buffered `RamadanDetector.isRamadan` (from the day before 1 Ramadan to Eid day) never drives it.

**E9 — Revisions.** `revisions: List<MethodRevision(validFrom: LocalDate, change)>`, so a date before a rule change gets the old rule:

- Qatar: Fajr − 1 from 2020-08-20.
- Syria: Maghrib + 2 from 2025-03-30, provisional: the day after the last row of the Ramadan 1446 tables (sunset + 0). The 1447 tables (from 2026-02-19) are the first official ones with + 2; the Arbin mosque calendar that copies them also has + 2 in its rows before Ramadan 1447, the earliest of which fit autumn 2025 (supporting evidence only, Q12).
- Qatar Calendar House: Dhuhr and Asr + 1 from 2025-06-26 (1 Muharram 1447).
- UAE: Jumu'a at 13:15 from 2022-01-07 and 12:45 from 2026-01-02 (A18).
- Saudi Fajr 19° before 1430: not shipped (Q4). KACST's own service, the published source, gives 18.5° for every year (Medina 2000 and 2008: 94–95 % of Fajr times exact at 18.5°, none at 19°). The 19° rests only on secondary sources (a PrayTimes.js code comment), and no traveller needs a date before 2009.

A future change by an authority ships as a new revision in an app update.

**E10 — Terrain class for Maghrib.** `maghribTerrain`. Yemen: Maghrib + 6 at or below 650 m, + 10 at or above 1,100 m. A place's own class comes from its catalogue `dem` (6.8); between 650 and 1,100 m it takes the nearest official location's class, so Seiyun (`dem` 663 m) and Marib (1,095 m) keep their own. `NEAREST` (6.5) maps a place to the nearest official location of its own class within 60 km: plain nearest would give 7.9 % of Yemen's populated places a class that does not match their elevation. A mapped place uses that location's class, and its elevation for the `Pressure` horizon, like every other zone parameter. With no location of its class within 60 km, the place takes the nearest location of either class, with that location's class and elevation, however far (owner rule, 2026-10-11; 8.8). Only at exact coordinates («حسب موقعي بالضبط», A2, where the validity guard acts, and in the guard's reference at the place, 6.5) does a place use its own class and `dem` (Q2).

**E11 — Extra events: not built (A14).** No Imsak, first-adhan or other extra row is stored or shown in the first release. The values the research found stay in 8.2 and 8.7.

**E12 — Maghrib by angle** is the existing `maghribAngle`, listed for completeness. No shipped method uses it: it served the Jafari methods, which are not offered (A4).

**Order of operations.** The README states the same order, so the Python reference follows it too.

0. Pick the method's revision for the actual date (E9).
1. Raw sunrise, sunset, transit and Asr of D's solar cycle (Solver, step 1), plus Maghrib by angle (E12), with the E2 horizons, the E4 evaluation, E13 and the E6 points.
2. The 5-hour minimum day and night on sunrise and sunset (D6): for the Diyanet presets wherever the day or night is shorter than 5 h; for the others only at places whose year has a day without a sunrise, a sunset or an Asr; and `irn`'s Mecca cycle in its polar seasons.
3. Polar substitution, only for the times still absent.
4. All of Maghrib's adjustments: the method offset (with its E9 revision and E10 class), the E8 Ramadan offset on Ramadan days, the zone's Maghrib offset (published fixed, E7, or derived from its base zone, 8.8), and its E16 elevation shift.
5. Fajr and Isha by angle, or by interval: an Isha interval counts from step 4's Maghrib, with the E8 Ramadan interval (Saudi Arabia: 120); an Asr by interval (E15) counts back from step 4's Maghrib.
6. The high-latitude rule, on the night given by steps 1–3; `irn`'s southern summer rule takes, for Isha and for Fajr separately, the days of the mapped zone's `switchDays` range for that event where it has one (6.5), otherwise those of the fitted switch rule of 7.4; with no zone (exact coordinates) both follow the fitted rule.
7. The other events' method offsets, their E8 Ramadan offsets, their zone offsets (published, fitted as in E7, such as Qatar's towns' Fajr + 1, or derived from the base zone, 8.8) and the E16 elevation shifts of Fajr, sunrise and an Isha by angle (an Isha by interval already follows Maghrib's shift from step 4).
8. The `ishaLatest` cap, and a custom method's `ishaEarliest` floor; then the validity envelope (E19, 7.7): every method at a mainland French place, and a custom method anywhere (6.11).
9. The user's adjustments, applied after the cap so that an adjustment is never swallowed by it. Each stepper moves its own time: the Maghrib stepper does not carry into an interval Isha, and the Isha stepper still moves Isha. Then the window check: a time that was not past a bound of the sun's disk and that the adjustments carried past it by more than 1 min is held at the bound and marked ESTIMATED, with `explain()` naming the adjustment; a time already past a bound before the adjustments (a preset's Dhuhr at transit outside mainland France, an official table's time, 6.5) keeps an adjustment toward the bound and is held, likewise marked, at its unadjusted time against one away from it. The bounds are Dhuhr before transit + 1.2, Asr before the factor-1 shadow, Maghrib before sunset, Fajr at or after the computed sunrise (not the adjusted one), and sunrise after its computed time, which ends Fajr's time (7.7). The twilight bounds (18°, 12°, the curve) stay the user's own, as `ishaEarliest` does.
10. The order guard ("Event order" below) on the unrounded instants: the walk within the day, then the night hold. Then one rounding per event (E1), and the walk again on the minutes. In the rounding, an Isha or Asr by interval takes Maghrib's mode, a Maghrib, Isha, Fajr or sunrise less than one minute from the lowest point of its night rounds to the nearest minute whatever its mode or E19 bound, and a time E19 moved to a bound rounds inward.

Steps 2 and 3, and the D5 rule of step 6, never act in Arab places. The seasonal curve of `mcw`, `london_unified` and `gmp`'s Fajr, and the summer forms of `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost` (7.4), still apply there if the user picks one.

**Event order.** The README states two orders, and the order guard of step 10 keeps the second.

- *Astronomical order*, on the unrounded instants of steps 1–3: sunrise(D) < transit(D) < Asr(D) < sunset(D) < sunrise(D+1), for every event present. A real Asr always follows transit, since its altitude is below the noon sun's, so step 3 never replaces it to make room (7.4): at Rovaniemi (66.5039°N 25.7294°E) on 2026-12-21 it comes 33 s after transit (10:15:07 and 10:15:40 UTC). The night of day D, N(D), from sunset(D) to sunrise(D+1) as the Night paragraph above defines it (after steps 1–3), has a *lowest point*: the instant of the sun's lowest altitude in it (where its altitude stops falling, found to 0.1 s by the bracketed root-finder) at the point where sunset is computed; on a night one of whose ends steps 2–3 set, the middle of N(D) instead, since the clamp centres the night on its own Dhuhr: for the Diyanet presets their published Dhuhr, transit + 5, so 5 min after the sun's lowest point (7.4). Day D and day D+1 therefore find the same instant. The sun's lowest point lies within 5 s of the middle of any night shorter than 8 h (0 s at the solstice, within 1 s on nights under 5 h) and up to about 1 min from it on the long winter nights of 65°N, where no time comes near it. Every real evening time comes before the lowest point and every real morning time after it; on a night shorter than 5 h the sun stays above −6.2°, so no twilight angle of the methods is reached there.
- *Displayed order*, on the final instants and again on the minutes: Fajr(D) ≤ sunrise(D) ≤ Dhuhr(D) ≤ Asr(D) ≤ Maghrib(D) ≤ Isha(D) ≤ Fajr(D+1). No time ever comes before the one it follows; the only ties are the permitted ones below, ordered by (day, event). A day's label, `PrayerDay.date`, is the civil date in `place.zone` whose cycle step 1 of the Solver selects.

The method's definitions, offsets, caps and floors, and the user's steppers, can each break the displayed order: a fixed interval longer than half a short night (`ecfr_90` at Luleå, 65.5848°N 22.1547°E, on 2026-06-21, a 53-minute night: Isha = Maghrib + 90 at 01:37 CEST on 22 June, 127 min after the next Fajr = sunrise − 90 at 23:30 on 21 June); a Dhuhr offset larger than the real Asr's lead on transit (`tunisia_18_18` at Rovaniemi on 2026-12-21: Dhuhr + 7 at 10:22 UTC, Asr at 10:16; Diyanet's Asr + 4 against its Dhuhr + 5 whenever Asr comes less than 1 min after transit); an `ishaLatest` cap before Maghrib (`mwl_angle`'s 22:45 at Bergen in June, sunset 23:11); an Asr by interval before Dhuhr (`yt_traditional`'s Maghrib − 168 where the winter day is shorter than about 5.6 h, as at Trondheim); and steppers of ± 30 where two times are less than 30 min apart. Precedence, highest first: (1) the displayed order; (2) the night's lowest point as the boundary between prayer days D and D+1; (3) no prayer shown before the start the method gives it, except Fajr, which the walk holds at sunrise rather than showing it after it, since sunrise ends Fajr's time and the walk never moves sunrise; (4) the real events and the method's own definitions and offsets, then the user's steppers. The guard comes after step 9, so it can hold a time a stepper moved; the stepper keeps its value.

1. *The walk*, within day D: Fajr is held at no later than sunrise, then Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha in turn at no earlier than the time before each. A Dhuhr offset that passes a real Asr therefore shows Asr at Dhuhr, never Dhuhr earlier: the method's Dhuhr is where it starts Dhuhr's time, and an Asr shown later is still within Asr's time. Diyanet's Asr = Dhuhr (D6, 7.4) is this tie. It is not D6's rejected "Asr = Dhuhr for every method": with no Asr at all, D6's midpoint stays.
2. *The night hold*: Maghrib and Isha of D at or before N(D)'s lowest point, Fajr and sunrise of D at or after N(D−1)'s. It needs only steps 1–3, so each day applies it alone and the two days of a night agree. It never moves a real angle time. It holds a fixed interval longer than half the night: an Isha after Maghrib or sunset (E8's Ramadan interval and E18's seasonal ones included), a Fajr before sunrise, `ru_dum_rt`'s intervals, the summer forms of 7.4 and custom intervals; that happens from about 62.5°N for 120 min and 64°N for 90 min up to the polar gate near 65.7°N (D6), beyond which the 5-hour night leaves room for any interval up to 150 min from sunset or sunrise unless the steppers lengthen it. It also holds an `ishaEarliest` floor, an E19 Isha floor that lies past the lowest point, the user's steppers and, on the shortest nights just south of the polar gate, a Maghrib or sunrise offset. Where both of a night's intervals are too long, Isha and Fajr meet at the lowest point; where one is, it alone moves. At Luleå on 2026-06-21 (sunset 00:06:48 and sunrise 00:59:48 CEST on 22 June, lowest point 00:33:18), `ecfr_90` gives Maghrib 00:07, Isha 00:33, Fajr of 22 June 00:33 and sunrise 01:00.

The hold takes the earlier of each evening time and the lowest point, and the later of each morning time and it, so it keeps the walk's order. A time either step moves is ESTIMATED, with the rule `HELD_IN_ORDER` or `NIGHT_LOWEST_POINT`, unless it was already ESTIMATED, when it keeps its own rule; `explain()` names the time it was held at and what moved it (the interval, the offset, the cap, the floor or the stepper). Rounding cannot invert the night: an evening time lies at or before the lowest point and a morning time at or after it, and those less than one minute from it round to the nearest minute whatever their E1 mode, so an evening minute never follows a morning one. The walk on the minutes can then only make ties, between instants less than a minute apart with different rounding modes, and a time it changes keeps its status.

**Permitted ties**, on the minutes:

- Dhuhr = Asr: Asr held at Dhuhr by the walk (a Diyanet day without an Asr shadow; `tunisia_18_18` at Rovaniemi on 2026-12-21, both 10:22 UTC, Asr ESTIMATED), or a real Asr less than a minute after Dhuhr rounding to its minute (66.53°N 25.73°E on 2026-12-21 under `mwl`: transit 10:15:07, Asr 10:15:25, both 10:15 and COMPUTED).
- Isha(D) = Fajr(D+1): both less than a minute from the lowest point, real (an angle the sun only just reaches, under a rule that keeps real times) or estimated (the middle-of-the-night rule of 7.4, or held there: `ecfr_90` at Luleå, 00:33).
- Any other neighbours: only where the walk held one at the other or the night hold put both at the lowest point (each moved time ESTIMATED), or where the rounding and the walk on the minutes put two instants on one minute (status unchanged): instants less than a minute apart under one rounding mode, or less than two under different modes (`umm_al_qura` at Luleå on 2026-07-15 with Fajr + 30 and sunrise − 30: Fajr 01:45:31 CEST, rounded up, and sunrise 01:46:32, rounded down, both 01:46). Such a rounding tie can join any two neighbours, Isha(D) and Fajr(D+1) included when only one of them is less than a minute from the lowest point: an Isha rounded up just over a minute before it beside a Fajr rounded to the nearest minute, or an Isha rounded to the nearest minute beside a Fajr rounded down just over a minute after it.

**Consumers** (6.9) take each event's instant from the `PrayerDay` and rely on this contract instead of checking the order again: each reads only the events it needs, and a tie is a valid day. The adhkar scheduler resolves only the requested event, and a period between two prayers that a tie makes equal is empty that day (6.9). The silence planner builds each prayer's window from that prayer's own instant, so on a tie two windows are counted from the same instant, each with its own start, and UNSILENCE keeps the latest end (6.9). Wake alarms and hifz reminders relative to a prayer read that prayer alone; the night dial sorts instants, ties in (day, event) order.

**High-latitude rules and polar fallback.** They are described with the European facts that motivate them, in 7.4. Every preset and authority profile carries a rule and a polar fallback: `highLatitude` has no default, so each names the D5 rule or its own, with `polar = MIN_DAY_NIGHT_5H` (D6). This keeps "every preset carries a rule" true wherever a method is computed; until 2026-10-18 «طرق أخرى» also offered Arab profiles farther north (`dz_marw` or `ma_habous` in Paris), which it no longer does (6.4). In an Arab-first build (A15) M2 is not built yet, so AM1 builds D5's angle-based rule itself, which every preset and profile without its own rule evaluates every night except `mdf_12` and `luxembourg_shoura` (it never binds in Arab places), and adds the `HighLatitudeRule` and `PolarFallback` types with the other rule values of 7.4 (D5's alternatives, the absent-nights form of the angle-based rule of `mdf_12` and `luxembourg_shoura`, the presets' own rules, the polar rules of D6 and Diyanet's clamp) as stubs. `mdf_12` and `luxembourg_shoura` stay offered: 12° and 13° are reached every night in every Arab place, so their rule never acts there. The stubs throw in tests and debug builds if they ever act. A release build cannot reach them: the Europe switch keeps every place outside Tunisia and the Arab League closed (2), and until M2 the presets whose own rules come with it (`mcw`, `london_unified`, `gmp`, `dum_rf`, `uk_18_nearest_day`, `mwl_angle`, `diyanet_eu`, `diyanet_tr`, `bg_grand_mufti`, `irn`, `ru_dum_rt`, `hr_miz_zagreb`, `si_islamska_skupnost`, `apbif`) are not offered, even under «طرق أخرى». So `day()` still never throws there. The tests that need an M2 rule join at M2 (9.2).

**Output.**

```kotlin
enum class TimeEvent { FAJR, SUNRISE, DHUHR, ASR, MAGHRIB, ISHA }
enum class EventStatus { COMPUTED, ESTIMATED, ABSENT }

data class EventTime(
    val event: TimeEvent,
    val at: Instant?,          // rounded to the minute; null iff ABSENT
    val status: EventStatus,
    val rule: RuleName?,       // the rule or fallback that produced an ESTIMATED time, or the order guard's HELD_IN_ORDER or NIGHT_LOWEST_POINT (6.2)
)

/** Prayer day [date], a civil date in [zone]: the solar cycle that step 1 of the solver selects for it (6.2). Fajr may fall on date − 1 and Isha of date's evening on date + 1, in [zone]. */
data class PrayerDay(
    val date: LocalDate, val zone: ZoneId, val placeKey: String,
    val events: List<EventTime>,
)

object PrayerEngine {
    val SUPPORTED_YEARS = 2020..2100          // the actual dates, the span validated against DE441
    fun day(place: PrayerPlace.Coordinates, date: LocalDate, method: PrayerMethod,
            ramadanDay: Boolean = false, zone: OfficialZone? = null, baseZone: OfficialZone? = null,
            offsetWindow: ClosedRange<LocalDate>? = null,
            adjustments: Map<TimeEvent, Int> = emptyMap()): PrayerDay   // the choice's steppers (step 9); the validity guard passes none (6.5)
    fun explain(place: PrayerPlace.Coordinates, date: LocalDate, method: PrayerMethod,
                ramadanDay: Boolean = false, zone: OfficialZone? = null, baseZone: OfficialZone? = null,
                offsetWindow: ClosedRange<LocalDate>? = null,
                adjustments: Map<TimeEvent, Int> = emptyMap()): EngineExplanation
}
```

The engine knows nothing about the Hijri calendar. The repository passes `ramadanDay`, true when the date is a Ramadan day under the method's `ramadanDays` (E8), and the zone the place maps to (6.5): its point, per-event points (E6), elevations, pressure and temperature (E2 `Refraction`), `PerZone` horizons (E2) and offsets (published or fitted, E7). For a zone with a `baseZoneId` (Jordan's further places, Algeria's attached places, Kuwait's places, Palestine's listed places, `bosnia_iz`'s Takvim places in BA, HR and RS and its five Montenegrin towns, Slovenia's five places, Albania's ten cities and Kosovo's seven towns, 8.8), the repository also passes that zone of the same authority as `baseZone`. The engine then runs the method at the base zone and, in steps 4 and 7, adds the zone's offset: the published one for Kuwait, Palestine, Slovenia, Albania, Kosovo's seven towns and Montenegro's five towns, for Jordan's further places the derived offset for the date's month: for each event, the zone's own unrounded time (steps 1-7 at its own points and elevations) minus the base zone's on the 15th of that month, rounded to the half-minute; and for Algeria's attached places the ministry's own rule: for each event, the ceiling of the largest difference between the zone's and the base zone's unrounded times over `offsetWindow`, the window that contains the date, which the repository passes (6.9; the ministry's Hijri month, its start taken from the 12 month starts per Hijri year at the top of `official-zones/dz_marw.json`, or from `HijrahDate`, Umm al-Qura as in E8, for a year not there yet; for a place whose base zone has `halfMonthWindows`, the Adrar zone, days 1–15, then days 17 to the end, which also serve day 16), so the place's time is never earlier than its own computed time, each offset being cached per zone and window (8.8); and for `bosnia_iz`'s Takvim places (Bosnia's 110, and Croatia's four and Serbia's 11 at points fitted the same way to their printed differences; each at its E6 point φ, λ) the Takvim's rule for the date's month, from Sarajevo at 43°52'N 18°26'E: Δtλ = 4 × (18°26' − λ) and Δtφ = τ × g × (φ − 43°52'), with τ the month's Table 1 mean (`takvimMeans`: the north row, or the south row with its printed sign) and g 1.1 north, 0.9 south; Fajr and sunrise take Δtλ + Δtφ, Dhuhr Δtλ, Asr, Maghrib and Isha Δtλ − Δtφ, each of the three rounded to the whole minute (8.8). A place in Montenegro outside the five towns' areas has no listed zone: the repository passes a zone at the place's own point with `baseZoneId` Sarajevo and, on all six times, the whole minute nearest to 4 × (18.41 − its longitude) (8.8); it is the table the validity guard checks there, and where the guard acts the place gets `bosnia_iz` at its own point (6.5). Steps 8-10 follow, so each time is rounded once. With `zone = null` the method runs at the place's own coordinates. A zone with a `baseZoneId` (6.5) takes its base zone's unrounded times plus its own offset, rounded once: published or derived as above for each of the places listed there (8.8).

**Invariants**, tested on a grid of about 100k days and on the Arab grid (both 9.1), plus the polar and Asr edge cases of 9.2:

- `day()` never throws.
- No engine preset or profile leaves any of the six times absent: every one carries a rule and a polar fallback. `inm` never reaches the engine, and is exempt.
- The event order above holds: the astronomical order on the unrounded instants of steps 1–3; Fajr(D) ≤ sunrise(D) ≤ Dhuhr(D) ≤ Asr(D) ≤ Maghrib(D) ≤ Isha(D) ≤ Fajr(D+1) on the final instants and on the minutes, with no inversion; Maghrib(D) and Isha(D) at or before the night's lowest point and Fajr(D+1) and sunrise(D+1) at or after it, before rounding; and only the permitted ties, each with its status, ordered by (day, event).
- Results do not depend on the JVM's default time zone.
- On the Arab grid no time is estimated by the D5 rule, a polar fallback or the order guard: they never act there on a preset or profile (only a custom method or the user's steppers can bring the walk into play).

**INM, unchanged.** `InmPrayerDays.day(delegationId, date, settings = PrayerFormulaSettings.OFFICIAL)` takes the `DayPrayerTimes` that `InmPrayerTimes` already returns and relabels each hour:minute as an instant at the fixed offset +01:00. It does no math, so the INM times cannot drift. Tests check:

- the round trip for 258 delegations × 365 days.

### 6.3 Methods, chips and reference presets

`PrayerMethod` is a new `@Serializable` type in `method/`, separate from `PrayerFormulaSettings`, which stays the TV's contract.

```kotlin
data class PrayerMethod(
    val id: String,
    val fajr: FajrRule,                     // Angle(deg) | MinutesBeforeSunrise(n)
    val isha: IshaRule,                     // Angle(deg) | MinutesAfter(n, from = MAGHRIB | SUNSET, ramadan: Int? = null)
    val ishaLatest: LocalTime? = null,      // ICCI's 22:45 display cap; a capped Isha is ESTIMATED
    val ishaEarliest: LocalTime? = null,    // custom only: «العشاء ليس قبل» (6.11); a held Isha is not ESTIMATED
    val maghribAngle: Double? = null,       // E12
    val offsets: Map<TimeEvent, Int> = emptyMap(),   // includes SUNRISE and ASR (Diyanet −7 / +4)
    val dhuhrAfterTransitMin: Double = 0.0,   // E7: bh_calendar 1.1, the whole disc past the meridian (stated by the SCIA)
    val asrShadow: Int = 1,
    val asr: AsrRule = AsrRule.Shadow,      // E15: Shadow | SignedShadow (factor asrShadow; dz_marw, in Algeria only) | BeforeMaghrib(minutes)
    val elevationShifts: List<ElevationShift> = emptyList(),   // E16: re_cir's minutes by elevation band
    val seasonalOffsets: Map<TimeEvent, List<Double>> = emptyMap(),   // E18: xk_bik's minutes at 36 points a year
    val takvimMeans: List<Double> = emptyList(),   // bosnia_iz: the Takvim's Table 1, 12 monthly means north of Sarajevo then 12 south, with printed signs (6.2, 8.8)
    val asrSun: AsrSun = AsrSun.GEOMETRIC,  // E13
    val sunModel: SunModel = SunModel.MEEUS,          // E14 (D17): MEEUS | VSOP87 | TEXTBOOK
    val twilightLatitudeScale: Double = 1.0,          // E17: ly_awqaf 0.99330562 (1 − e²)
    val highLatitude: HighLatitudeRule,     // no default: every preset and profile names one (D5)
    val polar: PolarFallback = PolarFallback.MIN_DAY_NIGHT_5H,
    val minDayNightHours: Int? = null,      // Diyanet: 5
    val rounding: Map<TimeEvent, Rounding> = Rounding.ALL_NEAREST,   // E1
    val horizon: Horizons = Horizons.STANDARD,       // E2: sunrise and sunset models
    val dipOnTwilight: Boolean = false,              // E3
    val sunEvaluation: Map<TimeEvent, SunEvaluation> = emptyMap(),   // E4; EVENT_INSTANT where absent
    val ramadanOffsets: Map<TimeEvent, Int> = emptyMap(),   // E8
    val ramadanDays: RamadanDays = RamadanDays.PLACE_CALENDAR,   // E8
    val revisions: List<MethodRevision> = emptyList(),      // E9
    val maghribTerrain: TerrainRule? = null,                // E10
    val jumua: JumuaRule = JumuaRule.AT_DHUHR,              // A18
    val evidence: Evidence,                 // PUBLISHED | FROM_TABLES | APPROXIMATE, plus a one-line source
    val fit: FitStats? = null,              // the profile's aggregate record (FitStats, below); decides the chip only for sa_umm_al_qura (6.11)
)

data class PrayerMethodChoice(               // what the phone stores, one per country, with the separate keys of 6.4
    val methodId: String,
    val asrShadow: Int? = null,
    val highLatitude: HighLatitudeRule? = null,
    val polar: PolarFallback? = null,
    val adjustments: Map<TimeEvent, Int> = emptyMap(),   // −30..30, never 0 (omitted)
    val custom: PrayerMethod? = null,                    // angles 10..20 by 0.5, phone-only range
    val zonePick: ZonePick? = null,                      // Exact | Zone(id); null = the mapped zone (6.5)
    val chooserAnswered: Boolean = false,                // a first-run chooser was answered (6.4)
    val methodPicked: Boolean = false,                   // the user answered a chooser or picked a method in PrayerMethodSheet (6.4); an Asr, adjustment or zone change alone leaves it false
) { val key: String }                                    // stable hash of the resolved choice; zonePick in, chooserAnswered and methodPicked out
```

**Fit records.** The fitting harness (9.4) writes a `FitStats` for each fitted profile (its aggregate over its zones) and one for each of its zones (`OfficialZone.fit`, 6.5) into the profile and zone JSON. The explainer shows the zone's own record at the places mapped to it, and for `sa_umm_al_qura` the profile's anywhere in Saudi Arabia (6.11, 9.5); no record changes the chip (A6).

```kotlin
data class FitStats(                          // written by fit_authority.py (9.4); a profile's aggregate or one zone's own record
    val fittedOn: List<FitSample>,             // the official data the shipped parameters were fitted on
    val heldOut: List<FitSample>,              // official data never used to fit the parameters they score (9.5)
    val exact: Map<TimeEvent, Double>,         // held-out share exact per time (FAJR, SUNRISE, DHUHR, ASR, MAGHRIB, ISHA) over all of heldOut, quirk days left out
    val within1: Map<TimeEvent, Double>,       // held-out share within 1 minute, quirk days left out
    val daysPerMonth: Map<TimeEvent, List<Int>>,   // held-out days per calendar month, 12 counts per time
    val heldOutRamadanDays: Int,               // held-out Ramadan days, counted before quirk days are left out (E8 methods)
    val quirkDays: Int,                        // documented quirk days (8.3), counted apart
    val maxDiffMin: Int,                       // held-out maximum difference, quirk days included
    val regions: Set<String> = emptySet(),     // sa_umm_al_qura only: the admin1 regions of its held-out points
) {
    fun passesTierA(method: PrayerMethod): Boolean   // 9.5; computed on the phone from these fields, never stored
}

data class FitSample(
    val source: String,                        // "jo_ministry_2025_pdf", "kacst_api", "om_mara_html", ...
    val from: LocalDate,
    val to: LocalDate,                         // the period
    val days: Int,                             // days with official data in that period
    val zones: Int,                            // zones or sampled points
    val monthSplit: Boolean = false,           // scored by a month-split (9.4, 9.5)
)
```

**Chips.** One chip per method, from its evidence, its fit and the place; 6.11 gives the full rule and the lines that go with each chip.

- «رسمية» where the times follow an official source for the place: a national body's own definition in its own country (`inm` for a Tunisian place, `bosnia_iz` in BA, `diyanet_tr` in TR; principle 6), `sa_umm_al_qura` anywhere in Saudi Arabia, and an authority's table at the place's mapped zone in that zone's own country, whatever its fit record (A6, owner 2026-10-20).
- «فلكية» everywhere else: every preset, the A3 formulas, exact coordinates, a place where the validity guard acts, beyond every zone's reach or outside the authority's country, the Kurdistan Region, a table no authority publishes, and a custom method. The tier-A gate (9.5) no longer changes the chip; «طريقتك» shows the record.

«طريقتك» gives each method a one-line source. In Austria, for example, `diyanet_eu` reads "re-derived from IGGÖ's tables; summer times may differ by a few minutes".

**`inm` is for Tunisian places only.** It is the only method of `PrayerPlace.Inm` (D7), which has no method sheet, and the method sheet of a `Coordinates` place never lists it.

**Where the methods are listed.** The reference presets below serve every region. The 27 European presets are in 7.5, the 23 profiles in 8.7 (19 authority profiles and the four fitted country profiles of A3), and the four profiles of overseas France in 7.6. "Rule" means the generic rule of D5; every preset and profile carries it, or its own rule, with the polar rule of D6.

| id | Fajr | Isha | Offsets (min) | High latitude | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `inm` | 18° + dip | 18° + dip | Dhuhr +7, Maghrib +2 | n/a (Tunisian places only, through INM) | published |
| `tunisia_18_18` | 18° | 18° | Dhuhr +7, Maghrib +2 | rule | published values; the summer rule is ours |
| `mwl` | 18° | 17° | none (the libraries' convention, not the 1986 ±2) | rule | published angles |
| `isna` | 15° | 15° | none | rule | published |
| `karachi` | 18° | 18° | none | rule | published (University of Islamic Sciences, Karachi); Belgium's 18°/18° practice (5.6) |
| `umm_al_qura` | 18.5° | Maghrib + 90 (Maghrib + 120 on Ramadan days, `PLACE_CALENDAR`) | none; Saudi rounding (Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr and Maghrib up, sunrise down) | rule (Fajr) | from tables: KACST publishes no written method |
| `egyptian` | 19.5° | 17.5° | none | rule | EGSA's angles (8.2); evidence set with A6's chip rule (4) |
| `custom` | user: 12°–20° or minutes before sunrise (6.11) | user: 12°–18° or minutes after Maghrib (6.11) | user, within the ranges of 6.11 | user (the rules of 7.4) | — |

- `umm_al_qura` takes `sa_umm_al_qura`'s rounding only. Its Ramadan Isha (Maghrib + 120) keeps `PLACE_CALENDAR`, which outside Tunisia is the computed Umm al-Qura calendar (E8). Its chip is «فلكية» everywhere it is offered, European method sheets included; only `sa_umm_al_qura` is «رسمية», in Saudi Arabia.
- The former Europe presets `algeria` and `morocco` are replaced one for one by the fitted profiles `dz_marw` and `ma_habous` (8.7), which answer the open "Moroccan ministry's own parameters".
- In all, 61 presets and profiles plus `custom`: the 7 above, 27 European (7.5), the 23 profiles of 8.7 and the 4 of overseas France (7.6).

**Changes reschedule.** Changing the method, the Asr factor, the rule, an adjustment or the picked zone invalidates the caches and reschedules silence, wake alarms, adhkar and hifz reminders, as a location change does.

### 6.4 Country defaults, choosers and the per-country choice

`CountryMethodDefaults.forCountry(iso2, territory, admin1, admin2, point)` picks the default. `point` is the place's stored point (6.8), which tells Croatia's Zagreb area and Serbia's Preševo Valley (within about 12 km of Preševo, Bujanovac or Medveđa, 8.8) from the rest of their countries. `admin1` is an RU place's stored federal subject (`place_admin1`, 6.8), which tells Moscow, Moscow oblast and Tatarstan from the rest of Russia; Norway no longer needs the latitude, since `irn` serves all of it. `territory` is the resolved territory's zone id (6.8), which tells the Gaza Strip (Asia/Gaza) from the West Bank and Jerusalem (Asia/Hebron, or Asia/Jerusalem, which a place in the Jerusalem governorate can take, Q1); `admin2` is an FR place's stored département (`place_admin2`, 6.8), which orders the French list.

| Countries | Default | First-run chooser |
|---|---|---|
| TN (every Tunisian place is a delegation, 6.8) | `inm`, the only method (D7) | no |
| AT | `diyanet_eu`: IGGÖ's nine city tables within 25 km of the city, else Diyanet's table for the nearest of its Austrian places, as IGGÖ's site does (6.5, 7.5) | no |
| LU | `luxembourg_shoura`, the Shoura's one table everywhere in Luxembourg, with the validity guard (Troisvierges, near its threshold, is settled by the scan; 6.5) | no |
| BA | `bosnia_iz`: Sarajevo's table plus the monthly difference of the nearest of the Takvim's 110 places (Sarajevo included), whatever the distance, derived on the phone from the Takvim's own rule (8.8) | no |
| HR, SI | HR: `hr_miz_zagreb` (MIZ Zagreb's own table, one zone «زغرب») where Zagreb is the nearest of the Takvim's five Croatian towns, otherwise `bosnia_iz` with the Takvim difference of the nearest of Rijeka, Osijek, Split and Dubrovnik, whatever the distance (8.8); `forCountry` reads the place's point for HR, and until the user picks a method (`methodPicked` false) `onPlaceChanged` resolves it again on every Croatian place change, as in France. SI: `si_islamska_skupnost`, the Islamic Community in Slovenia's vaktija.si: Ljubljana's table plus the published offsets of the nearest of its six places, whatever the distance (8.8) | no |
| TR | `diyanet_tr` | no |
| AL | `al_kmsh`: KMSH's Tirana table plus the published shift of the nearest of its ten cities (8.8) | no |
| XK; RS in the Preševo Valley (Preševo, Bujanovac, Medveđa) | `xk_bik`: BIK's takvim, one table for both, with its seven towns' published shifts (8.8); `forCountry` reads the place's point for RS, as for HR | no |
| RS elsewhere | `bosnia_iz` with the published difference of the nearest of the Bosnian IZ's Serbian places (8.8); until the user picks a method, `onPlaceChanged` resolves the two RS rows again on every Serbian place change, as in Croatia | no |
| ME | `bosnia_iz` with vaktija.ba's five Montenegrin towns' differences, elsewhere `bosnia_iz` at the place's own point, since Sarajevo's times shifted by longitude fail the validity guard there, except in the far north around Pljevlja, near its threshold, which the scan settles (6.5, 8.8); the Islamic Community in Montenegro's own takvim was not found online | no |
| MK | `mk_bfi`: the BFI's Skopje times for North Macedonia, with the validity guard, which does not act at Tetovo; Struga, Ohrid and Debar, near its threshold, are settled by the scan (6.5; bfi.mk shows one day at a time; fitted in M3 from the days collected from AM0, 8.8) | no |
| BG | `bg_grand_mufti`: the Chief Mufti's table of the nearest of its 48 cities (8.8) | no |
| GR | `diyanet_tr` (inferred: the Thrace muftis' tables are not yet looked for; AM0 looks, 8.8) | no |
| PT | `mwl` (D15) | the Asr factor only |
| ES | `mwl` (preselected), `fajr15_isha15` | yes |
| IT | `mwl`, `fajr15_isha15`; no preselection | yes |
| IE | `ie_ifi`: the IFI's table of the nearest of its 33 towns, whatever the distance, with the validity guard (6.5, 7.5, Q23) | no |
| RU, Moscow and Moscow oblast | `dum_rf`: DUM RF's one table «موسكو» for both, the scope its sheet names, with the validity guard (6.5, 7.4) | no |
| RU, Tatarstan | `ru_dum_rt`: the table of the nearest of DUM RT's 44 places, Kazan, Naberezhnye Chelny and the district centres (6.5, 7.5) | no |
| RU, elsewhere | `mwl` (D15): no other muftiate's table was found or checked (12); until the user picks a method (`methodPicked` false), every Russian place change resolves these three rows again, as France's fallback is | no |
| FR | `mdf_12` (12° / 12°, D15), `fajr15_isha15`, `diyanet_eu`, `mwl`; in Rhône (69) `rhone_ctir` is added and listed first, in Bas-Rhin (67) `diyanet_eu` is listed first; no preselection; every time inside the validity envelope (E19, 7.7) | yes |
| BE | `karachi`, `be_sunrise_90`, `diyanet_eu`, `mwl`; no preselection | yes |
| NL | `diyanet_eu`, `mwl`, `fajr15_isha15`; no preselection | yes |
| CH | `fajr15_isha15`, `fajr15_isha90`, `mwl`, `diyanet_eu`, `mdf_12`; no preselection | yes |
| DE, SE | `diyanet_eu` (preselected), `mwl` | yes |
| GB | `fajr15_isha15`, `mwl`; no preselection; plus the Asr factor; the UK presets under «طرق أخرى» (D15) | yes |
| NO, Svalbard included (D2) | `irn`: IRN's table for the place's municipality (D6, 6.5, 7.4) | no |
| every other European country, Finland and Poland included | `mwl` | no |
| RE | `re_cir`, from the CIR's region of the place (`NAMED_REGIONS`, 6.5; 7.6) | the Asr factor only |
| YT | `yt_traditional`, preselected, and `mwl` (A1, 7.6) | yes |
| MQ | `mq_ccim` (MWL as the IslamicFinder page the CCIM links gives it, 7.6) | no |
| GF | `gf_a2cmgf` (MWL as A2CMGF's calendars give it, 7.6) | no |
| GP, NC, PF, PM, BL, MF, WF | `mwl` | no |
| SA | `sa_umm_al_qura` | no |
| EG | `eg_egsa` | no |
| MA, Western Sahara included (A1) | `ma_habous` | no |
| DZ | `dz_marw` | no |
| LY | `ly_awqaf` | no |
| MR | `mr_islamic_affairs` | no |
| JO | `jo_awqaf` | no |
| PS, West Bank and Jerusalem | `ps_dar_al_ifta` | no |
| PS, Gaza Strip | `ps_gaza_awqaf` (A17); until the user picks a method, `onPlaceChanged` resolves the two PS rows again on every Palestinian place change, as in Croatia | no |
| SY | `sy_awqaf` | no |
| KW | `kw_awqaf` | no |
| QA | `qa_awqaf` (A5) | no |
| AE | `ae_awqaf` | no |
| OM | `om_mara` | no |
| YE | `ye_awqaf` | no |
| IQ | `iq_sunni_endowment` | no (A4) |
| LB | `lb_dar_al_fatwa` | no (A4) |
| BH | `bh_calendar` | no (A4) |
| SD, SO, DJ, KM | the country's fitted profile (A3: `sd_fitted`, `so_fitted`, `dj_fitted`, `km_fitted`, from the owner's mosque dataset) | no |
| A land neighbour outside both regions (6.8), and any other country not listed above | `mwl` (D4) | no |
| No known country (6.8) | `mwl`, with a one-line «تأكيد الطريقة» | no |

Every list also offers «طرق أخرى», with only the methods followed at the place (owner, 2026-10-18: Slovenia's method makes no sense in Riyadh or New York). The international conventions are offered everywhere: the reference presets of 6.3 (`tunisia_18_18` for the diaspora among them), `mcw`, and `diyanet_tr`, whose authority computes for every city in the world. A body's method is offered in its own area: Diyanet's European tables (`diyanet_eu`) and the ECFR's rule (`ecfr_90`) in Europe outside Turkey, Russia's two muftiates in Russia, MIZ Zagreb in Croatia, `rhone_ctir`, `gmp` and `apbif` in France (a French pick is kept for all of France), the UK presets in the UK, `cil_lisbon` in Portugal, `mwl_angle` in Ireland, Dar al-Ifta in the Gaza Strip too (A17) and Qatar Calendar House in Qatar. It is also offered at the places its own tables name in another country, which its zones attach: Habous's Ceuta and Melilla, Jordan's Jerusalem entry, the Bosnian Takvim's Croatian, Serbian, Montenegrin and Slovenian towns, BIK's Preševo valley. A practice no body publishes is offered where the mosque dataset found it (5.6): `fajr15_isha90` in Germany as well as Switzerland. Each country's own list is offered there by definition. A stored choice whose method is not offered at the place gives way to the country's default there, and applies again where it is offered. «حسب موقعي بالضبط» where the default has zones. A Tunisian place has no list (D7).

**Choosers.** One component, `MethodChooserSheet`, serves every chooser country; `PrayerMethodSheet` is the picker. Each entry shows its chip and today's Fajr, Maghrib and Isha (owner, 2026-10-19; Fajr and Isha set the methods apart, by 16 min to over 2 h at Ivry-sur-Seine and New York, Maghrib only by 6–9 min). «اختر ما يوافق مسجدك», and a note that it can be changed later.

- **Dismissal** keeps the country's fallback: the preselected entry where there is one (DE and SE `diyanet_eu`, ES `mwl`, YT `yt_traditional`); otherwise FR `mdf_12` (`rhone_ctir` in 69, `diyanet_eu` in 67), BE `karachi`, CH `fajr15_isha15`, and `mwl` in NL, IT and GB (5.6).
- After any dismissed chooser (DE, SE, ES and YT included), «تأكيد الطريقة» stays until the user answers.

**One choice per country.** The phone keeps one `PrayerMethodChoice` per country, `method_choice_<CC>` (plus the separate keys below), holding the method, the Asr factor, the adjustments, the zone pick, whether the chooser was answered (`chooserAnswered`, for «تأكيد الطريقة») and whether the user picked the method (`methodPicked`, which stops the default being resolved again on a move: FR, HR, RS, RU, PS).

- When the place's choice key changes (its country, or Ceuta or Melilla: the keys below), `onPlaceChanged` (6.8) loads the stored choice for the new key, or uses the default above if there is none. On return, the earlier key's choice comes back unchanged.
- A Tunisian place has no stored choice: it always uses `inm` (D7).
- A French pick is kept for all of France (owner, 2026-10-11): `rhone_ctir` chosen in Lyon stays the choice in Paris, computed at Paris, until the user changes it. Until the user picks a method (`methodPicked` false: the French chooser not answered and nothing picked in `PrayerMethodSheet`), the method stays the current département's fallback (D4): `onPlaceChanged` resolves it again on every French place change, so a chooser dismissed in Paris gives `rhone_ctir` in Lyon, and one dismissed in Lyon gives `mdf_12` back in Paris. Each overseas code (RE, YT, …) has its own key.
- Where the default depends on the place within one key (Croatia and Serbia by the point, Russia by `place_admin1`, Palestine by the territory), `onPlaceChanged` resolves it again on every place change in that country until the user picks a method there, as in France: Zagreb to Split gives `bosnia_iz`, Belgrade to Preševo `xk_bik`, Kazan to Moscow `dum_rf`, Ramallah to Gaza City `ps_gaza_awqaf`.
- A place with no known country uses `method_choice_ZZ`. Western Sahara places are Moroccan and use `method_choice_MA` (A1). Ceuta and Melilla keep their own keys, `method_choice_ES-CE` and `method_choice_ES-ML`, so a `ma_habous` pick there does not follow the user into mainland Spain.
- **Background moves** (6.8: `BootReceiver`, WorkManager) cannot show a chooser. The move uses the stored choice for the new place's key (its country, or `ES-CE`, `ES-ML`, `ZZ`), or that key's default above, in a chooser country its fallback. Only when the key belongs to a chooser country whose chooser has not been answered does it add «اختر ما يوافق مسجدك» to the «تم تغيير الموقع إلى …» notification and show «تأكيد الطريقة» until the user answers. The chooser opens from the notification or at the next app start.

### 6.5 Official zones

Most Arab authorities publish zones, not formulas (8.1). The engine takes the zone as an input.

```kotlin
data class OfficialZone(
    val authority: String,                // "om_mara"
    val id: String,                       // the authority's own id where it has one ("0" = Muscat)
```
