```kotlin
    val nameAr: String,
    val point: LatLngE5,                  // published, or fitted from the tables
    val morningPoint: LatLngE5? = null,   // E6
    val eveningPoint: LatLngE5? = null,
    val eventPoints: Map<TimeEvent, LatLngE5> = emptyMap(),   // E6: a time at its own point (Bahrain)
    val elevationM: Int? = null,          // E2, E10; the morning elevation where they differ
    val eveningElevationM: Int? = null,   // Jordan's separate evening elevation (8.6)
    val pressureHpa: Double? = null,      // E2 Refraction(P, T, fallback) and E13; standard 1010 when null
    val temperatureC: Double? = null,     // standard 10 when null
    val sunriseAltitudeDeg: Double? = null,   // E2 PerZone
    val sunsetAltitudeDeg: Double? = null,
    val offsets: Map<TimeEvent, Double> = emptyMap(),        // fixed published offsets (Kuwait, Palestine, Slovenia's five places, Albania's ten cities, Kosovo's seven towns, Montenegro's five towns) or fitted (E7; Qatar's towns: Fajr + 1, 8.8)
    val baseZoneId: String? = null,                           // the zone whose unrounded times this one shifts; its own point still maps: Jordan's further places (Amman + a monthly offset derived from the place's own points), Kuwait's places (Kuwait City + the published offset), Palestine's listed places (Al-Aqsa + the town offset), Algeria's attached places (their reference + the ministry's monthly offset), `bosnia_iz`'s Takvim places in BA, HR and RS (Sarajevo + the monthly difference derived from `takvimMeans`, 6.2), Montenegro's places (Sarajevo + a fixed difference), Slovenia's five places (Ljubljana), Albania's ten cities (Tirana) and Kosovo's seven towns (the takvim's table) + the published offset; 8.8
    val halfMonthWindows: Boolean = false,                    // Algeria's Adrar reference only: its attached places' offset windows are days 1–15 and 17 to the end, day 16 taking the second (6.2, 8.8)
    val admin1: List<String> = emptyList(),  // the provinces (the UAE: emirates) a zone serves where zones follow them (Jordan's Amman zone: four, 8.8)
    val admin2: List<String> = emptyList(),  // the second-order divisions a zone serves, matched against `place_admin2` (6.8): `rhone_ctir`'s 69 (Q33)
    val namedPlaces: List<NamedPlace> = emptyList(),  // the places the authority itself names for this zone (Named places, below): first the zone's own town wherever the zone is a place the authority lists or a town it names (Kuwait's 12 places, Libya's 22 cities, Algeria's 70 places, Egypt's cities, Oman's named towns, and so on), then the further towns or areas it names for it: the UAE's areas Awqaf lists for a location, Jordan's towns the ministry names for a zone, Qatar's Al Khor and Al Wakra for Doha's table, Réunion's localities the CIR lists for a region (7.6); 8.8
    val reachKm: Double = Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY,  // no limit unless the authority's own rule sets one: KACST's entries (25 km to start, AM2 sets each from the entry's extent) and IGGÖ's cities (25 km); or where a town's published shift holds only in its area: Kosovo's seven towns and Montenegro's five (8 km to start, M3 sets them from the town extents); 8.8. A named place's area (`NamedPlace.areaKm`) is not a reach and never limits mapping
    val mappingTier: Int = 0,             // `NEAREST`: among the zones in reach, the lowest tier wins, then the nearest (Austria: IGGÖ's nine cities 0, Diyanet's Austrian entries 1; 6.5)
    val switchDays: Map<TimeEvent, IntRange> = emptyMap(),   // irn south of 63.56°N only (6.2 step 6): the entry's own days on the summer rule of 7.4, for ISHA and FAJR, where the fitted rule misses them; an event absent takes the rule's days. Days 1–365 of a common year, both ends included, each the same calendar date in every year (90 = 31 March, also in 2028). ISHA: first day off 15° .. last day off; back at 15° on last + 1. FAJR: first day held at a clock time (that of the 16° Fajr of day first − 1; from 21 June, that of day last + 1) .. last day held; back at 16° on last + 1
    val fit: FitStats? = null,            // the zone's own record (6.3), shown in «طريقتك» at the places mapped here (6.11, 9.5)
)

data class NamedPlace(                    // a place the authority itself names for a zone (Named places, below)
    val centre: LatLngE5,                 // the town centre: the catalogue point of the town, set in AM2 or M3; for Egypt's cities, the zone's `point`; for Qatar's Al Khor and Al Wakra, the town's point
    val catalogueIds: List<Long> = emptyList(),  // the catalogue rows that are this place (GeoNames ids, as `catalogueId`, 6.8), matched by name and point when the asset is built
    val areaKm: Double = 0.0,             // the place's own area: a point within this distance of `centre` is this place; 0 = its catalogue rows only. Egypt's cities 15 to start (AM2 sets each from the city's extent), Oman's named towns 10, Qatar's Al Khor and Al Wakra 6 (AM2 may adjust; 8.8). Not a reach: it never limits mapping (`reachKm` does)
)
```

- **Asset.** `android-app/app/src/main/assets/official-zones/<authority>.json`, about 1,670 rows in total (about 250 KB, plus the fit records of 6.3, under 1 KB per fitted zone): about 820 for the Arab authorities (Algeria's 70 and Iraq's 28 included) and about 850 for Europe and overseas France (IRN's 358, Austria's 199, the Takvim's 136 places in Bosnia, Croatia, Slovenia, Serbia and Montenegro, Bulgaria's 48, DUM RT's 44, the IFI's 33 towns and about 35 others), plus Diyanet's Turkish entries (one per district outside the merged province centres, at most 973 rows, about 100–150 KB more; `DISTRICT` below). Each row carries its named places (centre, catalogue ids and, where it is not 0, `areaKm`; about 3,000 in all, about 120 KB more) and, for an IRN entry whose days the fitted rule misses, `switchDays` as `{"ISHA": [first, last], "FAJR": [first, last]}`, either event alone where only one is missed. It does not go in `data/prayer-formula`: the bundling plugin also copies that folder into the TV, and it is the javaTest classpath root of INM's `delegation_params.json`, whose entries `InmPrayerFormulaTest` iterates. If the zones ever move under `data/`, `BundledDataPlugin`'s hard-coded folder list (`BundledDataPlugin.java:20`) first becomes a per-app setting.
- **No new place kind.** The place stays `PrayerPlace.Coordinates` (or `Inm` in Tunisia). The repository resolves the zone each time it computes: `ZoneMapping.resolve(place, method, choice.zonePick, year)` returns an `OfficialZone?` for the year of the date computed (the validity guard's verdict is per year, below). It reads the place's `admin1` and `admin2` (6.8) for `DISTRICT`, `ADMIN_AREA`, Rhône's `NATIONAL` zone, the by-province `NEAREST` rows (MR, DZ, AE, JO) and Iraq's Kurdistan Region (`EXACT`). Null means exact coordinates: the `EXACT` mapping, no zone in reach (Saudi Arabia beyond every KACST entry's `reachKm`), the validity guard, or «حسب موقعي بالضبط» (A2). The cache key (place key + `PrayerMethodChoice.key`), prefs keys, routing, `getDateRange` and D12 then apply unchanged.
- **Named places.** `namedPlaces` lists the places the authority itself names for a zone (`NamedPlace`). A place is one of them when its `catalogueId` (6.8) is among the entry's `catalogueIds`, or when its point lies within the entry's `areaKm` of its `centre`; only a place picked from the catalogue (or the first run's provisional city) has a `catalogueId`, so a GPS place counts by its point only, never by the row that labels it «قرب X» (6.8). `OfficialZone.names(place)` is true when the place is one of the zone's named places. The asset build (M5) matches each named place to its catalogue rows by name and point, as it matches IRN's entries to their municipalities (6.8), and reports any it cannot match; an area that serves no mapping (the UAE's Al Yarmook, 8.8) is left out. `areaKm` is 0, the catalogue rows only, except for Egypt's cities (15 km from the town centre to start, each set in AM2 from the city's extent), Oman's named towns (10 km) and Qatar's Al Khor and Al Wakra (6 km, which AM2 may adjust). Only those three rules map by membership: a place that is one of a zone's named places takes that zone first (a catalogue match before an area, the nearest centre where areas overlap), and any other place takes the zone of the rule's next step, with no reach limit: the city with the nearest town centre in Egypt, the nearest zone point in Oman, the nearest of QCH's six places by town point in Qatar (8.8). Elsewhere `namedPlaces` enters the mapping below only as the points its rules name (the UAE's areas, Jordan's towns, Réunion's localities), and `names` is read only on the zone the mapping picked. The validity guard below never acts on a place for which the mapped zone's `names(place)` is true; where the guard applies, it checks every other attached place. An area is never a reach: beyond every area the place is still attached by its authority's rule, and of a zone's fields only `reachKm` (KACST, IGGÖ, Kosovo's and Montenegro's towns) bounds the distance at which the zone applies. So Egypt's 15 km decides whether the guard runs and, where areas differ, which city a place takes, never whether a place has a table.
- **Mapping**, per authority:

| Mapping | Rule | Authorities |
|---|---|---|
| `NEAREST` | The nearest zone of the authority within the same country (and admin1 where set; for `ma_habous`, also the Ceuta and Melilla localities from a place in Ceuta or Melilla, 6.8; for `jo_awqaf`, also its Jerusalem entry from a place in Jerusalem, 8.8), with no reach limit unless the authority's own rule sets one (Austria: IGGÖ's nine cities within 25 km, below), and with the validity guard below. Where the guard acts, or for a place outside the authority's country: exact coordinates with the method, the chip «فلكية» (6.11), and the zone line «حسب موقعك (لا يوجد جدول رسمي قريب)». | OM (the zone named after a town within 10 km, otherwise the nearest of the 86 zone points, with no reach limit; 8.8), AE (by emirate and the nearest area Awqaf lists, with no reach limit; 8.8), MA (no reach limit, with the validity guard: every place in Morocco, Western Sahara included, takes the nearest Moroccan Habous locality except where the guard acts, such as Tichla in Western Sahara; 8.8), EG (first the city the place is a named place of, by its catalogue row or within the city's own area, 15 km from its town centre to start; otherwise the city with the nearest town centre, with no reach limit; the validity guard checks every other place; 8.8), LY (no reach limit, with the validity guard; 8.8), YE (the nearest location of the place's own terrain class within 60 km, otherwise the nearest location, with no reach limit, with the validity guard; E10, 8.8), SY (the nearest of the ministry's 14 governorate tables, with no reach limit and no admin1 constraint; 8.8), MR (by wilaya: the capital's zone whose `admin1` lists the place's wilaya, whatever the distance; with no wilaya, the nearest capital; with the validity guard; 8.8), DZ (by wilaya, `place_admin1`, 6.8: the listed place of the place's wilaya, the nearest of them where a wilaya has several, otherwise the nearest of the 70, with no reach limit; each attached place takes its reference's times plus the monthly offset derived on the phone, `baseZoneId`; 8.8), IQ (outside the Kurdistan Region, with no reach limit; 8.8), JO (by governorate, the valley's elevation and the nearest town the ministry names, with no reach limit; its further places included; 8.8), BG (`bg_grand_mufti`'s 48 cities, with no reach limit; 8.8), AT (`diyanet_eu`: IGGÖ's nine city tables within 25 km, otherwise the nearest of Diyanet's Austrian entries, with no reach limit; below), IE (`ie_ifi`: the nearest of the IFI's 33 towns, each computed at its own point, with no reach limit and with the validity guard; 7.5, Q23) |
| `NEAREST_LOCAL` | The nearest listed place, only when the user is within its own area. The radius is set in AM2 from each entry's extent; it starts at 25 km for every KACST entry, whatever its type, and the nearest entry in reach decides where areas overlap (8.8). Anywhere else, the method at the user's coordinates. In Saudi Arabia that is KACST's official output for the point, which its prayer-times page and API compute for any coordinates (8.8), and under the ministry's rule each town's mosques use the town's own Umm al-Qura time, so these times are not marked unofficial; the zone line then reads «حسب موقعك، أقرب مدينة: …». | SA |
| `LISTED_PLACES` | The listed place nearest to the user, whatever the distance (no `reachKm`), except in Montenegro and in Kosovo and the Preševo Valley (below). Palestine: the nearest of the 16 places Dar al-Ifta lists in Palestine, whatever the distance (8.8), with its offset on all six times. Kuwait: the nearest of the 12 listed places (Kuwait City and the sheet's 11), whatever the distance, so `reachKm` does not apply there: Kuwait City's times plus that place's published offset on all six times (8.8, Q15). Bosnia and Herzegovina (`bosnia_iz`): Sarajevo's times plus the nearest Takvim place's monthly difference, derived on the phone from the Takvim's rule, whatever the distance; in Croatia and Slovenia, if `bosnia_iz` is chosen, the nearest of the Takvim's five towns of that country (8.8). Slovenia (`si_islamska_skupnost`): Ljubljana's times plus the nearest of its six places' published offsets on each time, whatever the distance (8.8). Qatar: Doha's table in Al Khor, Al Wakra and the coast between (QCH's rule), elsewhere the nearest of QCH's six Qatari places whatever the distance, so `reachKm` does not apply there (8.8). Of these, only Montenegro's five towns and Kosovo's seven set `reachKm`: beyond Kosovo's, the takvim's table unshifted (8.8); beyond Montenegro's, Sarajevo's times plus the whole minute nearest to 4 × (18.41 − the place's longitude) (8.8). | KW, PS, QA; BA and HR outside Zagreb's area (`bosnia_iz`, 8.8); SI (`si_islamska_skupnost`, 8.8); XK and RS in the Preševo Valley (`xk_bik`, 8.8); AL (`al_kmsh`: Tirana's times plus the nearest listed city's published shift on all six times, whatever the distance, since KMSH gives no rule for other places; 8.8); RS outside the Preševo Valley (`bosnia_iz`: Sarajevo's times plus the published difference of the nearest of its Serbian places, whatever the distance; 8.8); ME (`bosnia_iz`: Sarajevo's times plus the published difference of one of vaktija.ba's five Montenegrin towns inside that town's area; elsewhere no listed place applies and the place takes Sarajevo's times plus the whole minute nearest to 4 × (18.41 − its longitude), computed on the phone, so Podgorica's shift is − 3, not Gusinje's − 6; the validity guard below then gives every such place but those of the far north around Pljevlja, near its threshold, `bosnia_iz` at its own point, Podgorica included; 8.8) |
| `DISTRICT` | For a place in Turkey: Diyanet's table for the place's district, whatever the distance. Diyanet's list names every entry as a district (`IlceAdi`). Each Turkish entry's `admin2` lists the GeoNames districts it serves, matched against `place_admin2` (6.8): its own district and, for the province's central entry (the one named after the province: İSTANBUL, ANKARA, KOCAELİ), every district of the province that has no entry of its own (İstanbul's 19 entries serve its 39 districts, Ankara's 17 its 25, Kocaeli's 9 its 12). A Turkish place whose district no entry lists takes the nearest Turkish entry, with no reach limit. Each entry's times are `diyanet_tr` at the entry's own point, fitted from its table (7.5, M3), so the default reproduces Diyanet's table, not the user's point (owner rule, 2026-10-11). Diyanet publishes no rule for places it does not list; this one follows its list, and the owner's dataset supports it. Of 64 Istanbul-area mosques whose address names the district, with the table each follows read from its Dhuhr, the rule gives 59 their table and the nearest entry gives 42. Where the two differ (23 mosques), the rule is right for 20, one of them a tie, and the nearest entry for 3: 10 of the 12 Bahçeşehir mosques, in Başakşehir district, call Başakşehir's table though Esenyurt's point is nearer. Outside Turkey (GR, or `diyanet_tr` picked anywhere else) the method stays at the user's coordinates (`EXACT`). | TR (`diyanet_tr`) |
| `NATIONAL` | One table everywhere it applies | BH (the SCIA's one calendar for the whole Kingdom, the Hawar Islands included, whatever the distance; 8.8); the Gaza Strip (`ps_gaza_awqaf`: one zone «محافظات غزة» for all five governorates, as the ministry publishes, computed at Gaza City's point until the AM10 fit, 8.8); LB (`lb_dar_al_fatwa`: one zone «بيروت» at Beirut's point for every place in Lebanon, with no reach limit and with the validity guard below (the far south and the north-eastern border at their own point), since Dar al-Fatwa's imsakiya is for Beirut only and prints no offsets or rule for other places; the zone line reads «جدول: بيروت»; Q10); Rhône (`rhone_ctir` in département 69, read from `place_admin2`, 6.8: one zone «الرون» at Lyon's point, so the zone line reads «جدول: الرون»; elsewhere the method at the user's coordinates, `EXACT`, 6.4; Q33); Croatia's Zagreb area (`hr_miz_zagreb`: one zone «زغرب» at Zagreb's point, the default where Zagreb is the nearest of the Takvim's five Croatian towns; elsewhere, if chosen, the method at the user's coordinates; 8.8); KM (`km_fitted`: every time from Moroni's point, the sunrise row at the user's point, A3); YT (`yt_traditional`: its own single zone «مايوت» at Moroni's point, so the zone line never names Moroni; the method's − 3 min on Fajr, Dhuhr and Maghrib, while Asr and Isha count from that Maghrib (6.2 step 5), so all five prayers are the Moroni table − 3; the sunrise row at the user's point, unshifted; A1, 7.6); MK (`mk_bfi`: the BFI's Skopje times for the whole country, the only place it publishes, with no reach limit and with the validity guard below; 8.8); LU (`luxembourg_shoura`: one zone «لوكسمبورغ» for every place in Luxembourg, with no reach limit and with the validity guard below, at a point fitted in M3, Luxembourg City's centre until then; the Austria and Luxembourg bullet below, 7.5) |
| `NAMED_REGIONS` | The zone of the region whose named localities (`namedPlaces`) include the one nearest to the place, with no reach limit: the source lists its localities by region and gives no rule for other places. The place's own elevation shift is added (E16) | RE (`re_cir`: the CIR's Nord, Est, Sud and Ouest, 7.6) |
| `ADMIN_AREA` | The authority's own attachment by administrative area, with no `reachKm`; each entry is computed at its own published or fitted point. Norway: the IRN entry of the place's municipality (`place_admin2`, 6.8), else the nearest of IRN's 358 Norwegian entries (357 municipalities and Svalbard; its Strömstad entry, in Sweden, is not used), whatever the distance; an entry whose southern switch days the rule of 7.4 misses stores its own first and last day on the summer rule, for each event the rule misses, in its zone row (`switchDays`). Tatarstan (`place_admin1`): the nearest of DUM RT's 44 places, whatever the distance. DUM RT publishes one table per town or district («Шәһәрне, яки районны сайла»), but GeoNames gives 27 of Tatarstan's 46 `cities5000` rows no district, so the district centre nearest the place stands for its district. Moscow and Moscow oblast (`place_admin1`): DUM RF's one table, a single zone «موسكو» at Moscow's point (fitted in M3), as its sheet («по Москве и Московской области») says, with the validity guard (6.5) like any bulk attachment. Elsewhere the default of 6.4 applies (owner rule, 2026-10-11). | NO (`irn`); RU: Moscow and its oblast (`dum_rf`), Tatarstan (`ru_dum_rt`) |
| `EXACT` | The method at the user's coordinates | IQ in the Kurdistan Region (6.11, Q8), `rhone_ctir` outside Rhône, `hr_miz_zagreb` outside Croatia's Zagreb area, `si_islamska_skupnost` outside Slovenia, `bosnia_iz` outside the countries its Takvim covers (8.8), `diyanet_eu` outside Austria, `luxembourg_shoura` outside Luxembourg, `diyanet_tr` outside Turkey (GR's inferred default included, 6.4), and every preset without zones |

**Validity guard (owner rule, 2026-10-11).** Attaching a place to the nearest official place with no reach limit can put a prayer before it begins there: Ghat would get Sabha's Maghrib, 487 km away, 9–19 min before its own sunset. So a place that the authority does not name itself (a listed place or a named town, with or without a published offset) takes the attached table only where it is valid there. Over the year, the table's Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha must not come before the place's own times under the profile's own rule (its dawn angle or interval, solar noon, Asr rule, sunset, and dusk angle or interval, each with the profile's own minutes), and its sunrise must not come after the place's own sunrise, where Fajr ends, by more than the table itself does at its own place on its worst day plus 4 minutes; the comparison below defines each of these terms. The table's own margin covers the authority's rounding (a Dhuhr rounded down, a sunrise rounded down) and whatever its zone adds to the profile there; a minute the profile adds everywhere is on both sides and cancels. The 4 minutes are what the former 60 km reach accepted between a place and its table (at most 3.6 min in Syria, about 3 in Egypt and Morocco), rounded up. With no allowance the check would act at almost every place away from its table's point, since most tables add nothing to Fajr, Asr and Isha, and the nearest table of principle 5 would hardly ever apply: Sabratha, which A2 keeps on Zawiya's table, gets Zawiya's Fajr, Asr and Isha about 1 min earlier than Zawiya does. Where a time fails on some compared day, the place takes the authority's method at its own point all year, as the authority computes each of its own places, with the zone line «حسب موقعك (لا يوجد جدول رسمي قريب)» and the nearest official places to pick from, among those the guard accepts there (a zone picked by hand is checked like a mapped one, below). The check is a 365-day scan, cached per place, zone, method and year, like D6's polar gate; before release it runs over every attached area of every profile (9.6). It never acts on a place the authority names itself (such as Kuwait's 12 places, Jordan's named towns or Algeria's 70 places), and never in Tunisia. A place the authority names itself is one the mapped zone names, `OfficialZone.names(place)` (Named places above): its catalogue row is listed in one of the zone's `namedPlaces`, or its point lies within that named place's `areaKm` (only Egypt's cities, Oman's named towns and Qatar's Al Khor and Al Wakra have an area); an area never limits the mapping, so a place beyond it is still attached, and checked. A place attached in bulk (by wilaya, governorate, oblast or one national table, the authority's own attachment rule included, such as Algeria's «… والولايات المرتبطة بها» or DUM RF's «по Москве и Московской области») is checked like any other, since a bulk rule cannot mean a prayer before its time. Clear cases, at approximate points over 2026: it acts at Nalut, Wazin, Tazirbu and Ghat (Libya: Maghrib 6–19 min before their sunset), at Aïn Ben Tili (Mauritania: Zouerat's sunrise about 16 min after its own), at Tichla (Western Sahara: Aousserd's Fajr up to about 5.5 min early), at Al-Tanf (Syria: Eastern Ghouta's sunrise about 9 min after its own), on Lebanon's north-eastern border and in its far south (Beirut's sunrise up to about 6 min late; its Fajr up to about 6 min early), at Lotoshino (Moscow oblast: DUM RF's Maghrib about 6.6 min before its sunset) and in Algeria's far Sahara (beyond about 100 km from the listed town, its Maghrib comes before the place's sunset on about 38 % of days). It does not act at Sabratha, Zuwara (about 3.5 min), Tabqa (about 2.5), Tetovo (about 2) or a Kuwaiti desert point on Jahra's offset (about 3). Places near the threshold (Ras al-Ayn, Sukhnah, Tell Abyad, Palmyra and Malikiyah in Syria; Dubna, Kolomna and Shatura in Moscow oblast; Bir Moghrein, Tichitt and the places west of their capital in Mauritania; Struga, Ohrid and Debar in North Macedonia; Troisvierges in Luxembourg; Mocha in Yemen) are settled by the scan with the fitted points (AM2, M3).

**The guard's comparison** (`ValidityGuard`, a pure function of the place, the zone with its base zone, the method and the year, which runs the engine as the repository does, Algeria's offset windows included (6.9); `ZoneMapping.resolve` calls it once it has picked the zone, a zone picked by hand (`zonePick`, 6.3) included, for a place the authority does not name itself, outside Tunisia, and returns null where it acts):

1. **The table (T).** The zone's six times for the place, each with its status, as the engine gives them with the zone and its base zone (6.2, steps 0–8 and the rounding of step 10), with the profile's own Asr factor and without the user's adjustments (step 9).
2. **The place's reference (R).** The same method at the place's own point, unrounded and with its status: the engine's times after step 8 with `zone = null` and `baseZone = null`, before steps 9 and 10, never through `ZoneMapping.resolve`, so the attached table is never compared with itself. These are the times the place would get where the guard acts, before the rounding and the user's own settings: the horizons E2 gives at exact coordinates (its fallbacks), its own E10 class, E15's signed shadow inside Algeria, its own E16 shift and the profile's own minutes (method offsets, `dhuhrAfterTransitMin`, E8, E9, E18), with no E6 point, zone offset or `switchDays`, and with the profile's own Asr factor, not the user's. For each time it is the profile's own rule at the place: an angle time at the instant the sun reaches the angle there (at E17's twilight latitude where set), Dhuhr at its transit, Asr by the profile's shadow rule (its factor, E4 moment, E13, E15), sunrise and Maghrib on its horizon, and an interval time by the same interval from the place's own sunrise or Maghrib (`km_fitted`'s and `yt_traditional`'s Fajr from its sunrise, their Asr and Isha from its Maghrib; an Isha at Maghrib + 90 for `sa_umm_al_qura` and `qa_awqaf`, at Maghrib + 80 for `hr_miz_zagreb`; `ie_ifi`'s seasonal intervals). An interval time is never measured against a dawn angle, dusk angle or shadow its profile does not use: against the factor-1 shadow, Moroni's own Asr (Maghrib − 168) comes up to about 19 min before it and up to about 24 after, so the comparison would measure the gap between two rules, not the distance.
3. **The reference at the table's own place.** The same calculation at the zone's `point`, with its `elevationM` (h = 0 when null) and, in Yemen, the zone's own class (E10), compared with the zone's times there. A zone built at the user's own point (Montenegro outside its five towns' areas, 6.2) has no place of its own: its base zone's point and unshifted table (Sarajevo's) stand in.
4. **Leads and margin.** Each day, a time's lead is R − T in minutes, and the sunrise's T − R. Each time's margin is the table's largest lead for that time at its own place over the year, with its sign: it holds the authority's rounding and whatever the zone adds to the profile there (published, fitted or derived offsets, E6 points, fitted horizons; never a zone's `switchDays`, since every day they change is estimated on one side and not compared, item 5). A minute the profile adds everywhere is in R and T alike and cancels: with `ly_awqaf`'s Maghrib at sunset + 4, truncated, Sabha's own margin is about 1 min and Ghat's lead about 22.5 min (against the bare sunset, − 3 and 18.5: the same verdict). A minute that depends on the place's terrain (E10's class) is the place's own in R and the zone's in T, so a difference between them counts in the lead: a Yemeni place at or above 1,100 m on a lowland location's table (Maghrib + 6) is compared with its own Maghrib + 10, and Mocha on Taiz's table (+ 10) with its own + 6. A margin below 0 is kept as it is, never raised to 0, so a table that always comes after its own place's times leaves its places less than 4 minutes.
5. **Estimated and absent times.** A time is compared on a day only where T and R at the place both give it as `COMPUTED` (6.2), and the margin is taken over the days where T and R at the table's own place both do. Where a high-latitude or polar rule, a cap or the envelope makes it `ESTIMATED` (Fajr and Isha on nights without the angle or where an every-night rule binds, sunrise and Maghrib where D6's 5-hour minimum or the polar substitution sets them, the polar Asr, `ishaLatest`, E19, `irn`'s summer and Mecca seasons, whether on a zone's `switchDays` or on the fitted rule's days, `ru_dum_rt`'s intervals), it is not compared that day. An estimate is the profile's convention for a night without that twilight, not the start the guard protects, and across a rule's switch the comparison would measure the switch date rather than the distance: with `ru_dum_rt`, Kazan's Isha falls to sunset + 1 h 30 on the first night the sun stays above −18°, which a place 30 km south of Kazan's point reaches a night later in May and leaves a night earlier in August (2026), and on those nights Kazan's table puts Isha about 1 h before that place's own 15° Isha; a place 30 km north already uses sunrise − 2 h on a night when Kazan's real 18° Fajr comes 1.7 to 2 h earlier. `ABSENT` does not occur, since every profile carries a rule and a polar fallback (6.2); it would count as estimated. In the Arab places nothing is estimated (6.2), so every time is compared on every day.
6. **Not compared.** A time the zone already gives at the user's own point: `km_fitted`'s and `yt_traditional`'s sunrise row.
7. **Verdict.** The place keeps the table unless, on some compared day, a time leads by more than its margin plus 4 minutes. The margin is cached per zone, method and year, and the verdict per place, zone, method and year, the method being the choice's method with its high-latitude and polar rules; the user's Asr factor and minute steppers change neither.

Real times are compared right up to the night an angle stops being reached, where the angle time moves fast with latitude: with `ru_dum_rt`, which keeps the real 18° Fajr until that night, Kazan's table puts Fajr before the 18° dawn of a place 10 km south of its point by more than the tolerance on about 2 nights a year (by up to about 6 to 9 min), and of a place 30 km south on about 15 to 17 nights (by up to about 15 to 20 min; 2026–2028), all next to its switch nights, so the guard gives such places `ru_dum_rt` at their own point (12).

- **Austria and Luxembourg (2026-10-11).** In Austria, `diyanet_eu` maps `NEAREST` in two tiers, IGGÖ's own rule as its site («Nächstgelegenen Ort verwenden», which asks `/api/prayer-times/at/`) applies it. First, IGGÖ's nine city tables (the state capitals: Bregenz, Eisenstadt, Graz, Innsbruck, Klagenfurt, Linz, Salzburg, St. Pölten and Vienna) with `reachKm` 25: a point within 25 km of a city's centre gets that city's table (Dornbirn → Bregenz and Wiener Neustadt → Eisenstadt, though Diyanet lists both towns; 24.5 km south of Graz → Graz, 25.6 km → Leibnitz). Beyond, the site gives Diyanet's own table for the nearest entry of Diyanet's Austrian list (namazvakitleri.diyanet.gov.tr, 190 entries on 2026-10-11): Wels, Eferding, Pöchlarn, Tulln, Zell am See, Leibnitz, and Heiligenblut for a point in Lienz, which Diyanet does not list. So the second tier is those entries, with no reach limit, each named as the place catalogue names its town (D3) and computed at the town's point; an Austrian place always has an official table, and «حسب موقعي بالضبط» is the option. Each IGGÖ zone keeps its city centre as `point`, from which the 25 km are measured, and its table's fitted point as its E6 morning and evening points, as Qatar's towns do (7.5, 8.8). In Luxembourg, `luxembourg_shoura` maps `NATIONAL`: the Shoura publishes one table for the Grand Duchy and no other place or offset, so every Luxembourg place gets it where the validity guard above allows (Troisvierges, in the north, is settled by the scan), under «جدول: لوكسمبورغ», computed at a point fitted in M3 (Luxembourg City's centre until then).
- **Zone line.** «جدول: مسقط» in the list of times; «الجدول الرسمي المعتمد: مسقط» in the settings, or «الجدول المعتمد: الرون» where the zone is not an authority's table, or not the place's own authority's (`rhone_ctir`, `re_cir`, `km_fitted`, `yt_traditional`, and `bosnia_iz` in Montenegro; 6.11). Tapping either lists, of the five nearest zones of the authority, those the validity guard (6.5) keeps at the place, each with today's Fajr and Maghrib, for a user whose mosque follows a neighbouring zone.
- **A zone picked by hand** is stored in `PrayerMethodChoice.zonePick`, which is cleared when the method's authority changes. When a travel update moves the place (6.8), a picked zone is kept if it is still among the five nearest zones of the new point, and cleared otherwise. When the mapped zone changes, the «تم تغيير الموقع إلى …» notification also fires, naming the new zone.

### 6.6 The time model in the app (D9)

Three designs were compared:

- an adapter that hands the engine's output to existing code as `DayPrayerTimes`;
- moving every consumer to instants first;
- widening `DayPrayerTimes` in place.

Both judges rejected widening in place. An unmigrated reader, one of 17 TV files among others, would place an after-midnight Isha at 01:07 on the wrong day, with no compile error. The former plan staged the other two: the adapter first, instants once a region was validated. D9 (decided 2026-10-11) moves the phone to instants now, so there is no adapter.

- **One prayer type on the phone.** `PrayerTimesRepository.prayerDay(date)` returns a `PrayerDay` (6.2): six instants with their statuses, for the prayer day of that date. android-app no longer reads `DayPrayerTimes`; it stays the TV's and the desktop's type, and `InmPrayerTimes` keeps returning it to them.
- **`PrayerClock`** (shared) answers next, current and "which prayer day is it" over D−1..D+1 by instant, with ties ordered by day. D is today in the place's zone, never the phone's date: the two zones can be 25 h apart (a Pacific/Pago_Pago phone and a Kiritimati place). So at 00:30, yesterday's 01:07 Isha is still next.
- **Display (D8).** `formatClockTime(instant)` writes the instant in the phone's zone. A time whose civil date there differs from the prayer day's date carries «بعد منتصف الليل» or «قبل منتصف الليل». A Casablanca place on a Muscat phone shows its Isha after midnight that way.
- **Clock settings.** A clock time tied to a prayer (a fixed silence start or end, an edited Jumu'a time, Tunisia's 13:15 of D12) is read with `ZonedDateTime.of` in the place's zone. When the place's zone differs from the phone's, the silence and Jumu'a editors show and take it in the phone's clock, like the list of times (D8), converted with the place's zone on the date shown; what is stored stays a clock time in the place's zone. Standalone clock alarms (wake at 06:00, adhkar at a fixed time) stay in the phone's zone. In an autumn overlap `ZonedDateTime.of` takes the earlier instant, and in a spring gap it moves the time later by the gap.
- **No wall time turned back into an instant.** Prayer times stay instants from the engine to the alarm, so the repeated autumn hour cannot misplace one. The case found during the merge becomes a test (9.6): on 2026-10-25 a Muscat place on a Paris phone has Fajr at about 00:53 UTC, shown as 02:53 CEST in the first pass of the repeated hour, and a Riyadh place has Fajr at about 01:38 UTC, 02:38 CET, in the second pass. Both are silenced at their instants.
- **INM places.** `InmPrayerDays` relabels INM's times as instants at +01:00 (6.2), and the 258 × 365 identity test checks every delegation and day. With the phone elsewhere this fixes today's bug: Tunisian times shown and silenced at Tunis wall-clock numbers in the phone's zone, which on a Paris phone in summer is 1 h early.
- **Unset Eid and Jumu'a times**, today an hour of −1, become nullable values, and the 0–23 filters go.
- **Six times every day.** Every preset and profile carries a rule and a polar fallback, Asr included, so a `PrayerDay` has six times every day. Consumers still handle a null day, for safety.
- **Caches and keys.** A `PrayerDay` depends on the place, the method and, for methods with Ramadan rules, the Ramadan days. Caches and Compose `remember` keys include the place key and the method key; the Ramadan days of E8 are computed, so no calendar version is needed abroad. What is shown also depends on the phone's zone, a state updated on `ACTION_TIMEZONE_CHANGED`, like `rememberCalendarToday`.
- **Source-scan test.** A unit test forbids `DayPrayerTimes`, `InmPrayerTimes`, `PrayerTime(…)`, `prayerMinutesOfDay` and `Calendar.set` on prayer times in `android-app/app/src/main`, outside the repository and an allow-list.
- **Equivalence oracle.** The M0 snapshots of today's `SilenceScheduler` arming and `WakeAlarmComputer` triggers at `Africa/Tunis` (11): the new code gives the same trigger instants, except the expected differences of 9.1.
- **The TV and the desktop keep `DayPrayerTimes`.** The TV calls `InmPrayerTimes` directly, and the desktop reads its CSV tables through `PrayerDataLoader`. The shared `SilenceAlarmComputer`, which the desktop uses, stays as it is; the phone's windows come from a new shared `SilenceWindowPlanner` (6.9).

### 6.7 Clocks and time zones

- **Instants first.** The engine computes UTC instants (6.2), and the phone shows them in its own clock (D8). On a phone with stale tzdata and automatic time, alarms at computed times (prayers, and offsets from them) still ring at the right instant. Only the displayed clock is wrong, by the same amount as the phone's status bar.
  - A clock time the user types is different: a fixed silence start or end, an edited Eid prayer time, a fixed-time wake or adhkar alarm. The app reads it with the phone's stale rules, so it rings an hour off the official time.
  - "Today" in the place's zone also turns an hour early or late.
  - If the user turns off automatic time and sets the clock by hand to show the official time, the phone's UTC is an hour off and every alarm rings an hour off. No app can detect this from the clock alone.
- **No stale-data notice (A9).** The phone's clock is the user's responsibility (D8), so the app neither detects old time-zone data nor explains it, and never changes any system setting. For reference, the tzdata releases that introduced the current rules of the zones concerned: Africa/Casablanca and Africa/El_Aaiun 2026c, Africa/Cairo 2023a, Asia/Amman and Asia/Damascus 2022e, Africa/Khartoum 2017c, Asia/Gaza and Asia/Hebron 2024a.
- **Tables in a fixed offset.** A perpetual Palestinian table is a wall clock at +02 ("winter time"). The fitting harness reads it that way (E5), which gives the official "+ 60 in summer" (confirmed by Dar al-Ifta's 2022 summer imsakiya). The app computes Palestinian times as instants for the actual date (A8) and shows them in the phone's clock.
- **Fitting** converts official local times to UTC in the authority's zone, with the rules in force on each date.
  - This covers Morocco's 2018–2026 history and the Habous rows already in GMT before 2026-09-20.
  - Habous gives Ceuta (id 24) and Melilla (id 40) in Moroccan time, so their rows are converted from Africa/Casablanca, not Africa/Ceuta.

### 6.8 Places

**Type.** A sealed `PrayerPlace` in `shared/place/`:

- `Inm(delegationId)`: zone `Africa/Tunis`, coordinates from INM's parameters. Today's locality label keys stay with it.
- `Coordinates(latE5, lngE5, elevationM?, zoneId, zoneSource, countryCode?, catalogueId?, admin1?, admin2?)`, where zoneSource is CATALOGUE, NEAREST_CITY, DEVICE or USER. `admin1` and `admin2` are the values `CountryResolver` returns with the country and `setPlace` stores in `place_admin1` and `place_admin2` (Département below), so the shared `ZoneMapping` reads them from the place and never needs the app's `PlaceCatalogue`. `elevationM` is the `dem` of the nearest Arab catalogue place within 40 km when the resolved country is an Arab League member (or the place is in Ceuta or Melilla), the `dem` of the nearest Réunion row within 40 km for a place in Réunion (E16), and null otherwise.

Both have a `cacheKey`, for example "inm:615" or "geo:4886000,235000,Europe/Paris" (e5 values verbatim). The `PrayerMethodChoice.key` is appended to it. That key replaces `delegation|year|month` in the adhkar months cache and the Compose keys, and replaces every `getDelegationId` caller. Labels live beside it in `SavedPlace(place, label, fromGps, provisional)`.

**Prefs.** One `edit()` in `setPlace`; no migration.

- New keys: `place_kind`, `place_lat_e5`, `place_lng_e5`, `place_elevation_m`, `place_zone_id`, `place_zone_source`, `place_country`, `place_admin2` (the département of FR places, the governorate of PS places, the district (ilçe) of TR places and the municipality of NO places, else absent), `place_admin1` (the federal subject of RU places, the wilaya of DZ and MR places, the emirate of AE places and the governorate of JO and IQ places, else absent), `place_catalogue_id`, `place_name_snapshot`, `place_provisional`, `method_choice_<CC>` (JSON, 6.4, with the `ES-CE`, `ES-ML` and `ZZ` keys), `jomoaa_time_<CC>` (6.9) and the two region switches.
- `delegation_id` and the locality keys stay for INM places. `setPlace(Coordinates)` clears `locality_id`, `locality_name`, `locality_kind` and `manual_reference_id` in the same edit, so no stale Tunisian label or source repair can act on a place abroad. No home delegation is kept (D20).
- `disabled_outside_tunisia` is deleted.

**Catalogue.** `scripts/places/build_catalogue.py` joins, at build time:

- for Europe (D1–D3): GeoNames `cities5000`, its `alternateNames` (Arabic, plus English, French, German, Italian and Spanish exonyms) and Wikidata's Arabic labels (CC0), joined by GeoNames id. It writes `android-app/app/src/main/assets/places/europe-cities.tsv`: id, country, admin1, admin2 (French, Turkish and Norwegian rows only, for 6.4 and 6.5 `DISTRICT` and `ADMIN_AREA`; Turkey's rows add every GeoNames district seat (PPLA2) missing from `cities5000`, so that each district has a row), dem (Réunion rows only, for E16), native name, ASCII name, Arabic name, aliases, coordinates ×1e5, zone index and population.
- for the Arab countries (A1): GeoNames `cities1000` plus every administrative seat, one Bahraini row for the Hawar Islands (GeoNames' Jazīrat Ḩawār, 290243, labelled «جزر حوار», 8.8), Arabic names by the chain of 5.5, `dem` elevations, and duplicates within 2 km removed (GeoNames carries tens of thousands of imported duplicates in Yemen and Morocco). The output is the binary `assets/places/arab-cities.bin`, with admin1 and `dem` per place, and admin2 for Palestinian rows: GeoNames' governorate (its 16 second-order divisions), for the Jerusalem governorate's clock (6.8). A row without one takes the nearest tagged row's, and the build reports how many.
- for overseas France (D2, 7.6): GeoNames `cities1000` rows of RE, YT, GP, MQ, GF, NC, PF, PM, BL, MF and WF, each with its own country code and IANA zone, never folded into FR, and `dem` for Réunion (E16), in `europe-cities.tsv`;
- for the land neighbours of the Arab countries, Israel excepted (14): GeoNames `cities1000` rows within 60 km of an Arab border, with country and zone only (A12, Country below).

It also reports Arabic coverage weighted by population, checks every zone with `ZoneId.of`, and enforces the 2 MB budget. `PlaceCatalogue` loads all of them into one 1° grid. The land-neighbour rows go into `arab-cities.bin`, flagged, and serve country and zone detection only: the picker and the «قرب X» label never use them (D2). The assets stay out of `data/prayer-formula`, which is bundled into the TV APK and iterated by `InmPrayerFormulaTest`. Attribution ships as `PLACES-LICENSE.txt` (GeoNames and Wikidata) and as the «أسماء الأماكن» line at the foot of «طريقتك» (the «مصادر البيانات» row was removed on 2026-10-19); the phone app has no about screen.

**Country (`CountryResolver`, A12).** For every fix outside the Tunisia outline, the country comes from the catalogue cities within 40 km. No country outlines are bundled.

- The catalogue also carries the GeoNames `cities1000` rows of the land neighbours within 60 km of an Arab border (IR, TD, NE, ML, SN, CF, SS, ET, ER, KE), with country and zone only, so their points get their own country (2). Israel is not in the first release (owner, 2026-10-11; 14): it has no rows, so a point there follows the general rules below (12). For this step only, Tunisia's delegation seats count as TN cities, since the catalogue has no Tunisian rows.
- If the network country (`TelephonyManager.networkCountryIso`) is among those cities' countries, it wins. That settles border towns such as Edirne, which has Greek and Bulgarian towns within 30 km, and points near Ras Jedir, Rafah or Oujda. Palestinian operators use Israel's mobile country code 425, so the network reads `il` in the West Bank and Gaza; with no Israeli rows, `il` is never among the cities' countries there, and the nearest city decides.
- Otherwise the nearest city's country.
- With no city within 40 km: the network country, then the region of the phone's zone (ICU `TimeZone.getRegion`).
- Otherwise the country is unknown, and the place gets `mwl` with «تأكيد الطريقة».
- The catalogue build gives GeoNames' Western Sahara rows (EH) the country MA (A1). It gives GeoNames' Svalbard and Jan Mayen rows (SJ) the country NO, the Svalbard rows taking IRN's Svalbard entry as their municipality (`place_admin2`), and keeps Longyearbyen (GeoNames 2729907, SJ's only town) even if `cities5000` drops it. `CountryResolver` reads a zone region SJ (Arctic/Longyearbyen) as NO. A Svalbard place therefore uses `method_choice_NO` and IRN's Svalbard entry (D2, 6.4). Ceuta and Melilla are ES by their rows; there `ma_habous`, mapped to their Habous localities (ids 24 and 40), is also offered under «طرق أخرى», and their choices keep their own keys (6.4).

**Département (6.4).** For a place whose country is FR, `admin2` is the département of the picked row when `catalogueId` names a French row. Otherwise it is the département of the nearest FR catalogue row to the stored point. Only FR rows count, so Kehl never decides for a Strasbourg fix. No département outlines are bundled, so a fix near a border can take the neighbouring département. `CountryResolver` returns it with the country, and `setPlace` stores it in `place_admin2`. Background moves, the fallback, a chooser opened later and `rhone_ctir`'s zone (6.5) read the stored value. GeoNames gives Lyon and Villeurbanne 69, so no code mapping is needed. A NO place's municipality is found the same way, but from the NO rows and IRN's entry points together, each point standing for its own municipality: `cities5000` has rows in only about 95 of Norway's 357 municipalities, so a fix in Hitra would otherwise take Orkland's southern table through Orkanger, 55 km away. The asset build (M5) matches each IRN entry to its GeoNames municipality code by name and point (`ADMIN_AREA`, 6.5). An RU place's federal subject (admin1) is found the same way from the RU rows and stored in `place_admin1`, and so is the admin1 of a DZ, MR, AE, JO or IQ place (its wilaya, emirate or governorate) from that country's rows of `arab-cities.bin`; Tatarstan's DUM RT places need no district code (6.5). A PS place's governorate (8.8) is found the same way from the PS rows and stored in `place_admin2`, and so is a TR place's district (GeoNames' second-order division) from the TR rows (6.5 `DISTRICT`).

Tests pin Edirne with a Turkish network, a point on the Rhine between Kehl and Strasbourg with each network (with an `fr` network: FR and département 67, even where Kehl is the nearest row), and a fix 20 km off Sfax outside the outline with a Tunisian network (→ TN, its nearest delegation with INM). Also Ramallah and Gaza City with an `il` network (→ PS), a fix in Bidya (→ PS, governorate Salfit), one in East Jerusalem with the phone at Asia/Jerusalem (→ zone Asia/Jerusalem) and at Europe/Paris (→ Asia/Hebron), a fix off Tabarka on an Algerian network (→ TN), and a fix in Longyearbyen with no network and the phone at Arctic/Longyearbyen (→ NO, IRN's Svalbard entry). Overseas: Fort-de-France with a `gp` network (→ MQ), Mamoudzou with an `re` network (→ YT), and skipped first runs in Saint-Denis de La Réunion on an `re` network (→ RE), Fort-de-France on a `gp` network with America/Martinique (→ MQ), Cayenne on a `gp` network with America/Cayenne (→ GF) and Mamoudzou on an `re` network with Indian/Mayotte (→ YT), none of them Tunis; each test pins the phone's zone.

**Zone (`ZoneResolver`, shared, pure).**

- In the Arab region, as elsewhere, a catalogue city carries its own zone: Gaza's cities Asia/Gaza, the West Bank's Asia/Hebron, Western Sahara's Africa/El_Aaiun, Ceuta and Melilla Africa/Ceuta. A place in the Jerusalem governorate (8.8) takes the phone's zone when it is Asia/Jerusalem or Asia/Hebron, as twin towns do below, and otherwise Asia/Hebron, the clock Dar al-Ifta prints in (Q1). East Jerusalem's phones are on Israeli networks, and 17 of the dataset's 22 Jerusalem mosques change clocks on Israel's dates; the two clocks differ only on a few days (2026-03-27 and 2026-10-24), and then only clock settings tied to a prayer and the place's date can differ (6.6). Asia/Jerusalem counts as the West Bank for the default (6.4). Catalogue cities of another country never lend a place their zone.
- Elsewhere a catalogue city carries its own zone. For a GPS fix, the resolver collects the zones of catalogue cities within 40 km:
  - if there is one, it uses it;
  - if there are several and the phone's zone is among them, it uses the phone's (twin towns such as Tornio/Haparanda and Valença/Tui);
  - otherwise it uses the nearest city's zone;
  - with no city within 40 km (at sea, outside both catalogues), it uses the phone's zone.
- Island zones (Canary, Azores, Madeira, Faroe, Ceuta, Kaliningrad, the Russian zones) come with their rows.
- Every stored id is parsed safely: first directly, then through a small alias table for renamed zones on old devices (Europe/Kyiv → Europe/Kiev), then the phone's zone as a last resort.

**Elevation.** `elevationM` as defined above, stored in `place_elevation_m`. It is used in four ways: by a method whose horizon needs it at exact coordinates (Yemen with its terrain class, `ma_habous` and `iq_sunni_endowment`, 6.2); by Yemen's mapping, which reads a place's terrain class from it and maps the place to the nearest location of that class within 60 km, otherwise to the nearest location (E10); by Jordan's mapping, which sends a place below sea level in Irbid, Balqa or Karak to its Ghor table (8.8); and by `re_cir`'s elevation-band shifts for a place in Réunion (E16, 7.6). A place mapped to an official zone computes its horizon with the zone's elevations (6.5).

**Routing.** `DelegationLocator.resolveFix` replaces `resolveGpsLocation` and returns `PlaceLocationResult`:

- Inside the Tunisia outline (`NeighborhoodIndex.isInsideCountry`): today's path, the nearest delegation with its sector label → `Inm`.
- Anywhere else → `Coordinates`. `OutsideTunisia` is deleted, so nothing is rejected.
- `TUNISIA_BORDER` is used only if `neighborhoods.bin` fails to load.
- A fix outside the outline that the country step puts in Tunisia (offshore near Djerba or Kerkennah, say) also takes its nearest delegation → `Inm`, since Tunisia has one method (D7). So no `Coordinates` place lies in Tunisia, and whether the outline includes the territorial waters no longer matters (Q24, closed).
- Lampedusa and Pantelleria become Europe/Rome.

**Routing by place and method.**

| Place and method | Path |
|---|---|
| `Inm` | `InmPrayerDays` with `PrayerFormulaSettings.OFFICIAL`: today's times, with no Asr factor or adjustment (D7) |
| `Coordinates` | the engine, with the zone the method maps the place to (6.5) |

A test pins that delegation 615 is byte-identical to today.

**GPS precision and privacy.**

- A GPS place is stored rounded to 0.01°: about 1 km, about 2 s of Dhuhr.
- It is labelled «قرب X» from the nearest catalogue city within 30 km, or «موقعي الحالي».
- The raw fix, its accuracy and its altitude are never stored.
- Analytics get the place kind and source only. Never the coordinates, city, zone, country, method id or official zone, since each reveals the country. `AnalyticsTracker`'s `delegation_source` labels follow.

**First run.** OnboardingScreen gets a location step: GPS, pick a city, or skip. On skip, the provisional place is the first that applies:

1. Tunis (615), if the phone looks Tunisian.
2. The largest catalogue city of the network country, in the phone's zone.
3. Otherwise (no network country, or none of its cities in the phone's zone, as in overseas France where Martinique, Guiana, Saint-Martin and Saint-Barthélemy read `gp` and Mayotte reads `re`), the largest catalogue city in the phone's zone, if that zone is not Tunisian.
4. Tunis.

A «تأكيد الموقع» banner stays until the user confirms or a fix arrives.

**Travel.**

- `DelegationLocator.updatePlaceFrom*` resolves each usable fix with `resolveFix` and compares the result with the saved place, in this order. Today's `updateDelegationFromLocation` already stores a changed delegation at any distance and refreshes the neighbourhood on every fix (`DelegationLocator.kt:198–201`); the rule keeps both.
  1. **A semantic change is stored at any distance.** The saved place is provisional (`place_provisional`, First run above); the kind changes (Tunis → Paris); an `Inm` place's delegation changes (60 pairs of delegations lie less than 3 km apart by the points `findNearestDelegation` measures from (`gouvernorats.json`; 57 by INM's own points), such as La Medina and Bab Bhar, 0.46 km, whose INM times differ by a minute on 450 of their 2,190 times in 2026); or a `Coordinates` place's choice key (6.4: its country, `ES-CE`, `ES-ML` or `ZZ`), zone id, `admin1`, `admin2`, method, official zone, or `elevationM` where its times read it, differs from the saved place's. The method is the one the new key's choice gives, with the default resolved again from the new point while `methodPicked` is false (6.4). The official zone is the one `ZoneMapping` gives under that choice, with a picked zone kept or cleared as 6.5 says, before the validity guard (6.5), for the saved place as for the fix, never the null that `ZoneMapping.resolve` returns where the guard acts: the guard's 365-day scan runs only for a stored place, and its verdict, like every time of the place, holds for the stored point, which a move under 3 km keeps. `elevationM` counts for a horizon at the place's own point and for `re_cir`'s elevation bands (Yemen's and Jordan's elevation rules show as a change of official zone). These values come from the same pure step that `onPlaceChanged` uses to load the choice and map the zone, run without storing anything on the place the fix would store (its point rounded to 0.01°, below), with the current network country and phone zone. So a fix across a border less than 3 km away (the Rhine between Strasbourg and Kehl, or Haparanda and Tornio: fixes 1.2–1.5 km apart, 9.1), across the limit of a département, a governorate or an official zone, or at the same point after the network or the phone's zone changed (twin towns, the Jerusalem governorate) is stored.
  2. **Otherwise only the distance counts.** A `Coordinates` place is stored when its rounded point moves by more than 3 km. A shorter move keeps the saved place, its point, its label and its schedules, so GPS noise under 3 km reschedules nothing.
  3. **Labels.** For an `Inm` place, a fix in the saved delegation writes only the new locality keys, in one edit, as `setGpsLocation` does today, with no `onPlaceChanged` and no notification. A `Coordinates` place's «قرب X» label is computed from the stored point whenever a place is stored, and only then.
- A stored place is the reference for the next 3 km. No hysteresis is kept at a limit, as for delegations today (12). After storing, `updatePlaceFrom*` calls `onPlaceChanged(old, new)` itself. That function:
  - invalidates the caches;
  - loads the method choice of the new place's key (6.4); in France, Croatia, Serbia, Russia and Palestine, until the user picks a method there (`methodPicked` false), it resolves the default again from the new place (in France the new département's fallback; 6.4); it keeps or clears a picked zone (6.5);
  - runs `ScheduleRefreshCoordinator.syncAll`: dhikr, silence, wake and hifz, with a full cancel and reschedule;
  - clears dismissals whenever a time moved by more than 45 min;
  - re-checks every saved adhkar reminder over 365 days at the new place (6.9);
  - when the kind, the choice key, the method, the mapped zone or a `Coordinates` place's label changed, posts one notification «تم تغيير الموقع إلى …» on a new `location` channel at default importance, naming the new zone where there is one. A change of the delegation alone (silent today), or of the zone id, an administrative value or the elevation alone, reschedules without one. (Today's only silence channel, `silence_guard`, is IMPORTANCE_MIN, for the guard's ongoing notification.)
- So `SilenceReceiver` and `SilenceVerifyWorker` no longer reschedule only silence: a background move also reschedules wake alarms.
- On `TIMEZONE_CHANGED`, `BootReceiver` runs `syncAll`. With auto-location on, it first tries a last-location fix with a 3 s timeout inside its `goAsync`. A slower fix is handed to a one-off WorkManager job that passes it to `updatePlaceFrom*`. Either fix goes through the comparison above with the new phone zone and the current network country, so a fix at the same point is stored when its resolved country or zone changed (twin towns, the Jerusalem governorate). With auto-location on and no fix from either, the job, not the receiver, posts a notification that opens the picker if the place's offset now differs from the phone's. It never guesses a city from the zone alone. Without auto-location it only reschedules (D20).
- The manifest has no background-location permission, so these background fixes may come back empty on Android 10+. This is checked on the emulator (9.1).

**Trips (D20).** No home delegation is kept, and without auto-location the app does nothing when the phone travels: the place stays until the user changes it, shown and silenced at its real instants (6.6), and auto-silence stays on (principle 7). With auto-location on, the Travel rule above moves the place both ways. Today's outside-Tunisia toast «أنت خارج تونس، تم إيقاف الكتم التلقائي» and the silence switch-off behind it (`MainScreen.kt:1722`, `:816`) go. A method choice made abroad stays in `method_choice_<CC>` and comes back on the next trip to that country (6.4), and so does an edited Jumu'a time (6.9).

**Regions.** Every region is open (2): a fix in any country becomes a place, and the picker lists every country.

**Picker.**

- `Locality` stays Tunisian. A new `CatalogueCity` row shares a `SearchableRow` interface with it, so the existing search engine serves both, and catalogue rows never pass through `withAvailablePrayerSource` and its nearest-delegation snapping.
- Search folds ø→o, ß→ss, ł→l, đ/ð→d, þ→th, æ→ae, œ→oe and ı→i. It adds ue/oe/ae and aa aliases, knows exonyms (كولونيا, Cologne → Köln), and breaks ties by population.
- With an empty query, the Tunisian governorates come first, then collapsible country groups with Arabic country names, each showing its 50 largest cities.
- The hint becomes «ابحث عن مدينة أو حيّ…».

**Labels.** City name and admin1 name only, never a guessed administrative word: ولاية means a province in Tunisia and a district in Oman. The country name of the 22 Arab members comes from an app-owned Arabic table, with PS = «فلسطين» (ICU's default name is «الأراضي الفلسطينية»). Other countries come from ICU in Arabic. Western Sahara localities carry Morocco's name (A1).

**Location card.** `MainScreen`'s source repair effect and its "source unavailable" warning (`:1628–1648`, `:1872–1880`) run only for `Inm` places. A `Coordinates` place shows its label and the Arabic country name.

**Qibla.** The math is unchanged. The fallback to the saved place's coordinates now applies whenever the phone's network country or zone matches the place, not only in Tunisia; the «حسب المعتمدية» labels become «حسب الموقع المختار».

### 6.9 Scheduling and derived features

**`PrayerTimesRepository`**

- Takes the saved place and method choice instead of an `Int` delegation id, resolves the official zone (6.5), works out `ramadanDay` from the method's `ramadanDays` (E8) and, for Algeria's attached places, `offsetWindow` (6.2, 8.8), routes as in 6.8, and returns instants (6.6).
- New entry points:
  - `prayerDay(date)`: the `PrayerDay`, with its instants and statuses.
  - `upcoming()`: the next times over D−1..D+1 by instant.
  - `defaultJumuaTime(place, choice, date)`: see "Jumu'a" below.
  - `getDateRange(place)`: 2020..2100 for both place kinds (the engine's `SUPPORTED_YEARS`).
- The "same day last year" fallbacks go.
- The caller changes are mechanical:
  - the 29 references in 9 files (27 calls plus 2 `SUPPORTED_YEARS` reads);
  - the 17 external `getDelegationId` calls in 8 files;
  - the `delegationId: Int` parameters of `FajrAlarmSuggestion`, `DateNavigationRow`, `WakeEditorSheet` and `QiblaCard`.
- Code that treats the id as a real delegation branches on the place kind: `GouvernoratRepository` (availability for `Inm` only), `LocalityRepository` and `PrayerTimeMethodDialog`.

**Silence**

| Where | Change |
|---|---|
| `SilenceScheduler` | On every run (`scheduleAll`, a reboot included) it takes the planner's windows of prayer days D−2..D+1, D being today's date in the place's zone, and arms the start of every window that has not started and the end of every window that has not ended, each under its own prayer day; a window that contains now is handled as today (silenced at once unless the user dismissed it). All of D+1 is armed a day ahead, so a 00:01 run that the maker's battery policy kills loses none of that day's windows; `scheduleTomorrowPrayers` and the reschedule 2 min after the last window (9998, `:26`, `:355–362`), which exist only because days share codes today, go. Request codes live in a new disjoint space with four day slots, `100 + (ordinal*2 + actionBit)*4 + floorMod(epochDay, 4)` (100–163, below 9998), so the four scanned prayer days never share a code, and a slot passes from prayer day k to k+4 only on civil day k+3, when every window of k has ended (endpoint domain below). Each intent carries its prayer day's epoch day. The pre-prayer location check gets the same day slot, `10_000 + ordinal*4 + floorMod(epochDay, 4)` (10_000–10_031), which stays apart because its intent action differs. `cancelPrayerAlarm(s)` and the stale-window cancel take the date, and `cancelAll` cancels all four day slots. The legacy codes 0–15, and the legacy location checks 10_000–10_007 (`10_000 + ordinal`, `:781`), whose codes and action the new checks of Fajr and Dhuhr reuse, are cancelled on every `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` before `scheduleAll`, with no "once" flag. `currentSilenceWindowPrayer/End` read the planner's windows containing now. The 00:01 reschedule is armed even when a day has no times, at 00:01 of D+1 in the place's zone (today in the phone's, `:494–508`), so the look-ahead below holds whatever the phone's zone. The outside-Tunisia checks go. |
| `SilenceWindowPlanner` (new, shared) | Builds each window as a start and an end instant from the prayer's instant, its delay, and its duration or fixed clock (through `SilenceEndpoints`), for prayer days D−2..D+1 around a given instant's date (endpoint domain below), and answers which windows contain an instant and which start before one. `SilenceScheduler`, `EidSilenceWindows`, `PrayerTimelineModel`, `MainScreen.prayerTimeMillis` and the phone's four `overlapForTrigger` callers (`WakeAlarmScheduler:840`, `AwakeCheckSilencePolicy:121`, `MainScreen:7010`, `WakeEditorSheet:3623`) use it in place of `Calendar.set` and `SilenceAlarmComputer`, so the conflict checks see the windows the scheduler arms. `AwakeCheckSilencePolicy` takes the planner's windows around its trigger, like the others, and `EidSilenceWindows.nearby` drops its own look-back (`EidSilenceWindows.kt:29`): an Eid window is a window of its prayer day in this scan. |
| Endpoint domain (planner and silence editors) | A window's start lies within 12 h of its adhan, before or after (a fixed start always does, D16), and its end at most 24 h after the adhan and not before the start. A window therefore lasts at most 36 h, and two occurrences of the same prayer may overlap. The scan and the day slots follow from it. *Look-back:* every adhan of prayer day k comes before solar noon of civil day k+1 (Isha(k) ≤ Fajr(k+1), 6.2), so every window of k ends by the afternoon of civil day k+2, a 23-hour summer-time day included, and none is open on civil day k+3; prayer day D−2 can still be open on D (Paris under D5: Isha of 21 June at 00:11 on 22 June, 7.3, with an end at adhan + 1,440, is open until 00:11 on 23 June). *Look-ahead:* a window of prayer day k starts at the earliest 12 h before Fajr(k), which is on civil day k or just before its midnight (Kazan, 9.2), so no window of D+2 starts before about noon on D+1, long after the next 00:01 run in the place's zone. *Identity:* the armed occurrences belong to four consecutive prayer days, hence the four day slots of `SilenceScheduler`. Today's editors allow far more: the precision sheet's limit is 10,080 min (`PrayerEndpointControls.kt:61`, `PrayerSilenceEditorSheet.kt:119–120`), but its field keeps 4 digits (`:216–219`), so it takes up to 9,999, and its stepper turns 9,999 + 1 into 1,000; the shared sheet takes −10,080 to +10,080 (`SharedSilenceSettingsSheet.kt:54`, `:104–105`); and the slider's edge extension has no limit (`PrayerSilenceRangeSlider.kt:495–537`). A 7-day domain would need about 17 prayer days (D−8..D+8) and as many slots per prayer and action, and a window more than a day long is a standing silence, which the manual silence already gives. The editors validate the domain: the precision sheet takes at most 720 min before the adhan, and after it at most 720 for a start and 1,440 for an end, and its stepper stops at the limit; the shared sheet takes −720 to +720 for the start and −720 to +1,440 for the end; `configResolvesToValidWindow` and `prayerTimelineConfigForWindow` (the slider's `isValid`, so its edge extension stops at the bound) reject a window outside it; and the D16 year run lists a day whose window leaves the domain among the days that do not fit and proposes an alternative (a Dhuhr at 12:30 with a fixed start at 00:20, the next night's, nearest the adhan, and a fixed end at 23:00 on the start's day ends at adhan + 34 h 30 min). At run time the planner clamps the start, once `SilenceEndpoints` has resolved it, to within adhan ±720, then resolves the end from that clamped start (a duration counts from it, a fixed end looks for its clock time on its day, D16) and clamps it to at most adhan + 1,440, so on such a day the clamp applies, not D16's + 30 cap; it skips a window whose clamped end does not come after its clamped start, as `hasInvalidAdhanRelativeEnd` does today. Values saved beyond the domain by today's editors are not migrated (the phone app is in development): they are clamped on every build of the window, and the cards and editors, the shared sheet's initial draft (`MainScreen:2509–2524`) included, show the clamped window. |
| Silence editors: `ui/PrayerSilenceEditorSheet.kt`, `PrayerSilenceSection.kt`, `PrayerSilenceRangeSlider.kt`, `PrayerEndpointControls.kt`, `SharedSilenceSettingsSheet.kt`, and the silence preview in `OnboardingScreen.kt` | Take the prayer's instant and the planner's window instants in place of `PrayerTime` and `prayerMinutesOfDay` (`PrayerTimelineModel.kt:87`), so an after-midnight Isha keeps its prayer day. |
| `SilenceReceiver` | An alarm names its prayer day (`SilenceScheduler`). UNSILENCE keeps today's rule (`isOtherSilenceWindowActive`, 5.1: another open window keeps the silence) and widens it: it re-checks the planner's windows containing now and stays silent until the latest end of any of them, another occurrence of the same prayer included. A tie between two prayers (6.2, Event order) gives two windows counted from the same instant, each with its own start (a `Coordinates` Dhuhr at adhan + 5 under D12, Asr at adhan + 0 by default); both are armed and UNSILENCE keeps the later end. Its location updates go through `updatePlaceFrom*`, which reschedules everything itself when it stores a new place (6.8). |
| `MAX_DELEGATION_SHIFT_MS` | The 45-minute tolerance stays for bridging and dismissal matching of the same prayer at the same place only. A dismissal records the prayer day of each window containing now, and SILENCE (which today matches the prayer's name alone, `SilenceReceiver.kt:34–38`) and `scheduleAll` honour it for those occurrences only, so a dismissal in Fajr(k)'s window does not stop Fajr(k+1)'s from silencing the phone at its start. After a place change, `onPlaceChanged` reschedules fully, so no bridging carries over. |
| `SilenceEndpoints` (new, shared) | Resolves every fixed endpoint in one place (D16): a fixed start takes the occurrence of its clock time nearest the adhan (±12 h); a fixed end takes its clock time on the start's day when that is after the start; otherwise it moves later, to at most 30 min after the user's time, and if that is still before the start it takes the offset from the adhan it had when the user saved it, stored beside it on the phone side (D16). The silence editors run the same rule over the year at the place before saving, and propose an alternative when some days do not fit (D16). Clock times are read in the place's zone (6.6). The planner calls it; the desktop keeps today's rule in `SilenceAlarmComputer`. `PrayerTimelineModel` draws from the planner's instants and stores a fixed clock as a clock time, not as minutes from the prayer day's midnight (today's `/ 60` and `% 60` would store 23:30 the previous evening as minute −30, which readers take as "unset"). |
| Dhuhr and Jumu'a defaults (D12, A18) | `PrefsManager` reports an unedited Dhuhr or Jumu'a start or end as "unset", because `getConfig` has no date. `SilenceEndpoints` resolves "unset" with the window's place and date. The Dhuhr start is a fixed 12:40 in `Africa/Tunis` for an `Inm` place, and adhan + 5 min for a `Coordinates` place. The Jumu'a start is `defaultJumuaTime(place, choice, date)`: the authority's rule, then the place's or country's `SilenceDefaults` entry, then Dhuhr (A18). The end, for an `Inm` place, is 13:15 in `Africa/Tunis`; for a `Coordinates` place, adhan + 45, counted from the resolved Jumu'a start on Fridays. A saved fixed end that no longer fits is flagged on the prayer card and handled as D16 says. An edit stores only the endpoint the user moved; the other stays unset. Today `prayerTimelineConfigForWindow` and `setConfig` write both, which would freeze the 12:40 start, or that day's adhan offset, as an explicit value. Since `PrayerModels.kt` stays byte-identical (6.12), 'unset' is kept on the phone side for each endpoint: an absent key, or the existing −1 clock values. |
| `SilenceVerifyWorker`, `ScheduleRefreshCoordinator` | The outside-Tunisia gate and `RESULT_OUTSIDE_TUNISIA` are deleted. |

**MainScreen**

- **Next prayer.** The countdown, the highlighted next prayer and manual-silence targeting use `upcoming()`, so at 00:30 yesterday's 01:07 Isha is still next.
  - `NextPrayerCountdownInfo` carries the instant and its prayer day.
  - The hero card (`:5181`) formats it through `formatClockTime`.
  - `resolveUpcomingSilence` (`:6746`) resolves against that day.
  - `resolveManualSilencePrayerEndMillis` (`:6671`) searches the planner's windows containing now (prayer days D−2..D+1).
- **Display.** `formatClockTime` takes an instant and shows «بعد منتصف الليل» or «قبل منتصف الليل» (6.6).
- **Filters.** The 0–23 filters at `:2258`, `:2472` and `:5642` go, and the overlap check at `:5658–5662` compares instants; an unset time is null. It does not warn when the next prayer is tied with this one (6.2, Event order).
- **Night dial.** It builds its segments from the sorted instants of Maghrib(D), Isha(D), the two third boundaries and Fajr(D+1), instead of the fixed order Isha → second third (`:2936–2947`). Isha can fall inside the second third (Paris in June). The card says when Fajr is estimated.
- **Wake-conflict editor.** `WakeSilenceConflictPrayerEditor` (`:5777`) takes the prayer day of the window in conflict, which `WakeSilenceConflictSummary` carries from the planner's windows (D−1, D or D+1: a start may come up to 12 h before its adhan, so Fajr(D+1) can start on D), not the civil date of the start nor a window that merely contains it: two occurrences of the same prayer may overlap (endpoint domain above).
- **Removed.** The flag checks at `:1672–1673` and `:6750`, the `onOutsideTunisia` callback (`:816–819`), the `OutsideTunisia` branch (`:1722`) and its toast.
- **From the place.** The Eid default and the `?: 13` Dhuhr fallbacks. «تقديري», the zone line and the zone notice come from `prayerDay()`.

**Adhkar, hifz and wake**

| Where | Change |
|---|---|
| `DhikrReminderScheduler.resolveTime` | The requested event's instant from `prayerDay(date)`, in place of the 0–23 check (`:397`) and of `LocalDateTime.atZone` (`:402`). The whole-day strict ordering (`:399`), which drops every reminder of a day on which any two neighbouring times are equal, goes: the scheduler relies on the engine's event order and its permitted ties (6.2) and checks only the event it needs, so a Fajr-relative reminder is armed on a day when Asr equals Dhuhr. The year clamp uses `PrayerEngine.SUPPORTED_YEARS`. A period from one prayer to a later one whose ends fall on the same instant that day because of a permitted tie (Dhuhr to Asr with equal offsets, on such a day) is empty: no nudge and no occurrence that day, neither `END_BEFORE_START` (`:257`) nor, for a legacy period with no next-day flag (`endNextDay` null), moved to the next day (`:307`); the rule's other periods and days keep theirs. Any other end at or before its start keeps today's handling (`:257`, `:307`), and the 365-day scan reports it from its first date, as any seasonal failure. `windowProblem` and `occasionProblem` compare instants with the same ties (the morning occasion's end, Dhuhr, may equal the evening one's start, Asr). The months cache and the scan key become place key + method key + zone + year + month. `invalidatePrayerCache()` (today never called) runs on every place or method change. The 365-day validation reports the seasonal days where an endpoint is estimated, instead of skipping them, and the days where a period is empty. |
| `DhikrReminderScheduler`, after a move | After a place change, every saved adhkar reminder is checked again over 365 days at the new place with `scheduleProblem`. Today that scan is keyed to the delegation and runs only in the editor (`DhikrReminderScheduler.kt:148`, `:159`). A reminder that no longer fits stays on for the days that still fit, as `resolveWindows` already does, and its card shows the first date that fails (wording with the mockups). Example: a fixed 15:45 evening reminder made in Riyadh (Asr at most about 15:25) fails after a move to Casablanca, where Asr (on GMT since 2026-09-20) runs from about 15:05 in December to about 16:20 in July, so from spring to autumn. |
| `DhikrRepository.collectionReadingPeriodKey` | A reading at 00:30 belongs to the previous evening (the prayer day runs from Fajr to Fajr). |
| `HifzReminder`, `Hifz.kt` | `afterPrayerAt` passes the prayer's instant. The silence end comes from the planner's window for that adhan, in place of "midnight + elapsed millis" (`:187`), which is 1 h off on change days. `firstRepeat` (`Hifz.kt:312`) searches from today − 1, as `nextRepeat` does. A first slot on prayer day today − 1 (tonight's after-midnight Isha) is labelled «الليلة», and `repeatUntil` counts prayer days from it. |
| `WakeAlarmComputer` (shared; its only callers are in the phone) | `PrayerDayContext` takes the day's `PrayerDay` instants, nullable, in place of `DayPrayerTimes`. Fixed-time and from-now alarms come from calendar days, and prayer-relative alarms and conflict checks skip a null day. |
| `WakeAlarmScheduler` | Builds a context for every calendar day from −1 to the look-ahead. |
| `WakeEditorSheet.resolveOneTimeWakeTrigger` | Prayer days from −1, as elsewhere, so tonight's after-midnight Isha (prayer day D−1) is found. |
| `FajrAlarmSuggestion` | Says when Fajr is estimated on some days of the year. |

**`SilenceDefaults` content (D12, A18: from the dataset of 5.6, mosques' published winter Friday times (Q30), and the published Friday times of Martinique and French Guiana, 7.6).** Entries are keyed by coordinates (about 25 km), not by city names, which come in several spellings. A new seasonal clock rule (one value in summer time, one in winter time, either of which may be `AT_DHUHR`) joins `AT_DHUHR` and `FixedClock`, and every clock value is clamped to no earlier than Dhuhr + 5.

- Algeria: west of 2°E `AT_DHUHR`; east `FixedClock(13:00)` (clamped as above), with `AT_DHUHR` for Annaba, Souk Ahras, Biskra, Bouira, Bou Saâda and Akbou.
- Europe, in summer time / winter time (Q30): Belgium 14:00 / 13:00, the UK 13:30 / 13:00, Portugal 14:00 / 13:00 and France 14:00 / 13:00 (low confidence; Paris's mosques split between 13:00 and 13:30 in winter); Spain max(Dhuhr + 5, 14:00) / 13:30, with Madrid 14:45 / Dhuhr and Barcelona 14:00 / 13:30; Germany 14:00 / 13:00 (low confidence) with Munich 13:30 / Dhuhr; Italy Dhuhr / 12:30; the Balkans (AL, BA, ME, MK, RS) 13:00 / 12:00.
- Mauritania 14:00; Martinique 13:00 (the CCIM's published Friday adhan) and French Guiana 13:30 (A2CMGF's Cayenne mosque), neither with summer time.
- Norway, Austria, Ireland and Luxembourg, and Italy in summer time: the fallback is known to be poor there, but no seasonal mosque time and too few dataset values were found to set one (Q30); most Italian October values are winter values left in place. The Netherlands, Switzerland and Sweden keep Dhuhr all year: 46 %, 33 % and 70 % of their mosques have that day's Dhuhr as their Friday time, and Switzerland's winter values (12:30 the most common) would be clamped to Dhuhr + 5 on 89 % of winter Fridays.
- The other Arab countries and Turkey: no entry; Dhuhr already fits.
- Winter values come from mosques' published winter Friday times, else from the dataset's values left from winter (5.6); they are never derived as summer − 60. A season with no value found is `AT_DHUHR`, and a place entry does not take its country's winter value (Q30).

**Eid (A10, A11).** Eid behaviour stays Tunisian (6.10). For a Tunisian place, the default Eid prayer time (sunrise), the Eid row (through the shared `getDefaultEidPrayerTime`) and the Eid silence windows (which read sunrise directly, `EidSilenceWindows.kt:60`, `SilenceScheduler.kt:764–770`) are today's. For a place abroad, nothing is armed for Eid and the Eid row is hidden. The research's Eid rules per authority are kept in Appendix C.
