**Jumu'a (A18).** `defaultJumuaTime(place, choice, date)` returns the profile's fixed clock where the authority sets one (UAE 12:45), otherwise the place's or country's Jumu'a time in `SilenceDefaults` from the owner's dataset or a local publication (Martinique, French Guiana), otherwise Dhuhr (A18). `SilenceEndpoints` resolves an unset Jumu'a start and end from it, because the revision depends on the date. The Jumu'a row shows the resolved time. An edited Jumu'a time is a mosque time, so it is stored per country: Tunisia keeps today's `jomoaa_time_hour` and `jomoaa_time_minute` (`PrefsManager.kt:647–662`), and another country uses `jomoaa_time_<CC>`, so the home mosque's time never applies in Mecca.

### 6.10 Ramadan, Eid and the Hijri date (A10, A11)

The owner decided on 2026-10-11 that the first release adds no Ramadan or Eid support outside Tunisia. The design the research produced for it is kept in Appendix C, updated only by the answers to Q4, Q7 and Q27.

- **The calendar stays Tunisian.** The phone keeps today's Hijri calendar at every place: the Tunisian announcements, polled and bundled as today, and the date row with its «بداية الشهر مؤكدة في تونس» badge. Abroad, the date row can differ by a day from the local calendar (12). None of the shared calendar files changes (6.12).
- **Ramadan and Eid behaviour is Tunisian only.** For a place abroad, the Eid silence windows (`EidSilenceWindows`, `SilenceScheduler.eidPrayerTime`) arm nothing, the Eid row is hidden, and the 90-minute Ramadan Isha silence bump (`RAMADAN_ISHA_MINUTES`) does not apply. For a Tunisian place, all three behave as today. The bump already follows the Tunisian Ramadan dates: `RamadanDetector.isRamadan` tests the day against that year's Tunisian dates as `OfficialIslamicDates` records them (`RamadanOverrideChecker.calendar()`, which returns `OfficialIslamicDates.calendar()`; not the current-year `cachedOverride`), the last day of Sha'ban and the first day of Shawwal included, and uses Umm al-Qura's month 9 only while no Tunisian date for that year is known; only its once-a-year key and its after-Ramadan test use Umm al-Qura's year and month (`HijrahDate.now()`, 5.1). It stays relative to INM's Isha.
- **The Ramadan bump stays a temporary override, and its restore never depends on the place.** Today's mechanism is kept (5.1, `PrefsManager.kt:396–484`): the user's Isha values are saved, 90 is written, and the saved values come back after that Ramadan unless an Isha edit dropped them. `applyRamadanIshaOverrideIfNeeded(context, place, today = HijrahDate.now())` runs in this order:
  1. Restore. When saved values exist and that Ramadan is over (the current code's test, `PrefsManager.kt:439–443`), they are restored, at every place. No place-kind, region-switch or auto-location test comes before this step. Neither caller skips the call for a foreign place: `SilenceScheduler.scheduleAllInternal` keeps it right after its auto-silence check and before it loads the place's times (`SilenceScheduler.kt:204`), and the Application's date observer calls it at every process start, whether auto-silence is on or off (`TunisianPrayerTimesApplication.kt:74`). An override written in Tunis is therefore restored on the first call after that Ramadan, even when the phone is in Paris by then.
  2. Apply, for an `Inm` place only, under the current code's conditions: inside the Ramadan window, an Umm al-Qura year not yet recorded, Isha's duration below 90. A foreign place neither writes the override nor records the year, so a return to a Tunisian place later in the same Ramadan applies it then. Today nothing gates the bump by place: the `disabled_outside_tunisia` flag never did (5.1), and it goes with the outside-Tunisia gate at M5 (6.9).
  3. Read at a foreign place. While saved values exist, Isha's window at a `Coordinates` place uses them in place of the stored 90 and the removed end offset (a saved UNSET reads as the default, 30 min and no end offset). `PrefsManager.getConfig` takes the place for this, and every reader passes the window's or the screen's place (they all have it from M5, 6.9), so the armed window and the editor agree. The stored keys are not touched, so a Tunisian place later in the same Ramadan still gets 90. An Isha edit made abroad saves the values the editor shows through `setConfig` and drops the saved copy, as any Isha edit does today (`PrefsManager.kt:476–483`), so that edit holds at every place, Tunisian places included, for the rest of that Ramadan and after it.
- **The official methods keep their own Ramadan rules** (E8). Saudi Isha at Maghrib + 120 and Qatar's Maghrib + 1 are part of those authorities' timetables, so they stay. They need no announcement: `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED` is computed on the phone, and `PLACE_CALENDAR` means `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED` outside Tunisia. Saudi Isha follows the computed Umm al-Qura month until Appendix C brings the announced dates (Q4).
- **Extra Ramadan rows**, such as Imsak, are not stored or shown (A14).
- **Names.** In the first release, Hijri month names are Tunisia's at every place: ربيع الثاني and جمادى الثانية (D19). For internationalization (14), the names come from the app's own table per country (today `HijriLabels` in the shared `IslamicDays.kt`, Tunisia's only), not from the locale: CLDR (JDK 21) gives every `ar-<CC>` the same ربيع الآخر and جمادى الآخرة. Months 3 and 5 read ربيع الأول and جمادى الأولى everywhere checked. A country leaves the default only on its authority's own statements or printed calendar; a generated date on its site does not count, since it can contradict them (KACST's API writes ربيع الثاني, the Umm al-Qura site ربيع الآخر; Oman's prayer page «ربيع ثانى», its ministry ربيع الآخر). Checked on 2026-10-11 (Q11), months 4 and 6 read ربيع الثاني and جمادى الثانية in Tunisia and Algeria (the ministry's 1448 calendar), ربيع الثاني and جمادى الآخرة in Jordan (Ifta announcements), and ربيع الآخر and جمادى الآخرة everywhere else, the default: Morocco (Habous, Bulletin Officiel), Egypt (Dar al-Ifta), Saudi Arabia (the Umm al-Qura site), the UAE, Qatar (QCH) and Oman (the ministry's statements). Mauritania and Kuwait write جمادى الآخرة in their ministries' texts and take the default for month 4 too: their ربيع الثاني appears only in a generated date on the ministry's site. Libya, whose Awqaf site formats the date with the browser's `ar-LY` locale, and the unchecked countries take the default. The Maghreb is not grouped into one name set.
- **Locale.** The first release keeps the forced `ar-TN-u-nu-latn` at every place, so Gregorian months read «جانفي» abroad too (D19), and none of the files below changes. For internationalization (14): Gregorian month names would come from the locale `ar-<CC>-u-nu-latn` in its place (`-u-nu-latn` keeps Western digits; European countries fall back to `ar`, يناير). The forced locale is set in three activities that call `Locale.setDefault` (`MainActivity.kt:117`, `OnboardingActivity.kt:17`, `wake/WakeAlertActivity.kt:128`), in `ui/HijriCalendarDates.kt:20`, and in seven formatters (`ui/MainScreen.kt:7061` and `:7066`; `ui/WakeEditorSheet.kt:1790`, `:3649` and `:3654`; `adhkar/DhikrReminderScheduler.kt:58`; `quran/hifz/HifzReminder.kt:72`). The month names written into `prayer_method_strings.xml` (lines 33 and 67, «جانفي») would become arguments formatted with that locale.

### 6.11 UI and text

**Prayer-tab settings** (`PrayerTabSettingsSheet`), above the explainer row:

- «طريقة الحساب» shows the method's name and its angles in an LTR isolate: a preset as `18° · 17°`, an authority profile with the authority's name first, for example «وزارة الأوقاف والشؤون الدينية (عُمان) · 18° · 18°». It opens `PrayerMethodSheet`, grouped into «الشائعة في بلدك», «طرق أخرى» and «مخصّصة». The one default inferred by analogy, Greece's `diyanet_tr` (the Thrace muftis' tables are not yet looked for; AM0 looks, 8.8), carries an "inferred" note. Each entry shows its parameters, its chip and evidence line, and today's Fajr, Maghrib and Isha. A Tunisian place shows none of the rows of this list (D7).
- «الجدول الرسمي المعتمد: مسقط» wherever the place maps to an authority's zone (6.5): the profiles of 8.7 that have zones and, in Europe, `diyanet_eu` in Austria (IGGÖ's city tables and Diyanet's Austrian places), `luxembourg_shoura` (the Shoura's table), `diyanet_tr` in Turkey (Diyanet's district tables), `bosnia_iz`, `hr_miz_zagreb`, `si_islamska_skupnost`, `al_kmsh`, `xk_bik`, `mk_bfi`, `bg_grand_mufti`, `irn`, `dum_rf`, `ru_dum_rt` and `ie_ifi`: «الجدول الرسمي المعتمد: فيينا». The single zones of `rhone_ctir` (a mosque council's table, Q33), `km_fitted` and `yt_traditional` (a traditional table no authority publishes, A3, A1, Q36) drop «الرسمي», as do `bosnia_iz`'s places in Montenegro (the Bosnian IZ's table, not the Islamic Community in Montenegro's; 8.8): «الجدول المعتمد: الرون», «الجدول المعتمد: مايوت», and likewise in the Comoros. `re_cir`'s four regions (a mosque body's table, 7.6) drop it too: «الجدول المعتمد: جنوب الجزيرة».
- «وقت العصر»: الجمهور / الحنفية. Every official Arab table uses factor 1; the toggle also serves Hanafi minorities.
- «الليالي القصيرة» lists the rules of 7.4 and warns that the "every night" ones move real times. It is shown only where the method's angles can fail (latitude ≥ 66.56° − the larger angle), where the method's high-latitude rule can move a time on some night (D5's angle-based rule from about 44.8°N with an 18° Fajr, 7.3), where the optional every-night cap of `mdf_12` or `luxembourg_shoura` would (from about 47.55°N for 12° and 47.1°N for 13°), or where the method's own rule applies (≥ 44.375°N for Diyanet), so Tunisia never sees it, and the Arab countries, Spain and southern Italy see it only if the user picks a method whose own rule acts there (`mcw`, `london_unified`, `gmp`, `hr_miz_zagreb`, `si_islamska_skupnost`).
- «تعديل الأوقات بالدقائق» has six steppers. Signed values use U+2212 inside U+2066…U+2069. A stepper stops where its time would cross a bound of the sun's disk (6.2, step 9).
- «مخصّصة» is a bounded method editor (owner question, 2026-10-12). It starts from the current method's values and offers only the inputs that are matters of ijtihad, each limited to the positions recognised bodies or credible observations hold, each value labelled with who holds it:

  | Input | Choices | Who holds them |
  |---|---|---|
  | الفجر | an angle from 12° to 20° in steps of 0.5°, or minutes before sunrise (60–120); in mainland France 12°–18° (E19) | 12° Musulmans de France; 13° the Luxembourg Shoura; about 14.5° the Rhône council's observed table; 15° ISNA (FCNA suggests it for the USA, 13° for Canada); 18° MWL, Diyanet, the Istanbul 2021 congress, APBIF and INM; 19° Morocco; 19.5° Egypt; 20° Indonesia |
  | العشاء | the red twilight, an angle from 12° to 17°; the white twilight, 18°; or minutes after Maghrib (60–120); and «العشاء ليس قبل» | 12° Musulmans de France and APBIF; 17° MWL and Istanbul 2021; 18° INM and the Hanafi view; Maghrib + 87 the Grande Mosquée de Paris; Maghrib + 90 Umm al-Qura |
  | العصر | shadow factor 1 or 2 | 1 the majority, the Maliki included; 2 the Hanafi |
  | الظهر | transit + 2 to + 10 min | the zawal needs about 1.2 min; + 5 Diyanet, Morocco and the Grande Mosquée de Paris; + 7 INM |
  | المغرب | sunset + 1 to + 10 min, a precaution on the start (a mosque's later adhan or iqama goes in the Maghrib stepper) | + 2 INM and Morocco; + 3 the Grande Mosquée de Paris; + 6 APBIF; + 7 Diyanet; + 10 Yemen's highlands (E10). The range stops at + 10 because the Maliki school keeps Maghrib's preferred time short; it is a limit of the editor, not the end of Maghrib's time nor a bound of E19 (7.7) |
  | الشروق | sunrise − 0 to − 10 min | − 7 Diyanet |
  | ارتفاع المكان | the horizon dip of the place's elevation on sunrise and Maghrib, off by default | INM uses it (E2, E3) |
  | الليالي القصيرة | the rules of 7.4, shown only where one can act | 7.4 |

  - The astronomy is not a setting: the sun model (E14), refraction and the sun's radius (−0.833°, E2), the time zone and the solver stay the engine's, and so does rounding (E1, inward at a bound). They are physics, accurate to seconds; the spread between the recognised definitions is what moves times, by far more (Fajr at 12° against 18°: 33–58 min apart at Marseille through the year, 36 min to about 2 h at Paris, where 18° is not reached on 19 nights).
  - Every choice shows today's times and the year strip at once, with each value's source line.
  - The ranges keep a custom time inside the recognised positions, and E19's bounds check it wherever it is used, not only in France; outside mainland France the Fajr floor is the editor's own 20°. On a night without a 12° instant (beyond about 54.6° of latitude, around the June solstice in the north and the December solstice in the south) the 12° Fajr bound does not apply, and the short-night rule's Fajr (7.4) stands, at or after the night's lowest point (6.2, Event order). A custom method shows the chip «فلكية» (A6).
  - An optional «العشاء ليس قبل» clock time (`ishaEarliest`, owner 2026-10-11) lets a user copy a mosque that holds Isha at a fixed time in winter: Ivry's Annour mosque never shows Isha before 19:00, and 243 French mosques do the same (5.6). Like the minute adjustments it is the user's own setting, so a held Isha is not marked «تقديري».
  - A custom interval longer than half a short night is held at the night's lowest point and marked «تقديري» (6.2, Event order).
  - A Tunisian place keeps INM only (D7).
- «طريقتك» ends with the method's source («المصدر») and the place names' credit («أسماء الأماكن»: GeoNames, CC BY 4.0, and Wikidata, CC0); the «مصادر البيانات» row that held them was removed (owner, 2026-10-19).

**Chips (A6).** One chip per method, «رسمية» or «فلكية» (owner, 2026-10-20: «either it is official or it is falakia»). It depends on the method, the place's country and the mapped zone only; `MethodPresentation.chip` holds the rule, and its tests cover each case. Before that a five-level scale also showed «مطابقة للجدول الرسمي», «طريقة منشورة», «مستخلصة من جداول» and «تقريبية»; «تقريبية» did not inspire confidence, and names built on the source («مبنية على مصادر محدودة», then one per method) were turned down too.

- «رسمية» for a national body's own definition in its own country (principle 6: `inm` for a Tunisian place, `bosnia_iz` in BA, `diyanet_tr` in TR), for `sa_umm_al_qura` anywhere in Saudi Arabia, at exact coordinates included, because KACST computes for any point, and for an authority's table at the place's mapped zone in that zone's own country, whatever its fit record (`jo_awqaf`'s included, A19). Tables no authority publishes (`rhone_ctir`, `km_fitted`, `yt_traditional`, `re_cir`) and Bosnia's table shifted for Montenegro are not official.
- «فلكية» everywhere else: with the A2 option «حسب موقعي بالضبط», where the validity guard acts (6.5), beyond every zone's reach, outside the authority's country, in the Kurdistan Region (no zone serves it, 8.8), for every preset and the A3 formulas, and for a custom method.
- «طريقتك» keeps the tier-A record (9.5) as evidence: the zone's own record, never the profile's aggregate in its place (Doha's record does not cover Mesaieed, nor Amman's a further place), and for `sa_umm_al_qura` the profile's, with held-out points in each of the Kingdom's 13 regions. Its line gives the record's held-out share over the six times (quirk days left out, rounded down to a whole percent), the table it was measured on and the years of its held-out days, for example «تطابق هذه الطريقة 98% من دقائق جدول مسقط الرسمي لسنة 2027، والفرق في الباقي دقيقة واحدة.» at a zone and «تطابق هذه الطريقة 98% من دقائق الجدول الرسمي في 20 مدينة لسنتي 2025 و2027، والفرق في الباقي دقيقة واحدة.» for `sa_umm_al_qura`, each number in an LTR isolate. The difference it names is the record's measured maximum, quirk days included. Where quirk days raise it, the line ends «…، ويبلغ دقيقتين في أيام قليلة.»
- In Algeria, except at a place where the validity guard acts (there the attached table would put a prayer before its start at the place, or its sunrise after the place's own sunrise, and the zone line of 6.5 says why), «حسب موقعي بالضبط» adds the line «تُحسب لموقعك بطريقة الوزارة، وقد تسبق جدولها الرسمي بدقيقة إلى خمس دقائق.» (A16).
- Some method rows add a line naming the source: `yt_traditional` adds «تتبع هذه الأوقات الجدول التقليدي المتداول في مايوت، ولم نجد جدولًا رسميًا منشورًا على الإنترنت لهذا المكان.» (Q36) `lb_dar_al_fatwa`'s method row adds «هذه أوقات مدينة بيروت، مستخلصة من إمساكية دار الفتوى، ولم نجد لدار الفتوى جدولًا منشورًا لمكان آخر في لبنان. وقد تختلف عن جدولها بدقيقتين أو ثلاث في بعض الأيام.», or, with «حسب موقعي بالضبط» and at a place where the validity guard acts (in the far south and on the north-eastern border; 6.5), «تُحسب لموقعك بطريقة مستخلصة من إمساكية دار الفتوى لمدينة بيروت.» In SD, SO, DJ and KM the method row adds «لم نجد جدولًا رسميًا منشورًا على الإنترنت لهذا البلد.» In the Kurdistan Region (the Erbil, Sulaymaniyah, Duhok and Halabja governorates), `iq_sunni_endowment`'s method row adds «لم نجد جدولًا رسميًا منشورًا على الإنترنت لإقليم كردستان، ولمساجده تقاويمها الخاصة، وقد تختلف عن هذه الأوقات بنحو 20 دقيقة في بعض الأيام.», the number in an LTR isolate (Q8).

**Choosers** (D4): `MethodChooserSheet`, one screen, as 6.4 describes. IQ, LB and BH have none (A4), nor do SD, SO, DJ and KM (A3).

**List of times**

- Estimated times carry a «تقديري» chip with a one-line explanation, for example «لا تنزل الشمس الليلة إلى 17° تحت الأفق، فقُدّر العشاء بقاعدة نسبة الزاوية من الليل.». A night when the sun reaches the angle but D5's rule moves the time gets its own variant, for example «تبلغ الشمس 17° الليلة، لكن قاعدة نسبة الزاوية من الليل قدّمت العشاء.». A polar variant starts «لا تغرب الشمس اليوم…». The order guard's holds (6.2) have their own, for example «يأتي الظهر في هذه الطريقة بعد بداية وقت العصر اليوم، فجُعل العصر مع الظهر.» and «الفترة التي تحددها هذه الطريقة بعد المغرب أطول من نصف الليلة، فجُعل العشاء في وسط الليلة.» The wording is settled with the mockups (M4).
- «بعد منتصف الليل» and «قبل منتصف الليل» mark times on another civil day.
- When the place's zone differs from the phone's, one line gives the place's offset, for example «UTC+2» in `ltr()`.
- The zone line «جدول: مسقط» where the method has zones (6.5).
- **Extra rows** (A14): none; they are not stored.

**Method explainer.** The dialog takes `(PrayerPlace, PrayerMethodChoice)`. For an `Inm` place it stays as today, since INM is the method there (D7).

- For a place abroad, a new «طريقتك» section comes first:
  - a parameter table generated from the method (no hard-coded 18°, +7 or UTC+1): for an authority profile, the authority, the fitted conventions (rounding per prayer, horizon, fixed minutes) and the fit record of the mapped zone (for `sa_umm_al_qura` the profile's record, which decides its chip, 6.11), otherwise the profile's aggregate (6.3): the held-out share exact per time with its period, days and table, and the data the fit used;
  - today's times with their markers;
  - where «الليالي القصيرة» is shown: a crash-safe `TwilightYearStrip` (365 cells marking the nights where the angle is never reached, plus polar days) and a paragraph giving, for each of the method's angles, the latitude from which it is never reached on the shortest night, 66.56° − the angle (7.1), computed from the method like the parameter table, for example 48.56° for 18°, 51.56° for 15° and 53.56° for 13°.
- The Tunis/INM worked example stays.
  - It is always fed delegation 615 (`EXAMPLE_DELEGATION_ID`). The gate that hides the whole dialog when the user's place has no INM data (`:151–152`) now gates only this example, on 615's data.
  - For a place abroad it is retitled «مثال: كيف يحسب المعهد الوطني للرصد الجوي مواقيت تونس» and collapsed.
- The INM "today" result and the "matches to the minute" card show only for an `Inm` place. Other methods get a source card with their evidence.
- The diagrams (`DayAltitudeChart`, `AltitudeProtractor`, `AsrShadowDiagram`, `SunPathChart`, `NoonShiftDiagram`) are fed only delegation 615, never a European or Arab place, whatever its latitude. Showing the user's place in a diagram would need three changes this plan does not make: the place's UTC offset (`NoonShiftDiagram` hard-codes UTC+1, `PrayerTimeMethodDiagrams.kt:98`, and so does `SunYear.dayOf`), a non-INM input (the diagrams take `InmLocation` and `InmDayExplanation`), and hemisphere awareness (`AltitudeProtractor` assumes the noon sun is to the south).

**Week.** The weekday chips and summaries in the wake, adhkar and hifz editors keep the Tunisian week, from Monday (`WakeScheduleDay`, `WakeModels.kt:26`), at every place (D19). For internationalization (14) they would start on the place country's first day of the week (ICU for `ar-<CC>`), with range detection wrapping around the week, so Sunday to Thursday reads «من الأحد إلى الخميس».

**Notices.** The «تأكيد الطريقة» and «تأكيد الموقع» banners, and the adhkar flag after a move (6.9).

**Wording**

| String | New text or change |

| `auto_location_title` | «تحديث الموقع تلقائيًا» |
| `location_permission_denied` | «يجب منح إذن الموقع لتحديد موقعك تلقائيًا» |

The app name stays (A13); the Play listing gains A13's source and independence lines.

### 6.12 Tunisia, the TV and the desktop

- **Byte-identical:** `InmPrayerFormula`, `InmPrayerTimes`, `PrayerModels`, `PrayerFormulaSettings`, `SilenceAlarmComputer` (the phone stops calling it, 6.6), `DayBanner`, `PrayerFlow`, `ClockGuard`, the `PrayerDataLoader` actuals, `data/prayer-formula/*` (with `delegation_params.json`), `gouvernorats.json` and the TV's `formula.js`.
- **Changed, with today's behaviour as the default.** The TV and the desktop keep today's behaviour, but these shared files are not byte-identical:
  - `WakeAlarmComputer`: takes `PrayerDay` instants, nullable (6.9); it has phone callers only.
- **New engine, method, place, zone and silence files:** the TV compiles them but never calls them.
- **Calendar files unchanged** (6.10): `RamadanOverrideChecker`, `OfficialIslamicDates`, `TunisianHijriCalendar`, `IslamicDays`, `ManualIslamicDates`, `platform/Preferences.kt`, the TV's `IslamicDatesSection` and `BundleDataAssets` stay as they are.
- **Unchanged tests:** `InmPrayerFormulaTest`, `PrayerFormulaSettingsTest`, the TV unit tests and `check_dashboard_formula.js` run unchanged.
- **Mosque TVs outside Tunisia** are a separate future plan: the TV forces `Africa/Tunis` (`KioskController.kt:181`, with the shared `TunisTime.ZONE` of `ClockGuard.kt:10`), and its settings file, `ClockGuard`, the dashboard and `formula.js` are INM-shaped.

## 7. Europe

Europe has no single authority: the user chooses among sound methods, and from 44.375°N (Diyanet's own rule; 48.56°N for an 18° angle), from about 45°N for the angle-based rule (D5's default), or at any latitude for `mcw`, `london_unified` and `gmp`'s Fajr, the engine needs high-latitude rules. 7.1–7.3 are the facts, 7.4–7.5 the parts of the design only Europe needs, 7.6 the defaults and the four profiles of overseas France, which sits behind the Europe switch (2), and 7.7 mainland France's validity envelope, with what French mosques and other apps show.

### 7.1 When twilight, the sun, or Asr is missing

The sun's lowest point at night is at altitude |φ| + δ − 90°. A depression angle α is never reached when |φ| > 90° − α − δ. At the June solstice (δ = 23.44°):

| Angle | Never reached north of |
|---|---|
| 18° | 48.56°N |
| 17° | 49.56°N |
| 16° | 50.56°N |
| 15° | 51.56°N |
| 13° | 53.56°N |
| 12° | 54.56°N |
| Sunset (−0.833°) | 65.73°N: midnight sun |
| Sunrise, winter | 67.40°N: polar night |

**Mornings in 2026 without an 18° Fajr.** Computed with the app's own sun model; ±1 at each end, because boundary nights miss the angle by hundredths of a degree (Paris on 11–12 June by 0.006°).

| Place | Mornings | Dates |
|---|---|---|
| Strasbourg | about 5 | |
| Paris | 18–19 | about 12/13–30 June |
| Frankfurt | 43 | |
| Brussels | 52 | |
| London | 60 | 23 May–21 July |
| Berlin | 70 | |
| Copenhagen | 95 | |
| Stockholm | 118 | |
| Oslo | 122 | 22 April–21 August |
| Tromsø | 174–175 | |

Tromsø also has midnight sun from 18 May to 25 July and polar night from 28 November to 14 January.

**Asr.** Asr needs the noon sun above the horizon (|φ − δ| < 90°). Near the polar circle it disappears on more days than the polar night, because refraction gives a sunrise while the sun's centre stays below 0° at noon. Days without Asr in 2026: Tromsø 48 + 9 (15–18 January, 23–27 November), Bodø 27 (8 December–3 January, a place with no polar night at all), Kiruna 14, Murmansk 40 + 10.

**Other civil days.** Even where the angle is reached, times leave the civil day:

- After midnight: Isha at 17° is at 01:07 CEST in Paris and about 01:21 in Brest in June.
- Before midnight: far east of a zone's meridian, Fajr can fall on the previous evening. Kazan's 18° Fajr of 5 May 2026 is at 23:53 on 4 May (UTC+3), and so is Vorkuta's on 1 April (23:01 on 31 March).
- Fajr at 18° is at 00:41 in Moscow and 00:45 in Warsaw.

Spain, Portugal and Italy south of 44.375°N need no rule for missing twilight, and neither does any Arab League place (2). There, only a method's own bound changes times, if picked: the seasonal curve of `mcw`, `london_unified` and `gmp`'s Fajr, the summer forms of `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost` (from about 31°N in June), and `mwl_angle`'s 22:45 latest Isha.

### 7.2 How European bodies compute

There is no single convention. The 2021 Istanbul congress (Diyanet with the European Council for Fatwa and Research) produced the closest thing to a common European scheme. Diyanet's European calendars and Austria's official IGGÖ timetable use it. Elsewhere mosques choose, often through Mawaqit.

| Body or practice | Fajr | Isha | Minute offsets | Summer rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Diyanet Europe = IGGÖ Austria | 18° | 16°, but 17° in Turkey, Greece, Bulgaria, Albania, Kosovo, North Macedonia and Montenegro (a split by country) | sunrise −7, Dhuhr +5, Asr +4, Maghrib +7 | *Takdir* from 44.375°N, the 2021 Prayer Times Congress's criteria as Diyanet's tables apply them (README H6): Isha = Maghrib + a third of the sharia night (Maghrib to the 18° Fajr) and Fajr = sunrise − 18/16 of that third, from 21 June with 20-minute transitions; where the 18° Fajr never forms (from 48.56°N) the last share of the night is kept; 5 h minimum day and night on the published times | re-derived from Diyanet's 2027 tables (144 entries, 43–78°N, `fits/diyanet_takdir`, 2026-10-16); IGGÖ's prose ("Isha = Maghrib + 1/3 of the night") holds for the sharia night, Maghrib to the 18° Fajr |
| Musulmans de France (ex-UOIF) | 12° | 12° | none adopted: a 2012 reproduction of its GEDIS calendar text (Havre de Savoir) gives Fajr − 5, Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 5, too old to adopt (owner, 2026-10-11); no text of 2020 or later gives any (Q32) | none needed below 54.56°N | 12° adopted at UOIF seminars in 1994 and 1996 (secondary sources); MF's own pages are gone |
| Conseil des Mosquées du Rhône (CTIR) | ≈ 14.5° | ≈ 13.5° | Dhuhr ≈ + 5.5, Maghrib ≈ + 2.6 (back-computed) | none needed (45–46°N) | primary: its unified calendar, built from field observations and adopted on 2019-11-30 by about 40 mosques as one table for the whole département (the Turkish federations' mosques excepted), and its 2020 table (cmrofficiel.fr), still the only one in 2026 (Q33); the angles are back-computed from the table, and the press's 13.5° Fajr and 14.5° Isha (Saphirnews, 2019-12-03) are the reverse of what the table shows; the table's sunrise, Maghrib and Isha drift by up to 3 min with the season against the formula |
| Grande Mosquée de Strasbourg | Diyanet's Strasbourg table | | | Diyanet takdir | primary: its panel equals Diyanet's table on all six times (one day, 2026-10-11) |
| APBIF (Paris region) | 18°; on nights without it, the local solar midnight: Fajr keeps the half of the night it has at the nearest place to the south, on the same meridian, where the sun just reaches 18° at solar midnight (north of APBIF's Strasbourg–Saint-Brieuc line at 48°33'N, near the June solstice; at Paris 13–30 June 2026, 7.1) | 12°, with no precaution: APBIF reports seeing the red twilight gone at 12° on several evenings, and notes that 12° is reached all year in France | + 6 on Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr and Maghrib, its precaution minutes; none on sunrise and Isha; sunset, and in its tables sunrise too, with the sun's centre at −1°; an imsak at Fajr − 20, which its tables print every day (not stored, A14) | the solar midnight above, + 6, with no ramp: its 2026 Paris table goes from 02:02 to 01:57 on 12–13 June and from 02:00 to 02:08 on 30 June–1 July | primary: its methodology page (apbif.fr, undated, about 2013) and its 2026 tables for 79 towns, 77 of them French (read 2026-10-13). At Paris this procedure gives all six times within 1 min on the 123 days of June, July, October and December 2026, with Asr factor 1, sunrise rounded down, Maghrib up and the rest to the nearest minute. Its Fajr matches 4 of the dataset's French mosques within 5 min on at least 90 % of days, and no mosque matches all six times (review of 2026-10-13); offered as `apbif` under «طرق أخرى» (7.5) |
| Grande Mosquée de Paris | 18°, bounded by the Moonsighting Committee's seasonal curve, as `mcw`: at Paris always the curve, sunrise − 92 to 118 min | Maghrib + 87 min | Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 3 | none needed: the curve and the Isha interval exist every night (Fajr 03:50, Isha 23:28 on 21 June) | primary: its October 2026 timetable, all six times within 1 min on all 31 days, Asr as adhan computes it; it publishes monthly images, no method. Its Ramadan 2023 and 2024 imsakiyat were a fixed clock table (18° in winter to about 14.5° in June) that 18 Paris-area mosques still copy (Q21) |
| Luxembourg Shoura | 13° | 13° | none | none needed below 53.56°N: its June and July times keep the real 13° (Q37) | back-computed from its 2026 PDF |
| Islamic Community of Bosnia and Herzegovina | 18° | 16° | sunrise −6, Dhuhr +1, Maghrib +6 (justified by 920 m of altitude) | none needed (43–45°N) | Takvim 2025, checked against vaktija.ba |
| Albania (KMSH) | 18° | 17° | as Diyanet, with the sun taken once a day at 0h UT (E4) | none needed | its 2026 Kalendari: Tirana (41°20'N 19°49'E) 97–99 % exact per prayer, all within 1 min; ten cities as Tirana plus a published whole-minute shift (8.8) |
| Kosovo (BIK) | Imsaku ≈ 18.3–19.5°; the Fajr prayer («Sabahu») is Imsaku + 20 | ≈ 17.6–19.2° | a hand-made table at 42.5°N 21°E with a stated 6-min temkin: by month, sunrise 3.5–9.6 min early, Maghrib 6.7–8.2 min late, Dhuhr transit + 2.0 to + 5.0 | none needed | its 2026 Takvim, one table for Kosovo and the Preševo Valley with seven town shifts; reproduced only through a seasonal curve (E18, 8.8) |
| North Macedonia (BFI) | «Sabahu» only, about sunrise − 50 | ≈ 15° | Dhuhr ≈ + 7, Asr ≈ + 1 to + 3, Maghrib ≈ sunset + 8 to + 10 | none needed | one day of Skopje times on bfi.mk (read 2026-10-11; the site shows only the current day, undated): Jacia 19:16, 11 min before Diyanet's 17° (8.8) |
| Bulgaria (Chief Mufti's Office) | ≈ 18° | ≈ 17° | ≈ Diyanet's | none needed | its monthly tables for 48 cities: 7 rows of October 2026 within 3 min of Diyanet's method at Sofia, Fajr 1–2 min earlier and Maghrib and Isha 2–3 later (8.8) |
| UK, London Unified Timetable | observation-based (about 12° in June, 16° in winter) | about 9–15° | sunrise −3, Dhuhr +5, Maghrib +3; Asr published with both factors | built in | East London Mosque; the Moonsighting Committee formula is within ±6 min at Fajr and 0 to +11 min at Isha |
| UK, Bradford 1983 ruling | 18° | the same principle | | nearest day, May–July | the ruling's text |
| Moonsighting Committee Worldwide | 18°, bounded by a seasonal curve | 18°, bounded by a seasonal curve | Dhuhr +5, Maghrib +3 | seasonal curve; one-seventh of the night from 55° | coefficients from adhan (MIT); no primary text |
| Ireland, Islamic Foundation of Ireland (IFI) and ICCI table | one perpetual Dublin table, not an angle: ≈ 16.5° in November to ≈ 11.3° in late June | ≈ 15.7° in November to ≈ 10.7° in June (ICCI); the IFI's is earlier from 18 May to 25 July, by up to 26 min, and never before Maghrib + 90 (exactly + 90 from 1 June to 9 July) | Dhuhr ≈ + 2; sunrise 0–6 min early and Maghrib 0–7 min late, by season | built into the table; its clock changes on 1 April and 1 November, not on the summer-time Sundays | primary: the IFI's timetable API (settings updated 2026-10-01, read 2026-10-11) and ICCI's own (settings updated 2018-06-04, archived 2022-08-02), equal on every time but the summer Isha; 9 of 21 Irish mosques in the dataset show it; the IFI's Ramadan 2026 tables for Dublin and 32 other towns (read 2026-10-11): Dublin's equals this table on all 180 times, and Cork's keeps Dublin's Fajr and Isha intervals (sunrise − Fajr, Isha − Maghrib) within 1 min on all 30 days, with Cork's own sunrise, Dhuhr, Asr and Maghrib (Dhuhr 8–9 min after Dublin's) (Q23) |
| Ireland, ICCI website (2026) | 18° | 17° | Dhuhr + 1 | angle-based rule; Isha shown no later than 22:45, ICCI's Isha iqamah | its site's adhan-js code at Dublin's centre, under a loading line naming an "IACAD Dublin Prayer Timetable"; it matches ICCI's own table on 3 of 355 Fajr and 2 of 355 Isha times (Q23) |
| Portugal, CIL Lisbon | 18° | Maghrib + 77 min | Maghrib +3 | none needed | Aladhan only |
| Russia, DUM RF (Moscow and Moscow oblast) | 18° | 15° | sunrise −5, Dhuhr +5, Maghrib +5 | angle-based every night (Fajr 0.30, Isha 0.25 of the night), with Isha at 0.30 of the night in June and a ramp around June that DUM RF sets by hand each year (7.4) | primary: its monthly Moscow tables (dumrf.ru; May, July, August and October 2026, June and July 2025; no June 2026 sheet was found, and the mosque dataset's June 2026 rows, support only, put Isha at 0.30 of the night); the 2026 sheets and June 2025 are reproduced within 2 min on all six times, July 2025's Isha within 3; each sheet notes its Council of Ulema's view that Fajr is the end of suhur; the libraries' "Russia" method (Fajr 16°) does not match it |
| Russia, DUM RT (Tatarstan) | 18° | 15° | none; Dhuhr at its zenith column (its fixed «Зухр» column, 12:00, is the mosques' prayer time, as its Fajr «in mosques» column is) | on nights when the sun stays above −18°, Fajr = sunrise − 2 h and Isha = sunset + 1 h 30, as its table notes say | primary: its 2026 workbook for Kazan, Naberezhnye Chelny and the district centres, 44 places (dumrt.ru, read 2026-10-11); Asr with factor 2 (Hanafi) only; at 8 places, at town-centre points, this is within 1 min on 97.5–100 % of days per prayer, the misses on the switch nights |
| Norway, IRN | 16° | 15° | Dhuhr + 5 or + 10, Maghrib + 4/5 | IRN's 3 zones (7.4): south of 63.56°N, Isha moved linearly (in UTC) from about 20:00 UTC to Maghrib + 40 at the solstice and Fajr held at a clock time, never later than sunrise − 60; from 63.56°N, Fajr and Isha capped at solar midnight ∓ 3 h and Mecca's day on local noon in summer, and in the polar night north of 66.33°N | primary: IRN's 2026 tables for all 357 municipalities and Svalbard (bonnetid.info, its national prayer-time project with ICC and DIF/Rabita, read 2026-10-11); south of 63.56°N Fajr exact and Isha within 1 min given the switch days, north of 66.33°N within 1–2 min; switch dates fitted (D6) |
| MWL | 18° | 17° | none in the libraries; the 1986 resolution, as the ECFR quotes it, says it is enough to add 2 min (Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib, Isha) and subtract 2 (Fajr, sunrise); its Arabic original was not found (2026-10-12) | Fiqh Council 1986 and 2009 (below) | |

**Asr factor.** Factor 1 is used by IGGÖ, Diyanet, the Grande Mosquée de Paris, the Shoura, Bosnia, KMSH and DUM RF. The London Unified Timetable and IRN publish both factors, and Birmingham Central Mosque and DUM RT (Tatarstan, so `ru_dum_rt`'s default) use the Hanafi factor 2.

**Rounding.** Every published table rounds to the whole minute, and none states its rounding rule.

**Horizon.** Sunrise and sunset are at −0.833°. No European body examined states a horizon-dip correction; INM is the exception.

**Fiqh basis for the summer rules**

- MWL Fiqh Council, 1986 (9th session, Rajab 1406), endorsed by the ECFR: 45–48° follow the signs; 48–66° give Isha and Fajr the share of the night they have at the nearest place where the signs are clear, and the Council proposes 45° (if Isha begins after a third of the night there, it begins after a third of the local night, "and the same for Fajr"); above 66° use the 45° pattern. It does not define the night; ICOP's 2009 paper, which evaluates the rule, measures the night from sunset to sunrise, and a night ending at Fajr could not place Fajr by a share (Q20).
- MWL Fiqh Council with ICOP, 2009: *Local Relative Estimation* for 48.6–66.6°. On nights without the sign, Isha = sunset + R·night and Fajr = sunrise − R·night, where the night runs from sunset to sunrise («الفترة من الغروب إلى الشروق», Q20). R is the place's average Isha share of the night over the stable nights that have one. The estimate is entered and left at 5 minutes a day. MWL distributed tables made this way for 19 European countries. Its 2010 tables for 16 UK cities confirm the night: on every estimated night Isha and Fajr take the same share of it (0.181–0.183 by city, 0.186 on Plymouth's 2 nights; Fajr's share gives Isha within 1 min), with R taken from an 18° Isha, not 17° (London 0.182).
- Diyanet and ECFR, Istanbul 2021: 18°/17°, estimation from the most recent day the sign was visible, a cap at one third of the night for hardship, and at least 5 h of day and of night.
- UK, Bradford 1983: 18° with the nearest day for May–July.

### 7.3 High-latitude rules compared

The table gives Fajr/Isha with MWL 18°/17° in 2026, local time, from the Europe research prototype. "+1" means after midnight. The "largest daily jump" rows show the largest change of the time from one day to the next.

| Place, date | Angle only | **Angle-based rule (every night; D5)** | One-seventh, absent nights only, no ramp | Middle of the night, absent nights only | Nearest day | LRE with ramp |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| London 15 May | 02:07 / 23:31 | **02:37 / 23:08** | 02:07 / 23:31 | 02:07 / 23:31 | 02:07 / 23:31 | 02:07 / 23:31 |
| London 21 Jun | — / — | **02:31 / 23:27** | 03:40 / 22:25 | 01:02 / 01:02+1 | 01:21 / 00:46+1 | 03:24 / 22:49 |
| London, largest daily jump | | **4 min** | 151 min | 23 min | 13 min | 13 min |
| Paris 21 Jun | — / 01:07+1 | **03:26 / 00:11+1** | 04:40 / 01:07+1 | 01:52 / 01:07+1 | 02:07 / 01:07+1 | 02:53 / 01:07+1 |
| Berlin 21 Jun | — / — | **02:34 / 23:35** | 03:42 / 22:35 | 01:08 / 01:08+1 | 01:30 / 00:48+1 | 03:27 / 22:50 |
| Oslo 21 Jun | — / — | **02:21 / 00:12+1** | 03:09 / 23:28 | 01:19 / 01:19+1 | 01:38 / 01:01+1 | 02:56 / 23:42 |
| Oslo, largest daily jump | | **6 min** | 161 min | 33 min | 16 min | 16 min |

What the table shows:

- **Applied only on absent nights, a portion rule jumps.** On the first night without the sign, the real time was near solar midnight. One-seventh of the night, or LRE without its ramp, moves Fajr by 2–2.7 h overnight, and back at the end of the season.
- **The angle-based rule is smooth, but it changes real times.** It bounds Fajr and Isha every night, but only binds from about 45°N at the June solstice (about 44.8°N for an 18° Fajr, 45.3°N for a 17° Isha), so never at Arab latitudes. In 2026 it moves 32 Fajr and 40 Isha times in London that the sun does define: on 15 May, Fajr moves from 02:07 to 02:37. Someone ending the suhoor at 18° would eat for 30 minutes after dawn.
- **Middle of the night and nearest day** jump less, but they put Fajr and Isha within minutes of each other for weeks. Middle of the night makes Isha(D) and Fajr(D+1) the same instant.
- **LRE with its ramp** never touches a real time and never jumps.
  - Where the gap season is long, it settles on R·night (Oslo, and London's Fajr).
  - Where the season is short (Paris's Fajr, London's Isha), the times follow the two 5-minute ramps and never reach R·night. Paris's 02:53 Fajr is pure ramp.
  - It costs one 365-day scan per place and year, cached.
- If one-seventh were applied every night, it would move Tunis Fajr 32 minutes later on 21 June. Every rule must therefore be gated as the method says.
- **What mosques publish** (the owner's dataset, 5.6): no calendar north of 45°N uses LRE; the angle-based rule, every night, is the most common exact match. So D5 is the angle-based rule, and LRE is an option.

### 7.4 High-latitude and polar rules in the engine

**High-latitude rules** (`HighLatitude`, step 6 of 6.2). Each rule has a gate. *Absent nights* rules act only where the angle is not reached, and are entered and left with the same 5-minute-a-day ramp from the last and next real times, so none of them jumps; `apbif`'s middle of the night is the one exception, unramped as APBIF's own tables are (7.5). *Every night* rules bound every night; the method sheet says they can move real times.

| Rule | Isha | Fajr | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Local Relative Estimation (an option) | sunset + R·night | sunrise − R·night | absent nights, ramped |
| One-seventh | sunset + night/7 | sunrise − night/7 | absent nights, ramped |
| Middle of the night | sunset + night/2 | sunrise − night/2 | absent nights, ramped (unramped for `apbif`, 7.5) |
| Nearest day | the nearest day with the sign (earlier first), its fraction of the night; or the last day's clock time (Bradford) | same | absent nights, ramped |
| Angle-based (angle/60; the default, D5) | min(angle time, sunset + α/60·night) | max(angle time, sunrise − α/60·night) | every night; absent nights only, ramped, for `mdf_12` and `luxembourg_shoura` (D5) |
| Clock caps (the Netherlands; Belgium) | earlier of the angle time and 23:30 (Belgium about 23:30, with the 18° Isha at 45°N near the solstice) | later of the angle time and 03:30 (Belgium about 03:24) | every night |
| Moonsighting seasonal | earlier of 18° (one-seventh from 55°) and the seasonal curve | later of the two | every night (part of the method; Fajr only in `gmp`) |
| Diyanet takdir | Maghrib + a third of the sharia night, from 21 June with 20-minute transitions, the share kept where the 18° Fajr never forms (README H6) | sunrise − 18/16 of that third, the same way | from 44.375°N, north only (part of the method) |
| DUM RF (`dum_rf`) | min(15° time, sunset + r·night): r = 0.30 from 1 to 30 June and 0.30 − n/240 in the n-th block of 3 days before 1 June or after 30 June, never below 0.25 (DUM RF's 2026 ramp) | max(18° time, sunrise − 0.30·night) | every night (part of the method) |
| DUM RT (`ru_dum_rt`) | sunset + 1 h 30 | sunrise − 2 h | nights when the sun stays above −18°, for Isha too (part of the method, as DUM RT's table notes say) |
| Summer form (`hr_miz_zagreb`, `si_islamska_skupnost`) | `si_islamska_skupnost`: earlier of the angle time and Maghrib + 90 (`hr_miz_zagreb`'s Isha is Maghrib + 80 all year) | later of the angle time and sunrise − 105, so never before the angle time (at Zagreb and Ljubljana in the window always sunrise − 105) | every night between two dates fitted in M3: Zagreb late March to late August; Slovenia 24 April to 23 May until vaktija.si's summer months confirm or replace them (8.8) (part of the method) |

- **R** is the mean of (Isha − sunset)/night over the place's stable nights with a real Isha in that year. A night is unstable if it differs by more than 10 minutes from a neighbouring night; unstable nights are left out of R but keep their real times. R is about 0.18 in Paris, London, Berlin and Oslo. It is cached per place, method and year.
- **Moonsighting seasonal.** The minutes are piecewise linear over dyy, the number of days since the winter solstice (dyy = day of year + 10 in the north and day of year − 172, or − 173 in a leap year, in the south, each wrapped into the year as adhan does; the south serves `mcw`, `london_unified` and `gmp` picked there, and E19's curve under a custom method, 7.7), with breakpoints at 0, 91, 137, 183, 229 and 275. The four values are base + k·|φ|/55:
  - Fajr: base 75, k = 28.65, 19.44, 32.74, 48.10.
  - Isha "general": base 75, k = 25.60, 2.05, −9.21, 6.14.
  - Isha "ahmer": base 62, k = 17.40, −7.16, 5.12, 19.44.
  - Isha "abyad": base 75, k = 25.60, 7.16, 36.84, 81.84.
  - Source: adhan-js 4.4.6 `Astronomical.ts` (MIT).
- **Diyanet takdir.** Re-derived by us from Diyanet's own 2027 tables used as test data (2026-10-16): the 2021 Prayer Times Congress's criteria, as README H6 and `fits/diyanet_takdir` give them, which replaced the first version's caps and k_F(φ), k_I(φ) lines fitted on IGGÖ's tables. A third-party fit (meypod's adhan-kotlin fork, MIT) served only as a cross-check of that first version.
- **DUM RF** (`dum_rf`, Q22). Re-derived from DUM RF's monthly Moscow tables. Fajr follows the angle-based rule (0.30 of the night) every night. Isha's fraction is 0.30 in June and 0.25 from 3 August to 28 April. Between them DUM RF ramps it by hand, differently each year: in 2026 in 3-day steps of 1/240 from 29 April to 2 August, in July 2025 daily, reaching 0.25 about 29 July. The preset uses the 2026 ramp, the current sheets. In Moscow it moves the real 18° Fajr on 25 nights and the real 15° Isha on 41, and puts June's Isha about 20 min after the plain 0.25 cap. It meets the 2026 sheets and June 2025 (154 days) within 2 min on all six times, and July 2025's Isha within 3, with the rounding the AM2 fit picks (9.4); the tables suggest Fajr and sunrise up, Maghrib and Isha down. Each new summer sheet is checked against it.

**Polar rules** (steps 2 and 3, D6). A web research on 2026-10-11 read what mosques above about 65.7°N publish; this follows it.

- *The 5-hour minimum day and night*, from the Istanbul 2021 declaration of Diyanet and the ECFR, is the default. Sunrise is no later than Dhuhr − 2:30 and Maghrib no earlier than Dhuhr + 2:30; in summer Maghrib is no later than Dhuhr + 9:30 and sunrise no earlier than Dhuhr − 9:30, so neither the day nor the night is ever shorter than 5 h. For every preset but Diyanet's it acts only at places whose year has at least one day without a sunrise, a sunset or an Asr: a 365-day scan, cached like R, from about 65.7°N (Kemi, Haparanda, Rovaniemi, Murmansk, Húsavík). So Luleå, Oulu, Reykjavik and Akureyri keep the real times their mosques publish (islam.nu, PSIY, Félag Múslima). The method's twilight rule then runs on the clamped night, and every substituted time is marked «تقديري».
- *Asr* when the noon sun stays at or below the horizon: the midpoint between Dhuhr and Maghrib, as northern Norway's tables do (owner).
- *The Diyanet presets* apply Diyanet's own clamp wherever the day or night is shorter than 5 h, with no gate, centred on its published Dhuhr (noon + 5), and set Asr = Dhuhr when there is no Asr shadow, as Diyanet's Nordic tables do (Umeå, Luleå, Kiruna, Rovaniemi, Reykjavik). This is the walk's tie of the event order (6.2), which holds any method's Asr at its Dhuhr when that Dhuhr would pass it, so Diyanet's Asr + 4 never comes before its Dhuhr + 5 either.
- *`irn`* is the default in all of Norway and on Svalbard (owner rule, 2026-10-11). It copies Islamic Council Norway's tables (bonnetid.info: one for each of the 357 municipalities and Svalbard, the master tables of its national prayer-time project), which the Tromsø mosque Alnor republishes for 23 northern towns. IRN splits Norway into 3 zones by latitude; each entry takes its zone from its own point.
  - *South of 63.56°N* (IRN's limit for 3°, 255 entries): Fajr 16°, Isha 15°, Dhuhr + 5 or + 10 and Maghrib + 4/5 all year, with a summer rule. Isha leaves 15° once the 15° time passes about 20:00 UTC (Kristiansand 1 April, Oslo 31 March, Bergen and Trondheim 25 March, before the clock change) and moves linearly in UTC, across the clock change, to Maghrib + 40 on 21 June, never earlier than Maghrib + 40, then back the same way to the 15° time (9–16 September). Fajr keeps 16° until the 16° time is about 2½ h before sunrise (151–155 min in the tables: Kristiansand 21 April, Oslo 13 April, Trondheim 31 March), then keeps that day's clock time, never later than sunrise − 60; from 21 June it keeps the clock time of the first 16° Fajr after the season instead (Kristiansand 03:30, then 03:20; Oslo 03:37, then 03:17). Both seasons are symmetric about 20 June (their day numbers sum to 340–342). Given these switch days, the 2026 tables' Fajr is exact and Isha within 1 min (78–94 % exact) at Kristiansand, Oslo, Bergen and Trondheim. M2 fits the switch rule on all 255 southern tables with the AM2 harness; for each entry the fit reads the table's first and last day on the summer rule for Isha (off 15°) and for Fajr (held at a clock time); where the rule misses an event's days, the entry stores that event's two days in its zone row (`switchDays`, 6.5, both ends included), and its other event keeps the rule's days. The held Fajr is the last 16° Fajr before the switch, so a switch day one day off moves it by about 5 min for the 5–8 weeks it is held.
  - *From 63.56°N* (Hitra, Steinkjer and Vefsn among the 36 entries up to 66.33°N, and the 67 to the north): in the polar seasons it places Mecca's day on the local solar noon: Fajr and Isha at Mecca's 18° offsets, Maghrib at Mecca's sunset offset + 4 or 5 min, sunrise at Mecca's sunrise offset, Dhuhr at noon + 5 (about 23 September to 20 March) or + 10. Svalbard uses this all year. Otherwise Fajr is at 16° and Isha at 15°, capped at solar midnight ∓ 3 h, and Maghrib at sunset + 4 or 5. The switches are fitted from the tables, as the owner chose: towards the midnight sun once the real sunset is later than about noon + 8h05 (Maghrib first, then Fajr and Isha, about 7 days each); towards the polar night over about 4 weeks from a sunset about noon + 3h20; Bodø, Rana and the entries south of 66.33°N (no polar night; Vefsn 124–125 min after noon in December) keep a Maghrib floor of about noon + 2 h instead. Asr follows the midpoint rule in the dark season. In Tromsø this gives Fajr 04:38, Maghrib 19:35 and Isha 20:55 on 21 June 2026, and Fajr 04:59, Maghrib 17:13 and Isha 18:27 on 21 December, as IRN publishes.
- Nearest latitude and the 45° parallel are dropped: no publisher uses them, and nearest latitude gives Tromsø a 53-minute day on 21 December and a 95-minute night on 21 June. Russia, Iceland and Greenland publish no polar rule, so the default applies there.

The owner left these to the plan's best judgement (2026-10-11). Each territory keeps its own country code, catalogue rows and default (D2, 6.4, 6.8), so France's list never reaches the tropics. Ramadan and Eid stay Tunisian, as everywhere abroad (A10, A11): nothing below adds a Ramadan rule, an imsak or an Eid time, even where the source is a Ramadan table.

### 7.7 Mainland France: the validity envelope, and what French mosques and other apps show

The owner asked on 2026-10-12 for France's times to be scientifically and religiously sound and as close as possible to the user's nearby mosques, since the French list of D4 matched only about a quarter of French mosques. The owner then set the limits of the answer (2026-10-12): no mosques are bundled in the app, because that cannot scale to the whole world, and because mosque administrators often set times without the astronomy and fiqh a correct time needs. So France keeps the list of D4, every start a method gives in France stays inside a validity envelope (E19), and a user matches their mosque with the bounded editor «مخصّصة» and the minute steppers (6.11).

**What the dataset shows.** The owner's dataset (5.6) is offline evidence only: never a source, never bundled (owner, 2026-10-12). Each record has 366 month/day rows but no year, method, source date, elevation or adhan/iqama field, so every figure below reads it as 2026 (29 February dropped) and computes standard sunrise and sunset (the sun's centre at −0.833°, E2) at each record's listed point. Of its 1,679 French records, 6 are in Mayotte (7.6; a seventh Mayotte record is filed under the Comoros, 5.6) and 1,673 in mainland France. Two analyses measured the mainland records with different populations, clocks and criteria, so their figures are not interchangeable; each figure below names its analysis.

- **The plan's analysis** (2026-10-12; scripts and results in the session scratchpad, `france/local`; the editor's reach below has its own population): 1,669 records, those with at least 200 days on which all six times lie within 150 min of a reference time. The four left out are a record whose sunrise column holds placeholders, one whose Dhuhr column is defective, one marked closed and one severely defective; two misfiled points (one filed at 0/0) were moved to the town their calendar fits. Every record is read as Paris clock time. A day with any time more than 150 min from its reference drops out of that record's count instead of counting as a miss.
- **The independent review's analysis** (2026-10-13; `docs/prayer-times-beyond-tunisia-review.md`, 12.3–12.7, with its per-record files, scripts, `summary.json`, `quality/summary.md` and `sources/RESEARCH-NOTES.md` in the git-ignored `work/france-prayer-review/`): 1,668 records for Fajr and Maghrib, 608,820 mosque-days. Left out: the record filed at 0/0, one with an ambiguous clock, one incomplete, the closed one and the severely defective one. The 14 records filed as UTC or Africa/Algiers whose clocks follow Paris are read as Paris time and flagged as inferred; the one calendar genuinely kept in UTC stays in UTC. A record fits when one fixed annual rule is within 5 min of it (2 min also reported) on at least 329 of the 365 days, a missing cell failing its day.
- The two populations share 1,666 records. The review's 1,668 hold 1,544 distinct calendars: neither analysis counts independent observations, and neither is a census of French mosques.

**Following nearby mosques** (the plan's analysis; `france/local/l1_loo.py`, `l3_report.py`). Each of the 1,669 records was predicted without its own calendar: by the French list, or from the other records within a radius of 3 to 30 km (identical calendars within 0.3 km of it dropped; a record with no neighbour in range takes the list's fallback), each neighbour's times moved to the record's point by keeping their solar phase (angle, shadow factor or minutes from the event). A record counts as matched when all six rounded predictions are within 5 min of its listing on at least 90 % of its scored days (the days with all six times present).

| Default or choice | Records matched (of 1,669) | Isha, median error |
|---|---|---|
| The French list (D4), chooser dismissed (`mdf_12`; `rhone_ctir` in 69, `diyanet_eu` in 67) | 12 % | 16 min |
| The French list, its best entry for each mosque | 19 % | 9 min |
| The timetable of the nearest other mosque | 23 % | 8 min |
| The most representative of the 10 nearest mosques within 20 km | 26 % | 6 min |
| The best of the 3 most representative nearby practices | 32 % | 5 min |

- Neighbouring mosques differ. Almost all add about + 5 min to Dhuhr and + 3 to Maghrib, but their Fajr and Isha vary from one mosque to the next, and in Île-de-France (348 of the 1,669 records) no automatic default matches more than 6 %, the list's best entry 9 % and the best of three nearby practices 11 %. Even a default built from bundled mosques would leave three mosques in four unmatched.
- **Fajr and Maghrib, in four outcomes** (review 12.1). The listings are sorted into four outcomes kept apart: matches a tested start rule; does not match those rules; possible delayed adhan or iqama; potential prayer-window or calendar error. Only the last calls for an error check, and even it stays a flag to verify at the source (the review's `REPEATED_PRAYER_WINDOW_FLAG_VERIFY_SOURCE`) until the year, clock, place and meaning of the field are confirmed and the local horizon checked. A mismatch with a start rule is no verdict on a prayer: the database cannot tell a start time from an adhan or an iqama, Fajr prayed after dawn is valid until sunrise, and a Maghrib held a steady few minutes after sunset is a delay. The app shows start times, so what it shows stays inside E19 whatever a mosque lists.
  - **Matches a tested start rule** (review). Of the 1,668 records, 463 (27.8 %) fit one of the two French Fajr procedures checked at their sources: 12° with no added minutes (459) or APBIF's 18° + 6 min with solar midnight on nights without 18° (4). 846 (50.7 %) fit the broader set (12°, 13°, 15° or 18° where the sun reaches it, one fixed estimate on nights where it does not, or APBIF's), and 1,066 (63.9 %) if every-night caps are allowed too. For Maghrib, 1,654 (99.2 %) fit sunset plus one fixed 0–10 min, and 844 (50.6 %) pass both. 184 records fit two or more angle families, so a fit does not identify a mosque's method. All six times together, against 26,136 fixed configurations: 270 (16.2 %).
  - **Does not match those rules.** The review leaves 822 Fajr calendars outside its broader set (the median of their median differences is 4 min, of their 95th percentiles 28.4 min: a small median can hide a mismatched summer) and 14 Maghrib calendars outside the offset screen. Read as the start of the fast, a Fajr later than the 12° instant, the latest start any French body uses, is too late under every recognised position, while the prayer itself stays valid until sunrise. The two analyses agree on how many: more than 5 min later on at least 10 days, 336 records in the review and 335 in the plan's arrays; more than 10 min, 244 and 243; more than 20 min, 172 in both. The plan's former 13.6 % (227) and 8.3 % (138) were the same count without its 110 congregation-time listings (next item); their late days fall almost all in May–August (summer caps, sunrise − N, Diyanet's takdir; median worst day 15.6 and 22.2 min). Earlier than the 18° instant, the review finds 12 records by more than 5 min on at least 10 dates when the sun reaches 18° (median worst day 39.0 min, at most 60.8); the plan finds 17 (1.0 %) by more than 3 min on at least 10 days, also comparing nights without 18° with solar midnight (`france/local/l12_fajr_maghrib.py`). Of the plan's 17, 7 are at worst 4–15 min early, from a deeper angle or a fixed sunrise − 107 or − 120 that comes before 18° outside summer (the sun at most 20.5° down): they do not match, and no more. The other 10 are clock or calendar faults: the UTC calendar read as Paris time, the ambiguous clock, and eight calendars whose early days break from the rest of their own year, by 27–61 min: four of the review's five early-Maghrib calendars and four more (fourth outcome).
  - **Possible delayed adhan or iqama.** The plan counts 110 records (6.6 %; 69 of them in its Diyanet family of calendars) whose Fajr is after the 12° instant on more than half of their scored days; for 90 % of them the median day lies between sunrise − 75 and − 35 min. The review flags 56 of these 110 as steady late-Fajr patterns that fit a delayed adhan, an iqama or a stale setting, which the database cannot tell apart; no record is called an iqama from its pattern alone. Two of the review's 14 Maghrib non-fits hold a steady delay, about sunset + 16 and sunset + 22 to + 30, which fits a delayed adhan or iqama too. The plan's former "sunrise − 37 to 72 min all year" is withdrawn: it summarised each record by its median day, and 8 of the 110 hold one summer clock that falls after sunrise (next item).
  - **Potential prayer-window or calendar error.** The review flags 20 records (1.2 %) more than 5 min after the computed sunrise, or before the computed sunset, on at least 10 days. 15 hold Fajr at one clock time all summer (05:48 to 07:00; four of them all or nearly all year), which falls after sunrise on 45–169 days, from May to August and for some from March to September, their worst day 16–94 min after sunrise; once the year, clock, place and field are confirmed, those days cannot serve as an ordinary Fajr schedule, whatever the field means. 5 list Maghrib before sunset: four about an hour early in blocks (three from the October clock change to 31 December, 68 days, and one through March, 31 days), and one early on 238 days by up to 29 min, whose months outside April and May fit a place about 3.5° of latitude further south. One of the five still passes the annual Maghrib fit, so the daily window check never depends on a fit score. 47 records (2.8 %) have at least one such day (1,611 Fajr days after sunrise, 528 Maghrib days before sunset); the 27 beyond the 20 have fewer than 10 each. The review's window check tests only sunrise and sunset; the plan's recomputation (2026-10-13) adds 4 records whose Fajr falls, on a block of 28–31 days mostly within one month, with the sun more than 20° and up to 28° below the horizon, deeper than any body puts dawn (20° at most, 6.11). So the outcome holds 24 records (1.4 %). The data cannot say whether a block comes from the mosque's own table, from the collection or from a table of another year. On the plan's arrays the same 15 Fajr and 5 Maghrib records stand out, plus two the plan misread: the UTC calendar read as Paris time (Maghrib 1–2 h early all year) and the ambiguous clock the review leaves out. The plan had reported these 7 as Maghrib "data errors" and "entry mistakes in the mosques' systems", had not tested Fajr against sunrise, and its 150-min filter had dropped 909 of the 15 records' 1,608 after-sunrise days (keeping 20–80 of each one's 45–169).
  - **Near sunset.** In the plan's count, 32 records (1.9 %) list Maghrib more than 1 min before sunset on at least 10 days. The 25 besides the 7 above are mostly 1–3 min early, within the uncertainty of a sunset computed without the place's elevation and horizon, so neither analysis counts them as errors (the review screens at 5 min).
  - **In all**, in the plan's count, 322 records (19.3 %) have, on at least 10 days, a Fajr more than 2 min after the 12° instant or more than 3 min before the 18° instant (solar midnight on nights without it), or a Maghrib more than 1 min before sunset, besides the 110 congregation-time listings. That figure mixes the four outcomes and two misread clocks: it measures disagreement with the conventions E19 uses, not wrong timetables, which are at most the 24 records of the fourth outcome. Under the observations rather than the conventions, 12° itself comes after the observed dawn on every observed morning (OpenFajr, at Birmingham, 52.4°N: 12.3–15.0°, and 13.6–15.0° in April and August–September; its authors caution against carrying its angles to other places), a dispute between scholars, not an error.
- **What the bounded editor reaches without bundling a mosque** (the plan's analysis; session scratchpad `coverage`, `c1_editor.py` and `c2_report.py`). Population: the dataset's 17,627 records with at least 300 scored days, 1,673 of them French, Mayotte's 6 and the record filed at 0/0 included, each record on its own zone label (so the 14 Paris clocks filed as UTC or Africa/Algiers count as misses), with Fajr fitted from 12° to 20° everywhere although mainland France allows 12°–18° (E19; 2 French matches rest on a deeper angle); each of these choices moves the French figures by under 1 point. Each of the six times is fitted on its own over the same 365 days with the editor's inputs and ranges (6.11) and nearest rounding; a record is matched when all six are within the margin on at least 90 % of its days with all six present, a time more than 200 min from a reference being left out. All six within 5 min for 66 % of the records (81 % in the Arab countries, 62 % in Tunisia, 34 % in France, 43 % elsewhere in Europe), within 2 min for 40 %. With the minute steppers on top, 75 % within 5 min and 72 % within 2 (France: 61 % and 54 %). The settings are fitted on the year they are scored on, so these shares say what a user who copies a timetable can reach, not how a setting chosen without it would do. The review's six-time bank of 26,136 fixed configurations (Fajr 12°, 15° or 18° and Isha 12°, 15°, 17° or 18°, estimated only on nights without the angle; Asr factor 1 or 2; Dhuhr 2–10 min after transit, Maghrib 0–10 min after sunset, sunrise 0–10 min early), with a missing cell failing its day, fits 270 of its 1,668 records (16.2 %) within 5 min on at least 329 days and 64 (3.8 %) within 2. The gap comes mainly from the editor's wider ranges (Fajr at any half degree from 12° to 20° or sunrise − 60 to − 120, Isha from 12° to 18° or Maghrib + 60 to + 120, every-night rules, the «العشاء ليس قبل» floor) and partly from cells the plan's count drops but the review's fails, not from a disagreement. What the editor misses is mostly European: Fajr and Isha set by clock times, summer caps and hand-edited days; Asr margins, which only the steppers add; and a Dhuhr at transit or + 1, below the editor's zawal floor of + 2.

**The validity envelope (E19).** The app shows computed starts: the start of each prayer's time, Fajr's also serving as the fast's cutoff (no imsak is shown abroad, A14), and sunrise, which ends Fajr's time. A mosque's adhan and iqama are other quantities, often set later, and a prayer at a later congregation can be valid: Fajr's time runs until sunrise, and Maghrib's does not end at sunset + 10 (below). E19 bounds the computed start, not the mosque's practice. Every start a method gives at a mainland French place stays inside these bounds, whatever the method (published tables, presets and custom methods alike), and so does a custom method's anywhere (6.11); the user's steppers can still move Fajr or Isha past a twilight bound (6.2, step 9). Most bounds are lower bounds, where an earlier start would come before the prayer's time; the only upper bounds are on Fajr, whose start a fasting user reads as the cutoff, and on sunrise. They come from a fiqh and science research of 2026-10-12, its main claims re-checked by a second agent (Appendix A); their reading as bounds on starts, and the removal of a Maghrib upper bound, follow the independent review of 2026-10-13 (Q38).

| Time | Bound | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Fajr | not before the 18° instant (solar midnight on nights without it) | the deepest angle of the bodies French mosques follow (MWL, Diyanet, the Istanbul 2021 congress, APBIF), and Tunisia's INM; no credible observation puts dawn deeper than about 17.8° |
| Fajr | not after the 12° instant (no upper bound on nights without it, which occur only beyond 54.56° of latitude, so only under a custom method, whose short-night rule's Fajr then stands, at or after the night's lowest point; 7.4, 6.11): the start a fasting user reads as the cutoff | the latest angle any body uses (Musulmans de France since 1994, a ruling made for ease, not from observation, by Havre de Savoir's account of 2026); every observation puts dawn before it (OpenFajr, Birmingham: 12.3–15.0°; the CTIR at Miribel: 14.0°; NRIAG, Egypt: 13.7–14.6°); for a Maliki, eating while dawn is in doubt costs a make-up day in an obligatory fast (the Mudawwana, as Dar al-Ifta's fatwa 7638 reports it; the fatwa itself follows the majority, who require none); a congregation set later can still be a valid Fajr prayer, whose time runs to sunrise, but shown as the start it would let a fasting user eat after dawn under every recognised position |
| Sunrise | not after the computed sunrise | not a start but the end of Fajr's time: a later sunrise would let a user pray Fajr after it |
| Dhuhr | not before transit + 1.2 min | the zawal is complete once the sun's trailing edge has crossed the meridian |
| Asr | not before the factor-1 shadow | the start for the majority, the Maliki included; factor 2 is later and valid |
| Maghrib | not before sunset + 1; no upper bound | Maghrib begins at sunset. Its time does not end at sunset + 10: Diyanet's ruling puts the end at the twilight's disappearance (the red for most schools, the white for Abu Hanifa), and the Maliki school measures Maghrib's preferred time by the time needed to pray it once its conditions are met (Khalil's Mukhtasar), not in minutes. A later Maghrib is a delayed start, not an invalid one, so E19 leaves it (review of 2026-10-13, Q38); no entry of the French list (D4) goes beyond + 7 (Diyanet), and the editor's input stops at + 10 (6.11) |
| Isha | not before the 12° instant; for a rule that estimates Isha on short nights (an interval such as the Grande Mosquée de Paris's Maghrib + 87, Diyanet's takdir, a cap), not before the Moonsighting Committee's «Shafaq Ahmer» curve (sunset + 56 to 79 min at Paris) | Isha begins when the red twilight has gone (the majority, the Maliki included); 12° is the shallowest angle a French body states for it; the curve is the only published model fitted to observations; the Istanbul 2021 congress, attended by participants from the «Muslim Council of France» (probably the CFCM) and «the Paris Mosque», allows estimation in exceptional hardship, such as an Isha or Fajr that begins after one third of the night |

- A time that crosses a bound moves to the bound, rounded inward (up from a lower bound, down from an upper one), and is marked «تقديري», with the bound named in `explain()`. An Isha between the curve and the 12° instant is marked «تقديري» too.
- The 18° floor and the bounds set by the sun's disk (sunrise, Dhuhr, Asr and Maghrib) keep a time within 1 min of them, since published calendars differ by that much in rounding and sun model. Without the allowance the envelope would move 80 % of mosque-days, mostly by a minute; with it, a time moves by more than 1 min on 17 % of mosque-days. The 12° Fajr bound and the Isha floor have no allowance. A Dhuhr at the bare transit lies 1.2 min before its bound, beyond the allowance, so a method that adds no Dhuhr minutes (`mdf_12`, `isna` and `mwl` in the French list, 6.4) shows Dhuhr at transit + 1.2, rounded up and marked «تقديري», every day at a mainland French place.
- E19 runs after the `ishaLatest` cap and before the user's adjustments (6.2, step 8), which stay the user's own, as `ishaEarliest` does, except for the window check of step 9 (6.2).
- The dataset's 2026 timetables often differ from these starts; mosque timetables are not a source (owner, 2026-10-12). Applied to a mosque's listed times, E19 would move one by more than 2 min on 12 % of mosque-days and by more than 5 min on 8 % (recomputed on 2026-10-13 without the former Maghrib upper bound: 11.9 % and 8.0 %, against 12.2 % and 8.2 % with it; the 17 % and 80 % above do not change). Mostly a Fajr listed after 12° in June and July (316 mosques on some summer days: 12° calendars with the summer cap, sunrise − 90, Diyanet's takdir, which moves Diyanet's Strasbourg Fajr up to 6 min past the 12° instant) and the congregation-time listings (above). Isha before the curve: 7 % of mosques on 10 days or more, by a median 3 min. Isha marked «تقديري» before 12°: 34 % of mosques, from mid-May to late July (5 % of all mosque-days).
- In the four outcomes of the dataset analysis above (review 12.1), such listings are mismatches with the tested start rules or possible delayed adhans or iqamas, not errors: a mosque may pray later, but the app shows the start. Only a potential prayer-window or calendar error is treated as an error, once the year, the clock, the place and the field's meaning are confirmed; an annual fit score never hides such a day, and none of the four outcomes changes what the app shows.
- A separate imsak would bridge the gap between the latest start of the prayer and the earliest credible dawn, but it is a Ramadan feature, and none ships abroad (A11, A14). So one shown Fajr serves both the prayer and the fast, and no single time is safe for both under every view: an earlier Fajr is a precaution for the fast but can come before dawn for the prayer under a shallower view. The 18° floor and the 12° bound keep the shown Fajr within the span of the recognised positions; where it falls in that span is the method's choice (D4, 6.11).
- Elsewhere E19 checks only custom methods (6.11); the rest of Europe and overseas France keep their methods as they are.

**What other apps do (2026-10-12).** Two research agents read the apps most used in France, and a third re-checked their main claims (Appendix A). Each app's default was run through the test above on the dataset:

| App or service | Default in France | Mosque times | Mosques matched (all six within 5 min) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Google Search's card | «Musulmans de France» 12°/12°, no offsets, by the country of the searched place | no | 10 % |
| Aladhan API | the method whose reference point is nearest: UOIF 12° for most of the mainland, MWL on the Channel coast, Algeria's at Perpignan, Tunisia's in Corsica; angle-based rule | no | 10 % (as UOIF) |
| Apps built on the adhan libraries, set to 12° | Swift and Kotlin: one-seventh of the night above 48°N and middle of the night below, both as limits | no | 8 % |
| Muslim Pro (about 557,000 French App Store ratings) | its «UOIF» 12° convention, with about Fajr − 5, Dhuhr + 5, Isha + 5 and Asr − 1 to − 2, and sunrise and Maghrib moved 1–4 min each way by town (an elevation-like horizon); in some cities a pre-calculated table under the same label, source not stated (Toulouse: about 15°/15°, Maghrib + 5) | pre-calculated tables in some cities | 22 % (its computed times, approximated) |
| IslamicFinder and its Athan app | UOIF 12°/12°, with small offsets of its own | no | not measured |
| Mawaqit (about 237,000 French ratings) | each mosque's administrator sets or uploads its yearly calendar (no method or angle fields), and users follow mosques; times calculated from the user's location are also offered, with «Muslim of France (ex UOIF)» among the methods, a high-latitude choice and minute adjustments | yes: the mosque's own table | each mosque's own |
| Masjidbox | the mosque's own calculation or uploaded table, one app per mosque | yes | each mosque's own |
| The Grande Mosquée de Paris's app | its own table, adjusted to the user's location | its own | not measured |
| This plan | the list of D4 by département, inside E19; «مخصّصة» within the recognised positions (6.11) | no | 12 % with the chooser dismissed, 19 % with the best entry |

- The general apps default to 12° with no tie to the user's mosque, which matches about one French mosque in ten; Muslim Pro's minutes raise that to about one in five. The apps that match mosques (Mawaqit, Masjidbox) show the table each mosque's administrator sets, with no check that it is valid.
- The most common French timetable (12°/12° with Fajr − 4, Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 3 and Isha + 5: 187 mosques in 66 départements, sunrise and Asr untouched) is no app's published default. It is minutes typed for each mosque in a display system, so it is not a method (Q32); a user reproduces it with «مخصّصة» and the minute steppers (6.11).
- Pitfalls the plan avoids:
  - Aladhan's nearest-method choice puts MWL, Algeria's or Tunisia's method in French border towns and in Corsica; the plan picks by the place's country (6.8).
  - adhan's one-seventh limit (Swift and Kotlin, strictly above 48°N) acts even where the angle is reached, jumps at 48°N and puts Paris's Fajr of 21 June at 04:40, 36 min after the 12° instant; E19 holds it at the 12° instant.
  - Muslim Pro serves a pre-calculated table in some cities (Toulouse: about 15°/15°) under the same «UOIF 12°» label; the plan names the method in the zone line and the method sheet.
- What French users of these apps get beyond the times: following one or more mosques, with their iqama and Jumu'a times and announcements, and a nudge to the nearest mosque when far from one's own (Mawaqit). The app does none of this, since it bundles no mosques (owner, 2026-10-12).
- Mawaqit's terms forbid reusing its data or API outside its own apps; the plan uses neither.

## 8. The Arab countries

Almost every Arab country has a state authority whose timetable the mosques follow, so the target is the mosque's minute. 8.1–8.6 are the facts and the evidence, and 8.7–8.8 the authority profiles and their zones.

### 8.1 What the authorities publish

Matches are exact-minute shares measured during the research. Misses are 1 minute unless stated.

| Country | Authority | What is published | Zones | Best measured match |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Saudi Arabia | KACST, Umm al-Qura calendar (ummulqura.org.sa; API `umqserv.kacst.gov.sa`) | Any coordinates, any year, JSON. Past years are recomputed with today's rule. | 173 official places with published coordinates (26 «city», 142 «governorate» and 5 «center» entries; the default within 25 km of one, 8.8); KACST also computes for any point | Fajr 98.1 %, sunrise 97.9 %, Dhuhr 98.2 %, Asr 98.6 %, Maghrib and Isha 98.4 %, 100 % within 1 minute (4,517 city-days of 2025–2027 in 13 cities, KACST's previous-date days left out; 97.8–98.2 % on the held-out 2025 and 2027 tables; Q4). Up to 2 minutes off on the previous-date days, about 17 a year (A8, 8.7). |
| Egypt | Egyptian General Authority for Survey (esa.gov.eg), endorsed by Dar al-Ifta fatwas 3012 (2015) and 4021 (2017) | Daily all-city table; monthly view of the current year (behind a form) | About 80 cities, coordinates unpublished | 178 of 198 exact on one day for 33 cities at approximate town coordinates (the other 20 within ±0.62 min); 41 of 42 for Cairo over 7 days with a fitted point; residuals −0.51 to +0.51 min on 7 cities (2025-08-13) and on Cairo's Ramadan 2024 (30 days). 3,694 of 3,738 times (98.8 %) exact on 8 days of 2025–26 (7 Wayback copies of the daily page and the 2026-10-08 copy; 78 cities at points fitted on these days, Q9), one sunrise 2 min off. Sunrise and Maghrib need a per-locality horizon: fitted on this month, with sunrise − 1 and Maghrib + 2, they give 85 and 86 of 88 on archived official tables of 2024 (Q5). |

### 8.2 Methods found

"Horizon" is the sun's altitude at sunrise and sunset. The standard is −0.833° (16′ semi-diameter + 34′ refraction) at sea level. "Ceil" and "floor" are the rounding of the time.

| Country | Fajr | Isha | Asr | Dhuhr | Maghrib | Sunrise, horizon | Rounding | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
