| Yemen | 18° | 16° | 1, noon shadow at the declination of 00:00 local time (E4) | transit + 10 | sunset + 6 (≤ 650 m) or + 10 (≥ 1,100 m) | refraction reduced by air pressure at the location's elevation: −(0.2667° + 0.5667°·e^(−h/8434 m)) | nearest | from tables (134 days from 2026-05-21; one rule throughout, whatever the endpoint's `season` field); Imsak (`amsek`) = Fajr on every day so far |
| Sudan, Somalia, Djibouti, Comoros | unknown | unknown | 1 likely (Shafi'i or Maliki majority); the Comoros' mosques: Maghrib − 168 (5.6) | — | — | — | — | no official table; the mosques' practice in the owner's dataset (5.6, A3): SD 18°/18°, SO and DJ 18°/17°, KM the Moroni table |
| Tunisia | INM | | | | | | | 100 % (unchanged) |

What the table shows:

- **The Fajr and Isha angles cluster on four pairs**: 18/17 (MWL: DZ, SY, IQ), 18/18 (JO, OM, UAE, Fadlallah), 19.5/17.5 (Egyptian: EG, MR, Gaza) and 18.5 with a 90-minute Isha (SA). The others are Morocco's 19/17, Kuwait's 18/17.5, Yemen's 18/16, Qatar's 18° with a 90-minute Isha, Libya's 18.35/18.35 (on a low-precision sun), Bahrain's 18/17.8 (Isha stated as 18°) and Dar al-Fatwa's 19.65/17.55 (on a perpetual table whose sun runs late, E5).
- **Fixed minutes are common**: Dhuhr + 1 to + 10, Maghrib + 1 to + 10, Asr + 1, + 3 or + 5 (the + 1 values are Qatar Calendar House's). Their purpose is never stated, except Bahrain's Dhuhr + 1.1, fitted to the SCIA's stated rule: the whole disc past the meridian (Q7).
- **Asr is shadow factor 1 everywhere.** No authority publishes a Hanafi (factor 2) table.
- **Elevation**: no authority dips Fajr or Isha; INM is the only one. Several lower the sunrise and sunset horizon, each in its own way.
- **No Ramadan change** found except Saudi Arabia (Isha) and Qatar (Maghrib). This was checked through Ramadan in the countries with year-round tables: the UAE, Egypt, Jordan, Oman, Iraq and Algeria. Libya's perpetual table has none: its row for 18 May 2018, in Ramadan, equals 18 May 2025. Syria and Mauritania publish only Ramadan tables, so it cannot be checked there; in Syria, the one mosque calendar that copies the 1447 tables keeps the same rule outside Ramadan, so no Ramadan change is assumed (Q12). Mauritania's one non-Ramadan figure, a static homepage block for about 8 July, has Maghrib at sunset + 0 but also an unshifted sunrise (the tables print it 1 min earlier), so it is a plain 19.5°/17.5° computation, not the ministry's rule; the imsakiya's rule is applied all year (Q6). Bahrain's 1448 calendar has none: its Ramadan rows fit the same rules (Q7). Kuwait's and Yemen's Ramadan rules are unknown.
- **Asr's noon shadow** is taken with the declination at transit in Qatar and Egypt (marked "noon-declination shadow" above; Egypt by Q9), which is the shared engine's own Asr definition (6.2), so it needs no extension. Yemen takes it at 00:00 local time of the civil date (E4 `LOCAL_MIDNIGHT_SHADOW`): 96.5 % exact, against 77.6 % at transit. Jordan, the UAE, Morocco and Iraq go further: the shadow is measured on the apparent, refracted sun (E13, 8.6, Q5; Iraq 95 % exact against 82 % on the geometric sun, Q8).

### 8.3 What makes an official table hard to reproduce

The INM fit (`scripts/prayer_formula/README.md`) showed that the last few percent come from conventions, not astronomy. The research found the same here:

| Convention | Where | Effect if ignored |
|---|---|---|
| Rounding direction per prayer | SA, DZ, SY, BH, LY (all six down), LB (Dhuhr and Asr down, Maghrib up), IQ (sunrise and Maghrib up), MA (Fajr), PS (Dhuhr) | About half of the minutes miss by 1 |
| When the sun is evaluated | OM: once per civil date at 0h UT. SA: declination at 21:00 UT of the civil date and equation of time at 09:00 UT for Fajr and Asr (Fajr 98.1 % against 56 % at its own instant, 2025–2027); sunrise, Dhuhr and Maghrib at the event instant (Q4). INM: its own pattern. (Iraq's former "6 h" quirk is its reference year, 2021: see perpetual tables.) YE: Asr's noon shadow at 00:00 local time (96.5 % against 77.6 % at transit). LY: once per civil date at 10:00 UT, noon in Libya, on the TEXTBOOK sun (E4, E14; Q14). | 2–40 % of minutes miss |
| A horizon other than −0.833° | UAE, JO, IQ, MA, MR (sunrise only), Fadlallah, YE | 0.5–8 min on sunrise and Maghrib (Morocco's Imilchil, about 2,150 m: − 8.3 and + 8.2; Yemen's highlands about 0.5) |
| Fixed minutes on some prayers | most countries | constant shifts |
| Zone reference points | Oman's zone points are truncated to whole arc-minutes, and Muscat's and Salalah's are their airports (the Muscat zone lies about 18′ west of the old city, 1.2 min). Bahrain uses two points. The UAE's Fajr and sunrise fit an eastern-edge point. | 1–2 min per zone |
| Perpetual tables | QA (reference year 2006), KW (1999), PS (a leap year such as 2012), LY (2006), IQ (2021; Fajr and Isha 365 of 365, against 300 and 298 computing 2026); MR's reprinted imsakiya (2023); LB's Beirut table (a year in 1903's leap phase) | 4–13 % (QA), about 10 % (LY), 14 % (IQ) to 20–26 % (KW) of times are 1 min off when the actual year is computed; in LB, Fajr and Isha are 2–3 min off on about a fifth of the days of 2026; MR, on its March–April dates, 4–22 % per prayer in 2026 and about 60 % of Fajr and sunrise times in a leap year |
| Rules that change on a date | QA Fajr − 1 from 2020-08-20; SY Maghrib + 2 from 2025-03-30, provisional (absent in the Ramadan 1446 tables, first official in 1447, Q12); QCH Dhuhr and Asr + 1 from mid-2025; MR 19.0°/17.0° in Ramadan 2022 only (Fajr 19.0° in all 13 capitals, reason unpublished; 19.5°/17.5° in 2021 and 2023–2026), and Maghrib standard sunset + 2 in 2021 (+ 3 from 2023); SA Fajr 19° before 1430 (December 2008; secondary only, not shipped, E9); UAE Jumu'a 13:15 from 2022 and 12:45 from 2026 | the wrong rule on past or future dates |
| Data quirks | KACST gives the previous civil date's times on 1 or 2 consecutive days early in each Hijri month (Hijri days 2 to 6, drifting by year; only the first is a literal duplicate), about 17 days a year (Q4). Habous rows of 13–19 Sep 2026 were already in GMT. Libya's Jalo and Awjila Asr is about 8 min late, on both days at hand (a zone offset, 8.7). Fadlallah's tables stay on UTC+3 from 2026-03-28 to 10-30, while the legal Beirut clock is on UTC+3 only from 03-29 to 10-24, so 7 rows are an hour off. Kuwait's sheets print a 29 February row whose sunrise is later than 28 February's (06:16 against 06:15), and a 1 March whose Fajr and sunrise equal 28 February's (04:56, 06:15; Q15). Qatar's Ministry API applies its Ramadan + 1 on its own Hijri labels, a day before Umm al-Qura's Ramadan in 1447 (2026-02-17 to 03-18) and 1448 (from 2027-02-07), so the fit counts those edge days as quirk days for `qa_awqaf` (Q3). | isolated misses |

### 8.4 Communities

- **Twelver Shia** (Iraq, Lebanon, Bahrain, Kuwait, Saudi Eastern Province). Primary rules from Sistani's rulings: Dhuhr at transit; Maghrib once the eastern redness has passed overhead (an obligatory precaution); Isha shares Maghrib's time until midnight, defined as the midpoint of **sunset and Fajr**. The Arab Shia timetables found use **18°** for Fajr (Karbala's al-Abbas shrine, the Fadlallah office), not the 16° of Qum's Leva Institute that apps call "Jafari". Maghrib is 4° below the horizon (Karbala, one day) or sunset + 13 minutes (Fadlallah's «غياب الحمرة»). Karbala's widget shows five rows: Fajr, sunrise, «الظهرين», «العشاءين», midnight. The app offers none of these methods (A4); this stays for reference.
- **Ibadi** (Q17). Ibadi law starts the prayers when the Maliki school does: Isha once the red twilight has gone, Fajr at true dawn (Sharh al-Nil, quoted by IslamQA 311155, 2019-09-03). In Oman (about 45 % of Omanis) the ministry publishes one table for everyone, and no Ibadi difference was found. No table was found for the Ibadi of the M'zab (Algeria) or of Jabal Nafusa and Zuwara (Libya), from an authority or from their own councils. In the owner's dataset, 11 mosques (7 calendars) of the M'zab hold Isha at Maghrib + 60 all year, 12–35 min (median 18) before `dz_marw`'s 17°: 9 in El Atteuf (Tajnint) and Bounoura, and one each in Ghardaia and Guerrara. No other Algerian calendar does. Their Fajr, Dhuhr (where not a fixed clock time) and Maghrib are within 1 minute of `dz_marw` on 87–93 % of days, their sunrise and Asr on 75–80 %. Most of the valley's other mosques fix Isha too: of its 49 mosques (with Berriane and Guerrara), 27 more hold it at Maghrib + 70 or + 75 (a median 5 min, and about 20 min in June, before 17°), as about 200 mosques elsewhere in Algeria do at + 75, and 20 hold Dhuhr at 13:00, 13:15 or 13:20. The dataset does not record a mosque's school. None of Djerba's 218 mosques fixes Isha. The dataset has no mosque in the Nafusa's Ibadi towns (Nalut, Jadu, Kabaw, Yefren) or in Zuwara. The unverified report of an Ibadi adhan 10 minutes or more before the Maliki one (IslamQA 311155, place and prayer not named) matches only the M'zab's Isha in the dataset (+ 60 against + 70 or + 75). **The app adds nothing for the Ibadi**: their places take the country's profile (`dz_marw`: Ghardaia's table, the Adrar reference with Ghardaia's derived offsets, which the M'zab's places take as their wilaya's listed place, with the validity guard (A16, 8.8); `ly_awqaf`: Zawiya's table, the nearest Awqaf city, with no reach limit, for Zuwara and the Nafusa towns, unless the validity guard gives a town the Awqaf formula at its own point, as it does Nalut (6.5, 8.8); the Awqaf row labelled «زلطن» is Zliten's table, its times fitting a point near 14.57 E, not Zaltan's), and 12 states the limit.
- **Zaydi** (Yemen's north). Sanaa's table applies one rule to Zaydi highlands and the Shafi'i coast alike. Its + 10 highland Maghrib follows terrain, not school: Taiz and Ibb get it too.
- **Hanafi** minorities. Every official table uses factor 1. The «وقت العصر» toggle (6.11) covers them.

### 8.5 A second, expert source: ICOP's Salat Calendar and Accurate Times

Salat Calendar (salatcalendar.com) is run by the International Astronomical Center (ICOP, astronomycenter.net) with Dubai's IACAD. Its help page says specialists in prayer times supervise it. It computes times for 239 countries and offers a calculation preset per country. Its help page (`/index.php/help`) says:

- **The country presets** use "the angle adopted in the official calendar" of each country, and likewise its official minute additions and its choice on elevation. No source document is named.
- **«تلقائي» (automatic)** applies the official choice of the place's country, "in some Islamic countries only". Where the site has no data for the country, the default is 18°/18° between latitudes 45°S and 45°N (18°/17° beyond), no added minutes, and elevation applied to sunrise and sunset (which the site recommends). Refraction is standard (10 °C, 1010 mbar). Its Hijri date is a conventional calculation, adjustable by ± 3 days.
- **The "official times" page** (Jordan, 33 locations; the UAE, 60 locations with Awqaf's ids) matches the official calendars "with a possible difference of one minute for some times on some days". The page itself says «شبه مطابق» (nearly identical).

Tested on 2026-10-09: Muscat's page applies Oman's preset (Dhuhr 11:58 = solar noon + 5, no elevation effect), so "automatic" follows the selected city's country. Khartoum's page matches the no-data default (18°/18°, no minutes, elevation applied: sunrise 05:37 against a sea-level 05:40) rather than the Sudan preset, which turns elevation off. So Sudan appears to be one of the countries without an "automatic" setting, even though its preset is in the list.

**How the plan uses it: supportive evidence only, when needed.** It never replaces an authority's table and is never the validation target. It is never used at runtime and is never labelled official. It serves:

- as a hypothesis to test first in the fitting harness (9.4) when an official table is ambiguous;
- as a second opinion on open questions (13);
- as the only expert, country-specific indication where no official table was found (Sudan).

Its presets read on 2026-10-09 (Fajr / Isha angle or Isha minutes; minutes added to Fajr / sunrise / Dhuhr / Asr / Maghrib / Isha; elevation applied to sunrise and sunset or not; Asr factor 1 for every Arab country), compared with what the research fitted from the official tables:

| Country | ICOP preset | Against the official-table fit (8.2) |
|---|---|---|
| Tunisia | 18 / 18; Dhuhr + 7, Maghrib + 2; elevation | Agrees (INM) |
| Algeria | 18 / 17; Maghrib + 3; no elevation | Agrees (the fit adds ceil rounding) |
| Egypt | 19.5 / 17.5; no offsets; no elevation | Agrees |
| Morocco | 19 / 17; Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 2; elevation | Agrees on the angles, Dhuhr + 5 and Maghrib + 2 (confirmed on coastal tables, Q5). Its elevation dip alone does not reproduce the horizon: Habous's is lower (about −1.17° at sea level, fitted per locality), and sunrise has − 1. |
| Oman | 18 / 18; Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib + 5; no elevation | Agrees exactly |
| Jordan | 18 / 18; no offsets; elevation | Agrees; supports reading the per-zone horizon as an elevation effect |
| Palestine | 18 / 18; no offsets; elevation | Agrees with the angles; supports the elevation reading of the table's sunrise and Maghrib (8.2) |
| UAE | 18 / 18; Dhuhr + 2; elevation | Agrees: Dhuhr + 2 at Accurate Times' western point, with no other minutes, fits Awqaf's 2026 tables (Q13) |
| Kuwait | 18 / 17.5; no offsets; no elevation | Agrees |
| Qatar | 18 / Maghrib + 90; Fajr − 1, Dhuhr + 1, Asr + 1, Maghrib + 2 | Agrees with Qatar Calendar House (`qa_qch`), not with the Ministry (Dhuhr and Asr + 0) |
| Saudi Arabia | 18.5 / Maghrib + 90 (120 in Ramadan); Dhuhr + 1 | Agrees on the angles and the Ramadan rule; its Dhuhr + 1 approximates KACST's rounding up |
| Syria | 18 / 17; Maghrib + 2 | Agrees with the 1447 tables; supports applying + 2 all year (Q12) |
| Libya | 18 / 18; no offsets; no elevation | Close to the fit (18.35° / 18.35°, Dhuhr + 4, Maghrib + 4, truncated, 8.2), but not it |
| Bahrain | 18 / Maghrib + 90 | Older rule: Bahrain moved Isha to an angle in 2021 (18° stated; the 1448 calendar fits 17.8°, Q7) |
| Iraq | 19.5 / 17.5; Dhuhr + 7, Asr + 7, Maghrib + 4 | Differs from the Sunni Endowment's table (18 / 17; Dhuhr + 5, Asr + 3 on the apparent sun, Maghrib + 1 on an elevation horizon), and no Iraqi mosque calendar in the owner's dataset uses it (Penjwen's uses 19.5 / 17.5 with no offsets). Not used; whether it was the Endowment's rule before its 2021–2022 review is unknown (Q8) |
| Yemen | 18 / 17; Dhuhr + 2; elevation | Differs from Sanaa's 2026 table (18 / 16; Dhuhr + 10; terrain Maghrib): over 134 days its Dhuhr is 8 min early and its Isha 4.5 min late, with no exact Dhuhr, Maghrib or Isha minute. No published Yemeni table matches it, so it is not used (Q2) |
| Sudan | 18 / 18; no offsets; no elevation | No official table found; the only expert, Sudan-specific setting known (A3). Khartoum's own page applies the no-data default (elevation on) instead. |
| Lebanon, Mauritania, Somalia, Djibouti, Comoros | no preset | — |

Caveats:

- The presets claim to follow each country's official calendar but name no document. Several are older than the tables the research fitted (Bahrain, perhaps Iraq), so they are evidence, not proof.
- Its "official" UAE times differ from Awqaf's by a minute on many days, as its own help page allows (the "official times" bullet above).
- Its terms of use are unknown, so only the preset parameters (facts) are cited here, and its tables follow A7.

**ICOP's Accurate Times software.** ICOP also distributes Accurate Times, a Windows program by Mohammad Odeh, ICOP's chairman. The owner installed version 5.7 (released 2022-07-11) on 2026-10-09, and its files were read in place. Its help file says it "is the official software adopted by the Jordanian Ministry of Islamic Affairs to calculate the prayer times in Jordan" and "the official software to calculate prayer times in UAE".

- **Engine.**
  - The sun comes from VSOP87 (its term tables ship with it), with Delta T applied (UT1). The help claims agreement with the Astronomical Almanac to about 1 s.
  - Refraction follows Hohenkerk & Sinclair (Explanatory Supplement, 1992, §3.281), using each location's pressure and temperature (standard 10 °C, 1010 mbar).
  - Elevation shifts sunrise and sunset only; the twilight angles have no dip.
  - "City Settings": the stored point is a city's western edge, and with a diameter of D km, "Fajer and Shuroq will be calculated for the eastern parts of the city", D km east, while Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha use the western point.
  - The high-latitude rule is MWL 2009. Its Hijri conversion ignores sightings.
- **Location database** (`loc.txt`, 2,188 places). Each place has its coordinates, time zone, elevation, city diameter, pressure, temperature and lapse rate. Arab League places: Saudi Arabia 55, UAE 66, Libya 34, Jordan 33, Egypt 32, Palestine 32, Iraq 31, Yemen 24, Tunisia 17, Algeria 13, Morocco 11, Kuwait 8, Qatar 8, Syria 6, Lebanon 5, Mauritania 3, Oman 3, Sudan 3, Bahrain 2, Somalia 2, Comoros 1, Djibouti 1. Some entries match what the research fitted from official tables:
  - Abu Dhabi is stored at 230 m with a 35 km diameter. The UAE fit found an effective horizon worth about 290 m of elevation, and an eastern edge about 35 km away. Al Ain: 287 m, 40 km. Awqaf's 2026 tables follow these inputs only in part (Q13). Abu Dhabi fits its stored point, diameter and elevation once its standard 1010 mbar and 10 °C give way to the climate values the other UAE entries store (987 mbar and 29 °C at that height: 713 of 732 times exact). Al Ain needs a point about 4 km west (24.213N 55.528E), 56 km, and 407 m in the morning and 277 m in the evening. Dubai matches none of the Dubai, Jabal Ali or Sharjah entries.
  - Amman: 1,100 m, 16 km, 891 mbar. The Jordan Valley entries are below sea level, at 1,013 mbar and 22 °C. These are the inputs behind the per-zone horizons the Jordan fit could only estimate.
  - Baghdad (33°20'19"N, 44°23'38"E, 46 m) is the point fitted for the Sunni Endowment's table (33.32–33.34N, 44.39–44.40E). In the owner's dataset (support only), the Mosul (15 mosques) and Kirkuk (4) calendars fit the program's Mosul (222 m) and Kirkuk (350 m) entries with the same rule and the 2021 sun: Fajr, Dhuhr and Isha 98.6–100 % exact, with a sunrise and Maghrib horizon that deepens with the stored elevation. With Asr on the apparent sun, this suggests the Endowment computes with the program's data and conventions (Q8). The help file lists revised Iraqi locations in version 5.3.4 but names no Iraqi user.
- **Stale time zone.** Its data still has Jordan at UTC+2 with optional summer time, as before October 2022. So on 2026-10-09 it showed Amman 1 hour early: 04:14, 05:30, 11:24, 14:42, 17:17, 18:33. Shifted by an hour, these sit within about a minute of the ministry's 8 October row (kept in `test-data-local/`, A7), with settings 18°/18°, no added minutes and elevation on. This is to be checked properly against the 9 October row. It is also a live example of A9.

How the plan uses it, again as supportive evidence only:

- **A local reference engine on the owner's PC.** Run it with the zone set by hand (Jordan + 3), and compare its output with the official tables. Where both agree, the fitting harness (9.4) has the official physics to reproduce, not just conventions to fit.
- **A source of seeds** for the Jordan and UAE zone fits, and for zone points elsewhere.
- **A design input**: the refraction model and city-diameter behaviour that E2 and E6 must be able to express.

It is copyrighted freeware: no code and no copy of its location database go into the repository. Only the published method it cites (Hohenkerk & Sinclair) is implemented from its source, and individual parameters it suggests are confirmed by fitting official tables before use.

### 8.6 Prototype results: Jordan and the UAE reproduced from the published method (2026-10-09)

A research prototype (Python, standard library; `scripts/prayer_formula/arab_prototype/`, with its README) implemented the method that Accurate Times documents, from published sources only. The binary was never decompiled. Its data (VSOP87D, the official tables, the fitted inputs) is in the git-ignored `test-data-local/arab-prototype/`.

- **Sun:** VSOP87D Earth series, downloaded from the CDS archive. FK5 correction, nutation, aberration, and Delta T by the Espenak–Meeus polynomial.
- **Refraction:** Bennett's formula scaled by pressure and temperature, as a stand-in for Hohenkerk & Sinclair.
- **Sunrise and Maghrib:** sun's upper limb on the apparent horizon, lowered by the geometric dip of the zone's elevation. Morning and evening elevations are separate.
- **Fajr and Isha:** 18°, geometric, no dip.
- **Points:** Fajr and sunrise at the zone's eastern point (city diameter), the other four at its western point.
- **Asr:** shadow factor 1 measured on the *apparent* (refracted) sun, at noon and at Asr.
- **Rounding:** nearest minute.

Zone inputs started from Accurate Times' `loc.txt` and were fitted to the official tables. It was compared with the ministries' own tables:

| Data | Result |
|---|---|
| Jordan, ministry 2023 calendar (opendata.gov.jo), 15 zones × 365 days, inputs fitted on it | Fajr 94.1 %, sunrise 99.3 %, Dhuhr 99.6 %, Asr 98.7 %, Maghrib 98.6 %, Isha 99.5 % exact. These are in-sample shares, not a held-out result. Fajr is held back by the simple step-by-step fitter, which widened the city diameter to fix sunrise, and the weakness is per zone, not per season: 95.1 % in January–February against 93.9 % in March–December, but 78.6 % at Karak, 83.0 % at Aqaba and 85.5 % at Jerash and Ajloun. Amman alone is 2,175 of 2,190 (99.3 %), and 6 of the 15 zones reach 97 % on every time (recomputed 2026-10-13 from the prototype's fitted inputs and its fit log). |
| Jordan, ministry 2025 calendar, held out (31 zone-days transcribed from the scanned PDF: 1 January for all 15 zones, 20 January and 8 February for 8) | **185 of 186 exact** with the 2023-fitted inputs (the one miss: Irbid's sunrise on 8 February, 1 minute). With the shipped `loc.txt` inputs unchanged: 143 of 186. A winter sample: 7 zones are scored on one day and the other 8 (Karak and Aqaba included) on three, so it shows that the 2023 inputs carry over to another year, not that any zone passes the tier-A gate, which needs held-out days in every month at that zone (9.5). |
| Jordan, the same calendar's monthly offsets from Amman for its 18 further places (pages 8–19, 1,296 values, transcribed 2026-10-11) | Place minus Amman on the 15th, rounded to the half-minute. At Accurate Times' entries unchanged (15 places): 892 of 1,080 exact, 1,069 within 0.5. With refitted points (the 15 within 4.4 km of their entries, except Hasa 20 km and Mudawwara 13 km; Jerusalem with a 12 km diameter; Irhab, the Karameh border post and Quraiqira from rough seeds): 1,268 of 1,296 exact, all within 0.5. Fitted on odd months only, the even months give 595 of 648 (91.8 %, the held-out figure; the 1,268 above are in-sample), all within 0.5 (Q19). |
| Jordan, 2026 zone calendars carried by the mosque dataset's mosques (support only), held out | 2023-fitted inputs, one calendar per zone in 9 of 11 zones: 97.8 % exact (Amman's 505-mosque calendar 99.2 %), 99.9 % within 1 minute. Fajr is the weakest (Karak 76 %, Jerash 84 %; the model 1 minute early). Mafraq's and Aqaba's calendars call Maghrib 1–3 minutes later (Q19). |
| UAE, Awqaf's 2026 tables (every third day), Dhuhr + 2 | Abu Dhabi with the shipped inputs unchanged: Fajr 120/121, Dhuhr 121, Isha 121, Asr 115. Fitted (2026-10-11, Q13): Abu Dhabi 717/732 (98 %), Al Ain 717/732 (98 %), Dubai 683/732 (93 %, at 25.115N 55.075E, 48 km, 200 m and 230 m); every time within 1 minute. Asr: 114, 117 and 121 of 122 (geometric: 108, 103 and 109). No point, angle, horizon, computation year or once-a-day sun lifts Dubai higher: its table is regular within a month but carries month-to-month biases of up to half a minute. |

**What this changes:**

- **Jordan's method is now documented by the ministry itself.** The introduction to its 2025 calendar (pages 2–3) says it is prepared "in cooperation with the International Astronomical Center".
  - Since 2001, each large city uses a site in its east for Fajr and sunrise and a site in its west for the other prayers. In Amman these are Marka and Sweileh.
  - It covers 18 named places in 15 zones; Zarqa, Balqa and Madaba follow Amman.
  - 18 further places get monthly per-prayer offsets from Amman in half-minutes (pages 8–19), which residents of places not in the calendar add to Amman's time (page 4). 15 of them are entries of Accurate Times' location list (Jerusalem under Palestine); Irhab, the Karameh border post and Quraiqira are not. The offsets are the computed difference from Amman on the 15th of each month (Q19, 8.8).
  - Month starts follow the Global Hijri Calendar (eastern zone), ICOP's two-zone calendar, not the unified Istanbul 2016 one and not Umm al-Qura (Q27). It matched all 5 of Jordan's listed Ramadan and Shawwal starts of 1445–1447, but Jordan began Dhu al-Hijja 1445 with Saudi Arabia, a day earlier, so Appendix C falls back to Umm al-Qura for Dhu al-Hijja; whether that always holds is still open (Q27). For Ramadan and Shawwal, the Iftaa Department's crescent committee observes and announces.
- **Two conventions the earlier fits missed.** Asr is measured on the apparent sun, which moved Asr from about 88 % to 97–99 %. Sunrise and Maghrib depend on an elevation and on pressure and temperature, separately for morning and evening (E2).
- **Salat Calendar's tables are not a validation target.** They differ systematically from the ministry's. For example, the two Jordan Valley zones are exactly 2.0 minutes off at sunrise and Maghrib, consistent with the site treating −212 m as +212 m. Held out against them, the fitted inputs score only 51–61 % on sunrise and Maghrib, while scoring 99 % against the ministry. This confirms the rule of 8.5.
- **The remaining work for these two countries** is ordinary AM2 fitting: a joint fit of the eastern point and the morning elevation (so Fajr is not traded for sunrise), Dubai's zone, the other UAE locations, and a held-out year for the UAE. For Jordan, the 18 further places are refitted with the zones (Q19), and the 2025 PDF's remaining daily pages (1 January to 9 February, about 590 zone-days) are transcribed as held-out data. They still cover only winter, so each zone's tier-A record (9.5) also scores a month-split of the 2023 calendar (refitted on odd months and scored on even months, and the reverse), which covers every month, until another official year is in hand (the ministry's 2026 search, 9.3).

### 8.7 Authority profiles

Each profile is a `PrayerMethod` with `evidence`, a `source` line and, once fitted, its `fit` record (`FitStats`, 6.3): the profile's aggregate, while each zone carries its own (`OfficialZone.fit`), which «طريقتك» shows at the places mapped to it; for `sa_umm_al_qura`, the profile's (6.11, 9.5). Profiles start from the values of 8.2 and are finalised by the AM2 fits (tier A, Algeria, Libya, Iraq's Sunni Endowment and the four A3 countries) or the AM10 fits (the rest: KW, PS, SY, MR, BH and LB). Every profile also carries the rounding of 8.2, even where the table below does not repeat them.

| id | Base | Notable parameters | Zones | Evidence today |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sa_umm_al_qura` | 18.5°, Maghrib + 90 (+ 120) | E1 ceil on four times, floor on sunrise; E2 `Fixed(−0.83°)` at sunrise, `Standard` at sunset; E4 `KACST` for Fajr and Asr; E8 `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED` | the nearest of the 173 official places (published coordinates) within 25 km, whatever its type; KACST at the user's point beyond (6.5, 8.8) | from tables |

The generic and reference presets offered beside these profiles (`egyptian`, and the shared `mwl`, `isna` and `umm_al_qura`) are in 6.3. `dz_marw` and `ma_habous` replace the former `algeria` and `morocco` presets.

**Not reproduced on purpose (A8).** On 1 or 2 consecutive days early in each Hijri month, KACST gives the previous civil date's times; only the first of them is a literal duplicate. They fell on Hijri days 4–6 in 2025, 3–5 in 2026 and 2–3 in 2027. So the app is 1 minute off, 2 at most, on about 17 days a year in Saudi Arabia (14 to 20 per city-year measured, 2025–2027; Q4). The fit report counts them apart: rows on Hijri days 2 to 6 whose five times match the profile's times for the previous date.

### 8.8 Official zones by authority

The zone type, its asset and the mapping kinds are shared (6.5). What follows is particular to some authorities.

- **Bahrain (Q7).** The SCIA publishes one calendar for the Kingdom: its method page computes it from two points («اعتماد موقعين في مملكة البحرين لاحتساب مواقيت الصلاة»), and it lists no other place, no offset and no rule for attaching one. The calendar's page on the Kingdom (from bahrain.bh) puts Bahrain between 25°32′ and 26°20′ N and between 50°20′ and 50°50′ E, which takes in the Hawar Islands. So every place in Bahrain takes the national table, whatever the distance, with the validity guard (6.5), which keeps it at Hawar: the islands' winter sunrise comes up to about 1.5 min before the table's (floored at the morning point 26.22N 50.68E) on about 150 days, well within the guard's 4 minutes; «حسب موقعي بالضبط» (the method at the user's point) is an option only. At Hawar that option differs from the table on most days of 1448 (Dhuhr 1–2 min earlier every day, Asr, Maghrib and Isha up to 3–4 min earlier, Fajr within 2 either way). The catalogue has no row on the islands: Qatar's Dukhan is about 26 km from Jazīrat Ḩawār and the nearest Bahraini row, Zallaq, about 51 km, so `CountryResolver` (6.8) would put a fix there in Qatar, even with a Bahraini network. The catalogue therefore carries Jazīrat Ḩawār (GeoNames 290243, 25.655N 50.755E) as a Bahraini row «جزر حوار», nearer than Dukhan from every point of Hawar island. It is also the nearest row on Qatar's coast facing the islands, so there the network decides, as on any border (6.8): a fix with a Qatari network stays in Qatar, and one with no network takes Bahrain's table (12). An SCIA note giving Hawar its own times or an offset would replace this rule.

- **Libya (2026-10-11).** The Awqaf lists 22 cities and attaches no other place: its site (read 2026-10-08 and 2026-10-11) has the city selector and no town offsets, imsakiya or timetable file, Dar al-Ifta's API serves Tripoli only, and three web searches found no table of city differences. Its 2014–2018 site listed 27 (also Nalut, Ubari, Jaghbub, Bin Jawad and Shahat); the current list wins. So a place in Libya takes the nearest of the 22 with no reach limit, then the validity guard (Sabratha keeps Zawiya's table; A2), and the Awqaf formula at the user's own point is the option «حسب موقعي بالضبط». The zone line names the city whose table is used («جدول: الزاوية» in Sabratha), with the city names in Arabic as the Awqaf's selector gives them, except that the selector labels Awjila (Ojala) «اجدابيا» like Ajdabiya: the app calls it «أوجلة». The table's margin (Dhuhr + 4, Maghrib + 4) covers those two times a little west of its city: Zuwara, 63 km west of Zawiya, gets Zawiya's Maghrib at its own sunset to within 2 s. Fajr, Asr and Isha have no margin and are truncated, so west of a city they come before the place's own beyond the table's rounding (computed for 2026: up to 2.1 min at Sabratha and 4.4 at Zuwara), and east of a city the truncated sunrise comes after the local one. Further west or south Maghrib and Dhuhr fail too: computed for 2026 at today's points, Nalut and Wazin would get Zawiya's Maghrib up to 6–7 min before their sunset, Tazirbu Kufra's up to 9, and Ghat Sabha's (487 km) 9–19 min before it, with Dhuhr 13–14 min before its noon. The validity guard (6.5) gives Nalut, Wazin, Tazirbu and Ghat the Awqaf formula at their own point, as the Awqaf computes each of its cities (12). Sabratha and Zuwara keep Zawiya's table: its Fajr, Asr and Isha come before their own by about 1 and 3.5 min more than at Zawiya itself, within the guard's 4 minutes. The owner's dataset (support only, 5.6): of 18 mosques more than 30 km from every listed city, 1 uses the nearest table as printed (Tukra, Benghazi's), 15 shift it by a whole number of minutes, mostly toward their own longitude (+ 1 to + 3 west of Zawiya and Ras Lanuf, + 5 to + 8 at Tiji and Rabyana, − 2 at Tarhuna and Garabulli), and 1 computes at its own point.
- **Algeria (2026-10-11, A16).** The ministry's calendar gives three references (Algiers, Djelfa, Adrar) and, under each month's table, whole-minute offsets per prayer for the places attached to it: 25, 26 and 16 places, the Adrar zone in two blocks (days 1–15 and 15–30). Read twice from all 48 grids of 1447 and 1448 and checked against the images, the offsets follow one rule: offset = ceil(max over the window of (T_place − T_ref)), with the unrounded times of the ministry's own formula at the place and at its reference. So the printed time (the reference's rounded-up time plus the offset) is never earlier than the place's own time on any day. The window is the Hijri month; in the Adrar zone it is days 1–15, then days 17 to the end (day 16 belongs to neither; the app gives it the second offset). The rule reproduces 99.9 % of 1448's offsets and 98.9 % of the held-out 1447 Algiers grids, every miss 1 minute and within 2 s of a whole minute; Tizi Ouzou's and Relizane's Dhuhr, whose longitude difference from Algiers is a whole minute, flip in a few months each year. The points: the references at whole arc-minutes (8.2) and each attached place fitted from its own offsets, median 1.3 km from the town (62 of 67 within 2 km); these are our coordinates, not the ministry's data. The windows need the ministry's 12 month-start dates per Hijri year (the first date of each monthly table: in 1448, 5 of 12 come a day after Umm al-Qura's). They ship at the top of `official-zones/dz_marw.json`, 12 dates per Hijri year, once the year's calendar is out (A7 applies); until then the computed Umm al-Qura starts stand in, which keeps every time within 1 minute (offsets 96 % exact). The mapping is by wilaya: each grid is headed «الفروق الزمنية لمدينة … والولايات المرتبطة بها», so a place takes its wilaya's listed place (the nearest of them where a wilaya has several), otherwise the nearest of the 70, with the validity guard (6.5). The owner's mosques (support only, 5.6) agree within 60 km; beyond it, in the Sahara, they compute at their own point. The guard acts in the far Sahara (beyond about 100 km from the listed town, the table's Maghrib comes before the place's sunset on about 38 % of days), not near the listed places. Every time is rounded up and only Maghrib has added minutes, so west of a listed place its Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr and Isha come before the place's own on some days (Zeralda, 0.8 min west of Algiers: on 279 to 324 days of 2026, up to 1.1 min), and east of it its sunrise comes after the local one beyond the table's rounding (Rouiba, 0.9 min east: on 337 days), but well within the guard's 4 minutes.

- **Morocco (2026-10-11).** Habous publishes 191 localities (ids 1–169 and 301–322, Ceuta 24 and Melilla 40 among them, 6.8) and no rule for other places: its prayer page heads them «حصة الصلاة الشهرية لجل المدن المغربية», and no ministry or delegation text attaching unlisted places was found. Each locality is computed at its own point (Q5), so there is no offset to derive either. A place in Morocco, Western Sahara included, takes the nearest Moroccan locality whatever the distance (`reachKm` does not apply) where the validity guard (6.5) allows it, with the zone line «جدول: …» and the profile's chip. Of the 502 GeoNames places with 1,000 people or more or an administrative seat, 116 are more than 25 km from a locality and 5 more than 60 km, all in Western Sahara: Oum Dreyga and Jraifia (Bir Anzarane's table), Tichla (Aousserd's), Bir Lehlou (Tifariti's) and Haouza (Smara's), 77–128 km away. Habous names none of them, so the validity guard (6.5) checks them as it checks every place Habous does not list, and it gives Tichla `ma_habous` at its own point: there Aousserd's Fajr comes up to about 6 min before the local dawn and its Maghrib about 2.5 min before the local sunset. At Jraifia they come about 4 and 1.4 min early (Asr and Isha also come early at both), and at Haouza, Bir Lehlou and Oum Dreyga the attached table's sunrise comes up to about 3–5 min after the local sunrise: these four are near the guard's threshold and are settled by the scan with AM2's locality points. Habous's only margins are its rounding and fixed minutes, so nearer places also get some times a little early, within the guard's 4 minutes: Touissit, 26 km from Oujda and 575 m higher, gets Oujda's sunrise up to about 2 min after its own and keeps Oujda's table. These distances use approximate locality points; AM2 places all 189 Moroccan localities from their own tables (id 30 «بوسكور» is in the eastern Rif, about 34.7°N 3.6°W by its table, not Bouskoura near Casablanca). The dataset agrees without deciding (5.6): of its 219 Moroccan mosques more than 15 km from every locality (4 with wrong coordinates left out), none computes Habous's method at its own point, and 23 follow a locality (Fajr, Dhuhr and Isha computed there, 90 % or more exact): 14 the nearest, 8 a farther one that is their province's seat (Had Soualem → Berrechid, not Casablanca; Ouled Abbou → Berrechid, not Settat; Touissit → Jerada, not Oujda; Jemaa Shaim → Safi, not Youssoufia) and 1 neither (Bejaad → Kasba Tadla, not Oued Zem). A rule published by Habous or its delegations (by province, for example) would replace the nearest locality. «حسب موقعي بالضبط» stays an option (A2).

- **Egypt.** EGSA publishes one daily table per city, 78 in its all-cities table of 2025–26, and nothing for other places. No town offsets were found, and no rule saying which table an unlisted place takes (2026-10-11). Dar al-Ifta's prayer page and widget list the same 78 cities, with the minutes of EGSA's method at its fitted Cairo point (6 of 6 on 2026-10-11). Its fatwa 3012 (2015) answered mosques in villages of Esna's Awqaf district. Some of them added the old village time differences («فرق التوقيت بالزائد والناقص») to the printed calendar and so ran 2–3 minutes off EGSA's times. The ruling: they must follow EGSA's calendar. So every place in Egypt takes the nearest of EGSA's cities, whatever the distance (`reachKm` does not apply), with no offset, and the zone line names it, under the validity guard (6.5). The fatwa does not lift the guard: it is Dar al-Ifta's, names no place and no table, and attaches villages in bulk, which 6.5 checks like any other. EGSA's times carry no margin (no offsets, each rounded to the nearest minute, Q9), so away from its city the table can come early: west of the city the table's Dhuhr comes before the place's noon, east of it the table's sunrise comes after the place's sunrise, and north or south of it Fajr comes early in one season and Maghrib in the other (Banha: Zagazig's Dhuhr about 1.3 min before its noon; Ras el-Hekma: Dabaa's about 2 min; Hasana: El Arish's Fajr up to about 3 min before its dawn in summer), within the guard's 4 minutes, so these places keep the table. Nakhl, where Ras Sudr's sunrise comes about 4 to 5 min after its own, is near the threshold and is settled by the scan with the fitted points (AM2). A place where the guard acts gets `eg_egsa` at its own point, with the zone line «حسب موقعك (لا يوجد جدول رسمي قريب)», as most Egyptian mosques in the dataset compute (below). A place that is one of a listed city's named places (`namedPlaces`, 6.5) counts as that city: a catalogue place the city lists, whatever its distance, or a point within the city's own area, a circle around its town centre, 15 km to start, each radius set in AM2 from the city's extent. It takes that city's table (where two areas overlap, the city with the nearer centre), and the guard does not act there. Unlike `NEAREST_LOCAL`'s radius in Saudi Arabia, the area is not a reach: `reachKm` stays unlimited, and beyond every area a place still takes the city with the nearest town centre, under the guard. Distance is measured to the city's own town centre, which is both the zone's `point` and its named place's `centre` (set in AM2). The fitted point is kept as the zone's E6 morning and evening points, as for Qatar's towns, because for a few cities it lies 15–50 km from the town (Taba about 48 km west; Baris, Kharga, Ras Gharib and Mahalla 23–32 km; 9.4). Measured to the fitted points, central Mahalla al-Kubra would sit within 1.5 km of a tie with Mansoura's and Tanta's points, and the coast south of Taba would take Nuweiba's table. Against EGSA's method at the place itself, the nearest city's table is up to about 3 minutes off within 60 km of a listed city (Ras el-Hekma, 58 km from Dabaa) and up to about 6 in central Sinai (Nakhl, about 107 km from Ras Sudr, at Fajr). EGSA computes for no other point, so «حسب موقعي بالضبط» stays an option. In the owner's dataset (5.6, support only), 46 of 52 Egyptian mosques compute the method at their own point: 97 % of their times, against 74 % for the nearest city's table, a median 7.5 km away. The page's city menu also names Menouf, Diyarb Negm, Abu Tisht and Samalut, which the all-cities table leaves out (Samalut was in it in 2024). Each becomes a zone once its table is collected through the form (9.3); until then, places near them take the next-nearest city.

- **Saudi Arabia.** KACST's site lists 173 places (26 «city», 142 «governorate» and 5 «center» entries, latitude and longitude only, unchanged from 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-11), and its prayer-times page and API compute for any coordinates. The ministry has each town's mosques call the town's own Umm al-Qura time (Jeddah its own, not the Haram's; 8.1). Each entry is an `sa_umm_al_qura` zone at its published point with `reachKm` 25 to start, whatever its type, since KACST's types do not follow size (Jeddah, Taif, Al Khobar and Yanbu are «governorate» entries; Tumair, Jalajil and Layla «city» entries); the nearest entry in reach gives its table. Beyond 25 km of every entry, the app computes at the user's point, as KACST's prayer-times page does with the user's location (its home page instead snaps its city card to the nearest entry, with no limit); a listed place 50 km to the east would put Maghrib up to about 2 minutes before the local sunset. AM2 sets each entry's radius from the place's extent (Riyadh's and Jeddah's built-up areas reach about 30 km from their points). The dataset agrees without deciding (5.6). Of the 15 calendars with KACST's rounding that can be told apart, none fits its own point better than a listed entry: 11 follow the nearest entry at 2.6 to 17.2 km (Jeddah at 17.2 km: 88–99 % of its minutes, against 49–52 % at its own point); Al Jumum's follows Mecca (22 km) though Al Jumum's entry is 5 km away, Ad Darb's follows Baish (49 km) though Aldarb's is 19 km away, and one in Riyadh follows Ad Diriyah. Beyond 25 km of every entry there are 7 calendars: 6 compute at their own point with nearest-minute rounding, and Al Mudhaylif's copies Al Qunfudhah's table 46 km away (all six times on all 31 days of October 2026 equal KACST's Al Qunfudhah month; 71–94 % equal its month for the town's own point). The zone line lists the five nearest entries for such mosques. A ministry rule naming a listed place for unlisted towns, or more KACST-rounded calendars beyond 25 km that follow one, would switch the far case to the nearest entry with no reach limit.

- **Jordan.** The ministry says which places follow which table (Q29). Its 2025 calendar (pages 2–4) names 18 places in 15 zones, adds «ويلحق بمحافظة عمان المدن التالية: (الزرقاء والبلقاء ومأدبا) إلا ما تم استثناؤه», and sends «المناطق التي لم تذكر» to the monthly offsets of its 18 further places. Its 2026 site names the first zone «عمان، البلقاء، الزرقاء، مادبا», which becomes that zone's `nameAr`. A place in Jordan takes, in order:
  1. below sea level (its `elevationM`) in Irbid, Balqa or Karak: Northern Ghor, Central Ghor or, in Karak, Ghor al-Safi, which is a further place, not one of the 15 daily zones (Amman's time plus its derived offset);
  2. otherwise, among the tables serving its governorate (`admin1`, that of the nearest Jordanian catalogue place, 6.8), the one with the nearest named town (`namedPlaces`, else its point).
  - Amman's table serves Amman, Balqa, Zarqa and Madaba (named towns Amman, Salt, Zarqa and Madaba), beside Central Ghor (Balqa), Dhiban (Madaba), Azraq and Dulail and Hashimiya (Zarqa). Irbid's serves Irbid with Northern Ghor; Jerash and Ajloun's both governorates; Mafraq's Mafraq with Umm al-Qutain; Karak, Tafila, Ma'an (with Shoubak and Petra) and Aqaba their own. Each of the 17 further places in Jordan (Q19) is a zone serving its governorate (`admin1`), with its own town as its named place (6.5), set in AM2 with the towns' coordinates.
  - Every place in Jordan gets a table, as the calendar has covered «جميع مناطق المملكة» since 2018 (2025 calendar, page 3), so `reachKm` does not apply, and no place in Jordan is computed at its own coordinates unless the user picks «حسب موقعي بالضبط» or the validity guard (6.5) finds the table invalid there, which the zones' eastern and western points (E6) make rare (owner, 2026-10-11). Zarqa, Russeifa, Salt and Madaba take Amman's table; Khalidiya (Mafraq) takes Mafraq's though Dulail is nearer.
  - An approximate check (governorates and the valley read from the mosques' addresses) gives 619 of the dataset's 649 followers their own table (95.4 %), against 480 (74.0 %) for the nearest zone point. Most misses are shared calendars: 8 mosques around Hashimiya and Sukhna call Amman's table, 6 of the 9 Central Ghor mosques call Amman's or Northern Ghor's, and 3 Northern Badia mosques call Mafraq's. The published rule stands (5.6).
  - Umm al-Qutain stays a zone, as the 2023 and 2025 calendars print it, though the 2026 site lists Ruwaished (a further place: Amman's time plus its derived offset, Q19) instead (Q29). The site's ids follow the 2025 calendar's own lists: 1–15 its zones (only Umm al-Qutain's 10 is left out) and 16–33 its 18 further places in printed order (Ruwaished 19, Jerusalem 33), so the site shows two further places, not a new zone. In the owner's dataset (support only, 5.6), one 2026 mosque calendar in Salhiya (Northern Badia, about 15 km from Umm al-Qutain) still follows Umm al-Qutain's 2023 table (75–94 % exact per prayer on the same dates, as Amman's 505-mosque calendar scores 72–94 % against Amman's), while the mosque 6 km from Umm al-Qutain and a second Salhiya mosque call Mafraq's shared calendar.

- **Qatar's towns (Q3).** QCH's wall calendar, the published source, prints day-by-day Fajr and Maghrib offsets from Doha for Al Shamal, Dukhan and Abu Samra, and no other town times; its site's daily `/cal` data gives six Qatari places, these four plus Mesaieed and Halul. Nothing is stored: each town is a listed place computed for the actual year at a point fitted to the print (Al Shamal 26.089, 51.271; Dukhan 25.365, 50.826; Abu Samra 24.647, 50.919; 8–14 km from the town centres, kept as the zone's E6 morning and evening points so that the mapping uses the town's own point), with Fajr at 18° without the Ministry's − 1 (a zone offset of Fajr + 1) and Maghrib at sunset + 2. On the 59 printed days of Rabi I–II 1448 (2026-08-14 to 10-11) this gives the printed Fajr on 53–58 days and Maghrib on 45–53 of 54, never more than 1 minute off; at the town centres with the − 1, Dukhan's Fajr matches on 4. Isha is the town's Maghrib + 90, and sunrise, Dhuhr and Asr are computed at the same point, as QCH's `/cal` data does (Dhuhr Dukhan + 3, Al Shamal + 1 on 2026-10-08); that day the `/cal` data equals this computation on 15 of the towns' 18 times, Abu Samra's Fajr included (+ 3, where the print has + 4). The print is the fit target and `/cal` a cross-check. AM2 refits the points on a full year of QCH pages (9.3): Abu Samra's 6 Fajr misses all fall from 2026-09-03 on, with the print 1 minute later each time. Mesaieed and Halul, which only `/cal` gives («مسيعيد» and «حالول»), are listed places of the same kind, computed at 24.991, 51.619 and 25.671, 52.571 (7 and 16 km east of their centres): 11 of 12 times each on the two `/cal` days at hand (2026-10-08 and 10-11), against 5 and 4 of 12 at the centres, every miss 1 minute. Two days fix only the longitude (points from 24.51 to 25.11 north fit Mesaieed as well, and from 25.11 to 26.03 north Halul), so AM2 refits both on a year of `/cal` days (9.3). **Mapping (owner rule, 2026-10-11).** Every place in Qatar gets one of these six places, so `reachKm` does not apply. A place that none of QCH's data names must still pass the validity guard (6.5) first (below). QCH's 1448 calendars (Muharram to Rabi II, the note on page 1) make Doha's times the reference for the east-coast cities from Al Khor in the north to Al Wakra in the south, which they say match Doha fully. Al Khor and Al Wakra are Doha's `namedPlaces`, counted only for a catalogue place listed as either town or a point within 6 km of the town's point (`areaKm` 6, 6.5; AM2 may adjust the radius): this keeps Al Wakra's southern districts, nearer Mesaieed than Doha, on Doha's table, while inland places west of Al Khor, which the note does not name, keep the nearest-place rule; the coast between the two towns is nearest Doha anyway. Any other place is attached to the nearest of the six by its town point, whatever the distance (Ras Laffan and Al Ghuwariyah to Al Shamal, Umm Bab and Al Jumayliyah to Dukhan, Al Kharrara to Abu Samra, Sealine and Khor Al Udaid to Mesaieed, Al Shahaniya to Doha). It keeps that table only where the validity guard (6.5) passes. Qatar's Dhuhr is at transit, its Asr and sunrise carry no offset, the towns' Fajr is at 18°, and every time is rounded to the nearest minute. So a place west of its table's point gets Fajr, Dhuhr and Asr before its own, and a place east of it gets a sunrise after its own, beyond the table's own margin, but by less than the guard's 4 minutes at the places checked, which keep their table: Khor Al Udaid (Mesaieed's Fajr up to about 2.2 min beyond), Al Shahaniya (Doha's Fajr, Dhuhr and Asr about 1.3 to 1.5 min beyond), Ras Laffan and Al Jumayliyah (Al Shamal's and Dukhan's sunrise about 1.4 to 1.6 min after their own). The guard does not act on the east coast from Al Khor to Al Wakra, which QCH's rule assigns to Doha: it skips Al Khor and Al Wakra within their 6 km and a place picked as Doha (Doha's named places, 6.5), and the coast between, nearest Doha anyway, is scanned and keeps Doha's table, whose times come there at most about 1.2 min earlier against the place's own than at Doha (Asr, towards Al Khor). A place where the scan (9.6) finds it acting takes `qa_awqaf` at its own point, with «حسب موقعك (لا يوجد جدول رسمي قريب)»; AM2 lists any such place. The 1444 and 1446 notes listed Al Khor, Simaisma, Doha, Al Wakra and Mesaieed; the current wording leaves out Mesaieed, south of Al Wakra, and `/cal` lists it apart, so it keeps its own times (within 1 minute of QCH's Doha on both days). «حسب موقعي بالضبط» stays an option. A QCH statement, or mosque calendars from Ras Laffan, Al Shahaniya or Mesaieed showing another table, would change this.
- **Palestine (Q1).** Dar al-Ifta lists 16 places in Palestine with their offsets from Al-Aqsa on all six times (the perpetual table, p. 2, still served on darifta.ps, Last-Modified 2026-09-17): 0 for Jerusalem, Ramallah, Bethlehem, Jenin and Nablus; − 1 for Jericho; + 1 for Hebron, Idhna, Dura, Beit Awwa and Tulkarm; + 1.5 for Qalqilya; + 3 for Gaza; + 4 for Rafah, Khan Younis and Deir al-Balah. It gives no rule for the places it does not list: the table and its 2025 imsakiya only tell residents outside Al-Aqsa to apply these differences, and its 2026 calendar has no prayer times. So, under the owner's rule (2026-10-11), a place takes the nearest of the 16, whatever the distance, with its offset: Salfit and Tubas take Nablus's 0, Bardala Jenin's 0, Bidya Qalqilya's + 2, and a Gaza Strip point the offset of its nearest Gaza place when `ps_dar_al_ifta` is chosen there (A17). The list's 13 Israeli places are not used (14). Qalqilya's published + 1.5 is applied as + 2, rounded half up: the only mosque of that group in the dataset (Ramla) uses + 2 on all six times. The list follows longitude (4 min per degree east of Al-Aqsa, within 0.5 min at all 29 places), but the app uses the published offsets and derives none for unlisted places. The dataset does not decide (5.6): of the 70 West Bank and Jerusalem mosques that follow the table, the nearest listed place fits 61 and the former governorate rule 65. The difference is Bidya, whose 4 mosques use 0 although Qalqilya is the nearest listed place, but both their calendars are shared with towns elsewhere, one of them giving Kafr Qasim 0 against its published + 1; 4 mosques near al-Fawwar use + 2 where Dura's is + 1. Each listed place is a zone at its own point, which `ZoneMapping` uses (6.5); every zone other than Jerusalem's has `baseZoneId` Al-Aqsa, so its times are Al-Aqsa's plus the offset. The governorate (GeoNames' second-order division, stored in `place_admin2` as the French département is, 6.8) now only gives the Jerusalem governorate's clock (6.8). In the Gaza Strip, the Ministry of Awqaf publishes one table for all five governorates: its decree of 2021-10-05 unifies the adhan times in every mosque of the Strip, its monthly tables are headed «محافظات غزة», and its radio posts and website widget name no city. So `ps_gaza_awqaf` has one `NATIONAL` zone «محافظات غزة» for the whole Strip, computed at Gaza City's point until the AM10 fit refines it.
- **Syria.** The ministry's mosque screens and its app «مسجدك» show, for each of 108 regions, a year table (tv.masjidak.mow.gov.sy, found 2026-10-18; region 763 is Beirut's table: the same screen software serves Lebanon). The files carry no names, so a sample of the screens' configuration pages (2,047) gives the towns each serves (`masjidCity`): a table zone is the town most of its screens are in, with every town its screens name as a named place, and computes at its table's own point (Jindires for region 783, computed at Aleppo's point; Salamiyah for 809, computed 60 km east of the town). About a quarter of the screens carry no table and run aladhan's API at their own point with its default for the point (Jordan's ministry's method for most of Syria, Turkey's near the northern border), keeping their first answer for good; the 28 towns whose sampled screens are mostly live (all of Deir ez-Zor governorate's, Quneitra, eastern and northern Aleppo, villages of Afrin and rural Homs) are zones of their own, computed with that default at the town. A Syrian place takes the zone that names it, else the nearest town centre or zone point (`namedFirst`), with no reach limit and the validity guard (6.5); tables that no sampled screen shows, computed in another zone's town or at another table's point (761 in Damascus, 762, and 786 at Khanasir), are left to manual picks (tier 1). Over 2026 at approximate town points, the guard keeps every place checked on its zone: Sukhnah and Al-Tanf take Palmyra's table (65 and 124 km away; at most 2.7 and 3.9 min beyond its margin), and Deir ez-Zor, Mayadin, Abu Kamal and Quneitra their live towns. The ministry's printed Ramadan tables (14 governorate tables, «مواقيت مدينة … وما حولها», 1446 and 1447), which `sy_awqaf` was fitted on until 2026-10-18, are another product, 1–2 minutes off the screens.
- **Lebanon (Q10).** Dar al-Fatwa publishes Beirut's table only, with no offsets and no rule for other places, so `lb_dar_al_fatwa` gives Beirut's table, at Beirut's point, to every Lebanese place, with no reach limit (`NATIONAL`, 6.5; A2). The validity guard (6.5) gives `lb_dar_al_fatwa` at their own point to the north-eastern border (Hermel, Qaa, Qoubaiyat), where the table's sunrise comes up to about 6 min after their own, and to the far south (Tyre, Naqoura), where its Fajr comes up to about 6 min before their own, more than the table's own floor rounding plus the guard's 4 minutes (12). «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the place's own point anywhere.
- **Yemen.** Sanaa's 31 locations serve the whole country, Aden-held areas included, since no other published Yemeni table was found (Q2). The authority publishes no offsets and no rule for places it does not list: its prayer page only asks the user to pick one of the 31 («يرجى تحديد موقعك للتحقق من مواقيت الصلاة», read 2026-10-11). So a place in Yemen gets a location's table (owner rule, 2026-10-11), with the validity guard (6.5), since Sanaa assigns no place it does not list: the nearest location of its own terrain class within 60 km, otherwise the nearest location, with no reach limit (E10). Al Mahabishah, about 1,570 m and 28 km from Abs, maps to Hajjah (34 km); Mocha, on the coast with no lowland location within 60 km, maps to Taiz (90 km). The guard acts at Abd al-Kuri (Kilmia, about 200 km west of Socotra's location) and Rumah (221 km north-west of Ghaydah), whose location's Fajr, Asr and Isha come up to about 8 and 9.6 min before their own (their Maghrib also up to about 2.5 and 3 min before their sunset): both take `ye_awqaf` at their own point. Checked against `ye_awqaf` computed at each place: over Yemen's 311 catalogue places (every 3rd day of 2026) this rule keeps all six times within 2 minutes on every day at 77.2 % of them, against 73.0 % for plain nearest (within 3 minutes: 88.4 % against 86.8 %); over GeoNames' 81,290 populated places (every 7th day) it is 83.0 % against 82.3 % (within 3 minutes: 94.7 % against 95.7 %), Maghrib comes more than 2 minutes early on some day at 6.0 % of them against 8.3 %, and + 6 is given where the place's own elevation calls for + 10 at 1,020 places against 3,100. Without the 60 km bound, a class match would send highland villages of Hadramawt to Ataq, up to 273 km away, though Seiyun or Mukalla lie 26–134 km off. The location names group the 31 by governorate («إب - المدينة», «إب - ذي السفال»), but nothing says a governorate's entry serves all of it, and the governorate's nearest entry fits worse (71.5 % of populated places within 2 minutes; GeoNames still files Abd al-Kuri under Hadramawt, 422 km from Mukalla), so the governorate is not used. A published attachment rule, from Sanaa or from Aden's ministry, would replace this one. `ye_awqaf` has margins only on Dhuhr (+ 10) and Maghrib (+ 6 or + 10) and rounds to the nearest minute, so a location's table fails at a place where its Fajr, Asr or Isha comes more than half a minute plus the guard's 4 minutes before the place's own (a place west of it) or its sunrise as much after (a place east of it). Computed for 2026, Rumah gets Ghaydah's Fajr, Asr and Isha 3–9.6 min early, with Maghrib up to 2.6 min before its sunset, and takes `ye_awqaf` at its own point; Yarim, 27 km from Dhamar, gets Dhamar's Fajr 0.7 min early and keeps Dhamar's table; Mocha gets Taiz's Fajr, Asr and Isha 2.6–3.8 min early on every day and is settled by the scan with the fitted points (AM2, M3). Maghrib comes before sunset at only 4 catalogue places: Rumah, Kilmia on Abd al-Kuri, Dahasuways and Al Abr. The comparisons above are of the attachment before the guard.
- **Mauritania (owner rule, 2026-10-11).** The ministry gives one table per wilaya and none for any other place: its imsakiya is «إمساكية ولايات الوطن» (2025 cover; the minister's foreword: «لجميع ولايات الوطن»), «تشمل الإمساكية نواكشوط وجميع ولايات الوطن» (2022) and «على مستوى انواكشوط وجميع ولاياتنا الداخلية» (2024), and each page is its capital's («إمساكية مدينة كيفه»). No offset or note for other places was found (affairesislamiques.mr, 2021–2025). So a place in Mauritania takes its wilaya's table (`admin1`, that of the nearest Mauritanian catalogue place, 6.8), whatever the distance, with the validity guard (6.5), since each page is its capital's: each capital's zone lists its wilaya in `admin1`, Nouakchott's the three Nouakchott wilayas, and `reachKm` does not apply. A place with no wilaya takes the nearest capital. On 36 towns (the department seats and Aïn Ben Tili, approximate points), the wilaya's capital is not the nearest for 8: Ouad Naga (52 km from Nouakchott) and Boutilimit (100 km from Aleg) take Rosso's table, M'Bagne and Bababé Aleg's (not Kaédi's), Maghama Kaédi's (not Sélibaby's), Djiguenni Néma's, Tamchekett Aïoun's and Tichitt Tidjikja's. Far from its capital, the table can differ from the place's own sun: about 23 minutes on Fajr and 18 on Maghrib at Aïn Ben Tili (465 km from Zouerat), 12 and 9 at Bir Moghrein, 5–8 at Bassikounou, Ouadane, Chami, Moudjeria, Djiguenni, Tamchekett, Tichitt, Boutilimit and Ouad Naga (12). At places east of their capital, the place's sunrise comes before the capital's table sunrise (Aïn Ben Tili about 16 min, Bir Moghrein about 6, Tichitt about 5, Boutilimit, Bassikounou, Ouadane and Chami about 4, Ouad Naga 1–2). The validity guard (6.5) gives Aïn Ben Tili `mr_islamic_affairs` at its own point and keeps Ouad Naga on Rosso's table; Bir Moghrein, Tichitt, Boutilimit, Bassikounou, Ouadane, Chami and the places west of their capital are near its threshold and are settled by the scan with the fitted points (AM2, M3). «حسب موقعي بالضبط» computes `mr_islamic_affairs` at the place. The owner's dataset cannot test the rule (5.6): 16 of its 19 Mauritanian mosques are in Nouakchott, the other 3 near Kaédi and Aïoun, and most of their calendars are hand-made.
- **Kuwait's places (Q15).** The sheet's footer («فرق التوقيت بالدقائق على حساب الباحث الفلكي د. صالح العجيري») gives one whole-minute offset per place and names no prayer: Kuwait City, Ahmadi and Wafra 0, Failaka − 2, Bubiyan − 1, Jahra + 2, Nuwaiseeb − 2, Al-Salmi + 6, Abdali + 2, Kubbar − 2, Qaruh and Umm al-Maradim − 3. The app adds it to all six of Kuwait City's times. The figures follow longitude to within a minute (at approximate points Jahra is + 1.3 min and Abdali + 1.0, both published as + 2; Al-Salmi + 5.2; Qaruh − 3.2), the part of the shift that is the same for every time; latitude, which moves Fajr and Isha by up to about 3 more minutes and the other times by up to about 2 over the year at Abdali, Wafra, Nuwaiseeb and the southern islands, is left out. In the owner's dataset, 96 of 97 Kuwaiti mosques (83 of the 84 within 60 km of Kuwait City, 5.6) follow the sheet with one constant shift on all six times; the nearest listed place gives 81 of the 96 their shift, and the others are one western-suburbs calendar at + 1 (14 mosques), a value the sheet does not list, and one Bneider mosque at 0, nearest to Nuwaiseeb. Each place is a `kw_awqaf` zone with `baseZoneId` Kuwait City, so its times are computed at Kuwait City's point. AM2 sets the place's own point, which serves mapping only (the town centre; for an island, its inhabited point). A place in Kuwait takes the nearest of the 12 listed places (Kuwait City, Ahmadi and Wafra included), whatever the distance, so `reachKm` does not apply (owner rule, 2026-10-11). The sheet is titled for «مدينة الكويت وضواحيها» and names no rule for other places. The ministry's fatwa committee (its fasting volume on content.awqaf.gov.kw, a file dated 2026-06-03, read only through a search extract) says the broadcast adhan keeps Kuwait City's time only, that people in distant areas such as Al-Salmi, Abdali and Wafra keep their own area's time, and that announced times should carry the differences for the other areas, which is what the footer gives. On an approximate outline, every point of Kuwait is within about 73 km of a listed place, and only 2.9 % of its land, the western desert along the Iraqi border around 29.7 N 47.05 E, lies beyond 60 km; where Al-Salmi is the nearest listed place, a point there takes its + 6. Where Abdali or Jahra is the nearest, their + 2 still comes before the point's own times: at 29.75 N 47.15 E, Dhuhr comes about 1.3 min before its solar noon every day and Maghrib up to about 2.2 min before its sunset, while at Abdali itself the + 2 never comes before noon. These gaps stay within the validity guard's 4 minutes (6.5), so such a point keeps the + 2. The former 60 km reach gave Kuwait City's 0, about 4 minutes early by longitude. All 97 dataset mosques lie within 30 km of a listed place, so the change moves none of them: the nearest place still gives 81 of the 96 followers their shift, the outlying ones included (the Khiran mosque at 28.66 N 48.36 E calls Nuwaiseeb's − 2, a mosque on the Subiya side Bubiyan's − 1). No offset is derived for places between the listed ones: at approximate points, rounding the longitude difference to Kuwait City to the nearest minute, up, or away from zero reproduces at most 7 of the 11 printed figures (Jahra + 1.3 is printed + 2, Nuwaiseeb − 1.7 is printed − 2, Qaruh − 3.2 is printed − 3), and rounding the year's mean, midpoint or extreme difference of the six times does no better (at most 7); a Jordan-style offset per event and month would not be one figure for all six times either. So the authority's rule cannot be deduced as Jordan's further places' can.
- **Jordan's further places.** The 2025 calendar prints monthly per-prayer offsets from Amman, in half-minutes, for 18 places outside its 15 zones (pages 8–19), to be added to Amman's time (page 4). Each is a `jo_awqaf` zone with its own fitted point (eastern and western points, morning and evening elevations) and `baseZoneId` Amman. For each month and prayer, the app computes the place minus Amman on the 15th, rounds it to the half-minute, adds it to Amman's unrounded time and rounds once. As for Qatar's towns, nothing is stored, because the fit reproduces the offsets: 1,268 of 1,296 exact, all within 0.5, with points fitted on all 12 months (595 of 648 on months left out of the fit; 8.6). Over 2025 (its PDF covers only about the first 40 days), with those points, the result equals Amman's printed minute plus the printed offset on 99.4 % of times (either neighbour of a half-minute) and is never 2 minutes off; the places' own points give 83.1 % (Q19). Jerusalem's entry serves only a place in Jerusalem with `jo_awqaf` chosen; `ps_dar_al_ifta` stays Jerusalem's default.
- **Bosnia and Herzegovina (owner's rule of 2026-10-11).** The Islamic Community's Takvim (2025; no 2026 edition is online) prints Sarajevo's table and, for every municipality (its own words), a monthly «mjesna vremenska razlika»: one figure for Fajr and sunrise, one for Dhuhr, one for Asr, Maghrib and Isha. vaktija.ba serves the same table: its 2026 API equals Sarajevo's table plus the difference on all 2,190 times of Sarajevo, Trebinje and Bihać. The Takvim (pages 8–12) also prints how the differences are made, from Sarajevo at 43°52'N 18°26'E: Δtλ = 4 × (18°26' − λ) min and Δtφ = τ × g × (φ − 43°52'), with τ its Table 1's monthly mean (one row for places north of Sarajevo, one for places south) and g 1.1 north, 0.9 south; Fajr and sunrise take Δtλ + Δtφ, Dhuhr Δtλ, Asr, Maghrib and Isha Δtλ − Δtφ, each rounded to the whole minute. The south row is used with its printed sign, as the printed differences show (Mostar's January Fajr is + 4, not + 1): without it, 1,024 of 1,584 morning values match instead of 1,308. Nothing else is stored, as for Jordan's further places: the profile carries Table 1's 24 means, and each of the Takvim's 110 places (vaktija.ba's 107, plus Bosanska Kostajnica, Teočak and Ustikolina, read from the Takvim's pages in M3) is a `bosnia_iz` zone with `baseZoneId` Sarajevo and a point fitted to its printed differences (a median 3.4 km from the town, at most 13 km; kept as the zone's E6 points, as for Qatar's towns, so the mapping uses the town's own point). For each month the app derives the place's three differences at that point and adds them to Sarajevo's minute. The fitted points give 3,814 of the 3,816 printed BiH values (Visoko and Banja Luka are 1 minute off once each); the towns' own points give 85–98 %. A place in BA takes the nearest of the 110 places, whatever the distance: the Takvim attaches a place to its municipality, and the app has no outlines. In the owner's dataset (support), 45 of the 60 BiH mosques call one place's table on at least half their times, for 40 of them the nearest listed place; five call a larger neighbour's (Zenica from Kakanj and Nemila, Sarajevo from Hadžići and Bukovica, Travnik from Kljaci). Sarajevo's table is perpetual (vaktija.ba's data, last changed on 2024-12-30, for Fajr and Isha only): at the published parameters, computed for 2026, it is exact on 33–86 % of times per prayer and never more than 2 minutes off, so M3 fits `bosnia_iz`'s point, horizon and rounding to it with the AM2 harness at its reference year (E5), and the app computes the actual year (A8). The town differences are the Takvim's, not the method at the town's point (12); «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the method at the user's point.
- **Croatia.** MIZ Zagreb's «Zagrebačka vaktija», which the Croatian Mešihat posts among its «Službene vaktije IZ-e» for the Zagreb area, is its own table, not Sarajevo plus the Takvim's Zagreb difference: on its Ramadan 2025 sheet Fajr is 7–13 minutes earlier and Isha is Maghrib + 80 on all 29 days. `hr_miz_zagreb` is fitted to it in M3 from MIZ Zagreb's Ramadan 2025 and 2020–2021 sheets; its summer Fajr, sunrise − 105 from late March to late August, comes from the dataset's Zagreb mosque (support), whose 2026 calendar equals the Ramadan 2025 sheet on 28 of 29 days, the other being the summer-time day. A Croatian place where Zagreb is the nearest of the Takvim's five Croatian towns takes `hr_miz_zagreb`; elsewhere `bosnia_iz` with the Takvim difference of the nearest of Rijeka, Osijek, Split and Dubrovnik, derived as in Bosnia (fitted points give 179 of the five towns' 180 values), under the validity guard (6.5). The Takvim's Dhuhr is only about 1 minute after its town's transit and its Fajr, Asr and Isha carry no margin, so a place west of its town gets them before its own, and one north or south of it gets the summer Isha or Fajr early. Zadar, 118 km from Split and 1.2° west of it, would get Dhuhr 4–5 min and Isha up to about 6 min before its own; it is settled by the scan with the fitted points (M3), as are Biograd, western Istria (from Rijeka), Slavonski Brod and Požega (from Osijek). In Zagreb's area the guard checks `hr_miz_zagreb` too (Karlovac, 0.4° west of Zagreb, gets Zagreb's times up to about 3 min before its own, within the guard's 4 minutes). No Croatian table outside Zagreb was found (12).
- **Slovenia.** The Islamic Community in Slovenia's vaktija.si (linked from islamska-skupnost.si) gives Ljubljana's table and one fixed offset per time for five places: Celje − 2 to − 4, Jesenice + 1 to + 3, Koper + 2 to + 4, Maribor − 4 or − 5, Novo mesto − 2. It is its own table: against Sarajevo plus the Takvim's Ljubljana difference, its Fajr is about 10 minutes earlier and its Isha about 14 later. `si_islamska_skupnost` computes Ljubljana's times and adds the nearest place's offset on each time, whatever the distance, as for Kuwait. Its feed serves only the current month and the next two (October–December 2026 read): there an angle fit is within 1 minute on 99–100 % of five times but 75 % of Isha, and its Isha equals the Bosnian table's pre-2025 Isha + 18 on 91 of 92 days. The Community's own Ramadan 2020 sheet shows a summer form, Fajr = sunrise − 105 and Isha no later than Maghrib + 90 (24 April to 23 May); the profile takes it until the feed's summer months, read monthly by M0's collectors, confirm or replace it, and M3 fits the profile. The dataset's only Slovenian mosque follows neither table.

- **The UAE.** Awqaf's prayer-times page attaches each of its 319 areas to one of its 41 locations, emirate by emirate (read from the page on 2026-10-11; 317 names, because Delma Island and Umm al-Quwain's Al Ra'fa appear twice; a local copy only). Abu Dhabi has 18 locations (Abu Dhabi city with 31 areas, Al Ain with 27), Dubai 3 (Dubai 59, its inland villages 9, Hatta), Sharjah 7, Ajman 3, Umm al-Quwain 1, Ras al-Khaimah 6 and Fujairah 5; two serve both Sharjah and Fujairah: Dibba (72) and the east coast (88: Khor Fakkan, Kalba and Fujairah's Murbah). Each area is a named place of its location (`namedPlaces`), geocoded in AM2 inside its emirate (a name with several candidates takes the one nearest its location), and each zone's `admin1` lists its emirates. A place in the UAE takes, among the locations serving its emirate (`admin1`, that of the nearest Emirati catalogue place, 6.8), the one with the nearest listed area or own point; for Dibba and the east coast, the areas of both emirates count. Every place gets a location, so `reachKm` does not apply.
  - Kalba (Sharjah) takes 88 as listed, though Fujairah city (73) is 9 km away and Khor Fakkan 30 km; Masfout (Ajman) takes 68, not Hatta's 71 (Dubai), 1.5 km away; Umm al-Zumul takes Al Qua'a (51), about 85 km away. Al Ghadeer, listed under Al Ain (37), is geocoded at the Al Ain place of that name (GeoNames 24.198N 55.608E), the candidate nearest its location.
  - An area that still geocodes more than 60 km from its location's points serves no mapping and is named in the fit report. The one seen is Al Yarmook, a Sharjah city district (GeoNames 25.350N 55.404E, the only place of that name GeoNames has in the UAE) listed under the east coast (88), about 94 km from Khor Fakkan, while the 68 areas of Sharjah's location 91 (the city and its surroundings) take 91; a point there takes 91. An east-coast place of that name, or a server answer of 88 there, would restore it.
  - The page's «الموقع الحالي» sends the user's coordinates and shows the location the server returns (`meta_data.location.official_id`, in the page's own script). The server's rule is not published; the 2026-10-08 research reported the nearest location inside the UAE. AM2 compares this mapping with the Open Data API's answers at sampled points, if that API takes coordinates, once the owner has read its terms; where they differ at a point that is not a listed area, the server's answer wins, stored as a named point of the location it returns (Q13).
  - The dataset cannot settle the attachment (5.6): 7 of its 10 Al Ain-region mosques share one calendar, which matches Al Ain's table (37) on 90 % of times; one of them is 4 km from Al Hayer, which Awqaf lists under Sweihan (63).

- **Oman (2026-10-11).** The ministry computes each of its 86 tables at a zone point, but publishes neither the points nor a rule for the places it does not list (none found in two web searches; its calendar page, read again on 2026-10-11, states neither). Every point was fitted from the ministry's all-zone tables (`calendar_page4.asp`, 9.3) at whole arc-minutes, as its points are rounded: on 25 dates of 2026 each zone has 142–150 of its 150 times exact (98.4 % in all), and on 6 held-out dates of 2027, 2029 and 2031, 97.0 % (95.9–98.1 % per prayer), every time within 1 minute. The six zones fitted earlier on full years agree within 1′, and at the new points their full 2026 tables are 96.5–98.3 % exact, every time within 1 minute. Of the 64 zones whose name OSM places, 54 points lie within 7 km of that town and 6 within 8–13 km; the oil fields, islands and desert stations have no town. Muscat's point is its airport, 32 km from the old town, and three are slips that the tables carry: «الحمرة» is computed at 23°47′N, 74 km north of Al Hamra (23°07′N, same longitude), «رخيوت» at 53°56′E, 55 km east of Rakhyut and offshore, and «قرن العلم» 66 km from the Qarn Alam camp. The app reproduces every table as published. A place in Oman takes, in order:
  1. the zone named after a town within 10 km of the place (`namedPlaces`: the catalogue point of the wilaya seat or town, set in AM2, with `areaKm` 10, 6.5), the nearest if several;
  2. otherwise the nearest zone point, with no reach limit (`reachKm` does not apply).
  - So Al Hamra takes «الحمرة» though Bahla's point is 13 km away, and Rakhyut «رخيوت» rather than «ظلكوت» (19 km), at the cost stated in 12. The wilayat the list leaves out (Seeb, Bawshar, Muttrah, Amerat, Wadi al-Maawil, Dima wa al-Taeen, al-Sunaynah, al-Mazyunah) take their nearest zone; Seeb, Bawshar, Muttrah and Amerat get Muscat's, which 7 of the dataset's mosques there use (about 80–98 % exact; the one in Wadi Kabir, by the old town, 96 %, though Muscat's table runs 2 minutes after the old town's own times). 5 or 15 km in step 1 give the same dataset result as 10.
  - In the owner's dataset, 21 of the 53 Omani mosques follow one zone table at 90 % or more exact, 19 of them the zone this rule gives (28 of 30 at 80 %). The other two are a shared calendar (Al-Abyad, on Barka's table 32 km away, not Nakhal's 1.5 km away) and a mosque near Liwa on Izki's table, 232 km away. Al Hamra's 3 mosques do not use «الحمرة» (35 % exact; Bahla's table or their own point 61–78 %); the published table stands (5.6). AM2 refits the 86 points on full-year `calendar_page2` tables and sets each zone's named town by hand (22 zones, among them the oil fields, islands and desert stations, have none).

- **Iraq's cities (owner rule, 2026-10-11).** The Sunni Endowment puts only Baghdad's table online (dws.gov.iq; its API ignores any place parameter, checked again on 2026-10-11 with Basra's coordinates), but its 2021 review covered «عموم محافظات العراق» (announcement of 2021-04-04), and its directorates hand out printed tables: the Tal Afar directorate handed out «مواقيت الصلاة بنسختها الجديدة طبعة الديوان» on 2022-03-31 (which cities the print covers is not known). Mosque calendars show the other cities computed as Baghdad's is: the method of 8.7 at the city's Accurate Times entry (coordinates, elevation, pressure and temperature, 8.5), for 2021. In the owner's dataset (support only), 26 mosques outside Baghdad use that computation for a city: Mosul 15, Kirkuk 7, Ba'qubah, Fallujah, Tal Afar and Tikrit 1 each. At their city's entry, 96.3 % of their Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr and Isha times are exact at 2021 and 99.8 % within 1 minute (sunrise and Maghrib, on a rough horizon 58–60 % exact and 99.5 % within 1 minute, wait for AM2's horizon fit); computed for 2026 (A8), the entry gives 85.8 % and the mosque's own point 73.5 %. Fallujah's mosque follows Fallujah's entry, not Ramadi's (96.3 % exact against 0), and Tal Afar's its own, not Mosul's: the tables are per city, not per governorate. So `iq_sunni_endowment` has 28 zones, Accurate Times' Iraqi entries outside the Kurdistan Region (Irbil, Dahuk and Sulaymaniyah left out): Baghdad at 46 m, 1008 mbar and 25 °C, the others at their entry's elevation with 1010 mbar and 10 °C. As for Jordan's further places, nothing is stored but the points: the app computes each city as the Endowment does. A place in Iraq outside the Kurdistan Region takes the nearest zone, whatever the distance (`reachKm` does not apply; the Endowment publishes no rule for the places between its cities). This gives 38 of the dataset's 39 followers, Baghdad's 13 included, their own table; the exception, at Al-Buwayr, uses Mosul's though Tal Afar is 5 km nearer. A place in the Kurdistan Region (6.11) gets no zone: the method at its own coordinates, with «فلكية» (Q8). AM2 names the zones in Arabic (بغداد، الموصل، كركوك …) and refits a city's point if its printed table is found.
