- **Albania (owner's rule of 2026-10-11).** KMSH, the Muslim Community of Albania, prints one table, for Tirana (41°20'N 19°49'E), and a whole-minute shift from it for ten cities, the same on all six times: Durrës + 2, Shkodër + 2, Vlorë + 1, Lushnjë + 1, Berat 0, Elbasan − 1, Kukës − 1, Peshkopi − 2, Librazhd − 2, Korçë − 5 (Kalendari 2026, page 2 and each month's footer). `al_kmsh` computes Tirana's times and adds the shift of the nearest of these eleven places (Tirana 0), whatever the distance: KMSH gives no rule for other places. In the south beyond Vlorë, Berat and Korçë (Sarandë, Gjirokastër, Tepelenë, Përmet, Ersekë) the city's June Fajr comes further before the place's 18° dawn than at the city itself (Sarandë 10 min against Vlorë's 8, Përmet 12 against Korçë's 7). Beyond the city's own, that stays within the validity guard's 4 minutes (6.5) at Sarandë, Gjirokastër, Tepelenë and Ersekë, which keep their city's shift; Përmet, about 5 min beyond Korçë's own, is near the threshold and is settled by the scan with the fitted points (M3). The 2026 table is Diyanet's parameters at Tirana's point with the sun taken once a day at 0h UT (E4 `DAILY_0H_UT`) and nearest rounding: 97–99 % exact per prayer, all within 1 minute (with the sun at each event, 21–90 %). Its eleven places abroad (Podgorica + 4, Pejë 0, Dibër and Prizren − 2, Gostivar − 3, Prishtinë and Ohër − 4, Gjilan − 5, Shkup, Kumanovë and Preshevë − 6) are not used: each of those countries has its own default (6.4). In the owner's dataset (support), 7 of Albania's 31 mosques follow this rule on 98–100 % of sunrise, Asr, Maghrib and Isha times (Tirana, Kukës, Koplik), and most others in Durrës, Shkodër and Elbasan on part of them.
- **Kosovo and the Preševo Valley.** BIK's Takvim (the Presidency of the Islamic Community of Kosovo, 2026, which it calls an official document) is one table for Kosovo and the Preševo Valley, computed at 42.5°N 21°E with a 1.5° (6-min) temkin: sunrise 6 min earlier and sunset 6 min later, for Kosovo's highest mountains. Its «Diferenca e vendeve» shifts seven towns on all times: Sharr (Dragash) + 2, Ferizaj, Gjilan, Prishtina, Podujevë and Vushtrri − 1, Preševo − 2; every other place takes the table as printed. The table is hand-made: no angle, point or horizon reproduces it (Dhuhr runs 2.0 to 5.0 min after transit by month, sunrise 3.5 to 9.6 min early), so `xk_bik` computes the six events at 42.5°N 21°E (18°, sunrise, transit, Asr factor 1, sunset, 17°), adds a fitted seasonal offset per time (`seasonalOffsets`, E18: 36 points a year, about one every 10 days, interpolated linearly, added before nearest rounding) and computes each actual year (A8). Fitted on half the 2026 days and tested on the other half, it is 66–82 % exact per prayer, 99–100 % within 1 minute and never more than 2 off. Its Fajr is the takvim's «Sabahu», Imsaku + 20, which BIK names as the start of the Fajr prayer; Imsaku is not shown (A14). A place inside one of the seven towns' areas (radius set in M3 from the town extents; start with 8 km) takes that town's shift; every other place in Kosovo, and in Serbia's municipalities of Preševo, Bujanovac and Medveđa (a point within about 12 km of one of the three towns; M3 sets the radius), takes the table unshifted. In the owner's dataset (support), the 26 mosques of Kosovo and the 3 of the Valley (20 calendars, Peja to Gjilan and Bujanovac) call the table unshifted on 92–100 % of sunrise, Asr and Maghrib times and 84–100 % of Isha times, those in Ferizaj and Gjilan included, whose published shift is − 1, and one in Dragash without Sharr's + 2; the published shifts stand (12). AM0 keeps each year's Takvim in `test-data-local/`.
- **Serbia outside the Preševo Valley.** The Islamic Community in Serbia, whose Mešihat in Novi Pazar belongs to the Islamic Community of Bosnia and Herzegovina, uses the Bosnian takvim: vaktija.ba publishes Novi Pazar, Sjenica, Tutin, Prijepolje, Priboj and Nova Varoš as Sarajevo's times plus a monthly difference per time, and the IZ BiH Takvim's time differences for the mešihats add Beograd, Novi Sad, Subotica, Preševo and Niš (monthly, for the morning, Dhuhr and the evening). A Serbian place there takes `bosnia_iz`'s Sarajevo times plus the published difference of the nearest of these places, whatever the distance, under the validity guard (6.5), as in Croatia: a place west of its town gets Dhuhr, Asr and Isha before its own, one north or south of it the summer Isha or Fajr, and the scan with the fitted points (M3) settles each (Sombor from Subotica, Loznica from Novi Sad, Valjevo from Beograd, Kruševac and Jagodina from Niš). In the owner's dataset (support), Tutin's 3 mosques call vaktija.ba's Tutin on 93 % of sunrise, Asr, Maghrib and Isha times, and a mosque near Novi Pazar the Takvim's Novi Pazar difference on 70 %. Belgrade's Bajrakli mosque, the seat of the other body, the Islamic Community of Serbia, calls Diyanet's Belgrade table instead (`diyanet_eu`: 96–98 % of sunrise, Dhuhr, Asr and Maghrib exact with the sun at 0h UT); no table of that body was found online, so the published Bosnian difference stays the default (12).
- **Montenegro.** The Islamic Community in Montenegro's own takvim was not found online. monteislam.com computes a Podgorica table (19.5° / 17.5°; in October 2026 with Fajr + 1, sunrise − 8, Dhuhr + 1, Maghrib + 8 and Isha + 2, 97–100 % exact on 31 days, Asr 81 %) and shifts eleven towns by whole minutes (Bar, Ulcinj and Pljevlja 0, Nikšić + 1, Herceg Novi + 3, Bijelo Polje − 1, Berane, Petnjica, Plav and Gusinje − 2, Rožaje − 4), but it is a private portal («Prvi Islamski Web Portal u Crnoj Gori»), it names the Islamic Community's takvim as the reference, and none of the dataset's 27 Montenegrin mosques follows it. vaktija.ba publishes five Montenegrin towns as Sarajevo's times with one difference all year (Pljevlja − 3, Bijelo Polje − 5, Gusinje and Plav − 6, Rožaje − 7), within 1 minute of the longitude difference from Sarajevo at 4 min per degree, and the dataset's mosques call Sarajevo's sunrise, Asr and Maghrib with such a shift (Bijelo Polje's 4 at − 5 on 92 % of days; 7 in Podgorica and Tuzi at − 3 or − 4; Herceg Novi and Budva at − 1), with an Isha of their own. So a place in Montenegro takes `bosnia_iz`'s Sarajevo times plus the published difference inside one of the five towns' areas (radius set in M3 from the town extents; start with 8 km, as for Kosovo's towns), and elsewhere the whole minute nearest to 4 × (18.41 − its longitude), computed on the phone (Podgorica − 3), under the validity guard (6.5). That shift has no latitude term and all of Montenegro lies south of Sarajevo, so the summer Fajr comes before the place's own dawn (Nikšić by about 11 min, Podgorica 14, Ulcinj 19): the guard gives every place outside the five towns' areas `bosnia_iz` at its own point, Podgorica included, except the far north around Pljevlja, where that Fajr comes about 4–6 min early, near the threshold, and the scan with the fitted points (M3) settles it. No Montenegrin authority's table was found, so the owner's rule does not settle this; the longitude shift is chosen because 4 of the 5 published differences equal it and Podgorica's mosques use − 3 or − 4, where the nearest listed town, Gusinje, would give − 6. Its zone line drops «الرسمي» (6.11). The Islamic Community's own takvim, once found, replaces this.
- **Bulgaria.** The Chief Mufti's Office publishes «Времена за намаз», a monthly table for each of 48 cities (Aytos to Yambol; read 2026-10-11), with no method or town differences stated. Its October 2026 rows (the city is not labelled; Sofia fits best of the cities tried) are within 3 minutes of Diyanet's method at Sofia's centre on the 7 rows read, Fajr 1–2 min earlier and Maghrib and Isha 2–3 later, so the table is not exactly Diyanet's; the dataset's two Sofia mosques follow Diyanet's method at their own points within 1 minute every day (support). `bg_grand_mufti` gives a place the table of the nearest of the 48 cities, whatever the distance, and starts from `diyanet_tr`'s parameters at the city. AM0 collects a year of the 48 tables (one page per city and month; the page shows the current month), and M3 fits each city's point, the offsets, the sun evaluation (E4) and the rounding on the months collected by then, refitted in an app update once the year is complete (12).
- **North Macedonia.** The Islamic Religious Community (BFI) shows only the current day's Skopje times on bfi.mk («Koha (Shkup)»: Sabahu, Dreka, Iqindia, Akshami, Jacia; no date, dawn or sunrise), and its printed Takvim was not found online. Read on 2026-10-11 they were 05:52, 12:28, 15:31, 18:09 and 19:16: Dhuhr about transit + 7, Maghrib about sunset + 9, Isha near 15°, 11 min before `diyanet_tr`'s 17° (19:27), so `diyanet_tr` does not reproduce them. `mk_bfi` gives the BFI's Skopje times, the only place it publishes, to every place in North Macedonia whatever the distance, with «Sabahu» as Fajr, as for BIK, and `diyanet_tr`'s sunrise, since the site shows none. The BFI does not say these times serve the whole country, so the validity guard (6.5) checks every other place: in December Skopje's 15° Isha comes up to about 4.5 min, and its Asr (shadow + 2) up to about 3.5 min, before the own times of Debar, Struga and Ohrid, and its Isha about 2 min before those of Tetovo and Bitola. So Tetovo, Gostivar, Kičevo, Resen and Bitola keep Skopje's times, as Kumanovo and the east do, and Debar, Struga and Ohrid, near the guard's threshold, are settled by the scan with the fitted point (M3). AM0 saves the page every day (a local copy, D13) and M3 fits the method at Skopje's point from the days collected; until then it starts from the values above and shows «فلكية». The printed Takvim, once found, replaces the collection, and its town differences become zones. In the owner's dataset (support), 11 of the 15 North Macedonian mosques share one calendar that follows neither (Isha 19:32 and Dhuhr fixed at 13:00 on 2026-10-11).
- **Greece.** The muftis of Thrace (Komotini, Xanthi, Didymoteicho) were not searched for a table on 2026-10-11. AM0 looks; a table found replaces `diyanet_tr`, inferred until then, under the owner's rule (6.4).

## 9. Validation and tests

### 9.1 The shared harness

Same layout as the INM harness:

- the math written out in the README's "Shared engine" section (6.2);
- one independent Python reference written from it, `scripts/prayer_formula/prayer_engine_reference.py` (standard library only, UTC output only), with its unittest `test_prayer_engine_reference.py`. It is written from the README, never from the Kotlin;
- Kotlin tests in `multiplatform/shared/src/javaTest` (folders `europe/` and `arab/`) that read `test-data/` through the existing resource folder: `test-data/europe-prayer-times/` and `test-data/arab-prayer-times/`.

Unlike INM, no European body publishes a formula that can be reproduced to the minute, so the European method tests assert tolerances and log the exact-match share. The Arab authority tests measure exact shares against the tiers of 9.5.

**Reference cross-check.** `EngineReferenceCrossCheckTest` runs the engine against the Python reference for every European place of 9.2 with 3–4 method and rule combinations, and for every authority profile at its zones, for actual years (A8):

- the same status, except where the order guard (6.2, Event order) compares two instants less than 1 s apart;
- instants within 1 s;
- the same rounded minute, except within 1 s of the event's rounding boundary (:30 for NEAREST, :00 for UP and DOWN) or of one minute from the lowest point of its night, where the rounding mode changes (6.2).

**Invariants.**

- About 100k grid days: latitudes 35–71°, 2026 and 2099, every method × rule, plus custom methods at the ends of 6.11's ranges (Fajr and Isha intervals of 60 and 120 min, Dhuhr + 10, Maghrib + 10, sunrise − 10), with the steppers at 0 and pushing neighbours together (Fajr + 30 with sunrise − 30, Dhuhr + 30 with Asr − 30, Maghrib + 30 with Isha − 30, Isha + 30 with Fajr − 30) (from M2 in an Arab-first build).
- The order guard alone (6.2, Event order), from AM1, as a function of unrounded instants: each case of the walk (Fajr after sunrise, Asr before Dhuhr, Isha before Maghrib), the night hold on each of its four times and on both sides at once, the rounding within one minute of the lowest point under NEAREST, UP and DOWN (Syria's Fajr DOWN with Isha UP included), the walk on the minutes with mixed modes, rounding ties of instants more than a minute apart under different modes (Fajr up with sunrise down; an Isha rounded up more than a minute before the lowest point beside a Fajr less than a minute after it), and the statuses (a time already ESTIMATED keeps its rule).
- The Arab grid: 13°S to 38°N and 18°W to 60°E, for 2026 and 2099, with every profile and every preset offered at that milestone. Six times in the event order of 6.2 every day, and no time estimated by the D5 rule, a polar fallback or the order guard (the seasonal curve of `mcw`, `london_unified` and `gmp`'s Fajr, and the summer forms of `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost`, may bound times).
- On both: all six times present; the event order of 6.2 on the unrounded instants and on the minutes (no inversion from Fajr(D) to Fajr(D+1); Maghrib(D) and Isha(D) at or before the night's lowest point and Fajr(D+1) and sunrise(D+1) at or after it; only the permitted ties; a time the guard moved ESTIMATED with its rule, a rounding tie with its status unchanged); a `ZonedDateTime` round trip gives the same instant; identical results with the default zone set to Pacific/Auckland, Africa/Tunis or UTC; 2099 asserted in UTC only, because projected zone rules differ between the JDK and Android.

**Time zones.** Summer-time days are asserted as instants, not wall-clock fields:

- 2026-03-29 and 2026-10-25 in London, Lisbon, Paris, Berlin, Helsinki and Kyiv, with Istanbul and Moscow as controls (more in 9.2);
- Africa/Cairo 2026-04-24 and 2026-10-29/30, Asia/Beirut 2026-03-29 and 2026-10-25, Asia/Gaza and Asia/Hebron 2026-03-28 and 2026-10-24; Africa/Casablanca 2026-09-20 (history); Asia/Amman and Asia/Damascus 2022-10-28.

These tests need a JDK whose tzdata is 2026c or newer. A guard test reads the JVM's tz version and fails with a clear message when it is older.

**Date line.** Step 1 of the solver (6.2), with `mwl`, asserted as instants and as the transit's date in the place's zone, in the engine tests and in the reference cross-check (the Python reference is given the zone's standard UTC offset at D's civil noon and derives U from it):

- Wallis-et-Futuna: Mata-Utu (13.28°S 176.17°W, Pacific/Wallis, UTC+12) on 2026-10-13 has U = 10-12; its transit is at about 23:31 UTC on 10-12, 11:31 on 10-13 in the place's zone; Fajr at about 16:09 UTC on 10-12 (04:09 local time) and Maghrib at about 05:42 UTC on 10-13 (17:42). An engine that read D as the UT date would return 10-14's cycle (transit at 11:31 on 10-14 local time), and the test fails on it.
- The opposite side: Shemya in the western Aleutians (52.72°N 174.11°E, America/Adak, UTC−9) on 2026-10-13 has U = 10-14; its transit is at about 00:10 UTC on 10-14, 15:10 on 10-13 local time; Fajr at about 16:52 UTC on 10-13 and Isha at about 07:19 UTC on 10-14. Across Adak's change to UTC−10 on 2026-11-01, the transits of 10-31 and 11-01 fall at about 00:07 UTC on 11-01 and on 11-02, 15:07 and 14:07 local time, each on its own date.
- Kiritimati (1.87°N 157.43°W, Pacific/Kiritimati, UTC+14, U = D−1), and Suva (18.14°S 178.44°E, Pacific/Fiji, UTC+12, U = D) as a control.
- At these four places, every day of 2026: the transit falls on D in the place's zone; consecutive transits are 24 h apart within 1 min, so no cycle is skipped or repeated; the night takes sunset(D−1) and sunrise(D+1) from those neighbouring cycles, and Isha(D) ≤ Fajr(D+1); a method with E4 `DAILY_0H_UT` takes its sun at 0h UT of U (2026-10-12 at Mata-Utu for 10-13).
- A far phone zone: Honolulu (21.31°N 157.86°W, outside both catalogues, so in the phone's zone, 6.8) on a Europe/Paris phone has U = D on every day of 2026, so 03-28 and 03-29, and 10-24 and 10-25, take consecutive cycles (anchored on the civil noon's own offset, 03-29 would repeat 03-28's cycle and 10-24's would be skipped); 03-29's Dhuhr is at about 00:36 on 03-30 and 10-24's at about 00:16 on 10-25, each marked «بعد منتصف الليل» (6.6).

**Tunisia.** All of these stay green:

- `InmPrayerFormulaTest` (565,020 times plus the 2020–2025 sample), `PrayerFormulaSettingsTest` and the M0 fingerprint, all unchanged;
- the 258 × 365 identity of `InmPrayerDays` against `InmPrayerTimes`;
- delegation 615 byte-identical;
- the M0 silence and wake snapshots, compared on trigger instants, with the receiver and override cases recorded beside them (M0, 11). Today's behaviour that they pin stays as it is: an UNSILENCE inside another open window (another prayer's, an Eid window, or one carried over from yesterday) keeps the silence (`SilenceBugsTest.M11`); a window that ends after midnight keeps its silence and end alarm through the 00:01 reschedule (`M14`); the Ramadan Isha override is applied on the Tunisian dates and restored after that Ramadan (`WakeAndPrefsBugsTest.M3`), but not over an Isha edit made in between (the M0 override case, since M3 does not cover it). Expected differences, each listed by date and prayer and reviewed once: the Dhuhr start at 12:40 (D12); D16's rules for fixed starts and ends; and the triggers the scan of 6.9 adds. Those are D's end alarm of a prayer whose D−1 window is still open (today that window's end replaces it until 2 min after, `SilenceScheduler.kt:315–317`, `:359`), a D−1 window that has not started yet (today only a D−1 window that contains now is kept, `:718–730`), a D−2 window still open, and every window of D+1, armed a day ahead (today the next day waits for D's last window to end, `:355–362`), and an endpoint beyond the endpoint domain of 6.9, armed at its clamped instant. The end alarm of an open D−1 window is not a difference: today's code arms it (`:318–329`). Every other trigger instant is identical.
- `check_dashboard_formula.js`.

**Trips (D20)**, in Robolectric: a delegation with auto-location off on a phone moved to `Asia/Riyadh` with a Saudi network gets no notification, and its times stay armed at their instants. With auto-location on, a fix in Mecca moves the place, and a fix back in Tunisia gives its nearest delegation.

**Travel persistence (6.8)**, in Robolectric (`TravelPersistenceTest`), with auto-location on and each fix's network country and phone zone pinned:

- **Across a border, under 3 km.** A fix on the Strasbourg bank of the Rhine (48.570N 7.799E) with an `fr` network, then one on the Kehl bank (48.572N 7.815E) with a `de` network, 1.5 km apart as stored: the place is stored, `onPlaceChanged` loads `method_choice_DE` (DE's default when there is none), `place_admin2` is cleared and one notification is posted. A Haparanda fix (65.836N 24.137E) with a Swedish network and Europe/Stockholm, then a Tornio fix (65.848N 24.147E) with a Finnish network and Europe/Helsinki, 1.2 km apart as stored: country FI, zone Europe/Helsinki, `method_choice_FI`, and one notification.
- **Across an administrative or official-zone limit, under 3 km.** With the French chooser dismissed, two fixes 2 km apart on either side of the line where the nearest FR row changes from a Rhône (69) town to an Isère (38) one: `place_admin2` becomes 38 and the fallback `mdf_12` replaces `rhone_ctir`, with the notification. Two fixes 2 km apart on either side of the midpoint between two Habous localities (`ma_habous`): the mapped zone changes, and the notification names the new one.
- **Same point, new zone.** A place stored from the Haparanda fix with a Swedish network and Europe/Stockholm, then, on `TIMEZONE_CHANGED`, the same fix with a Finnish network and Europe/Helsinki: the rounded point is unchanged, but the resolved country and zone are FI and Europe/Helsinki, so the place is stored and everything rescheduled, with one notification. A fix in East Jerusalem with an `il` network and the phone at Asia/Jerusalem, then the same fix with the phone at Asia/Hebron: zone Asia/Hebron, stored and rescheduled, with no notification.
- **Two Tunisian delegations.** A fix at La Medina's point (403), then one at Bab Bhar's (404), 0.46 km away: the place becomes `Inm(404)` and `onPlaceChanged` reschedules, with no notification. A later fix at Sidi Boumendil (36.7945N 10.1754E), 0.3 km from Bab Bhar's point and still nearest to it, in another neighbourhood, writes only the locality keys: no `onPlaceChanged` and no reschedule.
- **Provisional place.** A skipped first run on a Tunisian phone (Tunis, 615), then a fix inside 615: the place is stored from the fix, `place_provisional` is cleared and «تأكيد الموقع» goes, with no notification.
- **Coordinate-only moves.** A Paris place, then a fix 2.5 km east (2.2 km as stored) with the same country, zone, département and method and no official zone: nothing is stored or rescheduled, and the label stays. A fix 3.5 km east (3.7 km as stored) is stored and rescheduled.

**Silence scan and reboot (6.9)**, in `SilenceWindowPlannerTest` (`:shared:javaTest`) and `SilenceSchedulerRebootTest` (Robolectric, `ShadowAlarmManager`); in an Arab-first build, the London, Tromsø and Local Relative Estimation cases join at M2 (9.2):

- **Scan.** For every 15-minute instant of 2026 at Paris (D5, and `mwl` with Local Relative Estimation), London (middle of the night) and Tromsø, with every prayer at the domain's corners (start at adhan − 720 or + 720, end at adhan + 1,440), a brute-force search of prayer days D−6..D+3 finds no window that contains the instant, or starts before the next 00:01 in the place's zone, outside D−2..D+1; both summer-time days are included, and the armed occurrences of a prayer never share a day slot.
- **A multi-day window after a reboot.** Test prayer days with Isha at 01:07 after midnight, and an Isha window from adhan − 30 to adhan + 1,440. `BOOT_COMPLETED` at 00:30 on day D silences the phone at once (the window of prayer day D−2 runs from 00:37 on D−1 to 01:07 on D; a D−1..D+1 scan would leave it ringing until 00:37), arms that window's end under its own code, and arms D−1's window (00:37 on D to 01:07 on D+1) and every window of D and D+1 under theirs. At 01:07 that UNSILENCE leaves the phone silent, inside D−1's window.
- **Overlapping occurrences.** A Fajr window from adhan − 720 to adhan + 1,440 (36 h, so two occurrences of Fajr are open about 12 h a day). After a reboot inside the overlap, both occurrences keep their own start and end codes, and the earlier one's UNSILENCE leaves the phone silent, since the later one still contains now. Over 8 simulated days of 00:01 runs no armed code is replaced while its window is open, and every UNSILENCE fires at its own window's end. A dismissal while only the earlier occurrence is open leaves the later one to silence the phone at its start; one inside the overlap holds for both.
- **Another zone.** A Tunis place on an `Asia/Dubai` phone, with a Fajr window from adhan − 720: D is the Tunisian date, the daily run is armed at 00:01 Tunis time, and the window of 14 January 2026 (Fajr 06:00) silences the phone from 18:00 on 13 January, Tunis time. With the run at the phone's 00:01 (21:01 in Tunis), the run at 21:01 on 12 January would arm prayer days only up to 13 January, and the phone would ring from 18:00 to 21:01 on 13 January.
- **The domain.** The precision and shared sheets reject a start 721 min from the adhan and an end 1,441 min after it, the steppers stop at the limit, and the slider's edge extension stops at the bound; the Dhuhr window of 6.9 with a fixed end at adhan + 34 h 30 min is reported by the D16 year run; a saved end offset of + 3,000 is armed at adhan + 1,440 and a saved start of − 10,080 at adhan − 720, the stored values are not rewritten, and the editor opens on the clamped values.

**Source-scan and Compose tests.** No Arabic-Indic digits, signed values and percentages isolated, «رسمية» only where 6.11 allows it (never a preset outside its country, exact coordinates or a table no authority publishes), and the explainer opens for Tromsø without crashing.

**CI.**

- android-tests: a `:shared:javaTest` job on a JDK with tzdata 2026c or newer, plus path filters for `multiplatform/shared/**` and `test-data/**`.
- desktop-tests: the Python checks (`inm_prayer_times.py --verify` with its exit code fixed, `test_prayer_engine_reference.py`) and `check_dashboard_formula.js`.
- tv-tests: unchanged.
- Instrumented, on the API 26 and 34 CI emulators: the device's zone rules give the 2026 transitions for Paris, London and Helsinki.

**On device.**

- Only on a dedicated emulator instance started for these checks on port 5570 (`adb -s emulator-5570`), never one that Android Studio runs. With `cmd alarm set-timezone`, `set-time` and a mock location:
  - Europe: Paris today; Brest on 21 June after midnight; crossing 01:00 UTC on 03-29 and 10-25; the phone on London with the place in Paris; Tromsø in June and December; a background relocation on `TIMEZONE_CHANGED`;
  - Arab countries: Muscat, Riyadh in Ramadan, Cairo across the October change, Casablanca, Gaza, Moroni and a Dubai Friday;
  - Tunis against `docs/csv/615`.
- The shown times and the `dumpsys alarm` triggers are compared with the Python reference. Afterwards the zone and clock are restored and the emulator is stopped.
- The OnePlus gets read-only checks and screenshots only, with the owner's OK; its zone and clock are never touched.

### 9.2 Europe

In an Arab-first build (A15), the tests that need an M2 rule or a preset hidden until M2 join at M2: every test that uses a hidden preset (the London scenarios that pick `london_unified`, the Diyanet and `bg_grand_mufti` rows, the `gmp`, `dum_rf`, `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost` table rows, and the Kardzhali, Zagreb, Sisak and Maribor scenarios), every test on a day where an M2 rule acts (D5's alternatives, such as LRE in Paris in June and in London from 23 May to 21 July; Tromsø, Bodø, Murmansk, Luleå, `irn` and `ru_dum_rt`; the change-night tests of one-seventh and middle of the night), the European rule combinations of the reference cross-check (9.1) and the 35–71° grid.

**Astronomy (licence-clean).** `make_europe_fixtures.py` works in three steps:

- `--fetch`, run once by hand through the proxy: JPL Horizons DE441 sun positions, hourly, for 2026 and 2099, plus USNO samples.
- `--derive`, offline, for about 18 places from Valletta to Hammerfest across WET, CET, EET, Istanbul and Moscow:
  - the exact instants of transit;
  - rise and set at −0.8333°;
  - the morning and evening events at 12/13/15/16/17/18°;
  - Asr at factors 1 and 2.
- `--check`: re-derives the fixtures and diffs them.

`EuropeAstronomyFixtureTest` checks the engine against them:

- at most 5 s off on ordinary days, and at most 60 s within 3 days of an event appearing or disappearing;
- the USNO samples within 1 min, for example Oslo on 2026-06-21: rise 03:54, transit 13:19, set 22:44 (UTC+2).

**Methods.**

- The MIT adhan fixtures, with adhan's own conventions set explicitly in the test (its Dhuhr +1, its night, its rounding): London Moonsighting Committee within ±1 min, Ankara Turkey within ±2.
- Turkey (6.5 `DISTRICT`). In the local tests that skip without `test-data-local/` (D13): Diyanet's İSTANBUL, Silivri, Şile and Ankara tables, `diyanet_tr` at their fitted points with E4 `DAILY_0H_UT`, every minute within 1 and at least 98 % exact (98.2–98.5 % on 2026-10-11). `ZoneMapping`: Kadıköy and Bakırköy → İSTANBUL; Sincan and Gölbaşı → ANKARA; a Bahçeşehir point → BAŞAKŞEHİR, though Esenyurt's point is nearer; Gölcük → KOCAELİ; a Turkish point whose district no entry lists → the nearest Turkish entry; Tirana with `diyanet_tr` chosen → the user's coordinates.
- Authority rows, in the local tests that skip without `test-data-local/` (D13): IGGÖ's nine 2026 city tables, April–December (derislam.at shows no earlier 2026 month), each at its zone (6.5), Diyanet's Leibnitz table of 2026-10-11 to 11-10 at its entry's point (6.5), Luxembourg Shoura 2026 (Q37: its June and July Fajr and Isha follow the real 13°, not D5's cap), the IFI's Dublin table and its 33 towns' Ramadan 2026 tables (`ie_ifi`: Dublin's intervals at each town's own sunrise and Maghrib, Q23), KMSH Tirana (`al_kmsh`, the 2026 Kalendari), BIK's 2026 Takvim (`xk_bik`, its «Sabahu» as Fajr), the BFI's collected Skopje days (`mk_bfi`), the Chief Mufti of Bulgaria's city tables (`bg_grand_mufti`, once collected), Sarajevo and vaktija.ba's 2026 tables of Trebinje and Bihać (`bosnia_iz`: Sarajevo plus the derived Takvim difference), MIZ Zagreb's Ramadan 2025 sheet (`hr_miz_zagreb`), vaktija.si's October–December 2026 tables of Ljubljana and Maribor (`si_islamska_skupnost`), a Diyanet Reykjavik/Oslo row for the 5-hour minimum, Diyanet's Strasbourg table (`diyanet_eu`, the first entry in Bas-Rhin, D4), the CTIR 2020 table (`rhone_ctir`, on its 2020 dates, the reference year of A8), APBIF's 2026 Paris tables of June, July, October and December (`apbif`, within 1 min on all six times, 7.5), the CIR's tables of Ramadan 1444, 1445 and 1446 and Chawwal 1445 and Baytoul Hayaate's Ramadan 1447 copy in their four regions (`re_cir`, Fajr and Maghrib only, since the CIR publishes no Dhuhr, Asr or Isha; used for the daily formula, not as a Ramadan rule) and the Bras-Fusil display of 12–17 October 2026 (`re_cir`'s Isha), the 2026 table of horaires-priere-mayotte.fr, a private site's copy of the island's traditional table, since no authority's table was found (`yt_traditional`, the five prayers only, A1, Q36), the Grande Mosquée de Paris's October 2026 timetable (`gmp`, Q21), DUM RF's Moscow tables of May, July, August and October 2026 and June and July 2025 (`dum_rf`, Q22), IRN's 2026 tables (`irn`: Kristiansand, Oslo, Bergen and Trondheim in the south, Steinkjer and Vefsn, Tromsø, Bodø and Svalbard) and DUM RT's 2026 workbook (`ru_dum_rt`: Kazan, Naberezhnye Chelny, Almetyevsk, Bugulma, Agryz, Nurlat, Bolgar and Aktanysh).
- Tolerances:
  - fixed-angle methods ±1 min;
  - `rhone_ctir` ±2: the best whole-minute profile is within 1 min on 98–100 % of days for Fajr, Dhuhr and Asr, but on only 80–85 % for sunrise, Maghrib and Isha, which drift with the season in the table (7.2, Q33);
  - Diyanet-type ±2 (Strasbourg included), but ±1 on IGGÖ's nine tables and Diyanet's Leibnitz table, Fajr and Isha outside May–August (7.5);
  - `luxembourg_shoura` ±4 on Fajr and Isha until its fit is settled (its table runs 0–4 min inside the computed 13° all year, Q37), skipping its misprinted rows (Maghrib on 27–28 March, Dhuhr on 2 October, Fajr on 25 October, and 18–19 November printed as October);
  - `dum_rf` ±2 on all six times on the 2026 sheets and June 2025, and ±3 on July 2025's Isha, whose ramp differs from 2026's;
  - `irn` ±1 on Fajr, Dhuhr, Maghrib and Isha south of 63.56°N with each entry's switch days (its `switchDays` where stored, else the fitted rule's, 6.5), ±2 on every time from 63.56°N and on sunrise and Asr everywhere, with the switch days counted and reported apart;
  - `ru_dum_rt` ±1 on all six times (the dawn and zenith columns, not the mosques' columns), its switch nights (at most two a year per place) counted apart;
  - `re_cir` ±1 on the CIR's columns, each from its region's zone at the row's actual date (A8; exact on 86–93 % of Fajr rows and 78 % of iftar rows, 7.6), and ±1 on the Bras-Fusil Isha at the mosque's own point («حسب موقعي بالضبط»), since a managed display computes for its mosque;
  - `gf_a2cmgf` ±1 on A2CMGF's January 2026 Cayenne and Remire-Montjoly calendars (exact on at least 85 % of days per time) and on the Cayenne mosque's October 2026 page, and `mq_ccim` ±1 on the October 2026 IslamicFinder page the CCIM links (7.6), all local copies;
  - `yt_traditional` within 2 min on at least 90 % of days per prayer, and never more than 4 off, on the site's 2026 table (today 91–98 % and 4), with its exact share logged: the table's 5-day blocks leave a daily formula exact on 25–39 % of days, and its sunrise column, the table's own and on average about 5 min after the real sunrise, is not compared (Q36);
  - `gmp` ±1 on its October 2026 timetable (Q21);
  - `al_kmsh` ±1 on the 2026 Kalendari, with its exact share logged (97–99 % today);
  - `xk_bik` ±2 on the 2026 Takvim (99–100 % within 1 today), with its exact share logged;
  - `bg_grand_mufti` ±3 and `mk_bfi` logged only, until their M3 fits;
  - `bosnia_iz` ±2 on Sarajevo's table until its M3 fit (2026 at the published parameters: 33–86 % exact per prayer, never more than 2 off), with its exact share logged; the derived town differences equal the printed ones on at least 3,814 of the 3,816 BiH values and 179 of the 180 Croatian;
  - `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost` ±2 until their M3 fits settle them, with the exact share logged;
  - estimated Fajr and Isha (takdir in summer, MCW against the London table, LRE) ±10.
- Full tables, if kept, stay in the git-ignored `test-data-local/` with an optional, skippable test.
- Norway's IRN tables are asserted for `irn` in all three of its zones, in the local tests (D13). With a fixture southern entry (no table needed) whose `switchDays` hold Isha 90..251 and no Fajr range: Isha is at 15° on days 89 and 252 and on the summer rule on 90 and 251, Fajr follows the fitted rule's days, and in 2028 day 90 is still 31 March; with a Fajr range 103..238, Fajr on day 103 is held at the clock time of day 102's 16° Fajr, from 21 June at that of day 239's, and is at 16° again on day 239. Mapping: a GPS fix at Mortensrud (Oslo) takes Oslo's entry through the Ensjø row, though Lørenskog's entry point is nearer; a fix in Fillan takes Hitra's entry and its Mecca cycle, not Orkland's southern table through Orkanger, the nearest `cities5000` row, 55 km away; Stjørdal keeps the southern rule and Hitra the Mecca cycle; a Svalbard place takes the Svalbard entry (the mapping; the validity guard then gives Ny-Ålesund `irn` at its own point, 9.6). Russia: Balashikha (Moscow oblast, about 20 km east of the centre) takes the Moscow table, and Lotoshino `dum_rf` at its own point by the validity guard (6.5; DUM RF's Maghrib about 6.6 min before its sunset), with no verdict asserted for Dubna, Kolomna or Shatura, near the guard's threshold; Kaipy (Laishevo district) takes Laishevo's DUM RT table, its nearest DUM RT place, not Kazan's (the mapping; the guard's verdict is left to the scan, 6.5); Nizhny Novgorod gets `mwl`.

**Vectors.**

- Moonsighting at 51.5°N:
  - 21 June: Fajr 119.73 min before sunrise, Isha (general) 80.44 min after sunset.
  - 21 December: 101.83 and 98.97.
- Angle-based: Paris Isha 17° on 21 June = sunset + 133 min (17/60 of the 469-minute night from sunset to sunrise on 22 June, Q20), not 189.
- Summer forms: Zagreb on 2026-06-21 under `hr_miz_zagreb`, Fajr = sunrise − 105; Ljubljana on 2026-05-10 under `si_islamska_skupnost`, Fajr = sunrise − 105 and Isha = Maghrib + 90; Riyadh on 2026-06-21 under `hr_miz_zagreb`, Fajr at its angle time (about sunrise − 94), not sunrise − 105.

**Edge cases.**

- **Summer-time days.** One-seventh and middle-of-night on the change nights measured from instants (wall clock would be 8.6 and 30 min off). The armed instants asserted.
- **Other civil days.** With `mwl` and no rule (or an absent-nights rule): Paris Isha at 01:07 and Brest at about 01:21 on 22 June (with D5's default, Paris at 00:11, 7.3); Kazan's 18° Fajr of 5 May at 23:53 on 4 May; Vorkuta's Fajr of 1 April. All flagged.
- **Absent twilight.** With no rule, the mornings without −18° equal the Python reference exactly (Paris, London, Berlin, Oslo, Tromsø), and are within ±1 at each boundary of the Horizons-derived fixtures.
- **Polar and Asr.**
  - Tromsø's midnight sun (18 May–25 July) and polar night (28 November–14 January): six ordered times, the substituted ones estimated, never throws.
  - Murmansk on 2026-12-21 under `mwl`: a 5-hour day centred on Dhuhr, Asr at the Dhuhr–Maghrib midpoint, all estimated times marked. Rovaniemi on 2026-12-21 under `mwl`: a 5-hour day with its real Asr (about 0.5 min after transit) kept. Luleå on 2026-06-21 under `mwl`: its real Maghrib at 00:07 is kept (no day without a sunset there). Bodø on 2026-12-21 under `diyanet_eu`: Asr = Dhuhr.
  - `irn` in Tromsø on 2026-06-21 and 2026-12-21, and in Oslo on 2026-04-13, 2026-06-21 and 2026-08-27: within 2 min of IRN's tables (local tables, D13).
  - On a midnight-sun day, a real Asr is not replaced.
  - **Winter Asr boundary** (6.2, Event order). Rovaniemi on 2026-12-21 (transit 10:15:07 UTC, real Asr 10:15:40): under `mwl` Dhuhr 10:15 and Asr 10:16, both COMPUTED; under `tunisia_18_18` Dhuhr and Asr 10:22, Asr ESTIMATED (`HELD_IN_ORDER`, with its real time and Dhuhr + 7 named in `explain()`). At 66.53°N 25.73°E the same day under `mwl`, Dhuhr and Asr both 10:15 (real Asr 10:15:25), both COMPUTED. At Rovaniemi on every day from 2026-12-08 to 2027-01-03 (Asr less than 7 min after transit) under `mwl`, `tunisia_18_18`, `diyanet_eu` (Asr + 4 against Dhuhr + 5) and a custom method with Dhuhr + 10: no inversion, and every tie a permitted one with its status. They join at M2, since the 5-hour day acts at Rovaniemi in December.
  - **Short nights and fixed intervals** (6.2, Event order). Luleå on 2026-06-21 (sunset 00:06:48 and sunrise 00:59:48 CEST on 22 June, lowest point 00:33:18): `ecfr_90` gives Maghrib 00:07, Isha 00:33, Fajr of 22 June 00:33 and sunrise 01:00, Isha and Fajr ESTIMATED (`NIGHT_LOWEST_POINT`; Maghrib + 90 would be 01:37, and sunrise − 90 23:30 on 21 June); `umm_al_qura` gives Isha 00:33 (held) and keeps its D5 Fajr, 00:44. On every night of 2026 at Luleå, Oulu (65.01°N) and Akureyri (65.68°N), under `ecfr_90`, `umm_al_qura` (with a Ramadan evening, Maghrib + 120), `fajr15_isha90`, `be_sunrise_90`, `gmp`, `ru_dum_rt` and custom methods with intervals of 60 and 120 min, with the steppers at 0 and at Isha + 30 with Fajr − 30: no inversion, Maghrib and Isha at or before the lowest point, Fajr and sunrise at or after it, and no real angle time moved. These join at M2 with the other Luleå tests.
- **The gate.** On every night where the angle is reached, an *absent nights* rule returns exactly the angle time, and Tunis in June is unchanged.
- **Continuity.** For every *absent nights* rule, estimated times change by at most 5 min a day plus the sunset drift at 48.6, 52, 55 and 60°N.

**App (Robolectric unless noted), `EuropeScenarioTest`.**

- **Paris** (`mwl` with Local Relative Estimation chosen, which keeps the real 17° Isha; under D5's default Paris Isha is 00:11, 7.3).
  - In June, Isha at 01:07 is silenced, is still "next" at 00:30, and its window survives the 00:01 reschedule.
  - A one-time "Isha − 10" wake alarm set at 00:30 rings at 00:57 that night.
  - An "after Isha" hifz plan chosen at 00:30 starts tonight, labelled «الليلة».
  - At 02:00 on 22 June the night dial highlights the right segment.

A `rhone_ctir` pick made in Lyon is still the choice in Paris, computed at Paris, while Tarare (69) shows Lyon's times under «جدول: الرون». A chooser dismissed in Paris gives `rhone_ctir` in Lyon, and one dismissed in Lyon gives `mdf_12` back in Paris, with «تأكيد الطريقة» still shown. No mosque is bundled or listed. The envelope (E19): a 12° practice with the summer cap at Lille on 21 June shows Fajr at the 12° instant, marked «تقديري»; Diyanet's Strasbourg Fajr on 21 June 2027 moves to the 12° instant; `gmp` on 21 June keeps its Isha (Maghrib + 87, before 12°), marked «تقديري», and a custom method with Isha 5 min before the curve moves to the curve; a Dhuhr 2 min before transit + 1.2 moves to it, one 0.6 min before stays; a Maghrib at sunset + 12 stays (E19 has no Maghrib upper bound; the editor's input stops at + 10), and one 2 min before sunset + 1 moves to it; `mdf_12` at Lille on 21 June shows the real 12° Fajr and Isha, and with the cap chosen keeps its Fajr at the 12° instant and takes the capped Isha, marked «تقديري»; no time at a Tunisian place, nor a preset's or profile's at a Belgian or Réunion place, is touched by E19 (a custom method is checked anywhere, 6.11). A custom method with «العشاء ليس قبل» 19:00 at Ivry-sur-Seine in December shows Isha at 19:00, not marked «تقديري». `apbif` at Paris, each time within 1 min of APBIF's 2026 table: on 13 October Fajr 06:29, sunrise 08:06, Dhuhr 13:43, Asr 16:38, Maghrib 19:13 and Isha 20:14; on 21 June, a night without 18°, Fajr 01:58 (solar midnight + 6), marked «تقديري»; on 10 June the 18° Fajr, 02:17; no ramp at the season's edges (Fajr 02:02 on 12 June, 01:57 on 13 June, 02:00 on 30 June, 02:08 on 1 July); E19 moves none of its times. A Maghrib stepper of − 6 on `mdf_12` (Maghrib at sunset) is held at sunset, marked «تقديري»; a Fajr stepper of + 30 on a 12° method at Paris in June stays; a sunrise stepper of + 5 on `mdf_12` is held at the computed sunrise, marked «تقديري».

Zagreb and Sisak get MIZ Zagreb's table under «جدول: زغرب», Split `bosnia_iz` with the Takvim's Split difference, with no verdict asserted for Zadar (Split's Dhuhr 4–5 min and Isha up to about 6 min before its own, near the validity guard's threshold) or Sombor (from Subotica) until the scan settles them (6.5), and with no method picked a move from Zagreb to Split changes the method. Maribor gets Ljubljana's vaktija.si times − 4 on Fajr and sunrise and − 5 on the other four.
- **A fixed end that does not fit (D16).** A Dhuhr end of 13:00 saved at a Tunisian delegation when Dhuhr was at 12:25 (adhan + 35), then used at a Paris place on a July day with Dhuhr at 13:55: the start is 14:00, 13:30 (the + 30 cap) is still before it, so the window is 14:00–14:30. On a day where 13:00 + 30 min fits, the capped end is used instead.
- **Default Dhuhr and Jumu'a end (D12).** With no `SilenceDefaults` entry for the country, Paris, Madrid and Warsaw in January, and London in July, each get a 40-minute Dhuhr window (adhan + 5 to adhan + 45) and a 45-minute Friday window from the Jumu'a time with the default config, and no D16 notice. With a test entry for the country, and one for a place inside it, the Friday window starts at that entry's time (never before Dhuhr + 5) and ends 45 min later, the place's entry winning; a seasonal entry switches value with summer time, and its `AT_DHUHR` season starts at Dhuhr.
- **London.**
  - On 21 June with middle of the night, Fajr(21) is silenced at 01:02 with no ring gap after Isha(20).
  - A London place on a Paris phone: its times are shown in Paris time and silenced at their instants, and it has no Eid row (A10).
- **Places east of the phone.**
  - A Moscow place on a Paris phone, and a Tunis place on an `America/New_York` phone (Fajr on the previous evening in the phone's clock, marked «قبل منتصف الليل»), are silenced at the right instants.
  - The awake check after a "Fajr + 10" wake alarm on the New York phone counts as inside Fajr's window.
- **Across the date line** (6.2, Solver, step 1).
  - A Mata-Utu place (Pacific/Wallis) on Friday 2026-10-16: on a Pacific/Wallis phone the list shows that Friday's times, none marked «بعد منتصف الليل» or «قبل منتصف الليل»; with no `SilenceDefaults` entry for WF, the Jumu'a window is armed from its Dhuhr (A18), 11:30 local time, 23:30 UTC on 10-15, and none from Saturday's Dhuhr (23:30 UTC on 10-16). On a Europe/Paris phone the same instants are armed, and Fajr of 10-16 shows at about 18:07 on 10-15, marked «قبل منتصف الليل».
  - `umm_al_qura` at Mata-Utu: Isha at Maghrib + 90 on 2026-02-17 and + 120 from 2026-02-18 in the place's zone, Umm al-Qura's 1 Ramadan 1447 (E8); that evening's Isha is at about 08:14 UTC (20:14 local time).
  - A Shemya place on an America/Adak phone has Dhuhr of 2026-10-13 at about 00:10 UTC on 10-14 (15:10 local time), and its Dhuhr window is armed from 00:15 UTC (D12).
- **Tunisian delegation on a Paris phone.** It rings at INM's instants. On a July Friday its Dhuhr and Jumu'a windows end at 13:15 Tunis time, 14:15 in Paris (D12).
- **Tromsø.** In June and December, and on a fallback day where two times are under 30 min apart, UNSILENCE does not cut the next window.
- **Summer-time days.** Both days, including a user offset at 02:30 on 10-25.
- **Travel.**
  - A pre-prayer fix moving Paris → London recomputes the wake triggers.
  - Changing the default zone with the screen open changes the Isha shown.
  - Changing an adjustment invalidates the adhkar cache.
- **Adhkar.** The reading key at 00:30 belongs to the previous evening. On a day when Asr equals Dhuhr (a stubbed `PrayerDay` from M6 / AM6; with the engine from M2, Bodø on 2026-12-21 under `diyanet_eu` and Rovaniemi on 2026-12-21 under `tunisia_18_18`), a Fajr-relative reminder and an evening reminder from Asr to Maghrib are armed as on other days, a period from Dhuhr to Asr has no nudge that day and is no error, and the 365-day validation lists the day without failing the rule; a period from Dhuhr + 10 to Asr, not ending the next day, fails from the first date its end comes before its start, as any seasonal failure does (6.9).
- **Calendar (A10, A11).**
  - `umm_al_qura` in Paris has the Ramadan Isha (Maghrib + 120) on the evenings of Umm al-Qura's Ramadan 1447, from 2026-02-18 (E8).
  - A Paris place in Ramadan gets no Isha silence bump, and no Eid window on Eid day; delegation 615 gets both, on the Tunisian dates.
  - The override across a trip, with the user's Isha end offset of 45 min and the test's own 1448 dates: applied at delegation 615 on 10 Ramadan 1448 (90 stored, the offset removed and saved); moved to Paris on 20 Ramadan, Paris's Isha window ends at adhan + 45 and the stored keys are unchanged; back at 615 on 25 Ramadan, the window is 90 min; in Paris on 5 Shawwal, also with auto-silence off through the Application's call, the end offset 45 is restored. An Isha edit in Paris on 20 Ramadan drops the saved copy and holds at 615 for the rest of that Ramadan.

### 9.3 Collecting official tables (Arab countries, and Europe's daily and monthly sources)

Collectors are small read-only scripts that go through the normal proxy, never bypassing the sandbox or TLS checks, and are politely paced. One-off and yearly collectors are run by hand. The daily ones (Libya, Egypt's daily page, Gaza's radio posts and widget, QCH's `/cal` for Qatar's six places, and in Europe bfi.mk's Skopje times, 8.8) and the monthly ones (Morocco, Yemen's endpoint, Gaza's monthly images when posted, and in Europe the Chief Mufti of Bulgaria's 48 city pages and vaktija.si's feed, 8.8) run from a Windows scheduled task on the owner's PC, which the owner sets up. Most of these sources show only today or the current month, so a missed run is lost for good (vaktija.si also shows the next two months, and Yemen's endpoint any date from 2026-05-21). Only Tripoli can be recovered, up to 7 days back from api.ifta.ly; older Libyan days exist only where the Wayback Machine has them. Each run writes its manifest (URL, fetch date, SHA-256, the offset in force), and the owner looks for gaps once a week. Raw files go to the git-ignored `test-data-local/official/<cc>/`. What may be committed follows A7.

| CC | Source | Range available | Cadence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SA | KACST API, any lat/lon/year | any (past years recomputed with today's rule) | one-off: 20 cities × 3 years, at least one in each of the Kingdom's 13 regions (9.5) | also the API's `eid` field |
| EG | esa.gov.eg: `praytimes.aspx` (GET, today, all cities); the monthly view (an ASP.NET form postback) | today; current year | daily GET for a year; monthly once the postback is allowed | the monthly view is a form submission: owner's go-ahead first. The Wayback Machine holds the daily page on 48 days (2022-03 to 2026-09), a plain GET for backfill. The 4 menu cities missing from the all-cities table (Menouf, Diyarb Negm, Abu Tisht, Samalut, 8.8) need the same form. Coordinates per city must be fitted, on 2025 onward until the other 2024 Wayback days date why the 2024 summer copies do not fit (Q9). EGSA's Eid times. |

Record the `season` field (3 until 2026-07-14, 4 from 07-15), which moves no time; the first winter rows (December–February) are to confirm one rule all year (Q2) |

DJ: the unified adhan schedule of the Ramadan 2026 circular (Directorate of Islamic Affairs, or the Supreme Islamic Council in the Ministry of Islamic Affairs and Waqfs). KM: the Mufti of the Republic, or the Ministry of Justice (Islamic affairs), or a printed copy of the traditional Moroni table the mosques use (Q16). **Ramadan 1448** (expected to start about 2027-02-08) is the key window: Saudi Isha + 30; Qatar's Maghrib + 1, which the Ministry API already applies from 2027-02-07, its own 1 Ramadan and a day before Umm al-Qura's, so the minute Doha mosques call that evening and at the month's end checks `qa_awqaf`'s days (Q3); the Syrian, Mauritanian and Palestinian imsakiyas; Kuwait's Ramadan tables (Bahrain's 1448 calendar, in hand, has no Ramadan change); Yemen's first Ramadan in its endpoint. The collectors must be running by mid-January 2027.

### 9.4 Arab countries: fitting harness

`scripts/prayer_formula/fit_authority.py` (standard library only) generalises the INM method, as the sun-model study laid out. It grows out of the prototype in `scripts/prayer_formula/arab_prototype/` (8.6).

1. **Stages.** Dhuhr first (sun model, evaluation, offset, longitude), then sunrise and sunset (horizon, dip, latitude), then Fajr and Isha (angle or interval), then Asr (factor, declination instant, geometric or apparent sun).
2. **Windows, not least squares.** Each published minute bounds the unrounded time to a half-open minute. Invert that into a window per parameter, and keep the value covered by the most windows. Rounding up or down is "nearest after a shift of ± 0.5", so the rounding mode falls out of the offset window.
3. **Grid.** Sun model (Meeus 1900 as in INM, Meeus ch. 25, NOAA, USNO, VSOP87 with Delta T as in Accurate Times, 8.5, and the TEXTBOOK sun of Libya's table, E14 and 8.2); evaluation (event instant, daily 0h UT, daily 10h UT, KACST, Asr's noon shadow at 00:00 local time, transit); rounding per event; horizon model; reference year. Where ICOP has a preset (8.5), the harness tries it first as a starting hypothesis (Morocco's elevation dip plus Maghrib + 2, for example), and the fit report says whether the official table confirms it. For Jordan and the UAE, the fit also applies D17.
4. **Zones.** Fit each zone's point and horizon jointly across zones that share one rule set; for Jordan and the UAE, fit the eastern point and the morning elevation jointly, so Fajr is not traded for sunrise (8.6). Start Oman's zones from the points fitted on 2026-10-11 (8.8), at whole arc-minutes. Flag zones whose residuals point to a data error (Egypt's Taba, Mahalla and Kharga; Libya's Jalo and Awjila Asr).
5. **Report.** Exact share per prayer; every miss with its margin to the event's rounding boundary (:30 for NEAREST, :00 for UP and DOWN); a sensitivity check (move each parameter until exactness breaks). Documented quirk days are counted apart. Held-out years, months or zones are never used for fitting the parameters they score. For each zone and for the profile, the harness writes the `FitStats` of 6.3 (the held-out shares per time, the held-out days per month, the Ramadan and quirk days, and each sample's source, period and zones), from which 9.5 decides the chip; where only one official year is in hand, or the table is perpetual, it also refits each zone on the odd months and on the even months and scores each fit on the months it left out (a month-split).

The output is the profile and zone data of 8.7 and 6.5, written as JSON, plus a README part per authority.

### 9.5 Arab countries: acceptance

| Tier | Countries | Gate and resulting chip |
|---|---|---|
| A | Any country whose zone passes the gate (owner, 2026-10-17): today Saudi Arabia, Egypt (78 of 78 zones), Oman (86 of 86), Jordan (15 of 33), the UAE (8 of the 9 locations with a table), Bahrain, Iraq (Baghdad) and Syria (its 99 table zones) | Per zone, on that zone's own held-out official data with the coverage below (all six times, every month, a whole Ramadan for an E8 method), leaving out documented quirk days (8.3; for SA KACST's previous-date days of A8 and 8.7): at least 97 % exact per time and 100 % within 1 minute. Target 99 %. «طريقتك» shows a passing zone's record at the places mapped to it (6.11); the chip, «رسمية» at every official zone, does not depend on it (A6). `sa_umm_al_qura` is scored on its profile record (below). |
| B | The others today: KW, PS, SY, MR, LY, BH, QA, MA, YE, the rest of IQ (Sunni), LB (Dar al-Fatwa), DZ (its reference rows reach 94–95 % per reference over a full year, below the gate) | 100 % within 1 minute (DZ: on all 70 places, the derived offsets included; LY: Jalo and Awjila's Asr through their + 8; LB: 99.7 % of its sunrises, as printed, A8). Chip «رسمية» at their zones, as in tier A (6.11). |
| C | SD, SO, DJ, KM | No official test. They are checked against the owner's mosque dataset (A3, 5.6). |

- **Coverage (2026-10-13).** The tier-A gate is passed per zone, on the zone's own record (`OfficialZone.fit`, 6.3), never on the profile's aggregate (`sa_umm_al_qura` aside, under Zone below). `FitStats.passesTierA(method)`, a pure function in the shared module, is true only when all of these hold; the harness reports the same verdict, and the chip rule (6.11) calls it on the mapped zone:
  - **Held-out data**: the authority's own official days, never used to fit the parameters they score: another year of its table or, where only one year is in hand or the table is perpetual (Jordan's 2023 calendar, Qatar's tables), the months a month-split left out (9.4). A perpetual table's held-out days are scored as the app shows them: as printed, at the table's reference year (A8, revised 2026-10-17). ICOP's tables (8.5) never count. The owner's mosque dataset (5.6) counts only through a follower (owner, 2026-10-17): a mosque whose 2026 calendar reproduces the zone's official table on at least 20 official days, at least 98 % of its minutes exact and none more than 1 minute off, stands in for that table on its other days of 2026, a held-out body of the zone's record (`source` "mosque_follower"; per zone the follower with the most official days; days printed on a wrong clock, every differing time a whole number of hours off, are quirk days). The official days stay the fit's, and the dataset is read in place, never copied. Where an authority's zones are one table and whole-minute offsets (Kuwait), the year counts for every zone. Where followers keep their own times outside the official days (Ireland's mosques, their own Isha from May to August), their years do not count. `fits/mosque_years.py` and each fit's `mosque_year.py` apply it; the README's "A year from the mosques" lists the results.
  - **Times**: all six (Fajr, sunrise, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib, Isha), each at least 97 % exact and 100 % within 1 minute. A zone whose official data lack one of them does not pass: Qatar's three printed towns need a year of `/cal` days for the four times the print lacks.
  - **Season**: for each time, at least 7 held-out days in each of the 12 calendar months, so a winter sample or one official month never passes.
  - **Ramadan**: for a method with E8 rules (`sa_umm_al_qura` and both Qatari profiles), a whole held-out Ramadan (`heldOutRamadanDays` 29 or more, counted before quirk days are left out).
  - **Zone**: only the zone's own days count. A zone with `baseZoneId` shows the chip only when its base zone's record passes too, which the chip rule checks (6.11); its own record passes when its own times, from offsets derived at points fitted without the month scored, equal the base zone's official minute plus the printed offset (either neighbouring minute where the sum ends in a half-minute) under the same thresholds. `sa_umm_al_qura`, whose chip applies at any Saudi point (6.11), is scored on its profile record, whose held-out points include one in each of the Kingdom's 13 regions (9.3).

  A zone with no record, or one that fails, keeps «رسمية» (A6), and the explainer shows its record, or the profile's aggregate where it has none (6.11). Every share reported against the gate names its held-out period, its days and its zones.
- Quirk days are counted and reported separately in the fit report. They never lower a zone's chip.
- Qatar's gate is measured per zone: Doha's table, the three printed towns, Mesaieed and Halul, each on its own held-out days. Today the three towns rest on 59 printed days of August to October 2026 (Fajr and Maghrib only) and Mesaieed and Halul on two `/cal` days, so they, and the places attached to them (Ras Laffan and Al Ghuwariyah to Al Shamal, Umm Bab and Al Jumayliyah to Dukhan, Al Kharrara to Abu Samra, Sealine and Khor Al Udaid to Mesaieed; 8.8), stay below the gate at least until a year of QCH pages and `/cal` days covers them and their own records pass (9.3).
- Algeria is measured on all 70 places: the derived offsets against 1448's printed grids and the held-out 1447 Algiers grids (at least 99.5 % and 98.5 % exact, never more than 1 minute; local data, D13), and the final times against the printed calendar (100 % within 1 minute). Tizi Ouzou's and Relizane's knife-edge Dhuhr months are logged; Tamanrasset's and In Guezzam's June Asr follow the signed shadow, also at «حسب موقعي بالضبط» and at a point the guard computes on its own, while `dz_marw` chosen in Mecca in June keeps the shadow rule; Zeralda and Rouiba (unlisted, about 20 km west and east of Algiers) keep Algiers' printed times, about 1 min off their own and within the validity guard's tolerance (6.5), and the 70 listed places keep the printed calendar. With the computed Umm al-Qura month starts in place of the ministry's (until the year's dates ship), the derived offsets match about 96 % of the printed ones and every time stays within 1 minute of the printed calendar (8.8, 12).
- Jordan's gate is measured per zone on its 15 zones, and each of its 18 further places has its own record (Coverage, above): the derived monthly offsets against the printed ones, and the times against Amman's official minute plus the printed offset (8.8). Its official held-out days are 31 zone-days of 1 January to 8 February 2025 (7 zones on one day, 8 on three; about 590 zone-days to 9 February once AM2 transcribes the rest), so every month also comes from a month-split of the 2023 calendar until another official year is in hand (8.6). In-sample, 9 of the 15 zones are below 97 % on some time (Fajr 78.6 % at Karak), and the further places' times are not yet scored on held-out months (their offsets give 595 of 648 there), so those zones and the further places stay below the gate until their own records pass.
- IQ (Sunni) is a perpetual table computed for 2021 (8.3). Shown as printed since A8's revision (2026-10-17), Baghdad's zone passes the gate: 98.4–100 % exact held out over the twelve months, all within 1 minute; the 27 other cities have no official data. Its 2027 table, held out, confirms the reference year or shows a revision.
- A profile or zone below its tier's gate, or without the coverage above, still ships, never hidden, and «طريقتك» shows its record as it stands. Several of today's fits are below the tier-A gate (8.1), and most zones are not yet scored over a whole year (12).

### 9.6 Arab countries: tests

The Kotlin tests read the output of the shared Python reference in `test-data/arab-prayer-times/`, and the official tables in the git-ignored `test-data-local/` when it is present (D13, A7).

- **Authorities**: per profile, the exact share and the maximum deviation on the local full tables, in a test that skips when they are absent, so CI never needs them; with the tables present, each zone's shipped `FitStats` (6.3) is recomputed from them and must match (shares, days per month, Ramadan days, periods).
- **The tier-A gate and the chips** (6.11, 9.5), with synthetic records: `passesTierA` holds for a record at 97 % exact per time, all within 1 minute, with 7 held-out days in every month; it fails with one month at 6 days, with one of the six times absent, with one 2-minute miss, for an E8 method without a whole held-out Ramadan, and for `sa_umm_al_qura` with one of the 13 regions missing. At places (A6): an authority's zone in its own country shows «رسمية» whatever its record; exact coordinates, a zone in another country, a table no authority publishes, Bosnia's table in Montenegro, every preset and a custom method show «فلكية»; `sa_umm_al_qura` shows «رسمية» anywhere in Saudi Arabia.
- **Extensions**:
  - E1 rounding on boundary cases;
  - E2 horizon models against hand-computed values, and each `PerZone` and `Refraction` fallback;
  - E4 against the Oman, Saudi and Yemen fixtures (Yemen's Asr shadow at 00:00 local time), with Saudi Arabia's −0.83° sunrise (E2) and its previous-date days counted apart (8.7);
  - in the fitting harness, E5 on 29 February (Qatar: same as 1 March; Kuwait and Palestine: own row, Kuwait's read at the leap day after its reference year, Kuwait's 29 February and 1 March counted as quirk days, 8.3; Lebanon's Beirut table: own row, skipped at its non-leap reference year);
  - E8 on 2026-02-17, -18, 03-19 and -20 for `sa_umm_al_qura`, `qa_awqaf` and `qa_qch` (Q3);
  - E9 on both sides of 2020-08-20, 2025-03-30 and 2026-01-02;
  - E13 against hand-computed apparent-sun Asr values, on Jordan's, the UAE's and Iraq's local tables (A7) once their AM2 fits exist, and on Morocco's local Habous tables (Q5);
  - E14: VSOP87 sun positions against JPL Horizons and the prototype's output (8.6), and each profile's model choice against its local tables;
  - the order of operations: a Qatar Ramadan day gives Isha = Maghrib + 90, with Maghrib + 3; the Isha stepper moves an interval Isha, the Maghrib stepper does not.
- **Geography**: Moroni in June and December (southern hemisphere); Mogadishu (equator); Aden, Khartoum and Jazan on their overhead-sun days (Asr altitude 45°); Qamishli and Mosul in June (37°N); Dakhla in December (far west of its zone).
- **Zone mapping** (the zone `ZoneMapping.resolve` picks; where 6.5 applies the validity guard, its verdict is tested in the next item): Muscat → zone 0; Dukhan → its listed place, computed at its fitted point (8.8); Al Khor, Al Wakra and Al Wakra's south edge (25.13, 51.61) → Doha, as Doha's named places (within 6 km of the towns' points), while a point 7 km due south of Al Wakra's town point (outside its area while its `areaKm` is 6) → Mesaieed, the nearest of the six, Mesaieed → its own place, Ras Laffan → Al Shamal, Al Shahaniya → Doha and an inland point west of Al Khor (25.60, 51.20) → Dukhan, and no Qatari point is left without a zone (8.8); a Jordan Valley point (Deir Alla, below sea level → Central Ghor); Zarqa and Salt → Amman's table, Hashimiya → Dulail and Hashimiya, Khalidiya → Mafraq (8.8); Ruwaished (Amman's time plus its derived offset, 8.8); Ghor al-Mazraa (Karak, below sea level → Ghor al-Safi, Amman's time plus its derived offset); every Jordanian catalogue place under the default → one of `jo_awqaf`'s 15 zones or 17 further places in Jordan, never exact coordinates (8.8); Jerusalem with `jo_awqaf` chosen (its Jerusalem entry); Gaza against Rafah (Egypt); with `ps_dar_al_ifta`, Bidya (+ 2, Qalqilya being the nearest listed place), Qalqilya (+ 2 on all six times), Salfit and Tubas (Nablus, 0) and Jabalia when chosen in Gaza (+ 3); with `ps_gaza_awqaf`, Rafah and Beit Hanoun (the one zone «محافظات غزة», the same minutes); Ras Jedir; Zuwara (63 km) → Zawiya's zone, with no reach limit; Nalut (192 km) and Ghat (Sabha's, 487 km) → the `ly_awqaf` formula at their own point by the validity guard (6.5), since those tables' Maghrib would come up to about 7 and 19 min before their sunset (8.8); Madha (OM) and Nahwa inside it (AE: the east coast, 88); Kalba → 88 though Fujairah city (73) is nearer; Masfout (Ajman) → 68, not Hatta's 71; Al Faqa' (Dubai) → rural Dubai (70); a point in Sharjah city's Al Yarmook → 91, not its listed 88 (8.8); Umm al-Zumul → Al Qua'a (51), about 85 km away, not exact coordinates; Musandam (OM); Ceuta (zone Africa/Ceuta); Tichla (Western Sahara, about 127 km from Aousserd, its nearest Habous locality) → `ma_habous` at its own point by the validity guard (6.5), not Aousserd's table (its Fajr up to about 5.5 min early), and Touissit (26 km from Oujda, its nearest locality, at 1,124 m) → Oujda's table, which the guard keeps (its sunrise up to about 2 min after Touissit's), not Jerada's, whose table Touissit's mosques use (8.8); a point 10 km due east of Fes's locality, at its height → Fes's table; Hargeisa (SO, its A3 profile); Tripoli (LB) → Beirut's table; Hermel and Naqoura → `lb_dar_al_fatwa` at their own point by the validity guard (6.5; on the north-eastern border Beirut's sunrise comes up to about 6 min after the local one, in the far south its Fajr up to about 6 min before the local dawn); Khiran (KW, 28.66 N 48.36 E) → Nuwaiseeb (− 2 on all six times); a Kuwaiti point in the western desert at 29.75 N 47.15 E, more than 60 km from every listed place → the + 2 of its nearest listed place (Abdali or Jahra), which the validity guard (6.5) keeps, since it comes at most about 3 min before its own times (Dhuhr about 1.3, Maghrib about 2.2); it does not get Kuwait City's 0 (8.8); with `sy_awqaf`, Tabqa, Zabadani, Khan Shaykhun, Palmyra, Ras al-Ayn, Qamishli and Malikiyah → their own regions' tables (each its zone's town), Sukhnah and Al-Tanf (65 and 124 km) → Palmyra's, which the validity guard keeps (at most 2.7 and 3.9 min beyond its margin), Deir ez-Zor, Mayadin, Abu Kamal and Quneitra → their live towns, a catalogue pick of Jindires or Afrin → region 783 (computed at Aleppo's point) and of Aleppo → 804, and Damascus → 764–767's table, never 761's (8.8); Ouad Naga (MR, Trarza, about 52 km from Nouakchott) → Rosso's zone, which the validity guard (6.5) keeps (its sunrise about 1–2 min after Ouad Naga's), Boutilimit → Rosso's, not Aleg's, with no verdict asserted (about 4 min, near the guard's threshold), and Aïn Ben Tili → Zouerat's, about 465 km away, then `mr_islamic_affairs` at its own point by the validity guard, since Zouerat's sunrise comes about 16 min after its own (8.8); Hasana (EG, about 74 km from El Arish, 94 from Rafah) → El Arish's table, which the validity guard (6.5) keeps, since its Fajr comes at most about 3 min before Hasana's dawn; Banha → the table of Zagazig, the nearest EGSA city, also kept, its Dhuhr only about 1.3 min before Banha's noon; a point 10 km from Cairo's town centre → Cairo's table (inside its own area, so the guard does not act); points due west of Hurghada's town centre 1 km inside and 1 km outside its area (14 and 16 km while its `areaKm` is 15) → Hurghada's table both times, the next town centre, Safaga's, being about 60 km away: the inner one as Hurghada itself, never scanned, the outer one by the nearest town centre with no reach limit, scanned by the validity guard, which keeps it (its Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha each about 0.65 min earlier against the outer point's own times than against the town centre's, and its sunrise earlier than the point's, well within the guard's 4 minutes); with fixture zones under Egypt's rule, a point 12 km from the centre of a city with a 10 km area and 14 km from that of a city with a 25 km area → the second city, as a named place, and a catalogue place that a city lists in its `catalogueIds`, 20 km from its centre, outside every city's area and nearer another city's centre → the listing city, as a named place, while a GPS fix at the same point → the nearer city, scanned; central Mahalla al-Kubra → its own table, though its fitted point is about 23 km away, with Mansoura's and Tanta's under 1.5 km farther (8.8); Al Mahabishah (YE, about 1,570 m) maps to Hajjah, not Abs (E10), and keeps Hajjah's table, whose Fajr, Asr and Isha come only 1.0–1.3 min early, within the validity guard's tolerance; Mocha (YE, on the coast, no lowland location within 60 km) → Taiz (90 km), the nearest location (8.8), with no verdict asserted (Taiz's Fajr, Asr and Isha 2.6–3.8 min early, near the guard's threshold); Rumah (YE, Hadramawt, 221 km north-west of Ghaydah, its nearest location) and Abd al-Kuri (Kilmia, about 200 km west of Socotra's location) → `ye_awqaf` at their own point by the validity guard (6.5), since their location's Fajr, Asr and Isha would come well beyond its rounding plus 4 minutes before their own (at Rumah up to about 9.6 min, 8.8; at Kilmia, about 200 km west of Socotra's location, about 7 min by longitude alone); Al Hamra → «الحمرة», not Bahla, and Rakhyut → «رخيوت», not Dhalkut (the named town, 8.8); Seeb and Amerat → Muscat; a point in Oman 70 km from every zone point (its nearest zone); a point in northern Jeddah 20 km from Jeddah's «governorate» entry (Jeddah's table at its published point, «جدول: جدة»); a Saudi point within 25 km of two entries (the nearer one's table); a Saudi point 30 km from every listed place (exact coordinates, «حسب موقعك، أقرب مدينة: …», and still «رسمية», 6.11).
- **Validity guard** (6.5; each time is compared with the profile's own rule at the place's own point, and the tolerance is the table's own worst-day lead for that time at its own place over 365 days, with its sign (its rounding and what its zone adds to the profile), plus 4 minutes; the sunrise is checked as well as the five prayers; only clear verdicts are asserted): the authority's method at the place's own point for Nalut, Wazin, Tazirbu and Ghat (Maghrib 6–19 min before their sunset), Al-Tanf (Eastern Ghouta's sunrise about 9 min late), Hermel (Beirut's sunrise up to about 6 min late) and Naqoura (Beirut's Fajr up to about 6 min early), Tichla (Aousserd's Fajr up to about 5.5 min early), Aïn Ben Tili (Zouerat's sunrise about 16 min late), and Rumah and Abd al-Kuri (their location's Fajr, Asr and Isha up to about 7–10 min early); the attached table kept at Sabratha and Zuwara (Zawiya's truncated Fajr, Asr and Isha up to 2.1 and 4.4 min early, Zuwara's about 3.5 min beyond Zawiya's own), Zeralda and Rouiba (about 1 min), Khan Shaykhun and Zabadani (up to about 2 min), Tabqa (about 2.5), Tripoli (LB) and Sidon, Al Shahaniya, Khor Al Udaid and Ras Laffan (1.3–2.2 min), Hasana and Banha, Al Mahabishah, Touissit, Ouad Naga, Hawar (sunrise about 1.5 min late) and the Kuwaiti western-desert point (Jahra's + 2 up to about 3 min early); no verdict asserted at the places near the threshold (Ras al-Ayn, Sukhnah, Tell Abyad, Palmyra and Malikiyah; Bir Moghrein, Tichitt, Boutilimit and the Mauritanian places west of their capital; Mocha), which the pre-release scan over every attached area settles with the fitted points (AM2, M3); the table kept at every place the authority names, each picked from the catalogue (Kuwait's 12, Jordan's named towns, Algeria's 70, Libya's 22), and everywhere in Tunisia, without running the scan (a place for which the mapped zone's `names(place)` is true, 6.5, or an `Inm` place is never scanned); a time early by exactly the table's own worst-day lead at its own place plus 4 minutes passes, and by 0.1 min more fails (likewise a sunrise late); the cached verdict is reused for the same place, zone, method and year, and computed again when the zone (a hand pick included), the method (its high-latitude and polar rules included) or the year changes, not when the user's Asr factor or minute steppers change. The comparison itself (6.5): the reference never calls `ZoneMapping.resolve` and equals, before rounding, the times the guard gives where it acts with the profile's Asr factor and no adjustments; a minute the profile adds everywhere cancels (Ghat against Sabha: Sabha's own margin about 1 min and Ghat's lead about 22.5 against `ly_awqaf`'s Maghrib at each place, − 3 and 18.5 against the bare sunset, the same verdict); with a test table whose worst-day lead at its own place is negative, a time early by exactly that lead plus 4 minutes passes and by 0.1 min more fails; on a day where the table's time is `ESTIMATED` and the reference's `COMPUTED` (a fixture zone with `switchDays`), that time is not compared and does not enter the margin; Podgorica is judged against Sarajevo's own margin, not against its own point (6.2); a zone picked by hand is checked like a mapped one: Ghat's zone line does not offer Sabha's zone, and a stored pick of it gives Ghat `ly_awqaf` at its own point. An interval profile: Moroni's `km_fitted` table, each time compared with the same interval from the place's own sunrise or Maghrib (Fajr with its sunrise − 73, Asr with its Maghrib − 168, Isha with its Maghrib + 74) and its sunrise row, already the place's own, not compared: Fomboni (12.28S 43.74E) and Mutsamudu (12.17S 44.40E) keep it (every time 0.4 to 5.9 min after their own), Mamoudzou (12.78S 45.23E) and Sada (12.85S 45.10E) keep `yt_traditional`'s (4.8 to 10.4 min after), and a synthetic place passed to `ValidityGuard` with Moroni's zone, 1.5° west of Moroni on its latitude (−11.70, 41.75), fails, every time 5.5 to 6.5 min before its own, beyond Moroni's own 0.5 plus 4. An absent-twilight zone (joins at M2 with `irn`'s and `ru_dum_rt`'s rules, 9.2): a fix at Ny-Ålesund (78.93N 11.93E) on IRN's Svalbard entry, at Longyearbyen's point (78.22N 15.63E), where `irn` places Mecca's day on the local noon all year, has its Fajr, sunrise, Maghrib and Isha estimated at both points on every day, so they are not compared, and gets `irn` at its own point from its Dhuhr, the entry's coming about 15 min before its own; a point 30 km due south of Kazan's DUM RT point, checked against Kazan's table, has its Isha left out on 2026-05-05 and 08-07 (Kazan's sunset + 1 h 30, about 1 h before its own 15° Isha) and gets `ru_dum_rt` at its own point from its real 18° Fajr, which Kazan's comes up to about 20 min before in the nights next to the switch (2026); for a point 30 km due north, its Fajr on 2026-05-05 and 08-09 (its own sunrise − 2 h, 1.7 to 2 h after Kazan's real 18° Fajr) is left out.
- **App (Robolectric)**:
  - a Casablanca place on an `Asia/Muscat` phone (Isha after midnight in the phone's clock);
  - a Muscat place on a Paris phone on 2026-10-25: Fajr is silenced at its UTC instant (about 00:53), not an hour later (6.6);
  - a Riyadh place on a Paris phone on 2026-10-25: Fajr (about 01:38 UTC, 02:38 CET in the second pass) is silenced, and a Fajr-relative adhkar reminder and an after-Fajr hifz reminder are armed from that instant, not an hour earlier (6.6);
  - a Riyadh place across 1 Ramadan;
  - an Egyptian place across 2026-10-29 24:00;
  - an Iraq place gets the Sunni Endowment's table with no chooser, and no Shia method is listed (A4); a Fallujah place gets the Fallujah zone, not Ramadi's, a Kirkuk place (outside the Kurdistan Region) the Kirkuk zone, and a desert place far from every city maps to the nearest one (no reach limit, 8.8), the validity guard (6.5) then checking it; an Erbil place gets no zone and shows the method at its own coordinates with «فلكية» and the Kurdistan line (6.11, Q8);
  - a Dubai Friday and a Sharjah Friday in October and in February with the default config and no `SilenceDefaults` entry for AE: the Jumu'a window is 12:45 to 13:30;
  - travel from Bahrain (`mwl` chosen) to Saudi Arabia and back: the Bahraini choice returns;
  - a fix on the Hawar Islands (25.66N 50.79E), with a Bahraini network and with none: country BH, «قرب جزر حوار», and the national table, which the validity guard (6.5) keeps, since its winter sunrise comes at most about 1.5 min after Hawar's (8.8); a fix east of the islands on Qatar's side (25.60N 50.84E), nearer the Hawar row than Dukhan, with a Qatari network: country QA (8.8, 12);
  - a fixed 15:45 evening adhkar reminder made in Riyadh, then a move to Casablanca: the reminder stays on for the days that still fit, and its card is flagged with the first date that fails;

## 10. Files

**New**

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `multiplatform/shared/.../engine/SolarEngine.kt` | Sun at an instant by the E14 model (calls `InmPrayerFormula.sun`/`julianDay` for MEEUS, `Vsop87Sun` or `TextbookSun` otherwise), the cycle of a civil date and its transit (6.2, Solver, step 1), non-clamping hour angle, bracketed solver |
| `.../engine/PrayerEngine.kt` | `day()`, `explain()`, events, night, the order of operations with the order guard (6.2, Event order), rounding per event (E1), `SUPPORTED_YEARS`; the `zone` parameter (E2 `PerZone` and `Refraction`, E6, E7, zone offsets), E10 and E14–E19 |
| `.../engine/HorizonModel.kt`, `SunEvaluation.kt` | E2–E4 |
| `.../engine/Vsop87Sun.kt` and its term tables, `TextbookSun.kt` | E14: VSOP87 with Delta T (D17), and the TEXTBOOK sun of Libya's table (`ly_awqaf`, 8.2) |
| `.../engine/HighLatitude.kt`, `MoonsightingSeasonal.kt`, `DiyanetTakdir.kt`, `PolarFallback.kt` | The rules of 7.4, the ramp, the LRE cache (stubs first in an Arab-first build, 6.2) |
