## 11. Milestones

Each milestone ends in something that can be checked on its own.

- Work stays uncommitted on `codex` for the owner to commit.
- It starts only after the current uncommitted work has been committed.
- Changes spanning several files are made in the main session, not in workflow agents.
- Each region stayed behind its switch until its milestones were done; the switches were removed on 2026-10-19 (2).

The milestones keep both former labels: Mn for the shared core and Europe, AMn for the Arab countries; a shared milestone carries both. They are listed in the order A15 decided, Arab countries first.

**M0 / AM0 — Decisions, guard rails and data (no behaviour change).**

- Nothing is left to decide before building: section 4's decisions are settled (2026-10-11).
- An INM fingerprint: the exact minutes of `InmPrayerFormula.minutes()`, `explain()`, `sunAltitudeDeg()` and `sun(jd)`, recorded from today's code and asserted within 1e-6 min.
- Snapshots of today's `SilenceScheduler` arming and `WakeAlarmComputer` triggers at `Africa/Tunis` (Robolectric), recorded from the commit M0 starts on. They compare the trigger instants of SILENCE, UNSILENCE and wake alarms only: not request codes, and not the scheduler's own reschedule alarms (`9998`, `9999`), which are plumbing, not silences. They include an Isha window that ends after midnight across the 00:01 reschedule, an UNSILENCE inside an overlapping window, and the Ramadan Isha override's stored keys on a day of Ramadan 1448 and on the first day after its Ramadan window (5.1), with and without an Isha edit in between, with 1448 dates set by the test, since `data/official-islamic-dates/` holds 1447 only (9.1). Before they are recorded, the code facts of 5.1 are re-read against that commit and its line references refreshed.
- The two date-dependent `PrefsManagerTest` cases `ishaDefaultDuringRamadan_returns90` and `ishaDefault_outsideRamadan_is30` still expect the former read-time 90, so they fail on every day `RamadanDetector.isRamadan()` is true (for 1448 by Umm al-Qura, 2027-02-07 to 2027-03-09). They are pinned to fixed dates and assert the override as 5.1 describes it (test code only).
- CI: `:shared:javaTest` in android-tests on a JDK with tzdata 2026c or newer, the path filters, and the `--verify` exit code.
- The collectors are written, and the owner sets up the scheduled task for the daily and monthly ones (9.3). They run from now, so a year of Libya exists by late 2027 and Ramadan 1448 is captured.

**M1 / AM1 — Engine core.**

- The README "Shared engine" section first, with its "Arab authorities" part.
- Then the solver (with its civil-date anchor and the date-line tests of 9.1), events, night, offsets, rounding per event, E1–E19 (E5 only in the fitting harness, 9.4; E11 not built, A14; E15 for `km_fitted` and `yt_traditional`, A3 and A1, and for `dz_marw`'s signed shadow, A16; E16 for `re_cir`, 7.6; E17 for `ly_awqaf`, 8.2; E18 for `xk_bik` and `ie_ifi`, 8.8 and 7.5; E19, 7.7), the order of operations with the order guard and its unit tests (6.2, 9.1), `PrayerDay`, the `PrayerPlace` types and keys, `InmPrayerDays` with its 258 × 365 identity test, and `PrayerClock`.
- D5's angle-based rule itself, since every preset and profile without its own rule evaluates it every night except `mdf_12` and `luxembourg_shoura` (it never binds in Arab places); the `HighLatitudeRule` and `PolarFallback` stubs of 6.2 for the other rules, the absent-nights form of `mdf_12` and `luxembourg_shoura` included (it never acts in Arab places), if M2 is not built yet; the presets whose rules come with M2 stay hidden until then.
- The independent Python reference, written from the README, and the Horizons/USNO astronomy fixtures.
- All of it passes `:shared:javaTest`. No app code.

**AM2 — Fits (Arab track).** The fitting harness, then SA, OM, QA, JO, EG, AE, YE, DZ, MA and LY, in that order (best data first). Each fit tries the sun models of E14 (MEEUS and VSOP87, and TEXTBOOK for LY) and keeps the one that reproduces the tables best (D17); Jordan and the UAE start from VSOP87. IQ (Sunni) is fitted here too, at reference year 2021 (E5), and stays in tier B (A8, 9.5). Then SD, SO, DJ and KM, from the formulas the mosque dataset gave (A3, 8.7), with E15 for the Comoros. Kuwait's 11 listed places get their points here (the town centre; for an island, its inhabited point; 8.8, Q15), though KW's fit itself waits for AM10. Egypt's 78 cities get their town centres here, each the zone's `point` and its named place's `centre`, with their areas (`areaKm`, 15 km until set from the city's extent); Oman's named towns get their catalogue points (`areaKm` 10), and Qatar's Al Khor and Al Wakra keep their 6 km unless the fit moves them (6.5, 8.8). Each fit ends with its profile, its zones, the `FitStats` of each (9.5) and its README part; its tables stay local (A7). A zone passes the tier-A gate only when its record has the coverage of 9.5; a zone still short of a whole held-out year (Qatar's towns, Mesaieed and Halul, Morocco's localities, Egypt's cities, Yemen before its first winter; 12) is rescored in a later app update once the collectors (9.3) complete its year.

**M3 / AM3 — Methods, profiles and defaults.**

- The preset table, the authority profiles, the generic presets, `PrayerMethodChoice`, the country defaults (France's order by département, D4), the bounded «مخصّصة» (6.11) and the chooser logic. `rhone_ctir`, `apbif` (its rounding per event, on APBIF's 2026 tables, 7.5), `re_cir`, `dum_rf` (its rounding per event, 7.4, and its Moscow zone's point, 6.5), `ie_ifi` (its E18 offsets, from the IFI's Dublin table, Q23) and `luxembourg_shoura` (the 0–4 min its table runs inside the computed 13°, which then replaces its ±4 in 9.2, Q37, and its national zone's point), the table points of `diyanet_eu`'s nine IGGÖ zones (7.5) and the points of Diyanet's Austrian entries, checked against their tables (6.5), are fitted with the AM2 harness from their local tables (D13), and `yt_traditional` is derived from `km_fitted`. The same harness fits `bosnia_iz` (its point, horizon and rounding at its reference year, E5, and its zones' points, 8.8), `hr_miz_zagreb`, `si_islamska_skupnost` and `xk_bik`'s seasonal offsets (E18), and sets `xk_bik`'s town and Preševo Valley radii and those of `bosnia_iz`'s five Montenegrin towns from the town extents. It fits `bg_grand_mufti` (each city's point, the offsets, E4 and the rounding) on the months AM0 has collected by then, refitted in an app update once a year of the 48 tables is collected (12), and `mk_bfi` (the method at Skopje's point) on the days collected by then. Each of Diyanet's Turkish entries gets its point fitted the same way from its table, fetched into `test-data-local/` (6.5 `DISTRICT`, D13). The Balkan profiles are fitted with the same harness from their local tables: `bosnia_iz`'s point, horizon and rounding on Sarajevo's perpetual table at its reference year (E5) and the points of the Takvim's 110 places, `hr_miz_zagreb`, `si_islamska_skupnost`, `xk_bik`'s seasonal offsets (E18) and the radii of its seven towns and the Preševo Valley's three, and `mk_bfi` and `bg_grand_mufti` from what AM0 has collected by then (8.8). In an Arab-first build, the European presets whose rules come with M2 are hidden until M2.
- The method fixture tests (in an Arab-first build, those of 9.2 that need M2 join at M2).

**M4 / AM4 — Mockups.**

- HTML mockups with the Arabic copy: the method sheet, the choosers and «مخصّصة»'s editor, the chips with their lines, the markers in the list, the zone line and row, the location step and picker, the «طريقتك» section, and the adhkar flag.
- The owner approves them and decides A6's wording.

The phone's consumers move to instants with it (6.6), checked against the M0 snapshots.
- `SilenceEndpoints`, `SilenceWindowPlanner` and the D12 and A18 defaults land with the facade. From M5 a Tunisian delegation on a phone set elsewhere is silenced at its real instants, with 13:15 read in Tunis time (D12).
- The outside-Tunisia gate removed everywhere (`OutsideTunisia`, `disabled_outside_tunisia`, `RESULT_OUTSIDE_TUNISIA`, and their readers in `SilenceScheduler`, `ScheduleRefreshCoordinator`, `SilenceVerifyWorker` and `MainScreen`).

- The Arab League switch goes on in development builds.
- Emulator checks (9.1); Ramadan 1448 checked live.
- No release yet: the app ships with both regions (M9).

**M2 — High-latitude and polar rules (Europe track).**

- LRE with its ramp and cache, one-seventh, middle (unramped for `apbif`, 7.5), nearest day, the Dutch and Belgian clock caps (D5's options, 7.4), the absent-nights, ramped angle-based rule of `mdf_12` and `luxembourg_shoura` (D5), Moonsighting seasonal, Diyanet takdir (caps and constants re-derived from test tables), `dum_rf`'s June Isha ramp (7.4), the polar rules of D6 (the 5-hour minimum with its per-place gate, the polar Asr midpoint, Diyanet's ungated clamp) and `irn`'s Mecca cycle and southern summer rule (their switch days fitted on IRN's tables with the AM2 harness, and each entry's own `switchDays` stored where the rule misses them, 6.5, 7.4), and `ru_dum_rt`'s summer intervals, and the summer forms of `hr_miz_zagreb` and `si_islamska_skupnost` (7.4). They replace the stubs.
- Gate, continuity and vector tests, and every test deferred to M2 (9.2). The hidden European presets are offered from here.

**M9 — Validation, Europe, and the release of both regions** (after M2, M8 and AM9).

- The Europe switch goes on; with both switches on, the release build is made (both switches removed on 2026-10-19: every region is open, 2).
- Device checks on the emulator (9.1), then read-only checks on the OnePlus with the owner's OK.
- Performance of the 365-day scans (D6's polar gate, the validity guard of 6.5, and LRE when picked) and of the 365-day adhkar validation, measured on the OnePlus.
- Privacy policy and Play listing texts, with A13's source and independence lines, reviewed by the owner.
- Play availability (Q28): the production track's Countries/regions tab offers the release in every country and region Google Play serves, except South Korea, where Google's requirements ask developers who collect location for location-based services to hold a KCC licence (the cautious reading, since the app keeps location on the phone), and Israel (14). Play offers an app by the user's Play country, which follows the payment profile and changes at most once per 90 days, not by where the phone is: a traveller keeps Tunisia, but most of the 1.8 million Tunisians abroad live in Europe (Appendix A) and have a local Play country. Users whose Play country is South Korea or Israel can still install the GitHub APK. Since the release then reaches the EU, the privacy review above also checks the EU's rules (GDPR, and whether Firebase Analytics needs consent there).

**AM10 — Later fits**, as data arrives: KW, PS, SY, MR, LB (Dar al-Fatwa: a second official Ramadan imsakiya checks the reference year, and the sunrise horizon is refitted, Q10), IQ (Sunni: its 2027 table checks the reference year and the horizon); BH's fit is checked on its 1449 calendar, the held-out year (about June 2027); LY's city points are refined from the daily collection. A profile ships with its current chip and is upgraded in a later app update.

**Later.** Section 14.

## 12. Known limits

**Both regions**

- **Stale time-zone data.** A phone with old time-zone data shows a wrong clock wherever a zone's rules changed: Morocco today. Android 8.0 gets tzdata only by OTA, Android 8.1–9 only through its maker's tz-update APK, and Android 10 stays at 2026c, AOSP's last update for it. The same would hit the European zones if the EU changed its summer-time rule. Alarms at computed times stay right. Clock times the user typed ring by that wrong clock, and a clock set by hand to hide the error puts every alarm an hour off.
- **Background relocation.** With location permission only in the foreground, relocation while travelling may wait until the app is opened.
- **Places near a limit.** A change of country (or choice key), zone, delegation, administrative area, method or official zone is stored at any distance (6.8), with no hysteresis, as delegation changes are today. These limits come from the nearest catalogue row or official place and the network country, not from outlines, so a phone that stays near one can switch sides with GPS noise or a network change. Each switch reschedules everything; one that changes the choice key, the method, the mapped zone or a GPS place's label also posts «تم تغيير الموقع إلى …».
- **A place in a far phone zone.** A place with no catalogue city within 40 km (at sea, or outside both catalogues) takes the phone's zone (6.8). On a phone whose zone's standard time is more than 10.5 h from the place's mean solar time, whole days aside (a phone left on Tunis time in the central Pacific, for example), a prayer day's Dhuhr can fall on the previous date, up to about 16 min before midnight in that zone, or on the next date, up to about 14 min after midnight, or 1 h 14 min in the zone's summer time. The day's six times stay one solar cycle, and the list marks those on another date (6.2, Solver, step 1; 6.6).
- **A trip without auto-location** is not noticed: the place stays until the user changes it, and its times are silenced at their own instants, not the local ones (D20).
- **Bounded silence windows.** A silence start lies within 12 h of its adhan and an end at most 24 h after it (6.9); a longer silence is the manual silence's job. Values saved beyond that by earlier builds (up to ±10,080 min in the sheets, any value through the slider's edge extension) are clamped, not migrated.
- **Tunisians who live abroad** get no feature of their own (Q28): Ramadan and Eid stay Tunisian (A10, A11), and Google Play does not offer the app to users whose Play country is South Korea or Israel (M9); they can install only the GitHub APK.

**Europe**

- **Matching a mosque's table.** European times match a mosque's printed table only within its method's tolerance: usually ±1 min for fixed-angle methods, more for estimated summer times. Mosques in the same city differ by much more than that.
- **France** is fragmented: even neighbouring mosques differ, so no default is close for most users. The French list's best entry matches 19 % of the plan's 1,669 mainland French records on all six times within 5 min (12 % with the chooser dismissed), and a default built from bundled mosques would reach only 26 %; none is bundled (owner, 2026-10-12; 7.7). With «مخصّصة» and the minute steppers a user can match about 61 % of the editor analysis's 1,673 French records (Mayotte's 6 included) within 5 min (7.7). Inside the envelope (E19) no method shows a start outside the recognised positions, though the user's steppers can move Fajr or Isha outside them (6.2, step 9): E19 would move the dataset's French listed times by more than 5 min on about 8 % of mosque-days, mostly a Fajr listed after the 12° instant in June and July. Such a listing may be a summer cap, a delayed adhan or iqama or another practice, and a prayer at that time is valid until sunrise, so it is not counted as an error (7.7's four outcomes); but shown as a start it would let a fasting user eat after dawn, so on those days the app differs from such a mosque by design. The Isha floor rests on one published observation model (the Moonsighting curve), against one measured evening (the CTIR's 14° at Lyon in 2017); no French observation campaign exists, and if one finds the red twilight later, the floor rises (14). `rhone_ctir` is within 2 min of the CTIR table on its 2020 dates, not 1, because the table's sunrise, Maghrib and Isha drift with the season (7.2); the mosques reuse that table by date while the app computes for the actual year (A8), so its Fajr can be 3 min from theirs on up to about 20 days a year (2027, 2031).
- **Diyanet's summer estimation** is not published. The app's version is re-derived from its tables and can differ by 2–3 min (worst cases about 9 min); Diyanet's own tables disagree by up to 7 min.
- **Turkey's districts.** Diyanet publishes no rule for places it does not list. The district rule (6.5 `DISTRICT`) is ours: it is read from Diyanet's list and supported by the owner's dataset. A place's district comes from the nearest Turkish catalogue row, since no district outlines are bundled, so a fix near a district line can take the neighbour's table, usually 1 minute or less apart. GeoNames' district names are matched to Diyanet's entries when the asset is built; a district that matches none falls back to the nearest entry. Even on the right table, 1.5–1.8 % of minutes are 1 minute off until Diyanet's exact sun model is known.
- **London Unified Timetable.** It comes from observations and has no formula. The app's preset is a proxy: Fajr within ±6 min, Isha 0 to +11 min.
- **Norway's IRN tables** are the default everywhere in Norway (2026-10-11), with switch dates fitted from the 2026 tables (D6, 7.4); an entry's stored `switchDays` (6.5) are its 2026 dates, kept on the same calendar dates every year. In a later year IRN's own switch days may fall a day apart; a day moves the held southern Fajr by about 5 min for the 5–8 weeks it is held (Oslo, Kristiansand, Bergen), so each new year's tables are checked. A GPS place takes its municipality from the nearest NO row or IRN entry point (6.8), so near a municipal border it can take the neighbouring entry. Only 5 of the dataset's 29 Norwegian mosques (Brumunddal, Kristiansand, Porsgrunn, Sandefjord and Tønsberg) show their municipality's IRN table, Sandefjord with its own Isha; Oslo's Bosnian and Turkish mosques and Stavanger's Turkish ones follow Diyanet (`diyanet_eu`, under «طرق أخرى»).
- **Russia** has no national table. DUM RF's sheet covers Moscow and Moscow oblast and DUM RT's workbook covers Tatarstan, and each is the default only there (2026-10-11). DUM RF's Isha ramp around June changes from year to year (July 2025 differs from the 2026 ramp by up to 3 min); `dum_rf` keeps the 2026 ramp every year. Elsewhere in Russia the default stays `mwl`: Dagestan's muftiate shows its times only in its app, and no Bashkir, Chechen or other muftiate table was found; each one found later becomes its region's default in the same way. DUM RT's Fajr and Dhuhr are the dawn and the zenith; its mosques pray later (its «in mosques» columns, Dhuhr at 12:00), and the one Tatarstan mosque in the dataset (Kaipy, Laishevo district) shows those columns of Kazan's table, not its district's. The validity guard (6.5) compares DUM RT's real 18° Fajr up to the night it stops being reached, when that dawn moves fast with latitude: a place 10 km south of its table's point gets the table's Fajr beyond the guard's tolerance on about 2 nights a year (by up to about 6 to 9 min before its own dawn), one 30 km south on about 15 to 17 nights (by up to about 15 to 20 min; Kazan's point, 2026–2028), so many Tatarstan places south of their district centre get `ru_dum_rt` at their own point, under «حسب موقعك (لا يوجد جدول رسمي قريب)» though their district's table is near, while on most days DUM RT's method there is within a minute or two of that table; the scan with the fitted points settles each place (M3).
- **Bosnia, Croatia and Slovenia** (8.8). The default follows the official tables, not the method at the user's point. The Takvim gives Fajr the same difference as sunrise, so in the north its June Fajr is up to 10 minutes later and its Isha up to 7 earlier than `bosnia_iz` at the town; in the south its latitude term runs the other way, so Trebinje's June Fajr is 16 minutes earlier and its Isha 13 later, and its sunrise and Maghrib up to 9 off. «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the computed times. Sarajevo's perpetual table is reproduced only as well as its M3 fit allows, computed for the actual year (A8). In Croatia the table changes at the edge of Zagreb's area (MIZ Zagreb's Fajr is 7–13 minutes earlier than the Takvim's Zagreb row in March), and the other towns rest on the Takvim alone: the Mešihat's home page shows vaktija.eu, a third-party site, whose Croatian towns are Zagreb's table plus one shift on all six times (Split − 6 on 2026-10-11, where the Takvim puts Split between 4 minutes earlier and 1 later than Zagreb). Slovenia's profile rests on three months of 2026 and, for summer, the Community's 2020 sheet.
- **Ireland** (Q23): no preset reproduces the perpetual Dublin table that the Islamic Foundation of Ireland publishes and ICCI used (Fajr from about 16.5° in November to 11.3° in June), so `ie_ifi` reproduces it with E18's seasonal offsets (owner's rule of 2026-10-11; 7.5); `mwl`, the former default, never shows Isha before it (9–52 min later), but shows Fajr earlier on 329 of 355 days, by up to 53 min in April and May; 9 of the dataset's 21 Irish mosques show the table (5.6). The IFI's other towns keep Dublin's Fajr and Isha intervals at their own sunrise and Maghrib, a rule read from one table only (Cork, Ramadan 2026, 30 days); M3 checks it on the towns' year tables, and a town that breaks it gets its own fitted intervals.
- **LRE above 66.6°N** extends the 2009 decision beyond its stated range, and the engine keeps real times that ICOP would call unstable.
- **Short nights and the winter Asr** (6.2, Event order). No European body publishes what to show when a method's fixed interval is longer than half the night or its Dhuhr offset passes the real Asr, so the engine's order guard decides. From about 62.5°N (120-min intervals) or 64°N (90 min) to the polar gate near 65.7°N, such an Isha or Fajr is held at the night's lowest point and marked «تقديري» (`ecfr_90` at Luleå on 42 nights, 31 May to 11 July 2026). Near the polar night an Asr is shown at Dhuhr when the method's Dhuhr comes after it, as Diyanet's Nordic tables show Asr = Dhuhr (`tunisia_18_18` at Rovaniemi on 27 days, 8 December to 3 January; a method with Dhuhr + 5 and no Asr offset on 22).
- **Luxembourg** (Q37): the Shoura's 2026 table runs 0–4 min inside the computed 13° all year (implied Isha 12.6–13.0°, Fajr 12.8–12.9°), so the default `luxembourg_shoura` can be up to 4 min from the table that 9 of the dataset's 14 Luxembourg mosques print, until a fit settles it (9.2). Every Luxembourg place gets that one table where the validity guard (6.5) allows, as the Shoura publishes no other: in the north it is up to about 6 min from the local 13° Fajr and 7 from the local Isha in June (Troisvierges, 57 km from the capital, near the guard's threshold, which the scan settles), and «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the local times; the dataset's three northern mosques (Ettelbruck, Weidingen, Troisvierges) print other tables (support only).
- **Austria**: beyond 25 km of IGGÖ's nine cities, IGGÖ's site picks the nearest of Diyanet's Austrian entries by points neither publishes (a point at Melk got Pöchlarn's table on 2026-10-11, though Diyanet lists Melk), so near the midline between two entries the app can take the neighbour's table, usually under 1 min apart; entries with unusual names are placed by hand in M3, when the entries' points are checked against their tables. Within 25 km every place takes its city's table, up to about 1.6 min from its own sun, as IGGÖ intends.
- **Réunion** (Q34): the CIR's tables cover only 19 February to 10 May and print no Dhuhr, Asr or Isha. The zone points are fitted on those 81 days of one season, where several points fit equally well, and the CIR reuses one table by month and day while the app computes for the actual year (A8), so a region's Fajr or Maghrib is 1 min off that table on about 7–22 % of days. `re_cir`'s 18° Isha rests on one mosque display of 6 days (Bras-Fusil, 12–17 October 2026), and its Dhuhr and Asr on no published table: that display's minute offsets are not adopted (D15), and the Asr factor is asked once.
- **Martinique** (7.6): `mq_ccim` rests on one month of the IslamicFinder page the CCIM links (October 2026), whose Dhuhr and Maghrib run 1 min later than the same site's calendars that A2CMGF posted in January 2026, and whose Dhuhr to Isha match the times computed for the day before better than those of the day itself (exact on 90–97 % against 39–71 %; its Cayenne pages show no such lag), a quirk of the site that the app does not copy; if the site drops or changes that minute, MQ follows with an update. Neither Martinique nor French Guiana publishes a table for its other towns: both compute at the user's point, as their sources do.
- **Mayotte** has no authority's table (Q36). `yt_traditional` follows the island's traditional table, the Moroni table − 3, as a private site and 5 of the 7 dataset mosques show it; computed daily, it differs from that table's 5-day blocks by up to 4 min (within 2 min on 91–98 % of days).
- **The Balkans** (8.8). Kosovo's takvim is hand-made, so `xk_bik` follows it through a seasonal curve: 66–82 % of minutes exact, up to 2 min off; its Fajr is the takvim's «Sabahu», 20 min after its Imsaku. The dataset's Ferizaj and Gjilan mosques call the table without their published − 1. Montenegro's own takvim was not found: the default is the Bosnian takvim's in vaktija.ba's five towns and `bosnia_iz` at the place's own point elsewhere (the far north around Pljevlja, near the guard's threshold, is left to the scan, 6.5), while the Podgorica mosques use Sarajevo's times shifted − 3 or − 4, and their Isha is their own, 1–7 min after Sarajevo's with the same shift. Belgrade's main mosque calls Diyanet's Belgrade table, not the Bosnian takvim's Beograd difference; `diyanet_eu` is under «طرق أخرى». `bg_grand_mufti` rests on 7 rows of one unlabelled city, within 3 min of Diyanet's method, until a year of the 48 city tables is collected. `mk_bfi` rests on one day of the BFI's Skopje times until the collected days are fitted, and 11 of the dataset's 15 North Macedonian mosques share a calendar that follows neither it nor `diyanet_tr`. Greece keeps `diyanet_tr` by inference: the Thrace muftis' tables have not been looked for.
- **Friday times in Europe** are defaults, not mosque times. No winter Friday was collected: the winter values rest on mosque pages, some of unknown date, and on dataset values left from winter, only 10 and 5 of them in Belgium and Portugal (Q30).

**Arab countries**

- **Exactness has a floor.** Zones that pass the tier-A gate (9.5) still miss by 1 minute on up to 3 % of times per prayer until an authority's exact engine is known. Saudi Arabia is 1–2 minutes off on KACST's previous-date days, about 17 a year (8.7), and 1 minute off on some days from a mosque that calls a neighbouring listed place's table (Al Jumum's calls Mecca's) or, beyond 25 km of every entry, the nearest one's (Al Mudhaylif's calls Al Qunfudhah's: 2 to 9 of October 2026's 31 days per time); the zone line lists the five nearest tables (8.8). Several of today's fits are below the gate (8.1); their zones stay below the gate until their own records pass (9.5). Dubai's table is reproduced to only 93 % with one point, every time within 1 minute: it carries month-to-month biases of up to half a minute that no tested model explains (Q13), so Dubai's zone stays in tier B while Abu Dhabi's and Al Ain's are scored on their own records (9.5).
- **The tier-A record waits for a whole held-out year at each zone** (9.5). In hand today: Jordan's official held-out days are 31 zone-days of 1 January to 8 February 2025 (about 590 to 9 February once transcribed), so its records also rest on a month-split of 2023, where 9 of the 15 zones are below 97 % on some time even in-sample (Fajr 78.6 % at Karak, 83.0 % at Aqaba), and its further places' times are not yet scored on held-out months (their offsets give 595 of 648 there); Qatar's three printed towns have 59 days of August to October 2026 (Fajr and Maghrib) and Mesaieed and Halul two `/cal` days; Morocco's localities one official month each; Egypt's cities 8 days; Yemen no winter yet (Q2); Oman 6 held-out dates of 2027–2031; Saudi Arabia 13 sampled cities, not yet one in each of the 13 regions (9.3); the UAE no held-out year (8.6). These zones, and `sa_umm_al_qura`, stay below the gate until their own records pass, for most once the collectors (9.3) complete their year, whatever their profile's other zones show; their chip is «رسمية» meanwhile (A6).
- **UAE places between Awqaf's areas** take, among the locations serving their emirate, the one with the nearest listed area (8.8). Awqaf's server attaches coordinates by a rule it does not publish, so a place near the line between two locations may get the neighbouring location's table until AM2 has compared the two at sampled points.
- **Sharjah's Friday time**: Sharjah kept its own, earlier time from 2022 to 2025, and no 2026 statement of its own was found. The app uses the federal 12:45 there until one says otherwise (Q13).
- **Authorities change their rules without notice**: Qatar in 2020, Syria in 2025–26, Qatar Calendar House in 2025, and the UAE's Jumu'a in 2022 and 2026. A change reaches users with an app update; collectors re-run yearly to catch it.
- **Algeria**'s reference rows are reproduced on 94–95 % of minutes per reference, every miss 1 minute, and its 67 places' offsets are derived on the phone (A16, 8.8), so the app matches the printed calendar on about 94.5 % of minutes and within 1 minute everywhere it applies. Tizi Ouzou's and Relizane's Dhuhr can be 1 minute early in a few months a year. A place the ministry does not list keeps the printed times of its wilaya's listed place where the validity guard (6.5) allows, as about 20 km from Algiers (Zeralda and Rouiba, about 1 minute off); in the far Sahara, beyond about 100 km from the listed town, the guard gives it `dz_marw` at its own point, so its times differ from its wilaya's printed ones and from mosques that copy them, and in between the scan with the fitted points settles it. The offsets follow the ministry's month starts; with Umm al-Qura's instead, until the year's dates ship, the offsets match 96 % and stay within 1 minute. The calendar has misprints (1447: Isha an hour late on 11 days), where the app is right. Asr follows the ministry's signed noon shadow, up to 16 minutes earlier than the shadow rule at In Guezzam in June–July.
- **Jordan's further places** follow Amman plus a monthly offset derived on the phone (8.8). With points fitted on all 12 months it differs from the printed offset by half a minute on about 2 % of values (about 8 % on months left out of the fit, Q19), so about 0.6 % of times are 1 minute from Amman's table plus the printed offset.
- **The M'zab's Isha.** In the owner's dataset, 11 mosques of the M'zab, mostly in El Atteuf and Bounoura, call Isha at Maghrib + 60 all year, and 27 others of the valley at + 70 or + 75. No authority publishes these, so the app keeps `dz_marw`'s 17°: 12–35 min after the + 60 mosques, and in June about 20 min after most of the others (Q17, 8.4). The Isha stepper narrows the gap, but no single value fits the whole year.
- **Palestine's** perpetual table is not a clean formula: ±1 minute on 10–45 % of days per prayer. Dar al-Ifta gives no rule for the places it does not list, so a place takes the nearest listed place, whatever the distance (8.8, Q1): Bidya gets Qalqilya's + 2 although its mosques use 0, and Qalqilya's + 2 rounds the published + 1.5; some mosques differ from the list (4 near al-Fawwar use + 2 where Dura's is + 1). Gaza's one zone is computed at a fitted point, Gaza City's until the AM10 fit.
- **Oman** publishes neither its zone points nor a rule for the places it does not list (8.8): the 86 points are fitted from its tables, and the 10 km step and the nearest zone for the wilayat it leaves out are the app's rule. Three tables are computed 55–74 km from the place they name, and the app keeps them as published. Against the town's own position, «رخيوت» is 2–3 minutes early on every time all year, and «الحمرة» has Fajr 2–3 minutes early from April to August and sunrise up to 2 minutes late from November to January (equal on 38.5 % of times), where Bahla's table is within 1 minute. Al Hamra's 3 mosques in the dataset do not use «الحمرة». «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the town's own times.
- **Jordan's shared calendars.** Some mosques call a neighbouring zone's table rather than their own: in the owner's dataset, 8 around Hashimiya and 6 of the 9 in the Central Ghor use Amman's or Northern Ghor's, on average 1–3 minutes apart (8.8). The zone line lists the nearby tables. Umm al-Qutain's area keeps its 2025 zone until the 2026 calendar is read (Q29); its table runs 1–2 minutes before Mafraq's on almost every time of 2023 (equal on 17 Maghribs, 3 minutes on 25 sunrises), so if 2026 dropped it, those places are that much early.
- **Lebanon's** Beirut table (Dar al-Fatwa's 1445 imsakiya and the year table equal to it) looks perpetual and its sun runs 1 to 1.7 days late, so computing the actual year (A8) puts Fajr and Isha 2–3 minutes off it on about a fifth of the days (Fajr on 28 of the 30 days of Ramadan 1445); its only official source is one Ramadan (1445). Every Lebanese place takes Beirut's table (A2), since Dar al-Fatwa publishes no other place, offset or rule. Against the same method at the place's own point in 2026, at the north-eastern border (Hermel, Qaa, Qoubaiyat, Akkar's eastern villages) the table's sunrise is up to about 6 minutes after the local sunrise and its Fajr up to about 8 minutes later; in the far south (Tyre, Naqoura) its Fajr is up to 6 minutes earlier, its Dhuhr up to 2.4 minutes before the local transit (1.4 from the longitude, the rest the table's own floor rounding), its Asr up to 4.3 and its Isha up to 3.1 minutes earlier, and its Maghrib still at least 0.6 minutes after the local sunset. The validity guard (6.5) therefore gives both `lb_dar_al_fatwa` at their own point: the far south by its Fajr and the north-eastern border by its sunrise, each beyond the table's own margin plus 4 minutes; the far south's Dhuhr alone, 2.4 minutes early, would stay within it. «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the place's own point anywhere. In the North, 12 of the dataset's 23 Lebanese mosques (Tripoli, Mina, Koura, Qalamoun, Akkar) call another table, 5–8 minutes later than Beirut's table at Fajr and up to 6 at Dhuhr and Asr in 2026; its publisher is unknown, so no northern zone ships (Q10). The dataset does not decide between Beirut's table and the place's own point: no Lebanese mosque in it follows either (at most 39 % and 45 % of times exact) (5.6).
- **Morocco**'s sunrise and Maghrib rest on horizons fitted per locality from one official month, and their fixed minutes (− 1, + 2) are confirmed only on coastal archived tables; the dataset's year-long copies suggest 94–96 % exact over a year, below the tier-A gate, until a year of Habous tables (9.3) settles them. At exact coordinates `ma_habous` is within 1 minute on 97–100 % of days, not exact (Q5). A place beyond the localities takes the nearest one's table with no reach limit (8.8), with the validity guard (6.5). Within 60 km, Habous's method at the place's own point differs from the table by up to about 3 minutes at equal heights. The guard gives `ma_habous` at the place's own point wherever the table's sunrise comes after the local one, or another time comes before the local one, beyond Habous's rounding and fixed minutes plus 4 minutes: at Tichla (Aousserd's Fajr up to about 5.5 minutes before the local dawn). The rest of Western Sahara's interior (Oum Dreyga, Jraifia, Bir Lehlou and Haouza, 77–128 km away, 3–5 minutes off) is settled by the scan with the fitted points, and Touissit keeps Oujda's table (its sunrise up to about 2 minutes late). «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the place's own times.
- **Tunisian mosque displays** differ from INM by 1 minute on many days: in the owner's dataset only 2.5 % of Tunisian mosque calendars equal INM to the minute, though 75–90 % of times are within 1 minute (5.6). INM stays the only method (D7).
- **Egypt** publishes tables for about 80 cities only, so every other place takes the times of the city it is a named place of (by its catalogue row or within the city's own area, 6.5), otherwise of the city with the nearest town centre, however far, where the validity guard (6.5) allows (8.8). A city's own area is a circle around its town centre (15 km until AM2 sets it from the city's extent, 6.5), so the end of an elongated city can fall outside it; there the guard checks the city's table like any other attachment, and keeps it unless a time fails. EGSA's times carry no margin beyond their rounding, so a place outside a listed city's own area gets times a few minutes off its own, which the guard allows within its 4 minutes (Banha about 1.3, Ras el-Hekma about 2, Hasana up to about 3); farther out, as at Nakhl in central Sinai (Ras Sudr's sunrise about 4 to 5 minutes after its own), the scan with the fitted points settles whether it takes `eg_egsa` at its own point. Mosques that compute at their own point can differ from the app there by 1 to 3 minutes, or up to about 6 in central Sinai where the table is kept. Most Egyptian mosques in the owner's dataset compute at their own point (5.6), which «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives.
- **Computed mosque calendars** in Egypt, Saudi Arabia and Algeria round to the nearest minute and add whole-minute settings, so the app can differ by 1 minute from many mosque displays there (5.6).
- **Perpetual tables are not reproduced** (A8): computed for the actual year, Qatar, Kuwait, Palestine, Libya, Iraq and Mauritania's reprinted imsakiya differ from their printed tables by 1 minute on part of the days (Libya: about 10 % of times in 2026; Iraq: 14 %; Mauritania: about 60 % of Fajr and sunrise times in March and April of a leap year). Lebanon's Beirut table differs by 2–3 minutes on Fajr and Isha on about a fifth of the days (Q10).
- **Qatar** (Q3): on the Ramadan edge days where the Ministry API's own calendar differs from Umm al-Qura (2026-02-17 and 03-19; 2027-02-07), the app's Maghrib and Isha differ from the API by 1 minute. The three western towns rest on 59 printed days (August to October 2026), and Mesaieed and Halul on two `/cal` days (2026-10-08 and 10-11) that fix only their longitude, until AM2 refits them on a full year. A place outside the six and the east coast from Al Khor to Al Wakra is attached to the nearest of the six. Qatar's times have no margin beyond nearest rounding, so such a place's table can come about 1.3 to 2.2 minutes beyond its own times (Al Shahaniya, Khor Al Udaid, Ras Laffan), which the validity guard (6.5) allows within its 4 minutes. Mosque calendars from those places, or a QCH statement, would show which table they call.
- **Syria**'s screens show one table per region, which the app reproduces to the minute on 99.65–99.93 % of the days held out and shows as the screens do: in the north-east and on the coast's last four tables, whose files hold 365 days read by leap-year day, each date from 1 March to 30 December shows the next day's row, as on the screens there. The regions' towns come from a sample of the screens (2,047 pages), so a region no sampled screen shows is placed at its computing point, which may not be the town it serves, and a mosque whose screen shows another region's table than its town's differs (Damascus's Imam al-Dhahabi mosque, in the owner's dataset, prints 844's). The live screens (8.8) show aladhan's default for their point as it was when each was set up, and their Asr depends on that moment, so the live towns' Asr is within 1 min, not to the minute. «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the place's own times with the ministry's method.
- **Kuwait's places** (8.8, Q15): a place the sheet does not list takes the nearest listed place's whole-minute shift on all six times, whatever the distance. Where that shift would put a prayer before its time there by more than the table's own margin plus 4 minutes, the validity guard gives the place `kw_awqaf` at its own point instead (6.5); in the western desert, Abdali's or Jahra's + 2 comes at most about 3 minutes early, so it stays. The printed shifts follow longitude only, so at an unlisted place the shift differs from the longitude difference to Kuwait City by a median 0.7 minute and up to about 3 minutes (midway between Al-Salmi's + 6 and Jahra's + 2), and latitude moves Fajr and Isha by up to about 3 more. The western suburbs' calendar at + 1 (14 dataset mosques), a value the sheet does not list, is 1 minute from both neighbours (Kuwait City's 0, Jahra's + 2).
- **Borders** (A12): near a border the country comes from the nearest cities and the network country, so a point just across can get the neighbour's method until the user picks.
- **Israel** is not in the first release (14). With no Israeli rows, a point there takes the country of the nearest Arab city within 40 km (Palestine near the West Bank and Gaza, Jordan or Egypt near Eilat), and otherwise the network country's default, `mwl`. The owner's mosque dataset files about 90 mosques of Arab towns in Israel (Umm al-Fahm, Nazareth, Tamra, Haifa, Jaffa…) under Palestine, and they share their calendars with West Bank and Jerusalem mosques. For now the dataset is respected as it stands: those mosques count as Palestine in every analysis (owner, 2026-10-11), and the owner will review them.
- **Libya**'s formula is fitted (8.2), but the points of its cities other than Tripoli rest on two October days each; the daily collector checks them over a year (9.3). A place the Awqaf does not list takes the nearest of its 22 cities however far (8.8), so west of that city its times come early: by up to 3–4 min at Zuwara, and beyond about 1° of longitude Maghrib comes before the local sunset and Dhuhr before the local noon (Nalut and Wazin up to 6–7 and 4–5 min, Tazirbu from Kufra's table 9 and 6, Ghat from Sabha's 487 km away 9–19 and 13–14, with Fajr up to 25 min early). The validity guard (6.5) gives those places the Awqaf formula at their own point instead. Sabratha and Zuwara keep Zawiya's table: their Maghrib and Dhuhr stay after their sunset and noon, and Zawiya's truncated Fajr, Asr and Isha, up to 2.1 and 4.4 min before their own, stay within the guard's tolerance (Zuwara's about 3.5 min beyond Zawiya's own). «حسب موقعي بالضبط» gives the Awqaf formula at the user's own point anywhere.
- **Yemen** follows Sanaa's table everywhere, Aden-held areas included; whether those areas' mosques follow it is unknown. Its data starts on 2026-05-21, so winter and Ramadan are unchecked. A place's terrain class comes from the catalogue `dem` of a nearby place (a median 7 km away), which, with the nearest location of either class beyond 60 km, gives about 6 % of populated places the wrong Maghrib class (E10). A place far from every location would take the nearest one's table (8.8): 5.4 % of populated places (18 % of the 311 catalogue places) lie over 60 km from the location they map to and 0.5 % (7 %) over 100 km, up to about 280 km in the deserts of northern Hadramawt and Mahra and about 200 km on Abd al-Kuri, where the times would differ from `ye_awqaf` computed at the place by up to about 11 minutes; the validity guard (6.5) gives `ye_awqaf` at their own point where the location's times come before their own, or its sunrise after, beyond its rounding plus 4 minutes (Rumah, up to about 9.6 minutes early; Abd al-Kuri), and the scan with the fitted points settles the places near that threshold (Mocha, 2.6–3.8 minutes early; 8.8).
- **Mauritania** publishes only Ramadan tables. The app applies their rule all year (Maghrib + 3 included, Q6); times outside about mid-February to mid-May are not checked against any official figure. A place takes its wilaya's table whatever the distance, with the validity guard (6.5, 8.8): far from the capital, Fajr can differ from the place's own sun by about 23 minutes (Aïn Ben Tili), 12 at Bir Moghrein and 5–8 at Bassikounou, Ouadane, Chami, Moudjeria, Djiguenni, Tamchekett, Tichitt, Boutilimit and Ouad Naga (36 department seats checked, at approximate points). East of the capital the table's sunrise comes after the place's own, so the validity guard (6.5) computes such a place at its own point where that goes beyond the table's own margin plus 4 minutes: Aïn Ben Tili (Zouerat's sunrise about 16 minutes late). Ouad Naga (1–2 minutes) keeps Rosso's table, and the places near the threshold (Bir Moghrein about 6 minutes, Tichitt about 5, Boutilimit, Bassikounou, Ouadane and Chami about 4, and those west of their capital) are settled by the scan with the fitted points.
- **Iraq's cities other than Baghdad** are computed as the Endowment computes them, at Accurate Times' points (8.8). Their printed tables were not found online, so the points rest on that program's data and on mosque calendars in six cities (owner's dataset, support only). A place far from every city takes the nearest one's table, with the validity guard (6.5), and can be several minutes from its own times; in the dataset, Al-Buwayr's mosque calls Mosul's table though Tal Afar's point is nearer.
- **The Kurdistan Region** has no table found online, and its mosques follow their own calendars (Erbil's 35 share one: Fajr = its sunrise − 89, Isha = Maghrib + 75). The Sunni Endowment's method, shown there with «فلكية», is a median 1–9 min earlier at Fajr (up to about 20) and up to 14–24 min later at Isha (owner's dataset, support only). Users adjust with the minute settings.
- **Bahrain**'s profile is fitted to one year, the 1448 calendar (Q7): 1 minute off on about 5 % of times (Asr and Maghrib on about 9 % of days) until the 1449 calendar confirms or refits it. Bahrain's national table is not an envelope: at Hawar, Fajr comes up to about 80 s before the national time in winter, and in the south of the main island Maghrib can come after it. The app gives every place in Bahrain the national table, as the SCIA publishes it for the whole Kingdom, with the validity guard (6.5): Hawar keeps it, the table's winter sunrise coming at most about 1.5 minutes after its own, within the guard's 4 minutes (8.8). The Hawar row (6.8) is also the nearest catalogue row on Qatar's coast facing the islands, so a fix there with no network takes Bahrain's table; a Qatari network keeps it in Qatar.
- **Sudan, Somalia, Djibouti and Comoros** have no official table the app can use yet. Sudan's Khartoum mosques used three printed tables, up to 7 minutes apart. Djibouti's mosques differed until a Ramadan 2026 circular from the Directorate of Islamic Affairs unified the Maghrib and suhur adhan across the capital and the regions (Al-Qarn, 2026-02-19); no times were published with it. In the owner's dataset, Somalia's 3 mosques use MWL 18°/17° and 16 of 24 Comoros mosques the traditional Moroni table (5.6); their formulas are fitted from those mosque times (A3). Web searches on 2026-10-11 found no official or printed table for Somalia, Djibouti or the Comoros (Q16).
- **No Ramadan or Eid outside Tunisia** (A10, A11). Abroad the date row shows Tunisia's Hijri date, which can differ by a day from the local one, and nothing is silenced for Eid.

## 13. Open questions

Each has a stable id, cited elsewhere as (Qn). Q1–Q19 come from the Arab research, Q20–Q25 from the European research, Q26–Q27 from the merge, Q28 from the audience note, Q29–Q32 from the mosque dataset and Q33–Q37 from the France review.

- **Q1. Palestine (answered 2026-10-11)**: Dar al-Ifta gives no rule for the places it does not list, so under the owner's rule (2026-10-11) a place takes the nearest of its 16 listed places in Palestine, whatever the distance (Salfit and Tubas: Nablus's 0; Bidya: Qalqilya's + 2), and Qalqilya's + 1.5 is applied as + 2, the whole minute the dataset's only mosque of that group (Ramla) uses (8.8). In the Gaza Strip, `ps_gaza_awqaf` is one zone «محافظات غزة», since the ministry publishes one table for all five governorates (8.8). A place in the Jerusalem governorate takes the phone's zone when it is Asia/Jerusalem or Asia/Hebron, otherwise Asia/Hebron (6.8). Still open: Tubas and Qalqilya have no mosque data, so a calendar or Awqaf imsakiya from either would confirm or change their 0 and + 2; a Dar al-Ifta or Awqaf statement attaching unlisted places (by governorate or otherwise) would replace the nearest-place rule.
- **Q2. Yemen (answered 2026-10-11)**: Sanaa's published table (awqaf.gov.ye) serves all of Yemen, Aden-held areas included; ICOP's preset matches no published Yemeni table and is not used (8.5). The fit of 134 days × 31 locations gives the `Pressure` horizon at each location's elevation, nearest rounding with Asr's noon shadow at the declination of 00:00 local time (E4), one rule all year whatever the endpoint's `season` field, and Maghrib + 6 at or below 650 m and + 10 at or above 1,100 m, the gap taking the nearest location's class, with `NEAREST` to the nearest location of the place's own class within 60 km, and otherwise, under the owner rule of 2026-10-11, to the nearest location with no reach limit (E10, 8.8). Still open: the winter rows (from 2026-12) and Ramadan 1448 (no change until then, and never an Imsak row, A14); Dhuhr held out at 92.2 % (AM2's sun-model grid); Aden's own month starts matter only for Appendix C.
- **Q3. Qatar (answered 2026-10-11)**: Both Qatari profiles apply Ramadan's Maghrib + 1 on Umm al-Qura's days: the Ministry API's own Hijri labels are no fixed shift of it (a day behind on most dates sampled in 1442–1444, equal in 1446, a day ahead from Jumada II 1447 to Rabi I 1448 and again in Ramadan 1448), while Qatar's announced starts, QCH's dates and both Doha mosques of the owner's dataset follow Umm al-Qura (E8). Dhuhr and Asr stay at the Ministry's minute (A5): its API has no + 1 on 2026-10-11, and the State Grand Mosque's calendar dropped QCH's + 1 on 1 Muharram 1448 (its Friday time on 2026-10-09 is the Ministry's 11:21), while the dataset's other Doha mosque copies QCH. Al Shamal, Dukhan and Abu Samra follow QCH's print, computed at fitted town points, with sunrise, Dhuhr and Asr at the same points (8.8). Still open: the minute mosques call on 2027-02-07, from which the API already applies its Ramadan + 1 (Umm al-Qura's 1 Ramadan is 02-08); a full year of QCH pages to refit the town points (AM2); more Qatari mosque calendars (the dataset has two, both in Doha, none in the three towns).
- **Q4. Saudi Arabia (answered 2026-10-11)**: KACST is matched with E4 `KACST` for Fajr and Asr, the event instant for sunrise, Dhuhr and Maghrib, the Meeus sun and a −0.83° sunrise horizon (E2): 97.8–98.2 % exact per prayer on the held-out 2025 and 2027 tables and 100 % within 1 minute, KACST's previous-date days left out (8.7). Fajr 19° before 1430 is not shipped, since KACST's own service gives 18.5° for every year (E9); in Appendix C, Saudi Isha + 120 follows the announced Ramadan, the month the ministry's rule names, with the computed Umm al-Qura month as fallback (C.1). Still open: the few seconds behind the remaining 1-minute misses, the rule that picks the previous-date days, the −0.83° sunrise on a further KACST year (AM2), and a year in which the announced Ramadan differs from Umm al-Qura's.
- **Q5. Morocco (answered 2026-10-11)**: Fajr is 19° rounded down (archived official tables of 2024: 86 of 88, against 82 for 19.1° nearest). Sunrise is − 1 and Maghrib + 2, rounded to the nearest minute, on horizons fitted per locality from one official month (about −1.17° at sea level, lower with height; 85 and 86 of 88 on the same tables, against 73 and 69 with no fixed minutes); neither the town's height nor the terrain around it reproduces them (rms 0.10° and 0.07°), so the fitted values are stored and the formulas of 8.2 serve only at exact coordinates. Still open: the archived tables are coastal (Rabat, Laayoune), and inland the dataset's year-long copies fit Maghrib + 1 better than + 2 (96 % against 93 %, 7 of 8 localities), so a year of Habous tables (9.3) settles the inland minutes; Habous's own heights are unknown.
- **Q6. Mauritania (answered 2026-10-11)**: The imsakiya's rule holds all year, Maghrib + 3 on the standard sunset included: the 2021 and corrected 2024 tables call the later time «المغرب» (2025 calls it «الإفطار»), and the homepage block's + 0 comes with an unshifted sunrise too, so it is a plain 19.5°/17.5° computation, not the ministry's rule. The reprinted imsakiya's reference year is 2023, the year it first appeared (from 2003 on, only years of 2015's leap phase fit, and they fit alike). 2022's change (Fajr 19.0° in all 13 capitals, Isha 17.0°) has no published reason and stays a past-only fixture. Still open: any year-round or summer table, and whether the 1448 imsakiya (about 2027-02-08) is again the 2023 reprint, or names departments or offsets for places far from their wilaya's capital (the default follows the wilaya, 8.8; a published rule would replace it).
- **Q7. Bahrain (answered 2026-10-11)**: the SCIA publishes its method and its yearly calendar (almajles.gov.bh; 1448 covers 2026-06-16 to 2027-06-05), and `bh_calendar` is fitted to that calendar: Fajr 18° and sunrise at 26.22N 50.68E; Dhuhr = transit + 1.1, Asr factor 1, Maghrib at sunset and Isha 17.8° at 26.22N 50.44E; every time rounded down except Maghrib (nearest), on the VSOP87 sun. It reproduces 94.6 % of the calendar's times exactly and all within 1 minute (95.5 % on the dataset's mosque calendars before 2026-06-16, presumably the 1447 table, support only), while the stated 18° Isha would be 1–2 minutes late on 91 % of days. Still open: why the stated 18° Isha and 26.27N morning point fit worse; Asr and Maghrib exact on only 91 % of days; the 1449 calendar (about June 2027) is the held-out check (8.2, 8.7, 9.3). Coverage (2026-10-11): the calendar's page on the Kingdom puts it between 25°32′ and 26°20′ N, which takes in the Hawar Islands, and the calendar names no other place or offset, so the national table applies everywhere in Bahrain, Hawar included (8.8).
- **Q8. Iraq (answered 2026-10-11)**: The Sunni Endowment's 2026 Baghdad table is a perpetual table computed for 2021 (Fajr and Isha 365 of 365; the "6 h" quirk was that reference year), with Accurate Times' Baghdad point and elevation and Asr on the apparent sun, so `iq_sunni_endowment` is fitted at 2021 and computed for the actual year (A8, 8.2, 8.7); ICOP's preset matches neither the table nor any Iraqi mosque calendar and is not used. The Kurdistan Region has no table found online and its mosques follow their own calendars, so there the Endowment's method shows «فلكية» with a line saying so (6.11, 12). Elsewhere in Iraq (owner rule, 2026-10-11), each place takes the nearest of the Endowment's 28 city tables, computed as it computes them at Accurate Times' points (8.8). Still open: the 2027 table (a repeat of 2026 by month and day confirms 2021), the exact sunrise and Maghrib horizon and rounding (AM2), whether ICOP's preset was the rule before the Endowment's 2021–2022 review, a printed Endowment city table outside Baghdad to confirm the points (its 2023 and 2024 Ramadan imsakiyas, one image each on its Telegram channel, were not read), a published Kurdistan table, and, with Appendix C, Kurdistan's own Ramadan and Eid days.
- **Q9. Egypt (answered 2026-10-11)**: There is no Asr bias: EGSA takes the noon shadow at the declination of transit, the engine's own Asr (6.2), which matches 96.6 % of Asr times out of sample (78 cities, 8 days of 2025–26, points fitted on the other five prayers), against 92.8 % with the declination at the Asr instant, whose daily mean drifts from − 0.12 to + 0.19 min with the season and gave the + 0.36 min on 6 cities of 2025-08-13. The tie at exactly 30 s cannot be seen in a table, since any sub-second convention equals moving an unpublished city point by about 0.002°, so `eg_egsa` keeps NEAREST (E1) with no Asr offset. Still open, for AM2 only: the two 2024 summer copies of the daily page do not fit the 2025–26 points, though Cairo's Ramadan 2024 imsakiya does (8.1).
- **Q10. Lebanon (answered 2026-10-11)**: Dar al-Fatwa prints a Ramadan imsakiya for Beirut (its General Directorate of Islamic Awqaf; 1445 is on darelfatwa.gov.lb), but no year-round calendar was found on its site or in the press; a perpetual Beirut table that a mosque-screen service serves under its name equals that imsakiya on all 180 times, so `lb_dar_al_fatwa` is fitted from both (8.2, 8.7) and Lebanon moves to tier B (9.5). Still open: a second official year to confirm the table is perpetual, and who publishes the table the North's mosques call (12). Owner rule of 2026-10-11: the 1445 imsakiya says its times are Beirut's («وفق موقع مدينة بيروت») and prints no offsets for Tripoli, Saida, the Bekaa or any other place, and the site's media library and site searches of 2026-10-11 («إمساكية», «امساكية», «رمضان», «مواقيت») hold no other imsakiya or timetable, so every Lebanese place takes Beirut's table, the nearest official place, with no reach limit (A2, 6.5); the method at the user's point comes only with «حسب موقعي بالضبط» and, by the validity guard, in the far south and on the north-eastern border (6.5). An imsakiya or offset list from Dar al-Fatwa or one of its regional offices (Tripoli and the North, Saida, Zahle and the Bekaa) would add its places as zones, the North's table included if one of them publishes it.
- **Q11. Hijri month names (answered 2026-10-11)**: for internationalization only (D19), the app keeps its own table per country, since CLDR gives every `ar-<CC>` ربيع الآخر and جمادى الآخرة: ربيع الثاني and جمادى الثانية in Tunisia and Algeria, ربيع الثاني and جمادى الآخرة in Jordan, and ربيع الآخر and جمادى الآخرة elsewhere, the default; only an authority's statements or printed calendar move a country off the default (6.10). Still open: Mauritania and Kuwait show ربيع الثاني only in a generated date on the ministry's site, and Libya names neither month in a statement, so a statement naming month 4 would move them; Bahrain, Lebanon, Syria, Palestine, Iraq, Yemen, Sudan, Somalia, Djibouti and the Comoros are not checked.
- **Q12. Syria (answered 2026-10-11)**: `sy_awqaf` applies all year, Maghrib + 2 included, with no Ramadan-only rule: the ministry adopted MWL officially for prayer, imsak and iftar times with no Ramadan limit (2025-02-28), its latest tables (1447) have + 2, and ICOP's preset has + 2 with no Ramadan condition (8.5). In the owner's dataset (5.6, support only), the one calendar that copies the 1447 Damascus table (a mosque in Arbin) keeps the same rule outside Ramadan (Maghrib on 112 of 122 days from January to May 2026, Fajr and Isha 29 of 30 in April), and the calendar shared by 11 Damascus mosques, the Umayyad Mosque among them, uses 18° / 17° with Maghrib about + 2 all year; + 2 applies from 2025-03-30, the day after the last row of the 1446 tables, since the Arbin calendar also has it before Ramadan 1447 (E9, provisional). Still open: an official non-Ramadan table, summer above all (the Arbin calendar's June–August rows are not the ministry's), and the exact start date, which a dated announcement or a 2025 table could move. Superseded on 2026-10-18: the ministry's screens show year tables, which `sy_awqaf` now reproduces (8.8), so no rule is carried from Ramadan to the rest of the year.
- **Q13. UAE (answered 2026-10-11)**: Awqaf's 2026 tables follow Accurate Times' method (Fajr and sunrise at the eastern point, Dhuhr + 2 and the rest at the western point, Asr on the apparent sun) but not all of its stored inputs, so `ae_awqaf`'s zones are fitted in AM2 with `loc.txt` as seeds: Abu Dhabi and Al Ain 98 % exact, Dubai 93 %, every time within 1 minute (8.6). Jumu'a is 12:45 in all seven emirates, Dubai and Sharjah included, by the federal circular. Still open: the cause of Dubai's month-to-month biases (a full daily year from the Open Data API, or IACAD's inputs), Asr's June–July misses of about half a minute in Abu Dhabi and Al Ain, a held-out UAE year, a Sharjah statement or 2026 timetable that would give Sharjah its own `jumua` rule, and the rule by which Awqaf's server attaches coordinates to a location (8.8). Update (2026-10-18): Asr's June–July misses went with the noon shadow at Dhuhr (E4 `SHADOW_AT_DHUHR`). On the whole 2026 year that Awqaf emailed for nine locations, eight pass tier A held out by month-split (Al Ain with its sunrise at a point of its own, S6); Dubai does not, its single-minute misses scattered over every month whatever the point, angle or sun model, so only IACAD's own inputs would show its cause. A held-out year is still a second year (2027).
- **Q14. Libya (answered 2026-10-11)**: the Awqaf table is perpetual, keyed by month and day, with no 29 February row. It is 18.35° / 18.35° at a twilight latitude of (1 − e²)·φ, Dhuhr + 4, Maghrib + 4, Asr factor 1, truncated, on a low-precision sun taken at 10:00 UT (8.2). It reproduces 97 % of 642 official minutes, and Zawiya's 12 of 12. Eastern mosques largely follow the same formula at their own point (Benghazi and Ajdabiya 80–96 % exact in the dataset, support only). The eastern authorities' Ramadan and Eid days matter only for Appendix C. Still open: the other cities' points, from a year of the daily collection.
- **Q15. Kuwait (answered 2026-10-11)**: each place's published offset is one whole-minute shift of all six times, added to Kuwait City's computed times (Jahra + 2 on Fajr, sunrise, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha alike): the sheet's footer gives one figure per place and names no prayer, the figures follow longitude to within a minute, and 96 of the dataset's 97 Kuwaiti mosques follow the sheet with one shift on all six times (8.8). The February sheet prints its own 29 February row for years divisible by 4 (its values stay in `test-data-local/`, A7); the harness reads it at the leap day after the reference year and counts 29 February and 1 March, where the printed Fajr and sunrise go out of sequence, as quirk days (E5, 8.3, 9.6), while the app computes 29 February like any other date (A8). A place between or beyond the listed places takes the nearest one, whatever the distance (owner rule, 2026-10-11; 8.8). Still open: a written ministry or Al-Ojairi statement, or the ministry app's per-area times, to confirm both; per-area times from the ministry would replace the nearest-place rule where they differ.
- **Q16. Somalia, Djibouti, Comoros (answered 2026-10-11)**: no official or printed table is online. Web searches in Arabic, French and Somali and the sites of Somalia's federal ministry (mesa.gov.so), SONNA, Al-Qarn and Al-Watwan found only third-party calculators, which disagree with each other (the one Al-Qarn showed uses the Egyptian method, Fajr 6 min before Djibouti's mosques), and Ramadan or Eid notices only, so `so_fitted`, `dj_fitted` and `km_fitted` stay as fitted (A3). Still open: who prints the Comoros' traditional Moroni table, whose 5-day blocks suggest a printed sheet; a copy would be collected (9.3) and become `km_fitted`'s test target (9.5).
- **Q17. Ibadi communities of the M'zab and Jabal Nafusa (answered 2026-10-11)**: no table of their own was found, from an authority or an Ibadi council, and Ibadi law starts the prayers when the Maliki school does, so the app adds nothing: their places take `dz_marw` or `ly_awqaf`, with a line in 12 (8.4). In the dataset, 11 M'zab mosques hold Isha at Maghrib + 60, 12–35 min before 17°, and 27 more at + 70 or + 75. Still open: whether the + 60 comes from a calendar printed by the M'zab's Ibadi councils; if one is found, it is a candidate option for the valley under «طرق أخرى».
- **Q18. Shia outside IQ, LB and BH (closed)**: not needed, since the app offers no Shia method (A4).
- **Q19. Jordan (answered 2026-10-11)**: the 18 further places' monthly half-minute offsets (2025 calendar, pages 8–19) are the computed difference from Amman on the 15th of each month: refitted points reproduce 1,268 of 1,296 (595 of 648 on held-out months), all within 0.5, so the app derives them on the phone and adds them to Amman's time, as the ministry instructs (8.8); only 15 of the 18 are Accurate Times entries. No official full year beyond 2023 can be downloaded, but the 2025 PDF's daily pages (1 January to 9 February, all 15 zones, about 590 zone-days) extend the 31 transcribed zone-days in AM2; as support only, the 2023-fitted zones reproduce the 2026 zone calendars of the dataset's mosques at 97.8 % exact in 9 of 11 zones, 99.9 % within 1 minute. Still open: Mafraq's and Aqaba's mosque calendars call Maghrib 1–3 minutes after the fit (a mosque setting or a 2026 change; AM2 checks the ministry's 2026 site once the owner allows its form), and the dataset's one mosque each near Qatraneh and Safawi carries Amman's and Mafraq's table unchanged, not the offsets.
- **Q20. Night in the MWL 1986 rule (answered 2026-10-11)**: sunset to sunrise, never Maghrib to Fajr. The 1986 resolution does not define the night, but it places Fajr, like Isha, by its share of the night at 45°, which a night ending at Fajr cannot do; the MWL committee's 2009 text defines the night as sunset to sunrise, and MWL's 2010 tables give Isha and Fajr the same share of that night in all 16 UK cities. Every rule that takes a share of the night uses it (6.2, 7.4); the night dial keeps Maghrib to Fajr.
- **Q21. Grande Mosquée de Paris (answered 2026-10-11)**: its 2026 timetable is `mcw`'s Fajr (18°, bounded by the Moonsighting Committee's seasonal curve, so at Paris always sunrise − 92 to 118 min), Isha = Maghrib + 87, Dhuhr + 5 and Maghrib + 3, so it needs no summer rule (Fajr 03:50 and Isha 23:28 on 21 June); `gmp` takes it, fitted from its October 2026 image (every time within 1 min, 7.2, 7.5). The shared Paris table is the mosque's former fixed clock table, equal to its Ramadan 2023 and 2024 imsakiyat by date and replaced by October 2026, so it is not offered. Still open: a second 2026 month from the mosque itself (the summer half rests on the dataset's rows) and the date of the switch.
- **Q22. DUM RF (answered 2026-10-11)**: its monthly Moscow tables (dumrf.ru; May, July, August and October 2026, June and July 2025) use 18° / 15° with the angle-based rule every night, Fajr at 0.30 and Isha at 0.25 of the night, except that Isha is at 0.30 of the night in June with a hand-set ramp around it; `dum_rf` follows the 2026 sheets within 2 min and is fitted from them (7.4). Still open: the ramp changes from year to year (July 2025 is up to 3 min off the 2026 ramp), so each new summer sheet is checked, and whether DUM RF's practice holds outside Moscow and its oblast is unknown.
- **Q23. Ireland (answered 2026-10-11)**: ICCI's own timetable (its app API's table, settings updated 2018-06-04, archived 2022-08-02) is the perpetual Dublin table the Islamic Foundation of Ireland still publishes (read 2026-10-11), equal on every time but a later summer Isha; `mwl_angle` does not match it (3 of 355 Fajr and 2 of 355 Isha times, up to 54 and 71 min off), and its 22:45 is ICCI's Isha iqamah, which the 2026 website shows as the latest Isha. No preset reproduces the table, so under the owner's rule of 2026-10-11 IE's default is `ie_ifi`, fitted to it with E18 (D4, 7.5); `mwl`, the former default, never shows Isha before the table's and Fajr after it only by up to 5 min on 23 days, but its Fajr is up to 53 min earlier in April and May; `mwl_angle` stays under «طرق أخرى» with a corrected evidence line. The IFI also publishes Ramadan 2026 tables for 32 other towns (read 2026-10-11): Dublin's equals the perpetual table on all 180 times, and Cork's keeps Dublin's Fajr and Isha intervals within 1 min at Cork's own sunrise and Maghrib, so each IFI town is computed at its own point and an Irish place takes the nearest town (6.5). Still open: ICCI's printed card for 2026 and which times the Clonskeagh mosque calls; either would change only `mwl_angle`'s evidence line. Also open: the IFI's year tables for its other towns, which M3 checks against Cork's rule.
- **Q24. Tunisia's outline (closed)**: whether the OSM outline includes territorial waters no longer matters: every fix in Tunisia takes its nearest delegation (D7, 6.8).
- **Q25. Morocco (closed)**: the ministry's own parameters, open in the European research, are answered by the Habous fit (8.2, 8.7).
- **Q26. Neighbour polygons (closed by A12)**: whether the Natural Earth polygons of the land neighbours (TR, IR, ES and the others, 6.8) are whole countries or only the parts near an Arab border. Whole countries would decide places such as Edirne by polygon rather than by the city rule.
- **Q27. Jordan's Hijri estimate (answered 2026-10-11)**: no. The eastern zone is one of the two zones of ICOP's Global Hijri Calendar (Odeh, updated 2022-10: a month starts the day after an evening on which, on Odeh's criterion, the crescent is visible by telescope or better from any land between 180°E and 20°W), not the unified calendar the Istanbul 2016 congress chose; it matched all 5 of Jordan's listed Ramadan and Shawwal starts of 1445–1447, where Diyanet's Istanbul 2016 dates missed 2, but not Dhu al-Hijja 1445, which Jordan began with Saudi Arabia a day earlier. Appendix C gives Jordan its own eastern-zone table with Umm al-Qura for Dhu al-Hijja; still open, for later: whether Jordan's Dhu al-Hijja always follows Saudi Arabia, and whether the build code reproduces ICOP's table exactly.
- **Q29. Jordan's zone list (answered 2026-10-11)**: the ministry publishes it by governorate: its 2025 calendar attaches Zarqa, Balqa and Madaba to Amman except the places it names apart, and its 2026 site labels that zone «عمان، البلقاء، الزرقاء، مادبا». 8.8 maps a place by its governorate, the valley's elevation and the nearest town the ministry names, which in an approximate check gives 95.4 % of the dataset's followers their own table (74.0 % for the nearest zone point). Still open: the 2026 calendar's own list (17,858 KB on awqaf.gov.jo, listed on 2026-10-11; too large for the fetch tool, so AM2 downloads it with the owner's go-ahead), since the 2026 site lists Ruwaished and no longer Umm al-Qutain. Until then (decided 2026-10-11) Umm al-Qutain stays a zone and Ruwaished a further place, since the site keeps the calendar's ids and a 2026 mosque calendar of the Northern Badia still follows Umm al-Qutain's table (8.8). If the 2026 calendar drops Umm al-Qutain, the same rule maps its area without it (Mafraq's table); if it prints Ruwaished as a daily zone, or the site's Ruwaished table matches its own points better than Amman plus the derived offset, Ruwaished's zone drops `baseZoneId`.
- **Q30. Winter Friday times in Europe (answered 2026-10-11)**: winter values come from mosques' published winter Friday times, else from the dataset's values left from winter, never from summer − 60, each clamped to Dhuhr + 5, and a season with no value found is `AT_DHUHR` (6.9). Still open: a Friday between December and February, ideally from the owner's mosques, would confirm the weakest values (Belgium and Portugal on 10 and 5 dataset values, Paris split between 13:00 and 13:30, Spain mostly from Catalonia); Madrid's and Munich's winter, Italy's summer, and Norway, Austria, Ireland and Luxembourg still have no value.
- **Q31. The mosque dataset's origin and terms (closed)**: the owner collected it from the mosques, which agreed to its analysis, processing and copying (5.6).
- **Q32. France's 12° minutes (answered 2026-10-11)**: no Musulmans de France or GEDIS text of 2020 or later states minutes (musulmansdefrance.fr serves a casino page and no new MF site was found; Mosquée de Hautepierre 2025 and Havre de Savoir 2026 give angles but no minutes; iqrashop.com's 2026 «UOIF» calendar comes from the seller's own generator), so `mdf_12` stays 12° / 12° with no minutes (D15) and a user copies a mosque's minutes with «تعديل الأوقات بالدقائق» (6.11). The dataset shows no single minute set either: none of the 318 French mosques fitting 12° / 12° within 0.3° uses the 2012 text's minutes (Fajr − 5, Dhuhr + 5, Maghrib + 5, Isha + 0), and 178 share Dhuhr + 5 with Maghrib + 3. Reopened only by a printed GEDIS calendar of 2020 or later; its stated minutes would become `mdf_12`'s.
- **Q33. Rhône (answered 2026-10-11)**: No table after 2020: the council's page still links only its 2020 PDF (a file uploaded in May 2025), and the Lyon mosques' 2026 calendar equals it by date on 99–100 % of days, where a table recomputed for 2026 would change 53–60 % of the sunrise, Fajr, Maghrib and Isha minutes, so the mosques reuse it every year like a perpetual table (A8). It is one table for the whole département, used unshifted as far as Tarare (34 km from Lyon), so in Rhône `rhone_ctir` takes every time from one zone at Lyon's point (6.5). Of the 10 Turkish mosques 9 do not use it, nor do those around Villefranche-sur-Saône, while 4 mosques just outside Rhône (3 in Isère, 1 in Ain) do; the zone stays in 69, as published (5.6).
- **Q34. Réunion (answered 2026-10-11)**: the CIR's tables for Ramadan 1444, 1445 and 1446 and Chawwal 1445, and Baytoul Hayaate's Ramadan 1447 copy, are one table repeated by month and day; over 81 days from 19 February to 10 May they confirm `re_cir`'s 18° Fajr (within 0–2 min, almost always earlier), Maghrib = sunset + 3 (within 1 min) and elevation shifts, and none prints Dhuhr, Asr or Isha. The one managed mosque display found (Bras-Fusil, Saint-Benoît, 12–17 October 2026) shows Fajr and Isha at 18° on 6 of 6 days, so `re_cir`'s Isha becomes 18°; its minute offsets (Dhuhr + 5, Asr + 1, Maghrib + 2) are not adopted (D15), and the Asr factor stays asked once (that display uses 2). Since 2026-10-11 `re_cir` follows the CIR's four regions (`NAMED_REGIONS`, 7.6) at fitted points, with Maghrib = sunset + 2 rounded up, within 1 min of every CIR row. Still open: a Saint-Denis or Saint-Pierre timetable with Dhuhr, Asr and Isha, and a CIR month between May and February, which would also pin down the zone points.
- **Q35. French Guiana (answered 2026-10-11)**: A2CMGF's January 2026 monthly calendars for Cayenne and Remire-Montjoly (IslamicFinder pages it posts) name MWL, 18° / 17° with Asr factor 1, and its Cayenne mosque's Masjidbox page shows the same times in October 2026, so GF keeps MWL with Jumu'a at 13:30, since 2026-10-11 with the calendars' rounding (`gf_a2cmgf`, 7.6). The 2010 Cayenne table is no longer followed: its Dhuhr is 4–5 min later, its Maghrib 2–3 and its Isha 3–4. Still open: the live table on a2cmgf.com uses 15° / 15° (ISNA's angles) and names no method, so GF is revisited if the mosque's page or posted calendars move to it; no table was read for Kourou or Saint-Laurent-du-Maroni.
- **Q36. Mayotte (answered 2026-10-11)**: no authority's prayer table was found for Mayotte. horaires-priere-mayotte.fr belongs to MayExperInfo, a small IT company in Labattoir (SIREN 791525892) that names no source, so "officielles" is its own word; its 2026 table follows the island's traditional table, the Moroni table − 3 (all five prayers equal on 259 of 365 days, none more than 3 min off, and equal to the dataset's Koungou calendar on 327), and the Conseil cadial's communiqués give only religious dates, so `yt_traditional` keeps «فلكية» with the line of 6.11. Still open: a printed calendar of the Conseil cadial or the Grand Cadi, if one circulates offline, would replace the site as the test reference (9.2).
- **Q39. What the user may control (answered 2026-10-12)**: the owner asked for prayer times that are scientifically precise and Islamically valid, with the user in control of the parameters. The inputs fall into three groups: the place and date (latitude, longitude, elevation, time zone); the astronomy (the sun's position, refraction, the sun's radius, the solver), which is physics and stays fixed; and the definitions, which are ijtihad (Fajr's and Isha's twilight, the Asr shadow, the margins after zawal and sunset, the short-night rule). «مخصّصة» lets the user set the definitions only, each within the recognised positions and labelled with who holds them, and E19's bounds on the computed start check the result (6.11). On the dataset the editor alone reproduces 66 % of the dataset's 17,627 records with at least 300 scored days within 5 min on all six times, and 75 % with the minute steppers (7.7).
- **Q38. France's fragmentation (answered 2026-10-12)**: the owner asked for a strategy that is scientifically and religiously sound and as close as possible to the user's nearby mosques, since the French list matched only about a quarter of mosques. Measured on the dataset, each mosque predicted from the others (7.7): the list's fallback 12 % of the plan's 1,669 mainland records on all six times within 5 min, its best entry 19 %, the nearest other mosque 23 %, the most representative nearby mosque 26 %. The owner then decided that no mosques are bundled (it cannot scale to the world, and mosque administrators often lack the astronomy and fiqh a correct time needs), so France keeps the list of D4 inside a validity envelope (E19) set by a fiqh and science research (Appendix A), with the bounded «مخصّصة» (6.11), which with the minute steppers matches about 61 % of the 1,673 French records of the editor analysis within 5 min. Decided under the owner's rule to research and apply: the Isha floor is the 12° instant, or the Moonsighting red-twilight curve for a rule that estimates Isha on short nights, marked «تقديري» (12° for every rule would move the Grande Mosquée de Paris's June Isha up to 13 min later and Diyanet's Strasbourg Isha up to 17); E19 bounds the computed start, not a mosque's adhan or iqama, and the shown Fajr, which is also the fast's cutoff, is never after the 12° instant; no imsak line (A14); Maghrib's start is not before sunset + 1, with no upper bound since 2026-10-13 (a later Maghrib is a delayed start, not an invalid one; the editor's + 1 to + 10 keeps a custom start near sunset); the 18° floor and the disk bounds keep a time within 1 min of them; no flag after the first third of the night; the plan's "MWL 1986 ± 2 min" was found only in the ECFR's quotation of the resolution, and 7.2 now says so. Reopened by a French observation campaign (14). Revised on 2026-10-13 after the independent review (its 12.1–12.2): E19 bounds the starts the app computes, not a mosque's adhan or iqama, which can be later and still a valid prayer, so a timetable that crosses it is not called wrong; mosque timetables are sorted offline into four outcomes (matches a tested start rule, does not match those rules, possible delayed adhan or iqama, potential prayer-window or calendar error), and only the last is checked as an error (7.7); the Maghrib upper bound of sunset + 10 left E19, since Maghrib's time does not end there and the French list never reaches it; APBIF's published procedure (18° with the local solar midnight on nights without it, 12°, + 6, its −1° horizon) became the preset `apbif` under «طرق أخرى» (7.2, 7.5). A study of the apps used in France the same day (7.7) found the general apps on 12° with no tie to the mosque (Google, Aladhan and adhan-based apps about 10 % of mosques; Muslim Pro's minutes about 22 %), and the mosque-display systems (Mawaqit, Masjidbox) showing each mosque's own table with no validity check.
- **Q37. Luxembourg (answered 2026-10-11)**: the Shoura's current 2026 table (shoura.lu, English and Bosnian editions, identical in June and July) keeps the real 13°: on the 55 nights from 24 May to 18 July where D5 would move Fajr or Isha, it is within 2.4 min of the real Fajr and 4 min of the real Isha, and a median 13 min (up to 20) from the cap. So `luxembourg_shoura` takes `mdf_12`'s absent-nights, ramped rule (D5), which never acts south of 53.56°N; 9 of the dataset's 14 Luxembourg mosques print the table, to the minute on each of those nights (support).
