Still open: the table runs 0–4 min inside the computed 13° times all year (implied Isha 12.6–13.0°, Fajr 12.8–12.9°), so its test rows use ±4 until a fit settles it (9.2); this does not touch the summer rule.
- **Q28. Tunisians living abroad (answered 2026-10-11)**: nothing is built for them alone; they use the traveller's features (a place by GPS or city, the per-country chooser and choice, the minute adjustments, the per-country Jumu'a time), and Google Play offers the release in every Play country but South Korea and Israel, since Play follows the user's Play country, not the phone's location (M9). Ramadan and Eid by their country's calendar (Appendix C) and their other needs wait for internationalization (14). Still open: if the Play Console's installs by country show a large share outside Tunisia, internationalization moves up.

## 14. Later, if wanted

- **Internationalization** (owner, 2026-10-11): the app for people who live in other countries. It would bring A13's name question; each country's Gregorian and Hijri month names and week (`ar-<CC>` for the Gregorian names and the week, the app's own table for the Hijri names; 6.10, 6.11, Q11, D19's alternative); and the 12-hour clock below. Tunisians who live abroad get features of their own only then (Q28), first Ramadan and Eid by their country's calendar (below).
- **A London profile** fitted from the timetable London's mosques share (5.6), for London places only (D4).
- **A trip planned ahead**: pick the destination before leaving, so the times are ready on arrival (Umrah and Hajj groups).
- **Ramadan and Eid outside Tunisia** (A10, A11): the Hijri source per place, the per-country announcements and their publisher, the Eid times, the follow-my-mosque editor, the Istanbul 2016 table, Jordan's eastern-zone table (Q27) and a crescent-visibility baseline, as Appendix C describes.
- **Match my mosque** (D14): fit a preset and offsets from a winter and a June row of the mosque's table, show the three closest, and apply nothing without confirmation. It suits Sudan and Lebanon too. In v1 the bounded «مخصّصة» does part of this by hand (6.11).
- **A French dawn and dusk observation campaign**, on OpenFajr's model, to settle E19's Isha floor and Fajr bounds (7.7, Q38).
- **More rows**: Imsak and first-adhan rows, which A14 leaves out of the first release, and, with internationalization, a 12-hour clock option (common in Egypt, the Gulf and the Levant).
- **Israel** (owner, 2026-10-11: not in the first release, added later): its catalogue rows and country default, the release on Google Play there (M9 leaves Israel out of the Countries/regions tab; Q28), the Arab towns there through Dar al-Ifta's own offsets (Nazareth, Umm al-Fahm, Haifa, Acre…; Lod's and Ramla's + 1.5 as + 2, as for Qalqilya, 8.8; their mapping is decided then, since in the dataset the mosques of Tamra, Kabul, Isfiya and Kafr Qasim use 0 where the list gives + 1), and the owner's review of the mosques the dataset files under Palestine (12).
- **Mosque TVs outside Tunisia**, in Europe and the Arab countries: a zone setting, the clock guard, the dashboard (6.12).
- **More European presets** as primary sources appear (the Arab authorities get theirs through AM10).
- **Worldwide**: the engine and the GPS path already work anywhere. It would need a wider catalogue and more presets.

## Appendix A: sources and verification notes

- **How the research ran.**
  - Europe (2026-10-08): a code map; research into European bodies; three competing designs for the time model, judged against each other; numbers recomputed with an independent prototype; two rounds of adversarial review (about a hundred problems in a first draft, then 34 in the revision, all fixed).
  - Arab countries (2026-10-08): 55 agents: 7 code readers, 21 country researchers each checked by an independent skeptic (own searches, own downloads, own solar code), and 6 topic researchers. The shared web-search budget ran out early, so most skeptics worked from direct fetches of official sites and tz sources, and from their own computations. What they could not check is in section 13. A second round of 100 agents reviewed the draft (9 reviewers: country facts in four groups, code claims, consistency with the Europe plan, engine and time zones, completeness, and writing rules; each finding checked by a skeptic); the 85 confirmed findings and the 72 minor ones were applied. ICOP's sources and the Jordan/UAE prototype were added on 2026-10-09.
  - The merge (2026-10-09): both plans were mapped section by section and their 39 points of disagreement listed. Each was resolved by the rule the plans themselves state (the more specific or later one), except five that neither settles: D17, D18, D19, A19 and the region order already asked by A15. The merged text was then checked against both former plans for lost content, broken references and the writing rules, in four review rounds. Two numbers were computed during the merge with a simple solar model: where the angle-based rule starts to bind (7.3) and Muscat's Fajr on 2026-10-25 (6.6).
  - The audience note and first decisions (2026-10-11): the owner said that the app is for Tunisians, that the first release abroad serves Tunisians who travel, and that internationalization comes later. D20 and Q28 were added, A13 was revised, and the per-country locale and week moved to 14. The owner then decided A1 (Western Sahara under Morocco), A3 (a fitted formula per country), A4 (no Shia method at all), A10 and A11 (no Ramadan or Eid outside Tunisia, the former design moved to Appendix C), A5 (Qatar's ministry), A6 and A19 (the tier-A chip «مطابقة للجدول الرسمي»), A15 (Arab countries built first, both regions released together), A8 (the actual year for perpetual tables), A12 (the nearest cities only), A14 (no extra rows), A16 (Algeria's fitted method at the spot, revised later that day; see below), A17 (Gaza's ministry), D7, D8, D1 (GPS and a bundled city list), D6's approach (follow the mosques' published practice), D12 (with the Dhuhr start: 12:40 in Tunisia, adhan + 5 elsewhere), D13 and A7, D14 (the calibrator later), D15 (thin-evidence presets only under «طرق أخرى», which changed D4's recommendation), D16 (validate, propose, cap at + 30 min), D17 (the most precise sun model per country), A18, D19 (Tunisian names everywhere) and D20 (no home delegation; nothing without auto-location), and left D9 to engineering; A9 follows from D8. Q18, Q24 and Q26 closed. A review of the revision (three reviewers, each finding checked by a skeptic) confirmed 35 findings; those not overtaken by later decisions were applied. The owner's mosque dataset (5.6) was then analysed by five analysts, each checked by a skeptic; the owner decided A2 per country, D5, D15's revision, the Comoros' Asr and the Asr question in GB and PT from it, and a web research into polar mosques settled D6 (7.4). The owner then approved D4 and decided D2, D3 and A13's listing lines, so every decision is settled; the mosque dataset turned out to be the owner's own collection, used as support, not as the source of truth. Applying that rule, the owner then moved Egypt back to EGSA's city tables, gave Sabratha Zawiya's published table, and kept only the published angles of France's 12° entry; D5 stayed angle-based. A second review of the revised text (three reviewers, each checked by a skeptic) confirmed 48 findings; those still current were applied. A third review (each finding checked by a skeptic) confirmed 60 findings; those not overtaken by the owner's later answers (Egypt's city tables, Jordan's interim mapping) were applied. A France review followed (regional practice in the dataset, the short-night rule on French calendars, published regional sources and overseas France; each analysis checked by a skeptic). From it the owner ordered the French list by département, kept the real 12° time, rejected the 2012 text of the French minutes, added «العشاء ليس قبل» to custom methods, kept a French pick for all of France, reopened Mayotte, and left the rest of overseas France to the plan. Libya's formula was then recovered (2026-10-11): 107 official rows were collected (live APIs and archived Awqaf homepages from 2016–2018 and 2025–2026); two independent fits were compared, and a skeptic recomputed the winner with its own code (medium confidence; Zawiya has 2 official days). The other open questions were then researched the same day: 31 answers (all of Q1–Q37 except Libya's Q14, answered by the recovery above, and the closed Q18, Q24, Q25, Q26 and Q31), each researched and checked on its own, were applied to section 13 and the sections they cite, and the decisions they touch (A2, A5, A7, A8, A18, D5, D12 and D15) and Appendix C were brought in line with them. The owner then set one rule for every country with an official source (2026-10-11, principle 5): the default reproduces the authority's times everywhere its tables apply, at its own places with their published or deduced offsets, elsewhere by its own rule for attaching a place, otherwise at the nearest official place in the country with no reach limit. Exact coordinates stay an option, and are the default only where the authority computes for any point (KACST). Tunisia is the first case (D7). The validity guard (6.5) was added with it. The per-country answers that apply the rule (the Arab authorities, Turkey, Bosnia and Herzegovina, Croatia, Slovenia, the Balkans, Austria, Luxembourg, Norway, Russia and overseas France), each researched and checked on its own, were applied (6.5, 7.4–7.6, 8.8). Algeria's monthly offsets were deduced from the ministry's own formula and are derived on the phone (8.8), which revised A16. A2, D4, D6 and D15 were brought in line with the rule.
  - France's nearby mosques (2026-10-12): the owner asked for a sound strategy as close as possible to nearby mosques. The dataset's 1,669 mainland French records with at least 200 usable days (7.7) were each predicted from the others (session scratchpad `france/local`: the former list, the nearest mosque, a per-day median, whole-timetable and per-prayer medoids, best-of-two and best-of-three; radii 3–30 km), each mosque's practice was fitted and scored on held-out days, and the envelope's moves were counted. A fiqh and science research (about 27 searches and 25 fetches; `france/fiqh/report.md`) set the envelope, and a second agent re-checked its main claims against the primary sources. Applied as 7.7, E19 and Q38. The same day two agents studied the apps used in France (international apps and libraries; the French mosque-display systems), a third re-checked their main claims, and each app's default was scored on the dataset (`france/local/l10_apps.py`); applied in 7.7. The owner then decided not to bundle mosques (2026-10-12): the nearby default and the mosque picker designed from this analysis were removed, and the bounded editor's reach was measured instead on the 17,627 records with at least 300 scored days (scratchpad `coverage`, `c1_editor.py`).
  - France data review (2026-10-13): an independent reviewer re-measured the dataset's French records with its own scripts, the database opened read-only and fingerprinted (`docs/prayer-times-beyond-tunisia-review.md`, 12.3–12.7; the git-ignored `work/france-prayer-review/`: `mosque_assessments.csv`, `repeated_window_flags.csv`, `window_flag_days.csv`, the Fajr, Maghrib and six-time analyses, `summary.json`, `quality/summary.md`, `dataset_manifest.json` and `sources/RESEARCH-NOTES.md`). The plan's own figures were then recomputed from its scripts (`france/local/l12_fajr_maghrib.py` and `l3_report.py`, `coverage/c2_report.py`) and compared record by record with the review's: the same 15 Fajr and 5 Maghrib records flagged against sunrise and sunset, the review's 56 iqama-like patterns all among the plan's 110 congregation-time listings, and 335 against 336 records more than 5 min later than the 12° instant. The differences come from the populations, from the plan reading every record as Paris time and from its 150-min filter, which had hidden most after-sunrise days. The recomputation also found 4 calendars whose Fajr falls on a block of days with the sun more than 20° below the horizon, which the review's sunrise-and-sunset window check does not test. 7.7 now gives both analyses, sorted into the review's four outcomes, and withdraws the plan's "wrong" for timetables that only differ from the conventions, its "after dawn under every view", its "sunrise − 37 to 72 min all year" and its seven Maghrib "data errors" put down to "entry mistakes in the mosques' systems". The review's audit took the plan's 1,669 for its own count of 1,669 assessable records (it found no exclusion list); the two sets swap two records each way (7.7).
  - The independent review (2026-10-13, `docs/prayer-times-beyond-tunisia-review.md`): its 11 findings were each checked against the plan and the code by one agent, with its numerical counterexamples recomputed, and the fix then tried and corrected by a second; all 11 were confirmed. Applied: the solver anchors each day's solar cycle on its civil date (6.2, Solver; Wallis-et-Futuna); one event-order contract with permitted ties, a night hold at its lowest point and consumers that share it (6.2, Event order; 6.9); semantic place changes stored at any distance (6.8); a silence endpoint domain with its scan and alarm codes (6.9); the validity guard as a pure calculation with a comparator per time family (6.5); the official-match chip tied to each zone's own held-out record with whole-year, all-time and Ramadan coverage (6.3 `FitStats`, 6.11, 9.5; Jordan's 2023 fit recomputed per zone and per month, 8.6); named places with their own areas, separate from `reachKm`, and one `switchDays` (6.5); a refreshed current-code baseline (5.1, 6.10, 9.1); and, from its France section, E19 as bounds on computed starts with the four outcomes for mosque timetables, APBIF's procedure and both France analyses (7.7).
  - The Arab second pass (2026-10-17): the owner asked for another pass at the official-times status of every Arab country, chose to reproduce printed tables (A8 revised) and to open tier A to any country that passes (9.5), and allowed downloads for Oman, Yemen, Egypt, the UAE and Jordan. Fetched: EGSA's monthly view for every month of 2026 (78 zones; four menu cities have no table) and Oman's calendar_page2 for every month of 2026 at its 80 other zones; Yemen's endpoint serves no future date, Jordan's search form answered HTTP 500 to every query, and the UAE's open-data API needs the owner's own registration on the federal portal. Egypt (78 of 78 zones), Oman (86 of 86) and Baghdad pass tier A; Bahrain's calendar computes each time at a point of its own (S6 `eventPoints`, new), which leaves its Asr two days short; Qatar's Doha stays four days short on a September Maghrib column printed about 17 s late; Kuwait, Libya, Lebanon, Palestine, Algeria (the ALMANAC sun tried) and Morocco (one official month) stay in tier B. The federal portal registers UAE residents only, so the owner wrote to Awqaf, which emailed its 2026 tables for nine locations on the condition that the app makes no money from them (2026-10-17; kept in test-data-local, never committed): eight pass tier A on the whole year, Al Ain with its sunrise at a point of its own (S6), and Dubai, IACAD's table, stays in tier B, its single-minute misses scattered over every month whatever the point. A third pass (2026-10-18, the owner's same request again): Bahrain passes tier A once each time's point is refined on a finer grid (0.0005° around every grid point within 2 days of the best), the grid's 0.005° having hidden a narrow peak of Asr's latitude (held out 343 then 348 of 355); Qatar's Doha stays short on its table's own September Maghrib run (one minute a day for 16 days, then two) and on Fajr's precision. New sources found that day: Syria's ministry serves year tables for 109 regions to its mosque screens and app «مسجدك» (`tv.masjidak.mow.gov.sy`, 100 distinct tables), which 6 of the owner's 75 Syrian mosques print to the minute all year and none prints the Ramadan tables `sy_awqaf` was fitted on, which differ from them by 1–2 minutes; later that day every screen table was reproduced as aladhan.com's PrayTimes (MWL, Dhuhr + 1, Maghrib + 2, its low-precision sun once per time at fixed hours), each for its own year and point, with its own row layout and its Asr at the time it was fetched, so `sy_awqaf` moved to the screens: 99 table zones, all at tier A (99.65–99.93 % exact held out), named from a sample of the screens' pages, and 28 live towns, where the screens run aladhan's API with its default for the point (every time exact but Asr, which is each screen's fetch time's; its README); Jordan's complete 2026 calendar (16 zones, every day, a PDF without a usable text layer); the Sunni Endowment's Baghdad API for any date (a held-out year for Baghdad). Lebanon's year table is one of those screen files (Syria's region 763), though not an aladhan computation. No past months exist online for Morocco, Libya, Mauritania, Gaza (the radio posted for three weeks only), Bahrain (no 1447 calendar), Sudan, Somalia or Djibouti. Gaza's ministry emailed its 2026 table at the owner's request, and `ps_gaza_awqaf` is now fitted on it (54–66 % exact and 98.4–99.7 % within 1 min held out: a table smoothed by hand). Records: each fit's README section.
  - Methods only where people follow them (2026-10-18): the owner ruled that a method is offered only where it makes sense, as Slovenia's does not in Saudi Arabia or New York. Until then every place outside Tunisia offered all 60 presets and profiles under «طرق أخرى». Now it offers the international conventions everywhere, and each body's or practice's method in its own area and at the places its tables name abroad (6.4). A stored choice whose method is not offered at the place gives way to the country's default; New York's list fell from 60 to 8, Riyadh's to 9.
  - Every region open (2026-10-19): the owner asked to remove the region switches, «all regions / countries should be available». The developer switches of 2 (`isRegionOpen`, `REGIONS_OPEN_IN_RELEASE`, the debug «مناطق التطوير» row and the closed-region result of a fix) were removed, so every build, a release included, takes a place in any country.
  - Chip names (2026-10-20): the owner found that «تقريبية» did not inspire confidence and asked for better names for it and for «مستخلصة من جداول». Names built on the source followed: «مبنية على جداول منشورة», «مبنية على مصادر محدودة» («which sources?!»), then one name per method. The owner turned them down: «either it is official or it is falakia». The chip is now «رسمية» or «فلكية» (A6, 6.11); the tier-A gate and its record stay as evidence in «طريقتك».
- **Code map.** Every file named in 5.1 was read. Line numbers are from the working tree of 2026-10-08, which has uncommitted changes in `MainActivity`, `PrefsManager`, `MainScreen`, and in the wake, adhkar and Quran files, so cited lines may shift.
- **Numbers recomputed for Europe** with an independent prototype (the app's Meeus sun, event-time iteration, UTC instants, the EU summer-time rule), then recomputed again by a reviewer from scratch:
  - the persistent-twilight counts and dates in 7.1;
  - Paris Isha 01:07 and Brest 01:21;
  - London: 48 nights without a 17° Isha in 2026;
  - the 32-minute shift of Tunis Fajr under an ungated one-seventh rule;
  - the Moonsighting vectors;
  - the angle-based Paris case;
  - the rule comparison of 7.3, with R ≈ 0.18.

  The reviewer also computed the Asr-gap days, the previous-day Fajr cases (Kazan, Vorkuta) and the Dublin effect of the 22:45 cap (up to 72 min).

  - Diyanet's European tables (namazvakitleri.diyanet.gov.tr) and the 2021 Istanbul congress declaration (kurul.diyanet.gov.tr).
  - IGGÖ (derislam.at, Vienna PDFs; on 2026-10-11 its nine city tables for April–December 2026, its method page, and its locate service `/api/prayer-times/at/`, which gives a city's table within 25 km of the city centre and, beyond, Diyanet's table for the nearest entry of Diyanet's Austrian list, 190 entries on namazvakitleri.diyanet.gov.tr).
  - Luxembourg Shoura (2026 PDF; its downloads page lists one table for the country, in French, English and Bosnian, and the Ramadan 2026 waqtiyya, with no other place or offset, read 2026-10-11).
  - Grande Mosquée de Paris (October 2026 timetable; Ramadan 2023 and 2024 imsakiyat, Q21).
  - Islamic Community of Bosnia and Herzegovina (Takvim 2025 from islamskazajednica.ba, its pages 8–12 for the town differences; vaktija.ba's open data and 2026 API).
  - Islamic Community in Croatia (islamska-zajednica.hr, «Službene vaktije IZ-e»: MIZ Zagreb's Ramadan 2025 and September 2021 sheets) and Islamic Community in Slovenia (vaktija.si's feed, October–December 2026; its Ramadan 2020 sheet), read 2026-10-11.
  - KMSH (Kalendari 2026, kmsh.al: Tirana's table, its city shifts on page 2).
  - The Balkans (read 2026-10-11): BIK's Takvim 1447–1448 (2026), the Presidency's PDF as posted on dituriaislame.com (bislame.net); the Chief Mufti's Office of Bulgaria, «Времена за намаз» (grandmufti.bg, 48 cities, October 2026); monteislam.com's vaktija (Podgorica, October 2026, and its town list); bfi.mk's daily Skopje times (2026-10-11: Sabahu 05:52, Dreka 12:28, Iqindia 15:31, Akshami 18:09, Jacia 19:16); vaktija.ba's open data (its places and per-town differences) and the IZ BiH Takvim 2025's time differences for the mešihats, as collected for Bosnia.
  - East London Mosque (London Unified explained, 2026 table).
  - The 1983 Bradford ruling.
  - ICCI Dublin: its website code (islamireland.ie, read 2026-10-11) and its own timetable API (settings updated 2018-06-04, Wayback Machine copy of 2022-08-02); the Islamic Foundation of Ireland's timetable API and its Ramadan 2026 tables for Dublin and 32 other towns (islamicfoundation.ie, read 2026-10-11) (Q23).
  - DUM RF (dumrf.ru: its homepage widget and monthly Moscow tables, May, July, August and October 2026 and June and July 2025, the summer sheets through web.archive.org; Q22).
  - IRN (bonnetid.info: its prayer-time project pages and its 2026 tables for 35 municipalities, read through the site's own table requests on 2026-10-11), as republished by the Tromsø mosque Alnor; islam.nu, PSIY and Félag Múslima (the polar research of 2026-10-11, 7.4).
  - DUM RT (dumrt.ru: its «Намаз вакытлары» page with the table notes and its 2026 workbook for 44 Tatarstan places, read 2026-10-11; local copy only, D13).
  - MWL Fiqh Council 1986 and 2009 with ICOP (astronomycenter.net), and the ECFR via IslamOnline. For Q20 (2026-10-11): the 1986 text as quoted by Egypt's Dar al-Ifta (fatwa 2259), ICOP's high-latitude page and its 2009 Brussels paper, and MWL's 2010 UK tables (astronomycenter.net/UK.pdf), checked against a simple solar model.
  - The CFCM and Grande Mosquée de Paris Ramadan 1447 announcements.
- **France's validity envelope** (2026-10-12): openfajr.org (research paper, May 2016, and its September 2016 addendum), cmrofficiel.fr (the Miribel observation of 2016-07-10 and the Mont Thou observation of 2017-05-25), NRIAG Journal of Astronomy and Geophysics 5(1), 2016 (doi 10.1016/j.nrjag.2016.02.001), astronomycenter.net (the Qassim study of 2012; FCNA's adoption of the Moonsighting functions, 2012-07-30, since replaced by FCNA's own suggestion of 15° in the USA and 13° in Canada all year: fiqhcouncil.org, 2021-11-08, from its 2016–2017 meetings), kurul.diyanet.gov.tr (the Istanbul 2021 final declaration; and, read 2026-10-13, its rulings on the end of Maghrib's time and on praying Fajr at the imsak time), dar-alifta.org (fatwas 8707 of 2025 and 7638 of 2021), shamela.ws (the Mudawwana; Mawahib al-Jalil), havredesavoir.fr (2026-02-03, the basis of 12°), apbif.fr (its methodology page and its 2026 tables, read 2026-10-13), moonsighting.com, shoura.lu (2023-02-12), alukah.net (the MWL 1986 resolution), islamqa.info 292307 (the MWL's 19th session), ccme.org.ma (the CFCM's 2009 commission), and the Moonsighting coefficients of adhan-js (MIT). The MWL 1986 «± 2 min» is found in the ECFR's English quotation of the resolution (fiqh.islamonline.net, «Praying and fasting at high latitudes»), not in an Arabic original. The re-check confirmed all eight claims it tested, with four wording fixes applied: OpenFajr's 8 min is a margin of error, not an imsak rule; the Istanbul declaration names the «Muslim Council of France» and «the Paris Mosque» among its attendees; fatwa 7638 reports the Maliki make-up day but itself follows the majority; the 12° basis is Havre de Savoir's account.
- **Prayer apps in France** (2026-10-12): Google Search's prayer card (live results), app.muslimpro.com (French city pages) and support.muslimpro.com, islamicfinder.org (Paris and Marseille), api.aladhan.com and its methods list, praytimes.org (v3.2, 2025), the adhan libraries' source (github.com/batoulapps), moonsighting.com, github.com/mawaqit (`mawaqit-js` types and README, `prayer-times`, the mobile app's strings; no mawaqit domain was opened), muslim-share.com (2024, updated 2026), masjidbox.com and its help pages, the App Store's French listings and charts (Muslim Pro, Mawaqit, the Grande Mosquée de Paris's app), bladi.net, yabiladi.com forum threads (2023-12-13, 2024-05-24), saphirnews.com (2012-08-01), mosquee-mirail-toulouse.fr, mosquee-bagnolet.com (2025-03-22).
- **France and overseas France** (2026-10-11): cmrofficiel.fr (the CTIR unified calendar and its 2020 PDF, still its only table in 2026), saphirnews.com (2019-12-03, the calendar's adoption by about 40 mosques), mosquee-strasbourg.com beside Diyanet's Strasbourg table, apbif.fr, havredesavoir.fr (2012, the UOIF/GEDIS calendar text; 2026, the 12° reasoning without minutes), mosquee-hautepierre.fr (2025), grandemosqueedeparis.fr (monthly images), islam-reunion.fr (the CIR's Ramadan 1444, 1445 and 1446 and Chawwal 1445 tables), freedom.fr (Baytoul Hayaate's Ramadan 1447 table), masjidbox.com (the Bras-Fusil mosque's display), ccim.mq, horaires-priere-mayotte.fr (its 2026 PDF) with its publisher in the national company register (recherche-entreprises.api.gouv.fr), the Assemblée nationale's amendment 564 to the Mayotte bill (2025-06-19, on the Conseil cadial), the Cayenne mosque's 2010 table, and A2CMGF's a2cmgf.com (January 2026 monthly images from IslamicFinder, live table) and its mosque's Masjidbox page (read 2026-10-11). musulmansdefrance.fr no longer serves MF's pages (a casino page, rechecked for Q32 on 2026-10-11; fr.wikipedia still lists it as MF's site), and iqrashop.com's 2026 «UOIF» calendar comes from the seller's own generator and states no angle or minutes.
- **The owner's mosque dataset** (5.6): 17,758 mosques in 63 countries, collected by the owner from the mosques; git-ignored, never committed, and used as support, not as the source of truth.
- **Friday times in Europe** (2026-10-11, Q30): alfaruq.de (Essen, 2026 page), prayer-times.co.uk/jummah (updated 2026-07-17), French, German and British mosque pages found by search, grandemosqueedeparis.fr, islamireland.ie, izwien.at, and vaktija.ba's API (Sarajevo's computed podne).
- **Arab primary sources, by country.**
  - SA: ummulqura.org.sa and its API at umqserv.kacst.gov.sa; the ministry's circulars as reported by Okaz and by SPA (al-madina.com, 2021-04-12: Isha two hours after Maghrib in Ramadan).
  - EG: esa.gov.eg/praytimes.aspx; Dar al-Ifta's prayer page and widget (2026-10-11, 8.8); Dar al-Ifta fatwas 3012 and 4021.
  - MA: habous.gov.ma/prieres, and its archived tables of 2024 on web.archive.org (Rabat, captures 20240604112144 and 20241222165442; Laayoune, 20240604140741; Q5); Bulletin Officiel 7521 (decree 2.26.530).
  - DZ: marw.gov.dz/media/calendrier/1448/ (Algiers, Djelfa and Adrar) and media/calendrier/1447/Alger.pdf (the held-out year of A16, no longer linked from the site); craag.dz (products and services).
  - LY: awqaf.gov.ly; api.ifta.ly; lcrsss.ly.
  - MR: affairesislamiques.mr imsakiyas and its 2024-03-10 notice.
  - JO: awqaf.gov.jo/ar/Pages/PrayerTime; opendata.gov.jo; aliftaa.jo resolution 250.
  - PS: darifta.ps perpetual table (ssalah2012.pdf) and imsakiyas, including the 2022 summer imsakiya.
  - LB: darelfatwa.gov.lb (the Ramadan 1445 imsakiya, media item 15487); tv.masjidi.app (region tables 2 and 3); bayynat.org.lb.
  - SY: t.me/SyMOfE (posts 564–568, 596–598, 6569–6582, and 10403 of 2026-08-31 on the app «مسجدك»); masjidak.mow.gov.sy (8.8).
  - IQ: dws.gov.iq and its API; t.me/SunniaffairsIq (posts 8010 and 14073); alkafeel.net; sistani.org rulings.
  - KW: the ministry sheets as photographed on awgatalsalat.com; the ministry fatwa committee's ruling on regional time differences, in its volume «كتاب العبادات - باب الصيام» (content.awqaf.gov.kw/release2/2026/6/46a53c93fd0642829958aedbe6f38390.pdf, a 19 MB file dated 2026-06-03; read on 2026-10-11 only through a search extract, since the PDF is too large to open).
  - QA: the Ministry of Awqaf API; qatarch.com wall calendars and `/cal` data.
  - BH: almajles.gov.bh/Programs/calendar/ (the SCIA's method page and its 1448 calendar PDFs, 2026); GDN, 2021-08-05; GeoNames `cities1000` and Jazīrat Ḩawār (290243), 2026-10-11.
  - AE: awqaf.gov.ae and its English locale; its prayer-times page's area list and script (2026-10-11); GeoNames for Al Ghadeer and Al Yarmook; the federal Open Data API listing; The National and Khaleej Times (2025-12-10), Gulf News (2025-12-16 and 2026-01-02) and Al Khaleej on the Friday time.
  - OM: mara.gov.om calendar pages.
  - YE: the awqaf.gov.ye endpoint.
  - DJ: Al-Qarn (2026-02-19). KM: the 2018 constitution, art. 97–98; beit-salam.km.
  - SO, DJ, KM (Q16, 2026-10-11): mesa.gov.so, sonna.so, alqarn.dj and alwatwan.net, and web searches in Arabic, French and Somali; none shows a table.
- **Expert secondary source.** ICOP's Salat Calendar (salatcalendar.com, with IACAD): its country presets, read from its settings page on 2026-10-09; its help page (`/index.php/help`), which defines «تلقائي» and its defaults; its city pages for Muscat and Khartoum; and its "official times" pages for Jordan and the UAE (8.5). Also ICOP's Accurate Times 5.7, installed by the owner: its help file, location database and output headers, read in place on 2026-10-09 (8.5). Supportive evidence only. ICOP is also the MWL's partner in the 2009 rule, Local Relative Estimation, which D5 keeps as an option.
- **Libraries and software, as references and cross-checks only.** None is used at runtime.
  - batoulapps adhan-js 4.4.6 and adhan-kotlin 0.0.7 (MIT): method tables, the Moonsighting coefficients, the night definition, rounding.
  - praytimes.org v2 (LGPL, not copied) and praytime.js v3.2 (MIT).
  - The islamic-network PHP library (LGPL, not copied), and the Aladhan method list.
  - meypod's adhan-kotlin fork (MIT): a fit of Diyanet's takdir, used only as a cross-check.
  - None of them models Saudi rounding, Oman's offsets, Jordan's horizon or Yemen's Isha 16°. Only the Dart library prayer_time_plus has Oman's + 5 offsets, with a wrong Isha + 1.
- **Astronomy.** JPL Horizons DE441 (the accuracy of the app's sun, 2020–2100), the USNO Astronomical Applications API, NOAA's solar-calculator notes, Meeus; VSOP87D from the CDS archive (VI/81) for the prototype.
- **Time.** Directive 2000/84/EC and the stalled COM(2018) 639; IANA tz `africa`, `asia`, `backward` and NEWS (2016a–2026e); AOSP's documentation of the time-zone Mainline module; libcore's `IcuZoneRulesProvider`; `java.time` and `GregorianCalendar` on Android.
- **Hijri.** ICOP's official first-day lists for 1445–1447; R. H. van Gent's Umm al-Qura pages; the Android and OpenJDK `hijrah-config` files; ICOP's Global Hijri Calendar paper (Odeh, updated 2022-10) and the Istanbul 2016 congress's final statement (Q27).
- **Tunisians abroad and Google Play** (Q28, read 2026-10-11): more than 1.8 million Tunisians live abroad (the OTE's director, La Presse, 2026-05-11), more than 1.5 million of them in Europe: about 1 million in France, 269,000 in Italy and 119,000 in Germany (Tunisie Numérique, 2025-12-04; the year of the counts is not stated). Google Play Help, answer 7431675 (the Play country follows the payment profile and changes at most once per 90 days), and Play Console Help, answers 7550024 (availability follows the user's Play country, not the current location) and 6223646 (South Korea: a KCC licence for developers who collect location for location-based services; the EU: no unjustified geo-blocking).
- **Places.** GeoNames export, dumps and readme (CC BY 4.0); Wikidata (CC0); Natural Earth terms; OSM and geoBoundaries licences.
- **Not used.** mawaqit.net: its robots.txt disallows Anthropic agents, and mosque figures that came from its pages during research were discarded. Muslim Pro and Aladhan were consulted for their documented defaults only.
- **Kept locally.** The Arab research's downloaded tables, transcriptions, fit scripts and reports were copied from the session's scratch folder to the git-ignored `test-data-local/research-2026-10-08/` (one folder per country group, with a checksummed manifest and each reviewer's reasons). Left out: byte-identical duplicates, renders the kept files regenerate, bytecode, generic site bundles and pages without prayer data, and files holding session cookies or third parties' personal data. The Jordan and UAE prototype data is in `test-data-local/arab-prototype/`, with its scripts in `scripts/prayer_formula/arab_prototype/`. AM0's collectors extend these under the A7 rules.

## Appendix C: kept for later: Ramadan, Eid and the Hijri date abroad

The owner decided on 2026-10-11 that the first release adds no Ramadan or Eid support outside Tunisia (A10, A11). This is the design the research produced for it, kept as it stood so that later work can start from it. In the first release, D10, D11, D18, A10 and A11 are closed by that decision, and three answers of 2026-10-11 update this design: Q27 (Jordan's estimate and the phase-2 table below), Q4 (Saudi Isha + 120 on the announced Ramadan, C.1) and Q7 (Bahrain's Eid rule, C.2). Cross-references below point to the sections as they were.

### C.1 The Hijri calendar per place

- **Source per place (D10, A11).**
  - A new shared sealed `HijriCalendarSource`: `TUNISIA`, `UMM_AL_QURA`, `ISTANBUL_2016`, and `Announced(country)`.
  - It is held in an in-memory `HijriSourceSetting` (a StateFlow, default TUNISIA). The phone sets it at start-up from its own prefs. The TV and the desktop never set it, so both keep today's behaviour.
  - The phone derives the source from the place's country: TUNISIA for a Tunisian place, `Announced(CC)` in the Arab League, UMM_AL_QURA elsewhere. Western Sahara places use Morocco's announcements, `Announced(MA)`, as they use Morocco's Habous tables (A1). A user's explicit pick is stored per country (`hijri_source_<CC>`, 6.4) and wins: a Tunisian living in Europe can choose TUNISIA without it following them to Egypt.
- **`Announced`.** Manual dates first, then the country's announced dates, then Umm al-Qura. Under every source but TUNISIA, manual anchors are event-local (below).
- **One gate.** `OfficialIslamicDateStore` gets a `source: () -> HijriCalendarSource` (default TUNISIA).
  - Its private `officialSnapshot()` returns the Tunisian announcements, and the legacy cached override, only under TUNISIA; under `Announced` it returns that country's announcements.
  - `calendar()`, `calendarWith()` and `officialCalendar()` pass the source's baseline and anchor mode, and include the source in their cache keys.
  - `IslamicDays.yearDates` and the editor therefore never measure a European or Egyptian phone against Tunisia's dates.
  - `RamadanOverrideChecker.shouldStopPolling` reads the raw `updates` flow, but only tests call it.
- **One calendar for everything.** Today the date row and the Hijri dialog (through `rememberTunisianHijriCalendar`), the rescheduling observer in `TunisianPrayerTimesApplication`, and `MainScreen`'s refresh of the silence controls (`:332`, `:481–485`) all read the announcements without the manual dates, and the observer never fires on a manual change. Silence, the Eid windows and Ramadan detection already use `calendar()` with them. All four move to one `calendarUpdates` flow: announcements, manual dates and source.
- **Baseline and start source.** `TunisianHijriCalendar` gets an injected baseline (default Umm al-Qura, identical output). A query `startSource(year, month): DateSource` (MANUAL, OFFICIAL, ESTIMATE) tells a manual start from an announced or estimated one.
  - It is computed from separate sets of manual and announced starts. Day 30 of a month follows its month's start.
  - It is not a constructor field of `HijriCalendarDate` or `HijriCalendarMonth`, so their equality, `copy` and existing tests stay as they are.
- **Polling.** `RamadanOverrideChecker.shouldStartPolling()` and `OfficialIslamicDateStore.loadYear` act under TUNISIA and `Announced`, each on its own data and cache keys, and do nothing under UMM_AL_QURA or ISTANBUL_2016. So a European phone never polls, and an Egyptian phone polls only the merged file below.
- **Files.** Other countries' dates live in `data/official-islamic-dates/<CC>/<hijriYear>.json`, with the same schema (`docs/aid-and-ramadan-date-corrections.md`).
  - The Pages deployment merges them into one generated file per Hijri year, `data/official-islamic-dates/all/<hijriYear>.json` (under 5 KB), as it already generates the legacy `ramadan-override-<year>.json`. The device fetches only that file and picks its own entry, so the request names no country. `docs/privacy-policy.html` can keep saying that no personal data is sent in this request.
  - They ship offline like Tunisia's file: `BundleDataAssets` includes `**/*.json` (today it takes top-level `*.json` only, `BundleDataAssets.java:38`), and `SharedAndroid.init` imports each folder into that country's store (today only the top-level files, into the Tunisian store, `SharedAndroid.kt:23`). The cache key `official_islamic_dates_$hijriYear` gains the folder id.
- **Iraq, Lebanon and Bahrain** read their Sunni or national announcements: `IQ-sunni`, `LB-sunni` and Bahrain's national file. No Shia file is kept (A4).
  - Kurdistan places read `IQ-sunni`, with the «+1 يوم» quick fix (Q8).
- **Today.** For every source but TUNISIA, the phone sets the existing `RamadanOverrideChecker.todayProvider` to today in the place's zone, since announcements are per country. Under TUNISIA it keeps Tunisia's date. The TV's own provider and the `Africa/Tunis` fallback are unchanged, and the shared code is not edited for this. On the phone, "today" only feeds the polling windows and the current-year cache.
- **Follow my mosque.**
  - **Shared editor logic.** The TV's editor logic (`automaticDate`, `stepDate`, `DateStep`, `UpcomingDates`) moves from `IslamicDatesSection.kt` into shared `IslamicDays.kt` as public declarations. `IslamicDatesSection.kt` keeps thin internal forwarders with the same names and package (`typealias DateStep`, forwarding functions), so `DashboardBackendImpl` and the TV tests compile unchanged. The composable `rememberUpcomingDates` stays in the TV.
  - **Phone editor.** A phone `IslamicDatesEditor` uses this logic: Ramadan, Eid al-Fitr and Eid al-Adha, each ±1 day within ±3 days, with «تلقائي» and a conflict marker.
  - **Event-local anchors.** Today a manual Ramadan start also carries its offset to the following Eids (`TunisianHijriCalendar.reconcile`). That suits the TV, but under UMM_AL_QURA it would move the Eids wrongly: in France in 2026, Ramadan was one day after Umm al-Qura while Eid al-Fitr was the same day. For every source but TUNISIA, manual anchors are therefore event-local, through a parameter whose default keeps the TV's behaviour.
  - **Scoped by source.** `ManualIslamicDates` gains an optional `source` field holding the full source, `Announced(country)` included, defaulting to TUNISIA, which is what the TV writes. The calendar ignores manual dates set under another source. A correction made for a Paris mosque therefore does not follow a traveller back to Tunisia, where the official announcement applies again, and one made under `Announced(EG)` does not apply under `Announced(SA)`. When travel changes the source, the editor offers to keep or clear them.
- **Estimate and marks.**
  - Under TUNISIA: «بداية الشهر مؤكدة في تونس» for OFFICIAL starts; «حسب ما حدّدتَه» for MANUAL starts, under every source.
  - In Saudi Arabia the date row shows the Umm al-Qura civil date, the Kingdom's official calendar, with no mark; Ramadan and Eid behaviour (silence, Eid) follow the Supreme Court's announcements.
  - In the other countries of the first group of 5.4, the estimate is Umm al-Qura marked «تقديري». In the + 1 groups it is marked «تقديري، وقد يتأخر يومًا», with «+1 يوم» (the «+1» in an LTR isolate) on the date row from the 29th. Tapping it stores a manual anchor that starts the month announced that evening one day after Umm al-Qura's, so the current month gets a 30th day and later Hijri dates show one day less. An announced start under `Announced` gets a badge (D18).
  - In Europe, the Umm al-Qura estimate is marked as D18 decides, and its explainer calls it an estimate.
- **Jordan's estimate** is the Global Hijri Calendar's eastern zone, which its ministry follows for month starts (8.6). This is ICOP's two-zone calendar (Odeh, 2001, updated 2022-10), not the unified Istanbul 2016 calendar (Q27): a month starts the day after an evening on which, on Odeh's criterion, the crescent is visible by telescope or better from any land between 180°E and 20°W. It matched all 5 of Jordan's listed Ramadan and Shawwal starts of 1445–1447 (Diyanet's Istanbul 2016 dates missed Ramadan 1445 and Shawwal 1446, and Umm al-Qura missed 3), but not Dhu al-Hijja 1445, which Jordan began with Saudi Arabia on 2024-06-07, a day before the eastern zone. So under `Announced(JO)`, months not yet announced fall back to the eastern-zone table below, and Dhu al-Hijja to Umm al-Qura, marked «تقديري» without the + 1 group's «وقد يتأخر يومًا». ICOP's paper names the UAE too, but the UAE's announced starts matched Umm al-Qura in all 9 events, so it keeps Umm al-Qura.
- **Ramadan days in the methods.** Saudi Isha + 120 follows the Supreme Court's announced Ramadan, the month the ministry's rule names (its circular, reported by SPA on 2021-04-12: two hours between Maghrib and Isha in Ramadan, by the Umm al-Qura timing). Before any announcement it falls back to `UMM_AL_QURA_COMPUTED` (E8), as KACST's tables do (Q4). `umm_al_qura` follows the place's calendar.
- **Publishing (A11).** The existing PR job (`.github/workflows/check-islamic-dates.yml` with `scripts/detect_tunisian_lunar_dates.py`) is generalised. It keeps its four evening runs in the event windows, because announcements come after Maghrib on the 29th. It reads ICOP's "official first day" lists and opens one PR per country file, each on its own branch, for a human to merge. The device still reads only the project's Pages.
- **Ramadan Isha silence bump.** `PrefsManager`'s 90-minute silence bump (`RAMADAN_ISHA_MINUTES`) is keyed, with its after-Ramadan restore test, on the place's calendar year instead of `HijrahDate.now()`; the restore still runs first and at every place (6.10). In Tunisia it now follows the Tunisian Ramadan dates. It stays relative to whatever Isha the method gives, and is unrelated to Saudi Arabia's Maghrib + 90 Isha.
- **Eid prayer (D11, A10).** See 6.9. The Eid row shows «حدّد وقت صلاة العيد في مسجدك» two days before.
- **Phase 2: Istanbul 2016 (D10).** The month starts are precomputed at build time for 1441–1530 AH by the same shared Kotlin code: Meeus new moon and Moon position, the NASA Delta T polynomials, a sunset-terminator search and an Americas land mask. They ship as a table and serve as the injected baseline, with Umm al-Qura outside that range. The table ships only once it reproduces the published Diyanet/ECFR starts for 1440–1448 AH, including Ramadan 1447 = 2026-02-19 and Shawwal 1447 = 2026-03-20. Jordan does not use it (Q27). The same code builds Jordan's eastern-zone table for 1441–1530 AH: Odeh's criterion (the arc of vision against the topocentric crescent width at the best time, visible by telescope or better) over land between 180°E and 20°W. It ships only once the code, run over 1428–1450 AH, reproduces ICOP's published eastern-zone table (kept in `test-data-local/`, never committed) and Jordan's announced Ramadan and Shawwal starts, including Ramadan 1445 = 2024-03-12 and Shawwal 1446 = 2025-03-31.

### C.2 Eid prayer time

**Eid prayer time (D11, A10).** `PrayerTimesRepository.defaultEidPrayerTime(place, choice, date)` is chosen by the place's region first:

- a Tunisian place: sunrise;
- an Arab League place: the method's `eid` rule. Sunrise + 35 for `ps_dar_al_ifta`; a sun altitude of about 4.2° for `sa_umm_al_qura`; the sun's lower limb 4° above the horizon for `bh_calendar` (the SCIA's stated rule, Q7); EGSA's fitted rule for `eg_egsa` once collected; sunrise + 20 otherwise;
- anywhere else: the later of sunrise + 20 min (the sunrise possibly estimated) and 08:00 at the place (D11).

It is computed as instants (`date.atTime(8, 0).atZone(place.zone)`), then shown in the phone's clock (D8), so a London place on a Paris phone gets 08:00 London time. `MainScreen:2208/2217`, `EidSilenceWindows:59–60` and `SilenceScheduler.eidPrayerTime` (`:674–680`) all call it. The shared `getDefaultEidPrayerTime(Int)` stays for the TV and the desktop. The Eid rule is a method field, not an E11 extra, because extras are never silenced.
